## XXVIII Cope Absent from a Wedding / 第二十八章 科普缺席一场婚礼

===Original===
Cope went out on the square with his being a-tingle. If Hortense, on another occasion, had thrown a dash of brine, on this occasion she had rubbed in the salt itself. And he had struck a harsh blow in turn; the flat of his mind was still stinging, as if half the shock of the blow had remained behind. “But it was no time for half-measures,” he muttered to himself. “Not again; not twice!” he repeated.

Hortense remained for several days in a condition of sullen anger﻿—she was a cloud lit up by occasional unaccountable flashes of temper. “Whatever in the world is the matter with her?” asked her aunt in more directions than one. And Amy Leffingwell, blissfully busy over her little trousseau and her selection of china-patterns, protested and opened wide, inquiring blue eyes against the intrusion of such a spirit at such a joyous time.

But Hortense, though better days intervened now and then, did not improve essentially; and she contrived at the climacteric moment of Amy’s career to make herself felt﻿—unduly felt﻿—after all.

===Chinese===
科普（Cope）走到外面的广场上，周身还兀自震颤。如果说霍滕丝（Hortense）早先一回不过是往伤口上泼了一瓢盐水，这一回她简直是抓起盐粒直往伤口里揉。而他也回敬了一记不留情面的打击；他的心却像挨了刀背的一击，至今仍在刺痛发麻，仿佛那一击的震力有一半滞留未散。「可是那个时候容不得半点手软，」他喃喃自语。「不能再来了；决不能来第二回！」他又说了一遍。

霍滕丝一连几天郁郁含怒——她像一朵阴云，间或被无端迸发的脾气之电照亮。「她这到底是犯了什么毛病？」她姨妈四面八方、逢人便问。而艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）正喜滋滋地忙着张罗自己小小的一份嫁妆、挑选瓷器的花色，对这样一种心绪竟要侵扰这般喜庆的时节，睁大了一双探询的蓝眼睛，表示抗议。

然而霍滕丝虽说间或也有几天好转，实质上并没有见好；她终究还是在艾米一生的转捩关头，设法叫人感觉到了她的存在——过分地感觉到了。

===Original===
The wedding took place during the latter half of April, as demanded by the enterprising wooer. Then there would be a rapid ten-day wedding-journey, followed by a prompt, businesslike occupancy of the new apartment on the first of May exactly.

Pearson’s parents prepared to welcome Amy handsomely; and her own people﻿—some of them﻿—came on from Iowa to attend the ceremony. There was her mother, who had been rather disconcerted by the sudden shift, but who was satisfied with George Pearson the moment she saw him, and who found him even more vivid and agreeable than Amy’s photograph of him had led her to expect. There was the aunt, who had lived a bare, starved life, and who luxuriated, along with her sister, in the splendor of the Louis Quinze chamber. And there was a friendly, wide-awake brother of fourteen who was tucked away in the chintz room upstairs, whence he issued to fraternize in the ballroom with Joe Foster, whose exacerbated spirit he did much to soothe.

This young brother was alert, cheery, chatty. He was not at all put out by Foster’s wheeled chair and eyeshade, nor by the strange contortions which Foster went through when, on occasion, he left the chair for a couch or for some chair of ordinary type. He got behind the wheels, and together they made the tour of the landscapes, marines, and genre-pieces which covered the walls. The boy was sympathetic, without being obtrusively so, and his comments on the paintings were confident and unconventional. “So different from *ce cher* Pelouse,” said Foster, with a grimace. He enjoyed immensely the fragmental half-hours given him through those two days. His young companion was lavish in his reports on life’s vast vicissitudes at Fort Lodge, and was always ready with comparisons between things as observed in his home town and in Churchton itself. He came as a tonic breeze; and the evening after he departed, Foster, left moping alone in the letdown which followed the festivities, said to himself more than once, “If I had had a boy, I should have wanted him just like Dick.”

===Chinese===
婚礼应那位锐意进取的求婚者的要求，安排在四月下旬举行。随后是紧凑的十日新婚旅行，接着恰在五月一日当天，干脆利落、一派公务作风地住进新公寓。

皮尔逊家的父母准备隆重体面地迎待艾米；她自己家里的人——来了一部分——从艾奥瓦（Iowa）州赶来观礼。有她的母亲，她本来为这突如其来的变故弄得有些手足无措，可一见到乔治·皮尔逊（George Pearson）本人便满意了，觉得他比艾米那张照片让她预期的还要精神、还要讨人喜欢。有那位姨妈，她向来过的日子清寒匮乏，此刻同她姐妹一道，在路易十五（Louis Quinze）式卧房的富丽堂皇里尽情受用。还有一位友善而机灵的十四岁弟弟，被安置在楼上那间印花布装饰的房间里，从那里出来，到舞厅里去同乔·福斯特（Joe Foster）亲热做伴，把后者那颗焦躁的心抚慰了不少。

这位小弟弟机敏、快活、健谈。福斯特那把轮椅、那副遮光眼罩，一点儿也没叫他觉得别扭；福斯特偶尔从轮椅挪到卧榻或某把寻常椅子上时要经历的那番古怪的挣扎扭动，也没叫他感到不适。他站到轮椅后面推起车来，两人便一同把满墙的风景画、海景画和风俗画巡览了一遍。这孩子富于同情心，却丝毫不显突兀，对那些画的评点自信而不落窠臼。「同我那位亲爱的珀卢斯（*ce cher* Pelouse）真是天壤之别，」福斯特说着做了个鬼脸。那两天里零零星星分给他的每一段半小时，他都受用至极。他的小伙伴谈起福特洛奇（Fort Lodge）小城人生中的万般沧桑来滔滔不绝，而且总爱拿家乡的所见所闻，同丘奇顿（Churchton）本地的事物比较一番。他来时好比一阵提神的清风；待他走后的那个晚上，福斯特独自闷坐在喜庆散后的萧索里，不止一次对自己说：「我要是有过一个男孩，我就巴望他同迪克（Dick）一个模样。」

===Original===
Dick’s mother and aunt stood up as well as they could against the bustling, emphatic geniality of Medora Phillips; and they were able, after a little, to adjust themselves to the prosperity of the Pearsons. These, they came to feel, were essentially of the same origin and traditions as themselves: just plain people who, however, had settled on the edge of the Big Town to make money and had made it. Pearson the elder was hardly more prepotent than Mr. Lusk, the banker at home. George himself was a dashing go-ahead: if he turned into a tired businessman his wife would know how to divert him.

Medora Phillips provided rice. Also she satisfied herself as to where, if the newer taste were not too delicate, she could put her hand on an old shoe. She was happy to have married off Amy; she would be still happier once Amy got away. More room would be left for other young people. By “other young people” she meant, of course, certain young men. By “certain young men” she thought she meant Cope and Lemoyne. Of course she meant Cope only.

“If Carolyn keeps amiable and if Hortense contrives to regain her good-nature, we may have some pleasant days yet,” she mused.

===Chinese===
迪克的母亲和姨妈竭其所能地招架梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）那股子忙忙叨叨、不容分说的热络；过不多时，她们也就调适过来，能坦然面对皮尔逊家的富足了。她们渐渐觉得，这家人同她们本质上同源同俗：不过是些普普通通的人家，只是落户在大都会的边上挣钱，而且当真挣到了手。老皮尔逊的威风气派，并不见得赛过家乡那位银行家卢斯克（Lusk）先生。乔治本人则是个风风火火、敢想敢干的闯将：他日后纵然变成了个疲惫的生意人，他妻子也自有法子给他散心解闷。

撒场用的大米，梅多拉·菲利普斯早已备办妥当。她还预先摸清了底：只要新派的雅趣不至于太娇贵，她能从哪儿一伸手便拿到一只旧鞋（按：西俗婚礼，掷米、掷旧鞋皆系祝吉旧仪）。把艾米打发出了阁，她很高兴；待艾米一走远，她还会更高兴。家里便可腾出更多地方，容纳别的年轻人。所谓「别的年轻人」，她指的当然是某些青年男士；所谓「某些青年男士」，她自以为指的是科普和勒莫因（Lemoyne）；当然，她指的只是科普一个人。

「只要卡罗琳保持和气，霍滕丝也能想法子找回她的好脾气，咱们兴许还能过几天快活日子。」她暗自思量。

===Original===
But Hortense did not regain her good-nature; she did not even maintain her self-control. In the end, the ceremony was too much for her. George and Amy had plighted their troth in a floral bower, which ordinarily was a bay window, before a minister of a denomination which did not countenance robes nor a ritual lifted beyond the chances of wayward improvisation; and after a brief reception the new couple prepared for the motorcar dash which was to take them to a late train. In the big wide hallway, after Amy had kissed Carolyn and thanked her for her poem and was preparing for the shower of rice which she had every reason to think she must face, there was a burst of hysterical laughter from somewhere behind, and Hortense Dunton, to the sufficing words, “O Bertram, Bertram!” emitted with sufficing clearness, fainted away.

Her words, if not heard by all the company, were heard by a few to whom they mattered; and while Hortense, immediately after the departure of the happy pair, was being revived and led away, they left occasion for thought. Carolyn Thorpe cast a startled glance. The aunt from Iowa, who knew that Bertrams did not grow on every bush, and whose senses the function had preternaturally sharpened for any address from Romance, seized and shook her sister’s arm; and, later on, in a Louis Quinze *causeuse*, upstairs, they agreed that if young Cope really had had another claimant on his attention, it was all the better that their Amy had ended by taking George. And Medora Phillips, in the front hall itself﻿—

Well, to Medora Phillips, in the front hall, much was revealed as in a lightning-flash, and the revelation was far from agreeable. What advantage in Amy’s departure if Hortense continued to cumber the ground? Hortense must go off somewhere, for a sojourn of a month or more, to recover her health and spirits and to let the house recover its accustomed tone of cheer.

===Chinese===
然而霍滕丝并没有找回好脾气；她甚至连自持都维持不住。到头来，这场典礼终究叫她受不住了。乔治和艾米在一座花亭之下——那地方平日不过是一扇凸窗——当着一位牧师互订了终身；这位牧师所属的教派，既不认法衣礼袍，也不认任何超出任性即兴之所能的仪文；简短的招待会后，新婚夫妇便整装待发，准备乘车疾驰，赶一班晚车。在那宽敞的大厅里，艾米吻过卡罗琳（Carolyn），谢过她赠的诗，正准备迎接那阵她有十足理由料定躲不过的米雨，忽听得后头不知哪里爆出一阵歇斯底里的狂笑，紧接着，霍滕丝·邓顿（Hortense Dunton）一声「哦，伯特伦，伯特伦！」——吐字足够清楚，尽够该听见的人听见——便晕厥了过去。

她这句话纵然没有传入满座宾客的耳朵，却传到了几个与它关系非浅的人耳中；幸福的一对刚一离开，霍滕丝便随即被救醒、被搀走，而这句话却留下了叫人寻思的余地。卡罗琳·索普（Carolyn Thorpe）投去一记惊愕的目光。从艾奥瓦来的那位姨妈，深知伯特伦这样的青年并非遍地皆是，况且这场大典又令她的感官对任何来自罗曼史的言语变得超乎寻常地敏锐，一把抓住她姐妹的手臂摇了又摇；稍后，在楼上路易十五式的小双人沙发（*causeuse*）里，两人达成了共识：倘若年轻的科普当真另有一人竞逐着他的心思，那么她们的艾米到头来嫁的是乔治，就更是万幸了。至于前厅里的梅多拉·菲利普斯本人嘛——

是啊，就在那前厅里，一道闪电似的，许多事在梅多拉·菲利普斯眼前豁然显明，而这番昭示远非令人愉快。艾米走了，霍滕丝却还占着地盘碍事，这于事何补？霍滕丝必须出门到什么地方去，盘桓一个月或者更久，一来将养身体和精神，二来也好让这所房子恢复它惯常的欢快基调。

===Original===
Medora forced these considerations to the back of her mind and saw most of her guests out of the house. Toward the end of it all she found herself relaxing in the library, with Basil Randolph in the opposite chair. Randolph himself had figured in the ceremony. This had been a crude imitation of a time-hallowed form and had allowed for an extemporaneous prayer and for a brief address to the young couple; but it had retained the familiar inquiry, “Who giveth﻿—?” “Who *can* give?” asked Medora of Amy. Poor Joe was rather out of the question, and Brother Dick was four or five years too young. Was there, then, anyone really available except that kind Mr. Randolph? So Basil Randolph, after remembering Amy with a rich and handsome present, had taken on a paternal air, had stepped forward at the right moment, and was now recovering from his novel experience.

The two, as they sat there, said little, though they looked at each other with half-veiled, questioning glances. Medora, indeed, improvised a little stretch of silent dialogue, and it made him take his share. She felt dislocated, almost defeated. Hortense’s performance had set her to thinking of Bertram Cope, and she figured the same topic as uppermost in the mind of Basil Randolph.

===Chinese===
梅多拉强把这些思虑压回心底，把大多数客人送出了家门。到诸事将尽时，她发觉自己正在书房里松泛下来，对面椅子上坐着的是巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph）。伦道夫本人在典礼里也扮演过一个角色。那典礼是对一种古老公式的粗糙仿造，容得下一段即兴的祷告和对新人的一番简短致辞，却保留了那句人们耳熟能详的问话：「何人许嫁——？」「可谁*能*许呢？」梅多拉替艾米发问。可怜的乔全然不在考虑之列，迪克老弟又差着四五岁年纪。那么，除了那位好心的伦道夫先生，还有谁当真派得上用场呢？于是，巴兹尔·伦道夫在以一份丰厚体面的贺礼为艾米留过念之后，摆出了一副慈父的气派，在合宜的时刻跨步上前，如今正从这番新奇的经历中缓过神来。

两人坐在那里，话说得很少，只是用半遮半掩、含着疑问的目光彼此打量。说实在的，梅多拉在心里即兴编演了一小段无声的对话，还派定他也担了一角。她只觉得浑身筋节都错了位，几乎是被打败了。霍滕丝的那一番表演引她想起了伯特伦·科普，她也断定，同一个题目正高踞在巴兹尔·伦道夫的心头。

===Original===
“Well, you have about beaten me,” she said.

“How so?” she made him ask, with an affectation of simplicity.

“You know well enough,” she returned. “You have played off the whole University against my poor house, and you have won. Your influence with the president, your brother on the board of trustees﻿… If Bertram Cope has any gratitude in his composition.﻿…”

“Oh, well,” she let him say, “I don’t feel that I did much; and I’m not sure I’m glad for what I did do.”

“You may regret it, of course. That other man is an uncertain quantity.”

“Oh, come,” he said; “you’ve had the inside track from the very start: this house and everything in it.﻿…”

===Chinese===
「唉，你差不多已经把我打败了。」她说。

「怎么见得？」她让他装出一副憨直发问。

「你心里再清楚不过，」她答道。「你搬动整个大学来同我这可怜的家打对台，你赢了。你在校长跟前的影响，你那位坐在校董会里的兄弟……倘若伯特伦·科普的禀性里还有几分感恩之心……」

「唉，算了，」她让他接口道，「我不觉得自己出了多少力；至于出过的那些力嘛，自己是不是乐意，我也拿不准。」

「你尽管去后悔好了。另外那个人可是个未知数。」

「哦，得了，」他说；「你从一开头就占着里圈：这座房子，还有房子里的一切……」

===Original===
“You have a house of your own, now.”

“Your dinners and entertainments.﻿…”

“You have your own dinner-table.”

“Your limousine, your chauffeur﻿—running to the opera and heaven knows where else.﻿…”

“Taxis can always be had. Yes,” she went on, “you have held the advantage over a poor woman cooped up in her own house. While I have had to stick here, attending to my housekeeping, you have been careering about everywhere﻿—you with a lot of partners and clerks in your office, and no compulsion to look in more than two or three times a week. Of *course* you can run to theatres and clubs. I wonder they don’t dispense with you altogether!”

“There’s the advantage of a business arranged to run itself﻿—so far as *I* am concerned.”

===Chinese===
「如今你自己可是有一座房子的人了。」

「你的晚宴，你的招待会……」

「你自己也有一张晚餐桌。」

「你的豪华轿车、你的专职司机——动辄跑歌剧院，天知道还跑别的什么地方……」

「出租车总归叫得到。是啊，」她接着往下说，「你占足了一个困守自宅的可怜女人的上风。我不得不钉死在这儿照管家务，你却四处纵情驰骋——你事务所里有一大帮合伙人和文书，一个礼拜顶多不得不进去照看两三回，此外别无牵绊。你*当然*跑得起戏院、上得起俱乐部。我都奇怪，他们怎么不索性连你一起裁撤了！」

「这便是把生意安排得能自行运转的好处——就*我*而论。」

===Original===
“Yes, you have had the world to range through: shows and restaurants; the whole big city; strolls and excursions, and who knows what beside.﻿…”

Thus Medora Phillips continued silently, and with no exact sense of justice, to work up her grievance. Presently she surprised Randolph with a positive frown. She had made a quick, darting return to Hortense.

“I shall send her away,” she said aloud. The girl might join her studio friend, who had stopped at Asheville on her way North, and stay with her for a few weeks. Yes, Hortense might go and meet the spring﻿—or even the summer, if that must be. The spring here in town she herself would take as it came. “I shall welcome a few free, easy breaths after this past fortnight,” she finished audibly.

===Chinese===
「是啊，你尽可以在整个世界里纵横来去：看戏、下馆子；整座大城市；漫步、游览，还有天知道的旁的什么……」

梅多拉·菲利普斯就这样不出声地——而且也谈不上怎么公道地——继续酝酿着自己的委屈。不一会儿，她实实在在地板起面孔皱起眉头，倒叫伦道夫吃了一惊。她的思绪倏地一闪，又扑回了霍滕丝身上。

「我要把她打发走，」她出声说道。这姑娘不妨去会合她画室里的那位朋友——那人北上途中正在阿什维尔（Asheville）小驻——陪她盘桓几个星期。对，霍滕丝可以去迎候春天——事不得已，便迎候夏天也未尝不可。至于本城的春天嘛，她自己就照它本来的样子领受好了。「挨过了这过去的两个星期，我很乐意痛痛快快、自由自在地喘上几口气。」她末了又出声补了一句。

===Original===
Randolph squared himself with her mood as best he could. “You are tired and nervous,” he said with banality. “Get the last of us out and go to bed. I’ll lead the way, and will give these loiterers as marked an example as possible.”

Medora Phillips hushed down her house finally and went thoughtfully upstairs to her room. Amy had gone off, and Hortense was sentenced to go. There remained only Carolyn. Was there any threat in her and her sonnets?

===Chinese===
伦道夫尽力把自己挪正，去迁就她的心境。「你累了，神经也绷得慌，」他拿一句陈词滥调说道。「把我们最后这几个人送出门去，就上床歇着吧。我来领头开路，好给这些磨磨蹭蹭的人树一个尽可能鲜明的榜样。」

梅多拉·菲利普斯终于让宅子归于安静，思虑重重地上楼回自己房里去了。艾米走了，霍滕丝也被判定了要走。剩下的只有卡罗琳。她和她的那些十四行诗，可藏着什么威胁么？

## XXIX Cope Again in the Country / XXIX 科普重回乡间

===Original===
Medora treated Hortense to a few cautious soundings, decided that another locale was the thing to do her good, and sent her South forthwith.
“It’s a low latitude,” she said to herself; “but it’s a high altitude. The season is late, but she won’t suffer.”
Hortense, who had been sullen and fractious, met her aunt halfway, and agreed passively when Medora said:
“It will benefit you to see the spring come on in a new scene and in a new fashion. You will find the mountains more interesting than the dunes.” So Hortense packed her things and joined her friend for a brief sojourn in sight of the Great Smokies.
Thus, when Medora herself went forth to meet the spring among the sandhills, she had only Carolyn and the other members of her domestic staff. Yet no simplest weekend without a guest or so, and she asked Cope to accompany them.

===Chinese===
梅多拉（Medora）对霍滕丝（Hortense）作了几番谨慎的试探，断定换一个地方才对她的身体有好处，于是当即将她送去了南方。
「那是个低纬度的地方，」她自忖道，「不过海拔倒高。时节是晚了些，可她不至于吃苦头。」
霍滕丝本来一直闷闷不乐、动辄使性子，这回却也迁就了姨妈一半；当梅多拉说出下面这话时，她顺从地应承了下来：
「换一个新场景、以一种新方式看春天到来，会对你有好处。你会发现大山比沙丘更有意思。」于是霍滕丝收拾起行装，与她的朋友会合，到望得见大雾山（Great Smokies）的地方小住了一阵。
这样，等到梅多拉自己动身到沙丘间去迎接春天时，她身边就只有卡罗琳（Carolyn）和家务班子的其他成员了。然而再简单的周末也少不了一两位客人，于是她邀请科普（Cope）同行。

===Original===
“You need it,” she told him bluntly; “﻿—you need a change, however slight and brief. You are positively thin. You make me wish that thesises﻿—”
“Theses,” Cope corrected her, rather spiritlessly.
“﻿—that theses, then, had never been invented. To speak familiarly, you are almost ‘peakèd.’ ”
Cope, with the first warm days, had gone back to the blue serge suit of the past autumn, and he filled it even less well than before. And his face was thin to correspond.

===Chinese===
「你需要这个，」她直截了当地对他说，「——你需要换换环境，不管多么轻微、多么短暂。你实在是瘦了。你简直让我巴不得那些论文（thesises）——」
「是 theses，」科普纠正她道，颇有些没精打采。
「——那就但愿这些 theses 从来没有被发明出来好了。说句家常话，你简直有点『形容枯槁』了。」
天一转暖，科普便又穿回了去年秋天那套蓝色哔叽西服，而如今把它撑起来，比从前更见空荡。他的脸也相应地清瘦了。

===Original===
“Besides,” she went on, “we need you. It will be a kind of camping-out for a day or two﻿—merely that. We must have your help to pitch the tent, so to speak, and to pick up firewood, and to fry the bacon.﻿ ﻿… And this time,” she added, “you shall not have that long tiresome trip by train. There will be room in the car.”
She did not attempt to make room for Lemoyne. She was glad to have no need to do so; Lemoyne was deeply engrossed otherwise﻿—“Annabella” and her “antics” were almost ready for the public eye. The first of May would see the performance, and the numerous rehearsals were exacting, whether as regarded the effort demanded or the time. Every spare hour was going into them, as well as many an hour that could hardly be spared. Lemoyne, who had been cast originally for a minor female part, now found himself transferred, through the failure of a principal, to a more important one. For him, then, rehearsals were more exigent than ever. He cut his Psychology once or twice, nor could he succeed, during office hours, in keeping his mind on office-routine. His superiors became impatient and then protestant. The annual spring dislocation of ordered student life was indeed a regular feature of the year’s last term; yet to push indulgence as far as Arthur Lemoyne was pushing it﻿—!
Cope was concerned; then worried. “Arthur,” he said, “be reasonable about this. You’ve got real work to do, remember.”
But Lemoyne’s real work was in the musical comedy. “This is the biggest chance I’ve ever had in my life,” he declared, “and I don’t want to lose out on it.”
So Cope rolled away to the dunes and left Lemoyne behind for one Saturday night rehearsal the more.

===Chinese===
「再说，」她继续道，「我们也用得着你。不过是到外头野营个一两天——仅此而已。打个比方说，搭帐篷、拾柴火、煎培根，都少不了你的帮忙。……还有这一回，」她添了一句，「你不用再坐那趟又长又累人的火车了。车里有你的位置。」
她没有设法给勒莫因（Lemoyne）腾出位置。她庆幸自己无须这么做；勒莫因正深深地埋头于别的事情——《安娜贝拉》（Annabella）和她的「滑稽戏码」已差不多可以亮相于公众眼前了。五月一日便要演出，那为数众多的一场场排练着实苛刻磨人，无论从所费的气力还是所耗的时间来说都是如此。每一个能腾出来的钟头都投了进去，还有许多根本腾不出来的钟头也一样。勒莫因起初被分派到的是一个次要的女角，如今由于一位主演的告退，他被调去顶一个更重要的角色。这样一来，排练对他而言比以往任何时候都更为吃紧。他把心理学课逃了一两回；办公时间里，他也无法让心思守在例行公务上。他的上司们先是失了耐心，继而提出抗议。年年春天，规规矩矩的学生生活照例要错动一阵，这本是学年最后一学期的惯常景象；然而把放纵推到阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）这般地步——！
科普先是挂心，继而担忧。「阿瑟，」他说，「这件事上要讲些道理。你有正经工作要做，别忘了。」
可勒莫因的正经工作偏偏就在那出音乐喜剧里。「这是我这辈子碰上过的最大的机会，」他宣称，「我可不想错失了它。」
于是科普一路乘车去了沙丘，把勒莫因留在城里，多熬一个星期六晚上的排练。

===Original===
Duneland gave him a tonic welcome. Under a breezy sky the far edge of the lake stood out clear. Along its nearer edge the vivacious waves tumbled noisily. The steady pines were welcoming the fresh early foliage of such companions as dressed and undressed in accord with the calendar; the wrecked trunks which had given up life and its leafy pomps seemed somehow less sombre and stark; and in the threatened woodlands behind the hills a multiplicity of small new greeneries stirred the autumn’s dead leaves and brightened up the thickets of shrubbery. The arbutus had companioned the hepatica, and the squads of the lupines were busily preparing their panoply of lavender-blue racemes. Nature was breaking bounds. On the inland horizon rose the vast bulk of the prison. As on other excursions, nobody tried too hard to see it.
“It’s all too lovely,” exclaimed Medora Phillips. “And what is quite as good,” she was able to declare, “the house itself is all right.” Winter had not weakened its roof nor wrenched away its storm-windows; no irresponsible wayfarer had used it for a lodging, nor had any casual marauder entered to despoil. Medora directed the disposition of the hamper of food with a relieved air and sent Cope down with Peter for an armful or two of driftwood from the assertive shore.
“And you, Carolyn,” she said, “see if the oil-stove will really go.”
Down on the beach itself, where the past winter’s waste was still profusely spread, Cope rose to the greening hills, to the fresh sweep of the wind, and to the sun-shot green and purple streakings over the water. The wind, in particular, took its own way: dry light sand, blown from higher shelvings, striped the dark wet edges of the shore; and every bending blade of sandgrass drew a circle about itself with its own revolving tip.

===Chinese===
沙丘地带向他致以一剂滋补强身般的欢迎。微风习习的天空下，湖的远岸轮廓分明；近岸一带，活泼的浪涛喧腾翻滚。沉稳持重的松树们，正迎候着那些依照节令穿裳脱裳的伙伴们新生的早春嫩叶；那些早已舍弃了生命连同其繁叶盛景的残躯，不知怎的也不那么阴沉突兀了；而在山冈后那片备受侵凌的林地里，无数细小的新绿搅动着秋日的枯叶，把一丛丛灌木映得亮堂起来。五月花（arbutus）已与獐耳细辛作伴而开，羽扇豆的队伍正忙着置办它们淡蓝紫色总状花序的全副甲胄。大自然正在冲破藩篱。内陆的天际线上，耸起监狱庞大的躯体。与历次出游一样，谁也不去刻意看它。
「这真是太可爱了，」梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）赞叹道。「而同样可喜的是，」她得以宣称，「房子本身也安然无恙。」冬天没有削弱它的屋顶，也没有掀走它的防风暴窗；没有不负责任的过路人拿它当过宿处，也没有不期而至的劫掠者闯进来洗劫。梅多拉带着如释重负的神气指挥安置那篮食物，又派科普同彼得（Peter）一道，去那气势逼人的湖滨搬一两抱漂木。
「还有你，卡罗琳，」她说，「去看看那油炉究竟点得着点不着。」
下到海滩上——去冬的废弃物依旧狼藉遍地——科普的精神为之一振，迎向正在返青的山冈，迎向长风清新的扫拂，迎向水面上斜阳织入的绿紫条纹。风尤其自行其是：干燥的细沙从高处的坡台上刮落，给岸边深色的湿缘描上一道道条纹；每一茎俯仰的沙草，都用自己旋转的叶尖画出一个圆圈。

===Original===
Cope let the robust and willing Peter pick up most of the firewood and himself luxuriated in the spacious world round about him. Yes, a winter had flown﻿—or, at any rate, had passed﻿—and here he was again. There had been annoyances, but now he felt a wide and liberal relief. Here, for example, was the special stretch of shore on which Amy Leffingwell had praised his singing and had hinted her desire to accompany him﻿—but never mind that. Farther on was the particular tract where Hortense Dunton had pottered with her watercolors and had harried him with the heroines of eighteenth century fiction﻿—but never mind that, either. All those things were past, and he was free. Nobody remained save Carolyn Thorpe, an unaggressive girl with whom one could really trust oneself and with whom one could walk, if required, in comfort and content. Cope threw up his head to the hills and threw out his chest to the winds, and laid quick hands on a short length of weather-beaten hemlock plank. “Afraid I’m not holding up my end,” he said to Peter.
At the house again, he found that Carolyn had brought the oil-stove back into service, and, with Helga, had cast the cloth over the table and had set some necessary dishes on it. He fetched a pail or two of water from the pump, and each time placed a fresh young half-grown sassafras leaf on the surface. “The trademark of our bottling-works,” he said facetiously; “to show that our products are pure.” And Carolyn, despite his facetiousness, felt more than ever that he might easily become a poet. Medora viewed the floating leaves with indulgent appreciation. “But don’t let’s cumber ourselves with many cares,” she suggested; “we are here to make the best of the afternoon. Let’s out and away﻿—the sooner the better.”
The three soon set forth for a stroll through spring’s reviving domain. Cope walked between Medora and Carolyn, or ahead of them, impartially sweeping away twigs and flowering branches from before their faces. The young junipers were putting forth tender new tips; the bright leaves of the sassafras shone forth against the pines. Above the newly-rounded tops of the oaks and maples in the valley below them the Three Witches rose gauntly; and off on their far hill the two companion pines﻿—(how had he named them? Romeo and Juliet? Pelleas and Melisande?)﻿—still lay their dark heads together in mysterious confidences under the heightening glow of the late afternoon sun. Carolyn looked from them back to Cope and gave him a shy smile.

===Chinese===
科普任由健壮而殷勤的彼得去捡拾大部分柴火，自己则纵情沉浸于四周开阔的天地。是啊，一个冬天飞过去了——或者说，好歹是过去了——他又回到了这里。其间自有种种烦恼，但此刻他感到一种宽广而慷慨的解脱。比如，眼前这一段海滩，正是艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）夸赞过他的歌喉、并暗示过想为他伴奏的地方——不过那不必放在心上了。再往前那一片地方，则是霍滕丝·邓顿（Hortense Dunton）摆弄水彩、拿十八世纪小说里的女主人公们烦扰过他的所在——不过那也不必放在心上了。那些事都已过去，他自由了。留下来的只有卡罗琳·索普（Carolyn Thorpe）这么一个不咄咄逼人的姑娘，跟她相处尽可放心，若有必要，也可以同她一道走得安逸而满足。科普朝山冈昂起头，迎着风挺起胸，又敏捷地伸手抓住一截饱经风雨的铁杉短板。「只怕我这一头没尽到力，」他对彼得说。
回到房子那儿，他发现卡罗琳已让油炉重新投入使用，还和赫尔加（Helga）一起给桌子铺上了桌布，摆好几样必备的杯盘。他去水泵那儿提了一两桶水，每一回都在水面上放一片新摘的半大黄樟嫩叶。「这是我们装瓶工厂的商标，」他打趣道，「以表明我们的产品质地纯净。」而卡罗琳呢，尽管他说得俏皮，却比以往任何时候都更觉得，他轻而易举就能成为一位诗人。梅多拉怀着宽容的赏鉴望着漂浮的叶子。「不过，咱们可别让一大堆操心事拖累了自己，」她提议道，「我们来，是为了把这个下午过到最好。出去走走吧——越快越好。」
三人很快出发，到春天复苏的疆土上去散步。科普走在梅多拉和卡罗琳中间，或是走在她们前头，不分彼此地为她们扫开迎面的细枝与开花的枝条。年幼的杜松正抽出柔嫩的新尖；黄樟明亮的叶片在松树映衬下熠熠生辉。在脚下谷地里橡树枫树新近浑圆的树冠之上，「三女巫」（Three Witches）嶙峋耸峙；而在远处它们那道山冈上，两株相依为伴的松树——（他当初是怎么给它们命的名？罗密欧与朱丽叶？佩利亚斯与梅丽桑德？）——依然在愈见浓烈的午后余晖里，把暗色的头颅凑在一处，交换着神秘的私语。卡罗琳的目光从它们身上转回科普，朝他羞怯地一笑。

===Original===
He did not quite smile back. Carolyn was well enough, however. She was suitably dressed for a walk. Her shoes were sensible, and so was her hair. Amy had run to fluffiness. Hortense had often favored heavy waves and emphatic bandeaux. But Carolyn’s hair was drawn back plainly from her forehead, and was gathered in a small, low-set knot. “Still, it’s no concern of mine,” he reminded himself, and walked on ahead.
Carolyn’s sensible shoes brought her back, with the others, at twilight. The three took up rather ornamentally (with aid from Peter and Helga) the lighter details of housekeeping. Toward the end of the stroll, Cope and Carolyn﻿—perhaps upon the mere unconscious basis of youth﻿—had rather fallen in together, and Medora Phillips, once or twice, had had to safeguard for herself her face and eyesight from the young trees that bordered their path. But that evening, as they sat on a settle before the driftwood fire, Medora took pains to place herself in the middle. Carolyn was a sweet young flower, doubtless﻿—humbler, possibly, than Amy or Hortense; yet she too perhaps must be extirpated, gently but firmly, from the garden of desire.
“You look better already,” Medora said to Cope. “You’ll go back tomorrow a new man.”

===Chinese===
他没有怎么回以微笑。不过，卡罗琳也够好的了。她这一身穿戴正合散步之宜。她的鞋子明智，发型也同样明智。艾米（Amy）的头发一味趋于蓬软；霍滕丝则常常偏爱厚重的波浪和扎眼的宽发带。而卡罗琳的头发素净地从前额向后拢起，绾成一个小小的低髻。「话虽如此，这与我毫不相干，」他提醒自己，径自朝前走去。
暮色四合时，卡罗琳那双明智的鞋子带着她随众人一同归来。三个人（在彼得和赫尔加的协助下）相当点缀门面地料理起家务中较轻省的细务。散步将近尾声时，科普和卡罗琳——也许仅仅是凭着青春的无意识——已颇有些双双走在一处之势，害得梅多拉·菲利普斯有一两回不得不亲自护住自己的脸和眼睛，躲开小径两旁的幼树。但那天晚上，大家坐在漂木火前的高背长椅上时，梅多拉却费心把自己安置在了正中间。卡罗琳无疑是一朵甜美的年轻花朵——或许比艾米或霍滕丝更为低眉顺眼；然而她恐怕也一样得被从欲望的花园里连根铲除出去——温柔地，然而坚决地。
「你气色已经好些了，」梅多拉对科普说。「明天回去，你就会是焕然一新的一个人了。」

===Original===
Her elbow was on the back of the settle and close to his shoulder. His face caught the glow from the fire.
“Oh, I’m all right, I assure you,” he said.
“You *do* look better,” observed Carolyn on her own account. “This air is everything. Only a few hours of it﻿—”
“Another bit of wood on the fire, if you please, Carolyn,” said her patroness.
“Let me do it,” said Cope. He rose quickly and laid on a stick or two. He remained standing on the edge of the glow. He hoped nobody would say again that he was looking rather thin and pale.
“And what is Mr. Lemoyne doing this evening?” presently asked Mrs. Phillips in a dreamy undertone. Her manner was casual and negligent; her voice was low and leisurely. She seemed to place Lemoyne at a distance of many, many leagues. “Rehearsing, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
她的臂肘支在高背长椅的靠背上，紧挨着他的肩头。他的脸上映着火光。
「哦，我好好的，我向你保证，」他说。
「你气色确实真的好多了，」卡罗琳自顾自地发表意见。「这空气就是一切。才呼吸了几个钟头——」
「劳驾，卡罗琳，往火上再添一小块木头，」她的女恩主说道。
「让我来，」科普说。他敏捷地起身，添上一两根柴，随后便站在火光的边缘不再挪动。他指望再没有人说他看上去多么清瘦苍白。
「勒莫因先生今晚在做些什么呢？」过了一会儿，菲利普斯太太以一种悠悠的低调问道。她的态度闲散疏懒，嗓音低缓从容，仿佛把勒莫因放在了千山万水之外。「我猜，是在排练吧？」

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Cope. “This new play has absorbed him completely.”
“He will do well?”
“He always does. He always has.”
“Men in girls’ parts are so amusing,” said Carolyn. “Their walk is so heavy and clumsy, even if their dancing isn’t. And when they speak up in those big deep bass and baritone voices﻿ ﻿… !”
“Arthur will speak in a light tenor.”

===Chinese===
「是的，」科普答道。「这出新戏把他整个人都吸了进去。」
「他会演得好吗？」
「他一向演得好。从来都是。」
「男人演女角真是逗趣，」卡罗琳说。「他们走起路来那么沉重笨拙——就算跳起舞来并不如此。等他们亮开那些粗沉宽厚的男低音、男中音响亮说话的时候……！」
「阿瑟会用一种轻盈的男高音说话。」

===Original===
“Will his walk be heavy and clumsy?” asked Mrs. Phillips.
“He is an artist,” replied Cope.
“Not too much of one, I trust,” she returned. “I confess I like boys best in such parts when they frankly and honestly seem to be boys. That’s half the fun﻿—and nine-tenths of the taste.”
“Taste?”
“Yes, taste. Short for good taste. There’s a great deal of room for bad. A thing may be done too thoroughly. Once or twice I’ve seen it done that way, by﻿—artists.”
Cope, in the half-light, seemed rather unhappy.

===Chinese===
「他走起路来也会沉重笨拙吗？」菲利普斯太太问。
「他是位艺术家，」科普答道。
「但愿别艺术家得过了头，」她回敬道。「我承认，男孩子们演这类角色时，只有当他们坦坦荡荡、明明白白地显出男孩子的本色，我才最喜欢。那是一半的乐趣——也是十分之九的品味。」
「品味？」
「是的，品味。好品味的简称。坏品味的余地可大着呢。一件事是可能做得太彻底的。有一两回，我就见过有人把事情做成那样——由一些『艺术家』。」
半明半暗的光线里，科普显得相当不快活。

===Original===
“He finds time for﻿—for all this﻿—this technique?” Mrs. Phillips asked.
“He’s very clever,” replied Cope, rather unhappy still. “It does take time, of course. I’m concerned,” he added.
“About his other work?”
“Yes.” He stepped aside a little into the shadow.
“Come back to your place,” said Medora Phillips. “You look quite spectral.”
Cope, with a light sigh, returned to his post on the settle and to his share in the firelight. Silence fell. From far below were heard the active waves, moaning themselves to rest. And a featureless evening moved on slowly.

===Chinese===
「他还找得出时间来弄——弄这一切——这种技巧？」菲利普斯太太问。
「他非常聪明，」科普答道，仍显得相当不快活。「这当然的确要花时间。我心里有点不安，」他补了一句。
「为他的正业担心？」
「是的。」他侧身让了让，退进暗影里去。
「回到你的位子上去，」梅多拉·菲利普斯说。「你看上去活像个鬼影。」
科普轻轻叹了口气，回到高背长椅上自己的位置，回到火光里属于自己的那一份。沉默降临下来。远远的下方，传来活跃的浪涛声，哀吟着渐自入眠。而一个没有眉目的夜晚，缓缓地向前挪动。

## XII Cope Amidst Cross-Purposes / 第十二章 科普身处错综心事之间

===Original===
Next morning's mail brought Cope a letter from Arthur Lemoyne. The letter was short—at least when compared with Cope's own plentiful pennings; but it gave our young instructor a few points to think about while he was illuminating Clarissa Harlowe and making some careful comments on Joseph Andrews. Released toward noon, he read the letter over again; and he ran over it again during lunch. Lemoyne possessed a variety of gifts, but the gift of letter-writing, in an extended form, was not among them. He said all he had to say in four moderate pages.

"Yours received," he wrote. "Am glad the year has opened up so interestingly for you. Of course I want to come down as soon as I can, *if* I can, and be with you."

Well, the "if," as the latter part of the letter indicated, was not likely to prove insurmountable. The assurance that he wanted to come was grateful, though superfluous: who had supposed for a moment that he didn't? Still, the thing, put down in plain black and white, had its look of comfort.

===Chinese===
次日清晨的邮件给科普（Cope）带来了阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）的一封信。信很短——至少同科普自己笔下丰沛流淌的书信相比是如此；但它仍让我们这位青年教师多了几处可供思量的问题，就在他阐发《克拉丽莎·哈洛》（Clarissa Harlowe）、对《约瑟夫·安德鲁斯》（Joseph Andrews）细心评点的时候。临近中午得了空闲，他把信重读了一遍；午饭中间又通览了一遍。勒莫因天资多端，唯独洋洋洒洒写信的才具不在其列。他把要说的话，用四页不肥不瘦的信纸说尽了。

「信已收悉，」他写道。「很高兴你这一学年开局如此有声有色。我当然想尽早下来——只要（*if*）可能——同你在一起。」

好吧，正如信的后半部分所表明的，那个「*if*」看来不至于成为不可逾越的障碍。他愿意来的这份保证令人感慰，尽管纯属多余：又有谁曾有一刻以为他不愿意来呢？话虽如此，白纸黑字写了下来，自有它令人宽心的一面。

===Original===
"Of course the business is not gaining much through my connection with it. I expect father begins to see *that*, pretty plainly. As for the cathedral choir and the dramatic club and all the rest, I am willing to throw them over—expecting that larger interests can be opened to me by you." …

Cope paused on these points. He had suggested that Lemoyne enroll as a student in some slight course or other, with the hope that his voice might lead to his wearing cap and gown at chapel services and that his dramatic experience might give him some role in the annual operetta. In either of these quarters a good tenor voice was usually to seek. And as for the business.… Well, he had once overheard the elder Lemoyne's partner audibly wonder whether Arthur would ever learn how to ship a keg of nails out of their back door, even.

Cope pushed away his coffee-cup and asked the young Greek for a cut of pie.

===Chinese===
「当然，那桩生意并没有因为我的参与而增益多少。我想父亲已经开始把*这一点*看得相当明白了。至于大教堂唱诗班、戏剧社，还有其余种种，我都乐于抛开——指望你能为我打开更大的局面。」……

科普在这几处驻笔沉吟。他曾建议勒莫因随便注册修一门什么轻省课程，指望他那副嗓子能让他戴上方帽、披上长袍出席礼拜仪式，指望他的舞台经验能在年度轻歌剧里派上一个角色。在这两个地方，一副好的男高音嗓子通常都是遍寻难遇的。至于那桩生意。……唉，他有一回曾无意间听见老勒莫因的合伙人当众嘀咕，怀疑阿瑟到底学不学得会哪怕把一桶钉子从他们后门发运出去。

科普推开咖啡杯，请那位年轻的希腊人店家切一块馅饼来。

===Original===
> "I sort of sounded father the other day, but he was pretty huffy. I'll try again, soon; but I doubt if I can manage to come down until after the holidays. You begin a new term, then, I suppose. The fact is, I took a week off in the middle of September, and father hasn't forgiven it. One of our fellows in the choir had just bought a little roadster, and he invited me for a trip to Green Bay and beyond. We dipped along through Fish Creek, Ephraim, and so on. Good weather, good roads, good scenery, good hotels; and a pleasant time was had by all—or, rather, by both." …

Cope dwelt darkly on this passage. Arthur was flighty; Arthur was volatile; Arthur was even fickle, when the mood took him. Some arrangement that partook more of the hard-and-fast was needed. But there was comfort—of a kind—in the next passage.

> "Though father, at best, will do very little, and though I have just now little enough of my own, there may be somebody or other among your faculty or trustees who could find me a niche in the college library or in the registrar's office. Or have all such posts been snapped up by Johnnys-on-the-spot? A small weekly stipend would rather help our *ménage—hein*?"

===Chinese===
> 「前些日子我侧面探了探父亲的口风，他挺不给好脸。我不久会再试一回；只是我疑心，要到假期过后才下得来。那时候你们大概新开一个学期了吧。实情是，九月中旬我歇了一个礼拜，父亲至今不曾原谅。唱诗班里有一个伙伴刚买了一辆小跑车，邀我同去绿湾（Green Bay）及更远的地方走一遭。我们蜿蜒驶过菲什克里克（Fish Creek）、埃夫拉伊姆（Ephraim）等地一路前行。天气好，道路好，风景好，旅馆好；一行人尽兴而归——或者说，两个人尽兴而归。」……

科普把这一段反复咀嚼，心头罩上一层暗影。阿瑟轻浮多变；阿瑟喜怒无常；兴致所至，阿瑟甚至不免薄幸。需要的是一种更加牢靠不变的安排。好在下一段里也有几分安慰——勉强可算安慰的那种。

> 「虽说父亲至多也帮不了多少忙，虽说眼下我自己也所剩无几，你们的教职员或校董之中，说不定有哪一位能替我在学院图书馆或注册处寻得一席之地。还是说这类位置早已被眼疾手快的现成能人抢占一空？一笔小小的周薪，对我们的小家计（*ménage—hein*）不无小补吧？」

===Original===
This definite inquiry (which carried its own answer) seemed to drive one or two brass tacks with some definiteness. Cope himself was eking out his small salary with a small allowance from home; next year, with the thesis accomplished, better pay in some better place. A present partner and pal ought to be a prop rather than a drag: however welcome his company, he must bear his share.

"Look about a bit for quarters," Lemoyne went on, drawing toward his conclusion. "I presume room-rent is little more for two than for one. Possibly," he put down in an afterthought, "I might get a job in the city;" and then, "with warm regards," he came to a close as "Art."

Cope finished his lunch and walked out. If Arthur could do one thing better than another, it was to make coffee; his product was assuredly better than the Greek's. The two had camped out more than once on the shores of Lake Winnebago, and Arthur had deftly managed the commissariat. They had had good times together and had needed no other company. How had it been on Green Bay—at Eagle Cliff and Apron Bluff and all the other places lately celebrated in lithographed "folders" and lately popularized by motorists? And who was the particular "fellow" who ran the roadster?

===Chinese===
这一明确的询问（答案已寓于问题之中）倒像是把一两个实打实的要害敲定了下来。科普自己如今也是靠家里一笔小小的接济，把微薄的薪水贴补起来；明年，等论文一完成，便去某个更好的地方领更好的薪俸。眼前这位同住者兼密友，理应是一根支柱，而非一副拖累：无论他的陪伴多么受欢迎，他总得担起自己的一份。

「先留意一下住处，」勒莫因接着写道，渐渐收向结尾。「我料想两人的房租比一人多不了多少。兴许，」他又补记一句，「我能在城里寻个差事；」随后是「谨致热忱」，落款「阿特」（Art）。

科普吃完午饭，走了出去。阿瑟若有一件事做得比其余都出色，那便是煮咖啡；他的成品必定胜过那位希腊人店家。两人曾在温尼贝戈湖（Lake Winnebago）畔露营不止一回，阿瑟把伙食采办打点得利落妥帖。他们在一起度过许多好时光，无需再有旁人作伴。这一趟绿湾之行又是如何呢——鹰崖（Eagle Cliff）、围裙崖（Apron Bluff），还有所有那些近来在石印「导游册页」中被大书特书、近来又经驾车客们炒热的地方？况且，驾驶那辆跑车的「伙伴」究竟是哪一位？

===Original===
Late that afternoon Cope chanced upon Randolph among the fantastic basins and floral parterres of the court in front of the Botany building: Randolph had had a small matter for one of the deans. Together they sauntered over to the lake. From the edge of the bluff they walked out upon the concrete terrace above the general boiler-room and its dynamos. Alongside this, the vast tonnage of coal required for the coming winter was beginning to pile up. The weather was still mild and sunny and the lake was as valiantly blue as ever.

"It doesn't look like the same body of water, does it?" said Cope.

"It might be just as beautiful in its own way, here, as we found it yesterday, out there," returned Randolph. "I've asked my brother-in-law, I don't know how many times, why they can't do better by this unfortunate campus and bring it all up to a reasonable level of seemliness. But—"

===Chinese===
那天傍晚时分，科普在植物学楼前那座庭院的奇趣水池与花圃之间，与伦道夫（Randolph）不期而遇：伦道夫刚为一件小事找过某位院长。两人便一道信步朝湖边走去。他们从崖沿走上混凝土平台，平台下面便是总锅炉房和它的发电机组。平台一旁，为来冬所需的大宗煤炭正开始堆积成山。天气依然和暖晴朗，湖水也一如既往，蓝得昂扬如故。

「这看上去不像同一片水了，是吧？」科普说。

「在这里，它自有它的美处，未必逊于我们昨天在外头所见的那番，」伦道夫答道。「我不知问过我那位内兄多少回，为什么他们就不能善待这块时运不济的校园，把它整个儿提到一份体面合宜的水准上来。可是——」

===Original===
"You have a relative among the—?"

"Yes, my sister's husband is one of the University trustees. But he lives miles from this spot and hardly ever sees it. Besides, his aesthetic endowments are not beyond those of the average university trustee. Sometimes they're as hard on Beauty as they are on Free Speech."

"I see they're hard on beauty; and I may live to find free speech mauled, too."

"Well, you're not in Sociology or Economics. Still, don't trifle with a long-established aesthetic idol either. Trustees—and department heads—are conservative."

===Chinese===
「您在那些——之中有位亲戚？」

「是的，我姐姐的丈夫是大学的校董之一。可他住得离此地好几英里，几乎从不来看一眼。再说，他在审美方面的禀赋，也高不出寻常大学校董的水准。有时候，他们对待美，同对待言论自由一样严酷。」

「我明白他们待美是严酷了；说不定我还能活着见到言论自由也遭践踏。」

「好在你不治社会学，也不治经济学。不过，也别去轻慢一尊由来已久的审美偶像。校董们——还有系主任们——都是守成的。」

===Original===
"Oh, you mean about—?"

"About your immortal William. He wrote them. Don't try to rob him. Don't try to knock him off his pedestal."

"Oh, you're thinking about my thesis. What I said about Warwickshire was just a little flight of fancy, I guess—a bit of doorstep travel. I'm likely enough to stay where I am."

"Well, how about the thesis, really?"

===Chinese===
「哦，您是说关于——？」

「关于你那位不朽的威廉。那些作品是他写的。别想剥夺他，别想把他从基座上掀下来。」

「哦，您是想到了我的论文。我说的那些关于沃里克郡（Warwickshire）的话，我想不过是一点小小的遐想——一番坐在门槛上的神游罢了。我很可能仍旧留在原地。」

「那么，论文到底怎么打算？」

===Original===
"I think I shall end by digging something out of Here and Now. 'Our Middle-West School of Fiction,'—what would you think of that?"

"H'm! If you can make it seem worth while. …"

"Well, can't I?"

"Your work, from the very nature of it, must be critical. Now the critic, nine times out of ten, takes down a volume from its established shelf, dusts it off, ruffles the leaves a bit, and then puts it back where it was. The ruffling is sometimes very nice and interesting and often gives the ruffler a good position in the glorious company of earlier rufflers—"

===Chinese===
「我想，到头来我还是会从『此时此地』（Here and Now）里挖掘出些东西来。『我们的中西部小说学派』——您觉得这个题目如何？」

「嗯！只要你能让它显得值得。……」

「怎么，我难道不能吗？」

「你的工作，就其本性而言，必然是批评。而批评家，十之有九，是从既定的书架上取下一卷书，掸去灰尘，把书页拂动一番，然后放回原处。这番拂动有时十分雅致有趣，还常常使拂书人在先前历代拂书人的光荣队伍里博得一个好位置——」

===Original===
"I shouldn't be satisfied with anything like that. Things have got to move. I want to take some recent, less-known men and put *them* on the shelves."

"Yet you don't want to waste work on material which time may show as of transient value, or of none."

"A fellow must chance it. Who gives quickly gives twice;—I suppose that applies to praise as well as to money. It irks me to find more praise bestowed on the praised-enough—even on groups of secondary importance, sometimes just because they are remote (in England, perhaps), and so can be treated with an easy objectivity. To dig in your own day and your own community is harder, but I should feel it more rewarding."

===Chinese===
「我可不会满足于那样的东西。世事务必向前。我想把几位新近的、少有人知的作家接过来，把*他们*放上那些书架。」

「然而你也不愿把工夫耗费在这样的材料上——时间也许会证明它们价值短暂，甚或毫无价值。」

「总得碰一碰运气。给得快，便是给了两回；——我想这话对称赞与对金钱同样适用。叫我恼的是，眼见更多的称颂加诸早已受够称颂的对象——有时甚至是次一等的群体身上，只因为他们远隔重洋（兴许在英国），于是可以轻而易举地客观待之。在自己的时代、自己的社群里开掘固然更难，我却会觉得更有报偿。」

===Original===
"But aren't the English books really better? Haven't they more depth, substance and background?"

"Possibly—according to the conventions they themselves have established—and according to the society they depict."

"Well, Academe hasn't nailed you yet!"

"No; and I hope it won't. I should like to write a whole book about our new men."

"But don't write a thesis and then expect to publish it with profit *as* a book. That's a common enough expectation—or temptation."

===Chinese===
「可是英国书难道不是当真更好吗？它们难道不更有深度、更有分量、更有底子？」

「也许——按照他们自己立下的那套规矩——也按照他们所描写的社会。」

「呵，学园还没把你钉牢呢！」

「没有；我也盼望它钉不牢。我倒想就我们的这些新作家写上整整一本书。」

「不过，可别写完论文就指望把它*当作书*出版还能有利可图。那可是够常见的指望——或者说诱惑。」

===Original===
They turned away from the lake terrace and the imposing coal-pile. Cope, Randolph saw, was in quite a glow; a generous interest had touched him, putting fresh light into his eyes and a new vigor into his step. He had displayed a charming enthusiasm, and a pure, disinterested one. Randolph, under a quiet exterior, was delighted. He liked the boy better than ever, and felt more than ever prompted to attach him to himself.

"How are you pleased with your present quarters?" he asked, as they returned through the Botany court. He thought of the narrow couch, the ink-spotted cover on the deal table, the few coats and shoes (they *couldn't* be many) behind that calico curtain.

"None too well," replied Cope. "I shall soon begin to look for another room. I rather expect to change about holiday time."

"I am thinking of making a change too," declared Randolph.

===Chinese===
他们转身离开湖畔平台和那座巍然可观的煤山。伦道夫看在眼里：科普正满面生辉；一种慷慨的兴趣触动了他，使他眼中焕出新的光彩，步间添了新的气力。他显示出一种动人的热忱，而且是纯净的、无所图的热忱。伦道夫外表沉静，内心却喜不自胜。他比以往任何时候都更喜欢这个年轻人，也比以往任何时候更起意要把他系在自己身边。

「你对眼下的住处可还满意？」两人穿回植物学庭院时，他问道。他想起那张狭窄的卧榻、松木桌上墨迹斑斑的桌布、印花布帘后面那寥寥几件外衣和鞋子（*总归*多不到哪里去）。

「谈不上满意，」科普答道。「我不久就要开始另觅一间房了。我打算差不多到假期时分就换。」

「我也在打算换一换，」伦道夫宣称。

===Original===
"Why, could you better yourself?" asked Cope, in a tone of surprise. "I never knew a bachelor to be better fixed."

"I need a little wider margin of room. I can afford it, and ought to have had it long ago. And I learn that the lease of the people I'm with expires in the spring. My collection is growing; and I ought to have another bedroom. Think of not being able to put a man up, on occasion! I shall take a small apartment on my own account, catch some Oriental who is studying frogs' legs or Occidental theology; and then—open house. In a moderate measure, of course."

"That listens good—as the young fellows say," replied Cope. "A not uncommon ideal, possibly; but I'm glad that some man, now and then, is able to realize it."

"I should hope to see you there," said Randolph intently.

"Thank you, indeed. Yes, while my time lasts. But my own lease is like your landlord's—short. Next year—who knows where?"

===Chinese===
「哟，您还能住得更好？」科普用惊讶的语气问。「我还从不知道有哪个单身汉的安顿比您更妥帖的呢。」

「我需要稍宽绰一点的余地。这笔钱我出得起，而且早该如此。我还听说，与我同住的那家人租约春天期满。我的收藏在增多；我也该再有一间卧房。想想看，偶尔竟连一位客人都留宿不下！我要以自己的名义租下一套小公寓，物色一位正在研究蛙腿或西方神学的东方人；然后——便是门户常开。当然，尺度合度。」

「『听着不赖』——如今小伙子们这么说法，」科普答道。「这理想或许并不少见；不过我很高兴，间或总有那么一个男人能够实现它。」

「我盼望在那里见到你，」伦道夫凝神说道。

「多谢您。好的，只要我还在此地的日子。不过我自己的租约同您房东的一样——短。明年——谁说得准在哪里？」

===Original===
"Why not here?"

"Oh!" Cope shrugged, as if conscious of the need of something better, and of presently deserving it. "Some big university in the East?" wondered Randolph to himself. Well, the transfer, if it came, was still a long way ahead.

As he walked home to dinner he entertained himself by imagining his new regime. There would be an alert, intelligent Jap, who, in some miraculous way, could "do for him" between his studies. There would be a cozy dining-room where three or four fellows could have a snug little dinner, with plenty of good talk during it and after it. There would be, finally, a convenient little spare room, wherein a young knight, escaped from some "Belle Dame sans Merci," might lean his sword against the wardrobe, prop his greaves along the baseboard, lay his steel gauntlets neatly on the top of the dresser, fold his hands over the turned-down sheet of a neat three-quarter-width brass bedstead, and with a satisfied sigh of utter well-being pass away into sleep. Such facilities, even if they scarcely equaled a château on the Ridge or a villa among the Dunes, might serve.

===Chinese===
「为什么不就在这里？」

「哦！」科普耸了耸肩，仿佛自知需要更好的前程，且如今已当得起更好的前程。「东部某所大牌大学？」伦道夫暗自忖度。唉，那桩调迁，纵然会来，也还远在日后呢。

他走回家去用晚餐，一路畅想自己新的居家规程。会有一名机敏伶俐的日本仆役（原文作 Jap，系当时通行说法），能在求学之余以某种不可思议的方式替他「操持一切」。会有一间温暖适意的餐室，三四个伙伴可以在里面吃一顿熨帖的小晚饭，席间饭后都有说不尽的隽谈妙语。最后，还会有一间便利的小客房：某位刚从「无情的美人」（Belle Dame sans Merci）掌中脱身的年轻骑士，可以在里面把长剑倚上衣柜，将护胫顺着踢脚板支好，把一双钢手套齐整地摆上柜顶，在一张洁净的三尺宽黄铜床架上翻折下来的被单前交叠双手，带着周身舒泰的满足一声轻叹，沉入睡眠。这样的设施，即便难比「山脊」（the Ridge）上的一座府邸或「沙丘」（the Dunes）间的一座别墅，也尽可敷用了。

===Original===
Cope, on his own way to dinner, indulged in parallel imaginings. He saw a larger room than his present, with more furniture and better; a bookcase instead of a shelf; a closet, and hot and cold water in some convenient alcove; a second table, with a percolator on it, at which Arthur, who was a light sleeper and willingly an early riser, might indulge his knack for coffee-making to the advantage of them both. And Arthur had the same blessed facility with toast.

Then his thoughts made an excursion toward Randolph. Here was a man who was in business in the city, and who was related, by marriage, to the board of trustees. How soon might one feel sufficiently well acquainted with him to ask his friendly offices in behalf of the newcomer—the man who might reasonably be expected the first week in January?

===Chinese===
科普在自己去用晚餐的路上，也纵情做着平行的想象。他看见一间比眼下更大的屋子，家具更多也更好；是一只书橱而不是一块搁板；有一壁衣橱，某个方便的凹间里装着冷热水；还有第二张桌子，上面安着一台渗滤壶——觉轻而又甘愿早起的阿瑟，可以在桌边纵情施展他煮咖啡的妙技，叫两人同受其惠。何况阿瑟烤起吐司来，也自有一份同样可感念的天分。

随后他的思绪又往伦道夫那边游走了一遭。此人就在城里经商，又同校董会结着姻亲。还须多久，才好算同他熟到足以开口，请他看在友好的分上为那位新来者出力——那位大约在一月第一个星期里便可望抵达的人？

## XVII Cope Among Crosscurrents / 第十七章 科普身处乱流之间

===Original===
Next morning, at breakfast, Amy Leffingwell kept, for the most part, a rapt and meditative eye on her plate. Hortense gave her now and then an impatient, half-angry glare, and had to be cut short in some stinging observations on Cope. “But it *was* foolish,” Medora Phillips felt obliged to concede. “What in the world made you do it?”

But Amy continued to smile at the tablecloth. She seemed to be intimating that there was a special folly which transcended mere general folly and approximated wisdom.

After breakfast she spoke a few words to Carolyn. She had had all night to think the matter over; she now saw it from a new angle and in a new light.

“You should have seen how he shook himself free from that sail, and all,” she said. “And while we were swimming in he held his hand under my chin﻿—at least part of the time. And when we reached the sandbars he put his arm through mine and helped me over every one.” And in this state of mind she went off to her class.

===Chinese===
次日清晨用早餐时，艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）大半时候只管出神地、若有所思地凝望着自己面前的盘子。霍滕丝（Hortense）时不时朝她投来一记不耐而半含愠怒的白眼，几番正要就科普（Cope）发几句刻薄话，都被半路拦断了。「不过那也*确实*是犯傻，」梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）觉得不能不承认。「你到底是中了什么邪，才那样做的？」

可艾米只管对着桌布微笑。她那神情似乎在表示：世上自有一种特等的痴愚，超乎寻常泛泛之蠢，而几近于智慧了。

早餐过后，她对卡罗琳（Carolyn）说了几句话。她已有一整夜的光阴来把这件事细细想过；如今她换了一个新的角度、借着一片新的光亮来看待它了。

「你真该看看他是怎样从那张帆里挣脱出来的，还有其余种种，」她说。「我们往岸边游的时候，他把手掌托在我的下巴底下——至少有那么一段时间。到沙洲跟前，他就把自己的一条手臂穿进我的臂弯，搀扶我越过一道又一道。」她便是怀着这样一副心绪，上课去了。

===Original===
Cope was received by his own class with a subdued hilarity. His young people felt that he had shown poor judgment in going out on the water at all﻿—for the University, by tacit consent, left the lake pretty well alone. They thought that, once out, he had shown remarkably inept seamanship. And they thought that he had chosen a too near and too well-lighted stage for the exhibition of both. This forenoon the “Eighteenth Century Novelists” involved Smollett, and with every reference to the water looks of understanding traveled from student to student: that the class was of both sexes made the situation no better. Cope was in good enough physical condition﻿—the unspeakable draught from the unspeakable flask had ensured that﻿—but he felt what was in the air of the classroom and was correspondingly ill at ease.

He had had, for several days, an understanding with Basil Randolph that they were to go together to the next weekly reception of the president’s wife. Randolph wished to push Cope’s fortunes wherever he might, and to make him stand out from the general ranks of the young instructors. He had the entrée to the Thursdays at the president’s house, and he wanted Cope to meet personally and intimately, under the guidance he could provide, a few of the academic dignitaries and some of the wealthier and more prominent townspeople. Notwithstanding Mrs. Phillips’ confident impression, Cope’s exploit at her own table had gained no wide currency. The people she had entertained were people who expected and commanded a succession of daily impressions from one quarter or another. With them, a few light words on Cope’s achievement were sufficient; they walked straight on toward the sensation the next day was sure to bring. But of course the whole University knew about his second performance. Some of its members had witnessed it, and all of them had read about it, next day, in Churchton’s four-page “Index.”

===Chinese===
科普受到自己班上学生的接待，伴随的是一阵强自按捺的热闹。这些年轻人觉得，他竟然上得了水面，这件事本身便显得判断欠佳——因为依照大学里心照不宣的共识，那片湖是轻易不去招惹的。他们又觉得，他既已出了湖，操舟的身手又笨拙得可观。他们还觉得，他偏偏挑中了一处离得太近、灯火太亮的舞台，来同时展示这两桩。这天上午，「十八世纪小说家」这门课讲到了斯莫利特（Smollett），每逢有涉及水的字眼，会意的眼色便在学生之间往来传递：何况这一班男女同堂，情形便愈发不可收拾。科普的身体状况倒是够好——那只不便明言的小瓶里那口不便明言的烈酒，已确保了这一点——可他感觉得出教室空气里的意味，随之也就相应地局促不安。

几天来，他同巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph）有一个默契：两人要一道去赴校长夫人的下一次每周招待会。伦道夫（Randolph）巴不得处处替科普的前程推波助澜，让他从青年教师泛泛的行列中拔萃而出。校长府上的周四聚会，他自有出入的门路；他想让科普在他所能提供的引领之下，亲自而亲近地结识几位学界显要，以及城中几位更有财有势、更显赫的士绅。尽管菲利普斯太太自信不疑地以为，科普在她自家餐桌上的那番壮举早已传扬开去，其实它并未广泛流传。她宴请的那些人，本来就是坐等着、并且呼喝着要从这一方或那一方源源领受每日新鲜印象的人物。对他们来说，关于科普的壮举，轻飘飘几句闲话已尽足够；他们头也不回，径直奔着第二天必会带来的新轰动去了。至于他的第二场表演，全校自然无人不晓。有些人亲眼目睹，而所有人第二天都在丘奇顿（Churchton）那份四版面的《索引报》（The Index）上读到了它。

===Original===
The president’s wife was a sprightly lady, who believed in keeping up the social end of things. Her Thursdays offered coffee and chocolate at a handsomely appointed table, and a little dancing, now and then, for the livelier of the young professors and the daughters of the town’s best-known families; above all, she insisted on “receiving”﻿—even on having a “receiving line.” She would summon, for example, the wife of one of the most eminent members of the faculty and the obliging spouse of some educationally-minded banker or manufacturer; and she herself always stood, of course, at the head of her line. When Cope came along with Randolph, she intercepted the flow of material for her several assistants farther on, and carried congestion and impatience into the waiting queue behind by detaining him and “having it out.”

She caught his hand with a good, firm, nervous grasp, and flashed on him a broad, meaningful smile.

“Which saved which?” she asked heartily.

===Chinese===
校长夫人是位精神焕发的太太，笃信凡事总要维持住社交这一端。她的周四聚会在一张陈设华美的餐桌上供应咖啡与巧克力，间或还有一点跳舞，给较活泼的年轻教授们和城中望族的闺秀们助兴；而尤其要紧的，是她讲究「迎受」宾客——甚至要设一条「迎宾队列」。譬如，她会召来某位最负盛望的教授的太太，以及某位热心教育的银行家或制造商那位殷勤乐从的配偶；至于她本人，自然永远站在队列的排头。科普随伦道夫到来时，她截住了那股本该输送给她身后几位襄理的人流，又因把他留下、非「问个明白」不可，使排在后面的队伍拥塞起来、焦躁起来。

她伸出一只手，一把握住他的手，那握手饱满、坚实而带着神经质的劲道；又朝他绽开一个大大的、意味深长的微笑。

「到底是谁救了谁呀？」她热热络络地问道。

===Original===
Mrs. Ryder, who was farther along in the line, but not too far, beamed delightedly, yet without the slightest trace of malice. An eminent visiting educator, five or six steps behind our hero, frowned in question and had to have the situation explained by the lady in his company.

Cope, a trifle embarrassed, and half-inclined to wish he had not come, did what he could to deprive the episode of both hero and heroine. It was about an even thing, he guessed﻿—a matter of cooperation.

“Isn’t that delightful!” exclaimed the president’s wife to the wife of the banker, before passing Cope on. “And so modern! Equality of the sexes.﻿ ﻿… Woman doing her share, et cetera! For this,” she presently said to the impatient educator from outside, “are we coeducational!” And, “Good teamwork!” she contrived to call after Cope, who was now disappearing in the crowd.

Cope lost himself from Randolph, and presently got away without seeing who was pouring coffee or who was the lightest on foot among the younger professors. The president’s wife had asked him, besides, how the young lady had got through it, and had even inquired after her present condition. Well, Amy Leffingwell was enrolled among the University instructors, and doubtless the wife of the institution’s head had been well within her rights﻿—even duly mindful of the proprieties. But “The Index”! That sheet, staid and proper enough on most occasions, had seemed, on this one, to couple their names quite unwarrantably. “Couple!” Cope repeated the word, and felt an injury. If he had known that Amy had carefully cut out and preserved the offending paragraph, his thought would have taken on a new and more disquieting tone.

===Chinese===
赖德太太（Mrs. Ryder）在队列中站得靠前一些，但也不算太前，这时喜笑颜开，却没有一丝一毫的恶意。我们主人公身后五六步的地方，一位声名显赫的来访教育家疑惑地皱起眉头，只得由同行的女士把个中情形讲解给他听。

科普有点发窘，一半已开始懊悔不该来，只得尽力把这段事迹里的男女主角一并抹去。照他估量，两边出力大约不相上下——不过是一番合作罢了。

「这岂不妙极了！」校长夫人在放科普往前走之前，对那位银行家太太感叹道。「而且多么现代！男女平等。……女性也尽她的一份，等等！」接着她又对那位等得不耐烦的外来教育家说：「正是为了这个，我们才实行男女同校！」随后，她还想法子朝着已在人丛中隐没的科普背影喊了一声：「合作得漂亮！」

科普同伦道夫走散了，不多时便脱身而去，既没看清是谁在斟咖啡，也没看清年轻教授们中间究竟谁脚步最轻快。此外，校长夫人还问过他，那位年轻小姐是怎么撑过来的，甚至打听了她眼下的状况。是啊，艾米·莱芬韦尔本来就名列这所大学的教师名册，一校之首的夫人这样做，无疑全然在她职权之内——甚至还谨守着分寸礼数。可是那《索引报》！那份报纸平日里够庄重得体的了，独独这一回，竟把两人的名字硬生生「配作一对」，全无半点道理。「配作一对！」科普把这个字眼又咀嚼了一遍，只觉受了伤害。倘若他知道，艾米早已把那段惹是生非的短文细心剪下、妥为保存，他的念头便会换上一副全新而更教人不安的调子了。

===Original===
In the inquiry of the president’s wife about the condition of his copartner in adventure he found a second source of dissatisfaction. He had not called up to ask after Amy; but Mrs. Phillips, with a great show of solicitude, had called up early on Monday morning to ask after him. He had then, in turn, made a counter-inquiry, of course; but he could take no credit for initiative. Neither had he yet called at the house; nor did he feel greatly prompted to do so. That must doubtless be done; but he might wait until the first fresh impact of the event should somewhat have lost its force.

Mrs. Phillips’ voice had kept, over the telephone, all its vibratory quality; its tones expressed the most palpitating interest. It was already clear﻿—and it became even clearer when he finally called at the house﻿—that she was poetizing him into a hero, and that she regarded Amy herself as but a means, an instrument. At this, Cope felt a little more mortified than before. He knew that he had done poorly in the boat, and he was not sure that, in the first moment of the upset, he should have freed himself unaided; and he confessed that he had not been quite in condition to do very well on the way landward. However, all passed.﻿ ﻿… Within a fortnight or less the incident would have dropped back into its proper perspective, and his students would have found some other matter for entertainment. In the circumstances he grasped at the first source of consolation that came. Randolph was now installed in his new apartment and felt that, though not fully settled, he might risk asking Cope to dinner. “You are the first,” Randolph had said. Cope could not escape the flattery; it was almost comfort.

His prompt acceptance was most welcome to Randolph. Cope had dwelt, for a moment, on the actual presence of Aunt Harriet and on his need of her. Randolph had made no precise study of recent chronology, taking the reason given over the wire as a valid one and feeling glad that there was no hitch this time.

===Chinese===
校长夫人对他这位冒险搭档境况的这般垂询，在他心里是第二个不满之源。他并没有打电话去问候艾米；倒是菲利普斯太太极尽关切之能事，星期一一早便打电话来问候他。他于是也回问了一声，那是自然；可这份主动却算不得他的功劳。他也还没有登门去看望；也并不觉得有多大催促自己去这样做。这事无疑终归得做；但不妨等等，等这件事最初的鲜活冲击稍稍消散些力道再说。

电话里，菲利普斯太太的声音保持着一贯的颤动质地；那语调传达出最悸动难平的关切。事情已然清楚——待他终于登门拜访之后，就更加清楚了——她正在用诗笔把他渲染成一位英雄，并且把艾米本人只看作一个途径、一件器械。对此，科普比先前更添几分羞恼。他明白自己在船上表现得很糟，也不敢担保翻覆之初的第一霎，若非有人相助，他自己能否脱身；他也承认，朝陆地去的这一路，他的状态实在说不上好。不过，一切都过去了。……不出两星期，或者更短，这件事自会缩回它应有的远景里去，他的学生们也自会另找消遣的题目。处此境况，他一把抓住了迎面而来的第一个慰藉之源。伦道夫如今已在新公寓里安顿下来，觉得虽然尚未完全就绪，也不妨冒险请科普来吃顿晚饭。「你是头一位，」伦道夫这样说过。科普躲不开这份抬举；这几乎就是安慰了。

他爽快的应承最合伦道夫的心意。科普当初推辞时，曾在电话里着意坐实：哈丽雅特姑妈（Aunt Harriet）确确实实在场，而自己也少不了她。伦道夫未对近来的时序细加推究，把电话里给出的那个理由当作确凿有据，只暗自庆幸，这一回总算没有落空。

===Original===
Randolph gave Cope a rapid view of the apartment before they sat down to dinner. There were fewer pictures on the newly-papered walls than there were to be, and fewer rugs on the freshly-varnished floors. “My standing lamp will be in that corner,” said Randolph, in the living-room, “﻿—when it comes.” He drew attention to a second bedroom where a man could be put up on occasion: “you, for example, if you ever find yourself shut out late.” He saw Sir Galahad’s gauntlets on the dresser. He even gave Cope a glimpse of his kitchen, where a self-contained Oriental, slightly smiling but otherwise inexpressive, seemed to be dealing competently with the gas-range. But Cope was impressed, most of all, by the dining-room table and its paraphernalia. At Mrs. Phillips’ he had accepted the china, silver and napery as a matter of course﻿—an elaborate entity quite outside his own thoughts and calculations: it was all so immensely far beyond his reach and his needs. Randolph, however, had dealt as a bachelor with a problem which he himself as a bachelor must soon take up, on however different a scale and plane. For everything here was rich and handsome; he should not know how to select such things﻿—still less how to pay for them. He felt dashed; he felt depressed; once more the wonder of people’s “having things.” He sipped his soup in the spirit of humility, and did not quite recover with the chops.

Randolph made little talk; he was glad merely to have Cope there. He indulged no slightest reference to the accident; he assumed, willingly enough, that Cope had done well in a sudden emergency, but did not care to dwell on his judgment at the beginning. Still, a young man was properly enough experimental, venturesome﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
入席之前，伦道夫领着科普把公寓匆匆巡看了一遍。新糊了墙纸的墙上，挂着的画比日后将要挂上的少；新上了清漆的地板上，铺着的地毯也比日后将要铺上的少。「我的落地灯将安放在那个角落，」伦道夫在客厅里说，「——等它到货的时候。」他请科普留意第二间卧室，偶尔可以留一位男士过夜：「比如你，倘若哪天你发现自己深夜被关在了门外。」他仿佛望见，梳妆台上已端放着加拉哈德爵士（Sir Galahad）的那副长手套。他甚至还让科普瞧了一眼他的厨房，里面有一个独来独往的东方人，唇边噙着浅浅的笑，除此之外不露声色，正熟练地对付着煤气灶。然而给科普印象最深的，还是那张餐桌和它的全套行头。在菲利普斯太太府上，他把那些瓷器、银器和台布餐巾视为理所当然——一个繁复的整体，全然在他自己的思虑与盘算之外：这一切，同他的财力和需要都相距得太远太远。而伦道夫却已以一个单身汉的身份，料理了一桩他自己身为单身汉不久便须着手料理的难题——纵然规模与层面都大不相同。因为这里的一切都富丽精美；他实在不知道该怎样挑选这些东西——更不知道该怎样偿付它们。他感到气短；他感到沮丧；又一次惊叹起世人竟能「样样都有」。他怀着谦卑的心境啜着汤，及至排骨端上来，也未能完全缓过劲来。

伦道夫话不多；单是把科普留在跟前，他已觉得高兴。对那场事故，他绝口不提，一星半点也不沾；他很情愿地认定，科普在突发急难中应付得不坏，却不乐意深究他起初的判断。话虽如此，一个年轻人么，肯尝试、敢冒险，原本也是合宜的。……

===Original===
Cope had recovered himself by the time dessert was reached. He accomplished an adjustment to his environment, and Randolph was glad to feel his unaffected response to good food properly cooked and served. “He shan’t gipsy *all* the time,” Randolph said to himself. “I shall try to have him here at least twice a week.” Once in a while the evening might be stormy, and then the gauntlets would be laid on the dresser﻿—perhaps after an informal smoke in pajamas among the curios ranged round the small den.

Cope set down his demitasse with a slight sigh. “Well,” he said, “I suppose that, before long, I shall have to buy a few sticks of furniture myself and a trifle of ‘crockery.’ And a percolator.” Randolph looked across at him in surprise.

===Chinese===
上甜点的时候，科普已经缓过神来。他完成了对周遭环境的调适，伦道夫则高兴地察觉，他对烹制得法、伺候周到的美馔的回应毫无虚饰。「不能让他*老是*过吉卜赛人那样的日子，」伦道夫自忖。「我要设法每星期至少请他来这儿两回。」偶尔也许会碰上一个风雨之夜，到那时，那副长手套便会搁上梳妆台——或许，还得先在那间小书房四壁陈列的古玩中间，穿着睡衣随意抽上一口烟。

科普轻轻叹了口气，放下那杯小盏清咖啡。「唉，」他说，「我想过不了多久，我自己也得买上几件家具，再添一点『坛坛罐罐』。还要一只咖啡渗滤壶。」伦道夫隔着桌子朝他望去，满眼惊奇。

===Original===
“You are moving, then﻿—you too?” Not to greatly better quarters, he almost hoped.

“Yes; and we shall need a few small things by way of outfit.”

“We.” Randolph looked more intently. Housekeeping *à deux*? A roommate? Matrimony? Here was the intrusion of another piece on the board﻿—a piece new and unexpected. Would it turn out to be an added interest for himself, or a plain source of disconcertment? Cope, having unconsciously set the ball rolling, gave it further impetus. He sketched his absent friend and told of their plans for the winter and spring terms. “I shall try for a large easy chair,” he concluded, “unless Arthur can be induced to bring one with him.”

===Chinese===
「这么说，你要搬家了——连你也搬？」他几乎暗自指望，新居所可别好得太过悬殊。

「是啊；我们还得置办几样小件什物，权作安家的行头。」

「我们。」伦道夫的目光更加专注起来。两人搭伙持家（*à deux*）？一位同住的室友？还是成家娶亲？这是棋盘上又一枚棋子的闯入——一枚崭新而出乎意料的棋子。它到头来会成为给自己添的一份兴味呢，还是干脆变成一个明摆着的窘乱之源？科普在不知不觉间已让这只球滚了起来，此刻又给了它一把推力。他略略勾画了那位缺席朋友的样子，讲了他们冬季学期与春季学期的种种打算。「我要设法置一把宽大的安乐椅，」他收束道，「除非能劝得动阿瑟（Arthur）自己随身带一把来。」

===Original===
Randolph, by this time, had led Cope into the den, established him between padded arms, and given him a cigar. He drew Cope’s attention to the jades and swordguards, to the odd assortment of primitive musical instruments (which would doubtless, in time, find a place at the Art Museum in the city), and to his latest acquisition﻿—a volume of Bembo’s *Le Prose*. It had reached him but a week before from Venice﻿—“*in Venetia, al segno del Pozzo*, MDLVII,” said the title-page, in fact. It was bound in vellum, pierced by bookworms, and was decorated, in quaint seventeenth-century penmanship, with marginal annotations, and also, on the fly leaves, with repeated honorifics due to a study of the forms of address by some young aspirant for favor. Randolph had rather depended on it to take Cope’s interest; but now the little *envoi* from the Lagoons seemed lesser in its lustre. Cope indeed took the volume with docility and looked at its classical title-page and at its quaint Biblical colophon; but, “Just who *was* ‘Pietro Bembo’?” he asked; and Randolph realized, with a slight shock, that young instructors teach only what they themselves lately have learned, and that, in many cases, they have not learned much.

===Chinese===
这时，伦道夫已把科普让进了那间小书房，把他安顿进一把安乐椅软垫扶手的环抱里，递给他一支雪茄。他把科普的注意力引向那些玉器和刀镡，引向那批琳琅杂陈的原始乐器（日后无疑会在本市的艺术博物馆里谋得一席之地），又引向他新近到手的一件——一卷本博（Bembo）的《散文集》（*Le Prose*）。它是仅仅一星期之前从威尼斯（Venice）寄到他手里的——「*in Venetia, al segno del Pozzo*, MDLVII」，扉页上白纸黑字正是这么写的。全书以犊皮纸装帧，蛀孔累累，页边有十七世纪古雅笔迹的批注，衬页上还反复写满种种敬语——那是当年某个一心干求提携的年轻人研习称谓礼仪留下的手迹。伦道夫原本满指望靠它来抓住科普的兴趣；可此刻，这件来自潟湖水城的小小赠礼（*envoi*），光泽似乎黯淡了几分。科普倒也驯顺地把那卷书接了过来，看了看它古典式的扉页，又看了看那古雅的圣经体书尾题署；可是，「『皮埃特罗·本博』（Pietro Bembo）究竟*是*何许人也？」他问；伦道夫微微一震，省悟到：青年教师所教的，无非是他们自己新近才学到手的东西，而且，在许多情形之下，他们学到手的也没有多少。

===Original===
But in truth neither paid much heed to the tabulated vocables of the Venetian cardinal﻿—nor to any of the other rarities near by. Basil Randolph was wondering how he was to take Arthur Lemoyne, and was asking himself if his trouble in setting up a new ménage was likely to go for nothing; and Bertram Cope, while he pursued the course of the bookworm through the parchment covers and the yellowed sheets within, was wondering in what definite way his host might aid the fortunes of Arthur Lemoyne and thus make matters a little easier for them both. “*All’ ill.’mo Sig.’r paron ossevnd.’mo.﻿ ﻿… All’ ill.’mo et ecc.’mo Sig.’r paron﻿ ﻿… All’ ill’mo et R.R.d.’mo Sig.’r, Sig.’r Pio. Francesco Bembo, Vesco et Conte di Belluno*”﻿—thus ran the faded brown lines on the flyleaf, in their solicitous currying of favor; but these reiterated forms of address conveyed no meaning to Cope, and offered no opening: now, as once before, he let the matter wait.

===Chinese===
其实，两人谁也没有多少心思去理会那位威尼斯红衣主教开列成篇的字汇——也不曾理会近旁其他任何一件珍品。巴兹尔·伦道夫正在盘算，自己该拿阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）怎么办；正在自问，他张罗这一套新家宅（*ménage*）的辛劳，会不会尽数付诸东流；而伯特伦·科普（Bertram Cope）一边循着蛀虫的孔道，在犊皮封面和里面泛黄的书页间一路穿行，一边正在琢磨：他的这位东道主究竟可以用什么切实的法子帮衬阿瑟·勒莫因的前程，从而让他们两人的处境都松动一些。「*All’ ill.’mo Sig.’r paron ossevnd.’mo.﻿… All’ ill.’mo et ecc.’mo Sig.’r paron… All’ ill’mo et R.R.d.’mo Sig.’r, Sig.’r Pio. Francesco Bembo, Vesco et Conte di Belluno*」——衬页上褪成棕色的字行便是这样一路写下去，殷勤备至地谄奉着恩主；只是这一连串反复堆叠的敬称，对科普不具任何意义，也给不出任何话头：如今，一如从前，他且把这桩事搁下再说。

===Original===
Randolph thought over Cope’s statement of his plans, and his slight touch of pique did not pass away. Toward the end of the evening, he spoke of the wreck and the rescue, after all.

“Well,” he said, “you are not so completely committed as I feared.”

“Committed?”

“By your new household arrangements.”

“Well, I shall have back my chum.”

===Chinese===
伦道夫把科普那番关于自身打算的陈说细细想过，心头那一点小小的愠意并未消散。晚间将尽的时候，他终究还是提起了翻船与救援的事。

「嗯，」他说，「你还不像我所担心的那样，被完全拴住了。」

「拴住？」

「被你那套新的居家安排啊。」

「这个嘛，我可以把我的好伙伴接回来同住了。」

===Original===
Randolph put forward the alternative.

“I was afraid, for a moment, that you might be taking a wife.”

“A wife?”

“Yes. Such a rescue often leads straight to matrimony﻿—in the storybooks, anyhow.”

Cope laughed, but with a slight disrelish. “We’re in actual life still, I’m glad to think. What I said on one stretch of the shore goes on the other,” he declared. “I don’t feel any more inclination to wedded life than ever, nor any likelihood”﻿—here he spoke with effort, as if conscious of a possible danger on some remote horizon﻿—“of entering it.”

“It *would* have been sudden, wouldn’t it?” commented Randolph, with a short laugh. “Well,” he went on, “one who inclines to hospitality must work with the material at his disposal. I shall be glad, on some occasion or other,” he proceeded, with a slight trace of formality creeping into his tone, “to entertain your friend.”

“I shall be more than glad,” replied Cope, “to have you meet.”

===Chinese===
伦道夫把另一种可能摆了出来。

「我有一刻还担心，你莫不是要娶太太了呢。」

「娶太太？」

「是啊。这样的救援，常常径直通向婚嫁——至少，在故事书里是这样。」

科普笑了，笑里却带一丝不情愿。「所幸我们还活在实实在在的人世里。我在这一段湖岸上说过的话，到了那一段照样作数，」他宣称。「我对婚姻生活，不比以往多一分向往，也没有一分可能」——说到这里，他颇为吃力，仿佛觉察到某道遥远天际线上的潜在危险——「要跨进它去。」

「那样的话，*未免*也太仓促了些，不是吗？」伦道夫短短一笑，评说道。「好吧，」他接着说，「喜好款待宾客的人，只能就手边现有的材料来操持。往后遇上什么合适的机会，」他继续道，一丝拘谨悄然爬进了语气里，「我乐于款待你的朋友。」

「那我更岂止是乐意，」科普答道，「定要让你们见上一面。」

## XXVI Cope as a Go-Between / 第二十六章 科普居间传话

===Original===
This brief exchange might have passed for a quarrel and a reconciliation; and the reconciliation seemed to call for a seal. That was soon set by another of Randolph’s patient invitations to dinner.

“Let’s go,” said Cope; “I’ve got to go again﻿—sometime.”

“I don’t care about it, very much,” replied Lemoyne.

“If you want any help of his toward a position.﻿ ﻿… Time’s passing. And a man can’t be expected to bestir himself much for another man he’s never even seen.”

“All right. I’ll go with you.”

===Chinese===
这一场简短的交锋，尽可以算作一次口角继以一次言和；而言和这件事，似乎还须一方印记来加封。不久，伦道夫（Randolph）又耐着性子发出一封晚宴之邀，这方印记便很快盖下了。

「我们去吧，」科普（Cope）说；「我反正总得再去——早晚的事。」

「我不怎么热衷于此，」勒莫因（Lemoyne）答道。

「你若想让他帮衬着谋个职位。……日子可不等人。再说，人家连你的面都未曾见过，总不能指望人家为一个素不相识的人奔走出力。」

「好吧。我同你一起去。」

===Original===
Randolph was glad to see Cope again, whom he had not met since the half hour in Hortense Dunton’s studio. He was also glad to secure, finally, a close and leisurely look at Lemoyne. Lemoyne took the same occasion for a close and leisurely look at Randolph. Each viewed the other with dislike and distrust. Each spoke, so far as might be, to Cope﻿—or through him. Sing-Lo, who was prepared to smile, saw few smiles elsewhere, and became sedate, even glum.

Randolph felt a physical distaste for Lemoyne. His dark eyes were too liquid; his person was too plump; the bit of black bristle beneath his nose was an offense; his aura﻿—Yet who can say anything definite about so indefinite a thing as an aura, save that one feels it and is attracted or repelled by it? Lemoyne, on his side, developed an equal distaste (or repugnance) for the “little gray man”﻿—as he called Randolph to himself and, later, even to Cope; though Randolph, speaking justly, was exactly neither gray nor little. Lemoyne noted, too, the early banishment of Randolph’s eyeglasses, which disappeared as they had disappeared once or twice before. He felt that Randolph was trying to stay young rather late, and was showing himself inclined to “go” with younger men longer than they would welcome him. Why didn’t he consort with people of his own age and kind? He was old; so why couldn’t he *be* old?

===Chinese===
伦道夫很高兴又见到科普——自打在霍滕丝·邓顿（Hortense Dunton）画室里的那半个小时以来，两人还不曾会过面。他也很高兴，终于能够从容而贴近地把勒莫因打量一番。勒莫因也趁同一机会，把伦道夫从容而贴近地打量了一番。两人彼此相看，皆怀厌恶与猜疑。两人开口，尽可能只对着科普说——或是借他传话。辛洛（Sing-Lo）本来预备好了一脸笑容，却见席上别处难得有几个笑影，便也端凝起来，甚至有点阴沉了。

伦道夫对勒莫因生出一种近乎生理上的厌恶。他那双黑眼睛水光太足；他整个人太丰腴；鼻下那一小撮黑硬的髭须惹人嫌恶；他那股气息——可是，气息这等虚而无着的东西，除了觉得它、受它吸引或被它排拒之外，谁又能说出半句要领来？勒莫因这一面，也对那位「灰色小个子男人」生出同等的厌恶（或曰憎恶）——他心里这般称呼伦道夫，后来甚至当着科普也这样叫；虽然平心而论，伦道夫恰恰既不灰、也不小。勒莫因还留意到，伦道夫的眼镜早早便收没了踪影，一如先前一两回那样。他觉得伦道夫到了这般年纪还想把年轻留驻，未免留得太晚；而且他还露出一种意向，要同年轻人一道「走」下去，走的时日比人家乐意接纳的还长。他何不同自己年纪相仿、路数相同的人交往？他老了；那他为什么不能索性*就*老下去呢？

===Original===
The talk led﻿—through Cope﻿—to reminiscences of life in Winnebago. Randolph presently began to feel Lemoyne as a variously yet equivocally gifted young fellow﻿—one so curiously endowed as to be of no use to his own people, and of no avail for any career they were able to offer him. A bundle of minor talents; a possible delight to casual acquaintances, but an exasperation to his own household; an ornamental skimmer over life’s surfaces, when not a false fire for other young voyagers along life’s coasts. Yet Bertram Cope admired him and had become absorbed in him. Their life in that northern town, with its fringe of interests﻿—educational, ecclesiastical, artistic and aquatic﻿—had been intimate, fused to a degree. Randolph began to realize, for the first time, the difficulties in the way of “cultivating” Cope. Cope was a field already occupied, a niche already filled.

While Randolph was gathering (through Cope) details of the life in Winnebago, Lemoyne was gathering (through Cope) details of the life in Churchton during the past autumn. He began to reconstruct that season: the long range of social entertainments, the proposed fall excursions, the sudden shifting of domicile. Randolph, it was clear, had tried to appropriate Cope and to supplant (knowingly or unknowingly) Cope’s closest friend. Lemoyne became impatient over the fact that he was now sitting at Randolph’s table. However, if Randolph could help him to a place and a salary, that would make some amends.

===Chinese===
话题借科普居间，引到了对温尼贝戈（Winnebago）生活的追忆。伦道夫当下便开始觉得，勒莫因是个才艺驳杂却又不甚着调的年轻人——禀赋奇特得竟至于对本家人一无所用，对他们所能为他张罗的任何前程也全然无补。一小捆零零碎碎的才艺；泛泛之交或许觉得他讨喜，自家人却为他恼恨；他是一只只知华美地掠过人生表面的水鸟，再不然，便是人生海岸边一簇误导年轻航海人的假火。然而伯特伦·科普（Bertram Cope）偏偏敬慕他，而且已经为他倾心入迷。他们在那座北方小城里的生活——四缘环绕着种种兴味，教育的、教会的、艺术的、水上的——亲密无间，在相当的程度上熔铸成了一体。伦道夫这才头一回意识到「结纳」科普路上的难处。科普是一片已有人占耕的田地，一方已安放了偶像的壁龛。

当伦道夫（隔着科普）搜求温尼贝戈生活的细情时，勒莫因也在（隔着科普）搜求去年秋天丘奇顿（Churchton）生活的细情。他开始把那一季重新拼构起来：长长一串的社交宴集，拟议中的秋季出游，突如其来的迁居。事情很清楚，伦道夫曾想把科普据为己有，并且（有心也好，无心也好）要顶替掉科普最亲密的朋友。勒莫因想到自己此刻竟坐在伦道夫的餐桌上，心里老大不耐。不过，倘若伦道夫能帮他谋到一个位子、一份薪水，那倒也算得上几分补偿。

===Original===
Presently Cope, having served as an intermediary, became the open centre of interest. His thesis was brought forward as a suitable subject of inquiry and comment. It was a relief to have come to a final decision; but no relief was in sight for a long time from the slavery of close reading. Every moment that could be spared from his classroom was given up to books﻿—authors in whom he might be interested or not interested, but who must be gone through.

“A sort of academic convention,” said Cope, rather wanly; “but a necessary one.”

His eyes had begun to show excessive application; at least they looked tired and dim. His color, too, was paler. He had come to suggest again the young man who had been picked up from Medora Phillips’ dining-room floor and laid out on the couch in her library, and who had shown a good deal of pallor during the few days that followed. “Take a little more air and exercise,” Randolph counselled.

“A good rule always, for everybody,” said Lemoyne, with a withholding of all tone and expression.

===Chinese===
不多时，做了一回中人的科普，变成了公开的众目所聚。他的论文被端出来，成了一个合宜的问询与评骘的题目。总算落定了最后的决断，这是一桩宽慰；可是那苦役般的精读功夫，却久久望不见宽慰。凡能从课堂上匀出的每一刻，尽数交给了书本——那些作者，他或有兴趣、或无兴趣，却都必须通读一遍。

「无非是一桩学界的惯例，」科普说得颇为恹恹；「却又是一桩免不了的。」

他的双眼已开始显出用功过度之象；至少看上去倦怠而晦暗。他的气色也更见苍白。他又叫人想起当初那个从梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）家饭厅地板上被扶起来、安置在她书房长榻上的年轻人，以及那人随后几天里颇不轻微的苍白。「多吸些空气，多做些运动，」伦道夫劝道。

「对谁、对什么时候都是一条好规矩，」勒莫因说，声调与神情一齐敛尽，不置臧否。

===Original===
“I believe,” Randolph continued, “that you are losing in both weight and color. That would be no advantage to yourself﻿—and it might complicate Miss Dunton’s problem. It’s perplexing to an artist when one’s subject changes under one’s very eye.”

“There won’t be much time for sitting, from now on,” observed Lemoyne concisely.

“I might try to go round once more,” said Cope, “﻿—in fairness. If there are to be higher lights on my cheekbones and lower lights for my eyes, an hour or so should serve to settle it.”

“I wouldn’t introduce many changes into my eyes and cheekbones, if I were you,” said Randolph. Lemoyne was displeased; he thought that Randolph was taking advantage of his position as host to make an observation of unwarranted saliency, and he frowned at his plate.

Cope flushed, and looked at his.

===Chinese===
「我相信，」伦道夫接着说，「你的体重和气色都在亏损。这对你自己没有什么好处——还可能把邓顿小姐的难题弄得更复杂。画家眼看着自己的模特儿在眼皮底下变样，那是很叫人为难的事。」

「往后可没有多少工夫坐下来当模特儿了，」勒莫因说得简截。

「我不妨再过去坐上一回，」科普说，「——为公平起见。倘若我的颧骨上要添几笔亮部、眼窝里要加几笔暗部，个把钟头也就尽够定局了。」

「我要是你，就不会在自己的眼睛和颧骨上添那么多改动，」伦道夫说。勒莫因大为不快；他觉得伦道夫这是仗着主人的身份，说出一句不成体统的扎眼话来，便对着自己面前的盘子皱眉。

科普红了脸，也去看自己面前的盘子。

===Original===
The talk drifted toward dramatics, with Winnebago once more the background; but the foreground was occupied by a new musical comedy which one of the clubs might try in another month, and the tone became more cheery. Sing-Lo, who had come in with a maple mousse of his own making, smiled at last; and he smiled still more widely when, at the end of the course, his chief occidental masterpiece was praised. Sing-Lo also provided coffee and cigars in the den; and it was here that Cope felt the atmosphere right for venturing a word in behalf of Lemoyne. There had been few signs of relenting in Winnebago; and some modest source of income would be welcome﻿—in fact, was almost necessary.

“Of course work *is* increasing in the offices,” said Randolph, looking from one young man to the other; “and of course I have, directly or indirectly, some slight ‘influence.’ ”

He felt no promptings to lend Lemoyne a hand; yet Cope himself, even if out of reach, might at least remain an object of continuing kindness.

“But if you are to interest yourself in some new undertaking by ‘The Grayfriars,’ ” he said to Lemoyne, “will you have much time and attention to give to office-work?”

===Chinese===
话题飘向了演剧一道，背景又是温尼贝戈；前景却被一部新的音乐喜剧占去——有个社团再过一个月说不定要试排它——谈锋也随之轻快了些。辛洛端着他亲手制作的枫糖慕斯进来，终于绽开了笑容；等到这道菜吃完、他那件西方手艺的头号杰作受到称赏时，他笑得愈发开了。辛洛又在小书房里奉上咖啡和雪茄；正是在这里，科普觉得气氛适宜，可以放胆为勒莫因进一言了。温尼贝戈方面很少有转圜的迹象；一份进项微薄的差使总是受欢迎的——事实上，几乎是必不可少的。

「当然，办公室里的事务嘛，倒*是*在与日俱增，」伦道夫说，目光从这一位青年移向那一位；「我自然也直接间接地有上几分『影响』。」

他全然没有要向勒莫因伸出援手的冲动；然而科普本人，纵然求而不得，至少还可以继续做他善意相待的对象。

「不过，你若是要在『灰衣修士』（The Grayfriars）社的新举动里投入兴致，」他对勒莫因说，「还能拿出多少时间和心思来料理公务呢？」

===Original===
“Oh, I have time,” replied Lemoyne jauntily, “and not many studies. Half a day of routine work, I thought.﻿ ﻿… Of course I’m not a manager, or director, or anything like that. I should just have a part of moderate importance, and should have only to give good heed to rehearsals.﻿ ﻿…”

“Well,” said Randolph thoughtfully.

“I hope you can do something,” put in Cope, with fervor.

“Well,” said Randolph again.

This uncomfortable and unsatisfactory dinner of three presently drew to its end. “I’d have made it four,” said Randolph to Foster, a day or two later, “if I’d only thought of it in time.”

===Chinese===
「哦，时间我是有的，」勒莫因扬扬自得地答道，「功课也不多。我想的是，每天半天的例行公务。……当然，经理呀、导演呀那一类，我都不是。我不过挂一个不大不小的角色，只须把排练好好放在心上就是了。……」

「唔，」伦道夫若有所思地说。

「希望你能想点办法，」科普热切地插嘴。

「唔，」伦道夫又应了一声。

这场局促而不痛快的三人晚宴不久便近了尾声。「我要是当时想得到，就把它凑成四个人了，」一两天后，伦道夫对福斯特（Foster）说。

===Original===
“*I* don’t want to meet them again,” returned Foster quickly.

“Well,” said Randolph, “I’ve no fondness for the new fellow, myself; but﻿—”

“And I don’t care about the other, either.”

Randolph sighed. This was plainly one of Foster’s off days. The only wonder was he had not more of them. He sat in darkness, with few diversions, occupations, ameliorations. His mind churned mightily on the scanty materials that came his way. He founded big guesses on nothings; he raised vast speculative edifices on the slightest of premises. To dislike a man he could not even see! Well, the blind﻿—and the half-blind﻿—had their own intuitions and followed their own procedures.

===Chinese===
「*我*可不想再同他们见面，」福斯特立刻回敬。

「唉，」伦道夫说，「那个新来的人，我自己也谈不上喜欢；不过——」

「另外那个，我也不喜欢。」

伦道夫叹了口气。这分明是福斯特失意的日子之一。奇怪的反倒是这样的日子竟不多。他坐在黑暗里，消遣、事务、慰藉，样样寥寥。他的头脑凭着偶尔流到眼前的一点贫乏材料，搅动得厉害。他能在子虚乌有之上立起宏大的臆测，能在最单薄的前提上筑起浩大的悬想楼台。竟会厌恶一个他连见都见不着的人！唉，盲人——还有半盲的人——自有他们的直觉，也行他们的一套章程。

===Original===
“Then you wouldn’t advise me to speak a word for him?﻿—for them?”

“Certainly not!” rejoined Foster, with all promptness. “They’ve treated you badly. They’ve put you off; and they came, finally, only because they counted on getting something out of you.

“Oh, I wouldn’t say that of Cope.”

“I would. And I do. They’re completely wrapped up in their own interests, and in each other; and they’re coupled to get anything they can out of Number Three. Or out of Number Four. Or Five. Or out of X﻿—the world, that is to say.”

Randolph shrugged. This was one of Foster’s bad days indeed.

===Chinese===
「那么，你不主张我替他讲一句话了？——替他们讲？」

「自然不主张！」福斯特应声而出，毫不含糊。「他们待你太坏。他们把你一再往后推；末了肯来，也只因为他们指望从你身上捞到点什么。」

「哦，这话我不能这样说科普。」

「我能。我也这样说。他们一门心思全浸在自己的利益里、浸在彼此身上；他们俩串作一气，能从第三者身上刮多少是多少。或者从第四者。第五者。或者从X身上——也就是说，从全世界身上。」

伦道夫耸耸肩。这确确实实是福斯特的坏日子之一。

===Original===
“And what’s this I hear about Hortense?” asked Foster, with bitterness.

“That won’t amount to much.”

“It won’t? She’s out in the open, finally. She took that place for a month with one express object﻿—to get him there, paint or no paint. She’s fretful and cantankerous over every day of delay, and soon she’ll be in an undisguised rage.”

“What does her aunt say to it?”

===Chinese===
「我听说的霍滕丝那桩事，又是怎么回事？」福斯特怨愤地问。

「那成不了什么气候。」

「成不了？她终于把一切都摊到明面上了。她去那里一个月，为的就是一个露骨的目的——把人弄到那儿去，画不画在所不计。每拖一天，她就烦躁一天、横躁一天，要不了多久，她就要发起一场毫不掩饰的盛怒来。」

「她姨妈怎么看？」

===Original===
“She’s beginning to be vexed. She’s losing patience. She thinks it’s a mistake﻿—and an immodest one. She wants to send her away for a visit. To think of it!﻿—as soon as one girl lets go another takes hold﻿—and a third person holds on through all!”

“Joe! Joe!”

But Foster was not to be stayed.

“And that poetry of Carolyn’s! Medora herself came up and read it to me. It was a ‘tribute,’ she thought!”

===Chinese===
「她也渐渐动气了。她的耐性正在耗尽。她觉得这是个错处——而且是个不端庄的错处。她想把她打发出门去做客。想想看吧！——一个姑娘刚一撒手，另一个便攥了上去——还有第三个人，自始至终攥着不放！」

「乔！乔！」

可是福斯特哪里刹得住。

「还有卡罗琳（Carolyn）的那些诗！梅多拉竟亲自上楼来念给我听。她还当那是一篇『颂词』呢！」

===Original===
“That won’t amount to anything at all.”

“It won’t? With Hortense scornfully ridiculing it, and Carolyn bursting into tears before she can make her bolt from the room, and Amy wondering whether, after all﻿ ﻿… ! If things are as bad as they are for me up here, how much worse must they be for the rest of them below! And that confounded engagement has made it still worse all round!”

Randolph ran his palms over his perplexed temples. “Whose?”

===Chinese===
「那更是一点气候也成不了。」

「成不了？霍滕丝冷笑着挖苦它，卡罗琳眼泪迸涌、来不及从房里夺路而逃，艾米（Amy）则在寻思到底是不是……！要是楼上的我尚且如此难堪，楼下其余的人还得难堪到什么地步！那桩该死的婚约更是弄得四下里一团糟！」

伦道夫双掌摩挲着满是困惑的太阳穴。「谁的？」

===Original===
“Whose? No wonder you ask! Engagements, then.”

“When are they going to be married?”

“The first week in May, I hear. But Pearson is trying for the middle of April. His flat is taken.” Foster writhed in his chair.

“Why do they care for him?” he burst out. “He’s nothing in himself. And he cares nothing for them. And he cares nothing for you,” Foster added boldly. “All he has thought for is that fellow from up north.”

===Chinese===
「谁的？难怪你要问！订婚么，多着呢。」

「他们打算几时成婚？」

「听说是五月头一个礼拜。不过皮尔逊（Pearson）正力争四月中旬。他那套公寓已经定下了。」福斯特在椅子里扭动着。

「他们干吗把他放在心上？」他冲口而出。「他本人一无足取。他也不把他们放在心上。他也不把你放在心上，」福斯特大胆地补上一句。「他一心惦记的，只有北边来的那家伙。」

===Original===
“Don’t ask me why they care,” replied Randolph, with studied sobriety. “Why does anybody care? And for what? For the thing that is just out of reach. He’s cool; he’s selfish; he’s indifferent. Yet, somehow, frost and fire join end to end and make the circle complete.” He fell into reflection. “It’s all like children straining upward for an icicle, and presently slipping, with cracked pates, on the ice below.”

“Well, *my* pate isn’t cracked.”

“Unless it’s the worst cracked of all.”

Foster tore off his shade and threw it on the floor. “Mine?” he cried. “Look to your own!”

===Chinese===
「别问我他们干吗放在心上，」伦道夫答道，故意用着一份持重的清醒。「有谁不把什么人放在心上呢？又为的是什么呢？为的是那件恰恰差一点够得着的东西。他冷淡，他自私，他漠然。然而不知怎的，霜与火首尾相衔，偏接成了一个完满的圆。」他堕入沉思。「这正像一群孩子踮脚伸臂去够一支冰凌，转眼便磕裂了脑壳，滑倒在下面的冰上。」

「哼，*我*的脑壳可没裂。」

「只怕裂得最厉害的正是它。」
