福斯特一把扯下眼罩，掷在地上。「我的脑壳？」他喊道。「先照管你自己的吧！」

===Original===
“Joe!” said Randolph, rising. “That won’t quite do!”

“Be a fool along with the others, if you will!” retorted Foster. “Oh!” he went on, “Haven’t I seen it all? Haven’t I felt it all? You, Basil Randolph, mind your own ways too!”

Randolph thought of words, but held his tongue. Words led to other words, and he might soon find himself involved in what would seem like a defense﻿—an attitude which he did not relish, a course of which he did not acknowledge the need. “Poor Joe!” he thought; “sitting too much by himself and following over-closely the art of putting things together﻿—anyhow!” Joe Foster must have more company and different things to consider. What large standard work﻿—what history, biography, or bulky mass of memoirs in from four to eight volumes﻿—would be the best to begin on before the winter should be too far spent?

===Chinese===
「乔！」伦道夫起身说。「这可使不得！」

「你乐意，就同旁人一道当傻瓜去！」福斯特反唇相讥。「哦！」他继续说，「这一切我没有统统看在眼里么？没有统统感在身上么？你，巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph），也留神着你自己的行止吧！」

伦道夫想出了话，却咬住舌头没说。话会引出别的话，他很快就会发现自己卷进一场貌似辩护的东西里去——这一姿态他不乐于摆，这一步骤他也认无必要。「可怜的乔！」他想；「独坐的时候太多，把那门将诸事串拼到一处的功夫又追得太紧——不问青红皂白！」乔·福斯特必须多一些陪伴、换一些可思量的东西。趁冬天还不曾耗费得太深，该从哪一部大部的标准著作着手才最好呢——哪一部历史、哪部传记，或是哪一部四卷到八卷的庞然回忆录？

===Original===
Four or five days later, Randolph wrote to Cope that there was a good prospect for a small position in the administration offices of the University, and a week later Lemoyne was in that position. Cope, who recognized Randolph’s handling of the matter as a personal favor, replied in a tone of some warmth. “He’s really a very decent fellow, after all﻿—of course he is,” pronounced Randolph. Lemoyne himself wrote more tardily and more coolly. He was taking time from his Psychology and from “The Antics of Annabella,” it appeared, to acquaint himself with the routine of his new position. Randolph shrugged: he must wait to see which of the three interests would be held the most important.

===Chinese===
四五天后，伦道夫写信告知科普，大学行政各办公室里有个小小的职位颇有指望；一星期后，勒莫因便就了那个职。科普把伦道夫对此事的处置看作一番私人的情分，回信的口吻便带了些暖意。「他到底还是个很正派的人——当然是的，」伦道夫下断语说。勒莫因本人的信来得迟些，也冷淡些。看样子，他正从心理学课业和《安娜贝拉的滑稽戏》（The Antics of Annabella）里匀出工夫，来熟习新职的例行事务。伦道夫耸了耸肩：三种兴味之中究竟哪一桩会被当作顶要紧的，他只好等着瞧了。

## XXX Cope as a Hero / 第三十章 科普俨然英雄

===Original===
At ten o’clock Cope found himself tucked away in a small room on the ground floor. It had been left quite as planned and constructed by the original builder of the house. It was cramped and narrow, with low ceiling and one small window. It gave on a short side-porch which was almost too narrow to sit on and which was apropos of no special prospect. Doubtless more than one stalwart youth had slept there before him﻿—a succession of farmers’ sons who fed all day on the airs and spaces of the great out-of-doors, and who needed little of either through a short night’s rest. It was more comfortable at the end of April than other guests had found it in mid-August.

A little before eleven he awoke the house with a loud, ringing cry. Someone outside had passed his narrow window; feet were heard on the back porch and hands at the kitchen door. Peter was out as quickly as Cope himself; and the women, in differing stages of dress and half-dress, followed at once.

While Mrs. Phillips and Carolyn were clinging to Cope, who had rushed out in undershirt and trousers, Peter had a short tussle on the porch with the intruder. He came in showing a scratch or two on his face, and he reported the pantry window broken open.

“Some tramp along the beach saw our lights,” suggested Carolyn.

===Chinese===
十点钟，科普（Cope）发觉自己被安置进了底层的一间小屋。这屋子完全保持着房子原建者当初规划建造时的模样：局促狭窄，天花板低矮，只有一扇小窗。窗子开向一道窄得几乎坐不下人的短短侧廊，而那廊子也衬托不出什么特别的景致。毋庸置疑，在他之前，已有不止一个壮实的小伙子在这屋里睡过——那是一代接一代的农家子弟，白天在辽阔的户外饱餐天光与空旷，短短一夜安眠，这两样所需无几。四月底睡在这里，总比八月中旬来住的客人所觉的要舒服些。

十一点差一刻，他一声嘹亮震耳的喊叫惊醒了整座房子。外面有人从他那扇窄小的窗前经过；后廊上听得见脚步声，厨房门上有一双手在动。彼得（Peter）冲出门的速度不亚于科普本人；几位女士有的穿戴齐整、有的还半更着衣，形态不一，也随即跟了出来。

菲利普斯太太和卡罗琳（Carolyn）双双攀住科普——他只穿着汗衫长裤便冲了出来——这时彼得已在廊子上同那闯入者短短地扭作了一团。他进屋时脸上带着一两道抓痕，并报告说食品间的窗子被撬开了。

「准是海滩上哪个流浪汉看见了我们的灯光。」卡罗琳揣测道。

===Original===
“What was he like, Peter?” asked Mrs. Phillips.

“I couldn’t make out in the dark,” Peter replied. “But he fought hard for what he took, and he got away with it.” He felt the marks on his face. “Must have been a pretty hungry man.”

“It was some refugee hiding in my woods,” said Medora Phillips. She made her real thought no plainer. She never liked to see, in her walks, that distant prison, and she never spoke of it to her guests; but the fancy of some escaped convict lurking below among her thickets was often present in her mind.

Her fancy was now busy with some burglar, or even some murderer, who had made his bolt for liberty; and she clung informally to the clarion-voiced Cope as to a savior. She saw, with displeasure, that Carolyn was disposed to cling too. She asked Carolyn to control herself and told her the danger was over; she even requested her to return to her room. But Carolyn lingered.

===Chinese===
「他长得什么模样，彼得？」菲利普斯太太问。

「黑灯瞎火的，我没看清，」彼得答道。「可他为了到手的东西拼力相搏，还真让他带着跑掉了。」他摸了摸脸上的伤痕。「那准是个饿得够呛的家伙。」

「那准是躲进我林子里的什么逃亡者。」梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）说。她没有把自己真正的念头点得更明。散步时，她向来不愿望见远处那座监狱，也从不向客人提起它；可是想到或许有什么越狱的囚犯潜藏在她山下的灌木丛里，这念头却常常萦绕于心。

这会儿，她的想象正忙着勾勒某个夜盗、甚至某个杀人犯——一个刚夺路奔向自由的家伙；她不拘形迹地攀住那位嗓音嘹亮如号的科普，仿佛攀住一位救主。她不悦地看见，卡罗琳也有意要攀附上来。她要卡罗琳克制住自己，告诉她危险已经过去；甚至请她回自己房里去。可卡罗琳偏还流连不去。

===Original===
Medora herself stood with Cope in the light of the dying fire. She was dressed almost as inadequately as he, but she felt that she must cling tremblingly to him and thank him for something or other.

“I don’t know what you’ve saved us from,” she panted. “We may owe our very lives to you!”

Peter, in the background, again thoughtfully felt his face and became conscious of a growing ache in the muscles of his arms. He retired, with a smile, to a still more distant plane. The regular did the work and the volunteer got the praise.

Mrs. Phillips presently gave up her drooping hold on the reluctant Cope and called Peter forward. “Is anything missing?” she asked.

===Chinese===
梅多拉本人则和科普一同站在渐熄的炉火光里。她身上穿的差不多同他一样不成体统，可她觉得自己非得微微发颤地依偎着他，为这桩那桩说不上来的事向他道谢不可。

「不知道你把我们从什么祸事里救了出来，」她喘息着说。「我们这条性命，说不定都是欠你的！」

彼得在后面，又若有所思地摸了摸脸，渐渐觉得两条胳膊的肌肉一阵阵作痛。他微笑着退到更靠后的一层背景里去。正式当差的做下了事，编外来助的却得了夸奖。

菲利普斯太太不久便松开了搭在那不情愿的科普身上软塌塌的手，把彼得唤上前来。「丢了什么东西没有？」她问。

===Original===
“Only part of the breakfast, I expect,” said Peter, with a grin. “And maybe some of the lunch. He surely was a hungry man!”

“Well, we shan’t starve. See to all the doors and windows before you go back to bed.”

But going back to bed was the one thing that she herself felt unable to do. She asked Carolyn to bring her a wrap of some kind or other, and sat down on the settle to talk it over. Cope had modestly slipped on a coat. The fire was dying﻿—that was the only difference between twelve o’clock and ten.

“If I had known what was going to happen,” declared Medora volubly, “I never could have gone to bed at all! And to think”﻿—here she left Carolyn’s end of the settle and drew nearer to Cope’s﻿—“that I should ever have even thought of coming out here without a man!”

===Chinese===
「我看只丢了部分早饭，」彼得咧嘴一笑说。「兴许还有点午饭的吃食。那家伙准是个饿汉！」

「好啦，我们饿不死。回屋睡之前，把所有的门窗都查看一遍。」

可是回床上去睡，恰恰是她自己觉得唯一办不到的事。她让卡罗琳去给她取件什么外衣，自己在高背长椅上坐下，把这桩事细细谈论。科普已知趣地套上了一件外衣。炉火将熄——这是十二点钟与十点钟之间唯一的差别。

「早知道要出什么事，」梅多拉滔滔不绝地宣称，「我根本就上不了床！再想想看」——说到这里，她离开长椅上卡罗琳的那一头，朝科普这头挪近了些——「我当初竟会起念，连一个男人都不带就跑到这地方来！」

===Original===
She now rated her midnight intruder as a murderer, and believed more devoutly than ever that Cope had saved all their lives. Cope, who knew that he had contributed nothing but a loud pair of lungs, began to feel rather foolish.

Nor did the anomalous situation commend itself in any degree to his taste. But it hit Medora Phillips’ taste precisely, and she continued to sit there, pressing an emotional enjoyment from it. An hour passed before her excitement﻿—an excitement kept up, perhaps, rather factitiously﻿—was calmed, and she trusted herself back in her own room.

Breakfast was a scanty affair﻿—it must be that if anything was to be left over for lunch. While they were busy with toast and coffee voices were heard in the woods﻿—loud cries in call and answer.

===Chinese===
这会儿她已把她那位午夜闯入者断成了杀人犯，而且比任何时候都更虔诚地相信，是科普救了她们全体的性命。科普心里明白，自己贡献的不过是一副洪亮的嗓门，不由得渐渐觉得有些发傻。

这番反常的局面，也丝毫不合他的口味。可它偏偏正中梅多拉·菲利普斯的下怀，她继续坐在那里，从中榨取情感的享受。过了整整一个钟头，她那股兴奋——一股多半是硬撑出来的兴奋——才算平息，她才放心地让自己回到房里去。

早饭是一顿菲薄的饭食——若还要给午饭留下些什么，便也只能如此。众人正对付着烤面包和咖啡，林子里传来了人声——一递一声的高声呼喊，此起彼应。

===Original===
“There!” said Medora, setting down her cup; “I knew it!”

Presently two men came climbing up to the house, while the voices of others were still audible in the humpy thickets below.

The men were part of a search-party, of course﻿—a posse; and they wanted to know whether.﻿ ﻿…

“He tried to break in,” said Medora Phillips eagerly; “but this gentleman.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「瞧！」梅多拉放下杯子说。「我早就知道！」

不多时，两个男人爬上坡来到屋前，底下丘阜起伏的灌木丛里，还听得见旁人的声音。

这两人当然是一支搜索队——一支临时纠集的巡缉队——里的人；他们想知道，是否……

「那人想强行闯进来，」梅多拉·菲利普斯急切地说，「可是这位先生……」

===Original===
She turned appreciatively to Cope. Carolyn, really impressed by her well-sustained seriousness and ardor, almost began to believe that they owed their lives to Bertram Cope alone.

“Was he a﻿—murderer?” asked Medora.

The men looked serious, but made no categorical reply. They glanced at the wrecked pantry window, and they looked with more intentness at the long sliding footprints which led away, down the half-bare sand-slope. Then they slid down themselves.

Medora asked Carolyn to do what she could toward constructing a lunch and then walked down to the shore with Cope to compose her nerves. No stroll today along the ridged amphitheatre of the hills, whence the long, low range of buildings, under that tall chimney, was so plainly in view. Still less relishing the idea of a tramp through the woods themselves, the certain haunt﻿—somewhere﻿—of some skulking desperado. No, they would take the shore itself﻿—open to the wide firmament, clear of all snares, and free from every disconcerting sight.

===Chinese===
她满怀感佩地转向科普。卡罗琳当真被东家这份始终不衰的郑重与热切打动了，几乎也开始相信，她们的性命是伯特伦·科普（Bertram Cope）一个人救下的。

「他是不是——是个杀人犯？」梅多拉问。

那两人神色凝重，却没有给出干脆的答复。他们朝被砸坏的食品间窗子瞥了一眼，又更专注地察看那串长长的、拖着脚步的印迹——一路引下那片半裸的沙坡。随后他们自己也出溜下坡去了。

梅多拉让卡罗琳尽力拼凑出一顿午饭，自己则同科普往岸边走去，好平复平复神经。今天可不去那山冈环抱如剧场的冈峦高处散步了——从那里，那座高烟囱底下一长排低矮的厂房，正历历在目。更别提要徒步穿过林子本身了——那林子的某一处，准是某个潜行亡命徒的巢穴。不，他们要走的是湖岸本身——敞向辽阔的天穹，没有一处罗网，也免见一切败人清兴的景象。

===Original===
“Poor Carolyn!” said Medora presently. “How fluttered and inefficient she was! A good secretary﻿—in a routine way﻿—but so lacking in initiative and self-possession!”

Cope’s look tended to become a stare. He thought that Carolyn had been in pretty fair control of herself﻿—had been less fluttery and excited, indeed, than her employer.

But Medora had been piqued, the night before, by Carolyn’s tendency to linger on the scene and to help skim the emotional cream from the situation.

“And in such deshabille, too! I hope you don’t think she seemed immodest?”

===Chinese===
「可怜的卡罗琳！」梅多拉过了一会儿说。「瞧她当时那副张皇失措、百无一用的样子！做秘书是好的——例行公事那一流——可是太缺主见、太欠沉着！」

科普的目光渐渐变成了直瞪瞪的注视。他倒觉得，卡罗琳把自己的举止把持得相当不错——说实在的，比她的东家还镇定，还没那么慌乱激动。

可是头天夜里，卡罗琳一味流连现场、还想搭着撇取这场面里情感的醇浆，早已惹恼了梅多拉。

「还穿着那样一身不整的衣裳！你总不至于认为她显得有失体统吧？」

===Original===
But Cope had given small heed to their dress, or to their lack of it. In fact, he had noticed little if any difference between them. He only knew that he had felt a degree more comfortable after getting his own coat on.

“Carolyn understands her place pretty well,” mused Medora. “Yet﻿ ﻿…”

“Anybody might be excused for looking anyhow, at such a time,” observed Cope, fending off the intrusion of a new set of considerations; “and in such a sudden stir. I hope nobody noticed how I looked!”

“Well, you were noticeable,” declared Medora, with some archness. She had been conscious enough of his spare waist, his sinewy arms, his swelling chest. “It was easy enough to see where the noise came from,” she said, looking him over.

===Chinese===
可科普对她们的衣着——或是衣着的欠缺——并没怎么留意。事实上，她们彼此之间有什么差别，他几乎无从察觉。他只知道，自己套上外衣之后，确实觉得自在了几分。

「卡罗琳倒还相当明白自己的本分，」梅多拉沉吟道。「只是……」

「那样的时候，谁个模样如何都情有可原，」科普说，挡开又一层节外生枝的念头；「又赶上那样一场突然的骚乱。但愿没人留意我当时的模样！」

「哦，你可是叫人留意到了，」梅多拉带着几分狡黠宣称道。他那瘦窄的腰身、遒韧的双臂、隆起的胸膛，她早就看得再清楚不过。「那声响是打哪儿发出来的，一眼便看得出。」她说着，把他周身打量了一番。

===Original===
“Yes, I supplied the noise﻿—and that only. It was Peter, please remember, who supplied the muscle.”

She declined to let her mind dwell on Peter. Peter possessed no charm. Besides, he was prosaically on the payroll.

They continued to saunter along the sand. Yesterday’s sparse clouds had vanished, along with much of yesterday’s wind. The waters that had tumbled and vociferated now merely murmured. The lake stood calmly blue, and the new green was thickening on the hills. Confident birds flitted busily among the trees and shrubs. Spring was disclosed in its most alluring mood.

Suddenly three or four figures appeared on the beach, a quarter of a mile away. They had descended through one of the sandy and ravaged channelings which broke at intervals the regulated rim of the hills, and they came on toward our two strollers. Medora closed her eyes to peer at them. “Are they marching a prisoner?” she asked.

===Chinese===
「不错，声响是我出的——也只出了声响。出力气的是彼得，这一点请不要忘记。」

她可不肯让自己的心思在彼得身上停留。彼得毫无魅力可言。何况他不过是照本册领工钱的寻常雇工，实在乏味。

两人继续沿着沙岸缓步而行。昨日稀疏的云已经散尽，昨日的大半风势也随之而去。昨天还在翻腾喧嚣的湖水，此刻只是低低絮语。湖面一片澄静的蓝，山冈上的新绿日渐深浓。笃定的鸟儿在树木灌丛间忙碌地穿飞。春天正以它最妩媚撩人的情态展露出来。

忽然，四分之一英里外的海滩上出现了三四个身影。他们是打山冈齐整的边缘间或豁开的一道道沙蚀沟槽里下来的，正朝我们这两位漫步者走来。梅多拉眯起眼睛细望。「他们是不是押着一名囚犯？」

===Original===
“They all appear to be walking free.”

“Are they carrying knapsacks?”

“Khaki, puttees﻿—and knapsacks, I think.”

“Some university men said they might happen along today. If they really have knapsacks, and anything to eat in them, they’re welcome. Otherwise, we had better hide quick﻿—and hope they’ll lose the place and pass us by.”

===Chinese===
「看样子都自由自在地走着呢。」

「他们背着背包吗？」

「卡其装、绑腿——我想还有背包。」

「几位大学里的人说过，他们今天兴许会碰巧路过。他们要真背着背包，里面又有可吃的，那就欢迎之至。不然的话，咱们最好赶紧躲起来——还巴望他们找不着这地方，打旁边走过去。」

===Original===
One of the advancing figures lifted a semaphoric arm. “Too late,” said Cope; “They recognize you.”

“Then we’ll walk on and meet them,” declared Medora.

The newcomers were young professors and graduate students. They were soon in possession of the thrilling facts of the past night, and one of them offered to be a prisoner, if a prisoner was desired. When they heard how Bertram Cope had saved the lives of defenseless women in a lonely land, they inclined to smile. Two of them had been present on another shore when Cope had “saved” Amy Leffingwell from a watery death, and they knew how far heroics might be pushed by women who were willing to idealize. Cope saw their smiles and felt that he had fumbled an opportunity: when he might have been a truncheon, he had been only a megaphone.

The new arrivals, after climbing the sandy rise to the house, were shown the devastated kitchen and were asked to declare what provisions they carried. They had enough food for their own needs and a trifle to spare. Lunch might be managed, but any thought of a later meal was out of the question. “We’ll start back at four-thirty,” said Medora to Peter. “Meanwhile”﻿—to the college men﻿—“the world is ours.”

===Chinese===
一个行来的人影举起手臂，打了个旗语似的信号。「来不及了，」科普说。「他们认出你来了。」

「那咱们就迎上前去见他们。」梅多拉宣布道。

新来的是几位年轻的教授和研究生。不多时，昨夜那桩惊心动魄的始末他们便都已知晓；其中一位还表示，若果真需要一名囚犯，他愿意充数。待听说是伯特伦·科普在这荒僻之地救下了手无寸铁的女眷们的性命，他们不觉都露出笑意。他们中有两位，曾在另一处湖岸亲眼见过科普把艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）从没顶之灾里「救」起的情景，深知那些乐于把人理想化的女子，能把英雄事迹渲染到何种地步。科普看见他们的笑，觉得自己错失了一个机会：本可以做一柄警棍的，结果只做了一只扩音喇叭。

这批新客爬上沙坡来到屋前，先被领去看了那遭劫的厨房，又被要求申报随身携带的口粮。他们的吃食自给而有余，还能匀出少许。午饭尚可设法，再往后的任何一顿便无从谈起。「我们四点半动身回去，」梅多拉对彼得说。「在这之前」——又转向那几位大学人——「整个世界都归我们。」

===Original===
After lunch the enlarged party walked forth again. Mrs. Phillips had old things to show to fresh eyes: she formed the new visitors into a compact little group and let them see how good a guide she could be. Cope and Carolyn strolled negligently﻿—even unsystematically﻿—behind. Once or twice the personally conducted looked back.

“I hope she won’t tell them again how I came to the rescue,” said Cope. “It makes a man feel too flat for words. Anybody might think, to hear her go on, that I had saved you all from robbery and murder.﻿ ﻿…”

“Why, but didn’t you?” inquired Carolyn seriously.

===Chinese===
午饭后，扩大了的队伍又出外散步。菲利普斯太太有些老资格的名目要展示给新鲜的眼睛：她把新来的客人们拢成紧凑的一小团，让他们看看她做向导能有多么在行。科普和卡罗琳懒懒散散——甚至漫无章法——地缀在后面。有一两回，那些受她亲自导览的团员回过头来张望。

「但愿她别再向他们讲我怎么赶来搭救的了，」科普说。「这叫一个男人塌台得无以言表。听她那副说法，谁都会以为我把你们全体从劫财害命里救了出来……」

「哟，难道不是吗？」卡罗琳一本正经地问道。

## I Cope at a College Tea / 第一章 科普在一场大学茶会上

===Original===
What is a man’s best age? Peter Ibbetson, entering dreamland with complete freedom to choose, chose twenty-eight, and kept there. But twenty-eight, for our present purpose, has a drawback: a man of that age, if endowed with ordinary gifts and responsive to ordinary opportunities, is undeniably﻿—a man; whereas what we require here is something just a little short of that. Wanted, in fact, a young male who shall seem fully adult to those who are younger still, and who may even appear the accomplished flower of virility to an idealizing maid or so, yet who shall elicit from the middle-aged the kindly indulgence due a boy. Perhaps you will say that even a man of twenty-eight may seem only a boy to a man of seventy. However, no septuagenarian is to figure in these pages. Our elders will be but in the middle forties and the earlier fifties; and we must find for them an age which may evoke their friendly interest, and yet be likely to call forth, besides that, their sympathy and their longing admiration, and later their tolerance, their patience, and even their forgiveness.

I think, then, that Bertram Cope, when he began to intrigue the little group which dwelt among the quadruple avenues of elms that led to the campus in Churchton, was but about twenty-four﻿—certainly not a day more than twenty-five. If twenty-eight is the ideal age, the best is all the better for being just a little ahead.

Of course Cope was not an undergraduate﻿—a species upon which many of the Churchtonians languidly refused to bestow their regard. “They come, and they go,” said these prosperous and comfortable burghers; “and, after all, they’re more or less alike, and more or less unrewarding.” Besides, the Bigger Town, with all its rich resources and all its varied opportunities, lay but an hour away. Churchton lived much of its real life beyond its own limits, and the student who came to be entertained socially within them was the exception indeed.

No, Bertram Cope was not an undergraduate. He was an instructor; and he was working along, in a leisurely way, to a degree. He expected to be an M.A., or even a Ph. D. Possibly a Litt.D. might be within the gift of later years. But, anyhow, nothing was finer than “writing”﻿—except lecturing about it.

===Chinese===
男人最美好的年纪是几岁？彼得·伊贝森（Peter Ibbetson）全然自由地步入梦境、任凭自己挑选，选了二十八岁，便一直留在那里。可是二十八岁，就我们眼下的目的而言，有个缺憾：到了这个年纪的男人，只要天赋寻常、又肯回应寻常的机会，便无可否认地是一个男人了；而我们此处所需要的，是比这稍稍欠着一点点的什么。说实在的，需要的是这样一名年轻男子：在比他更年轻的人眼里，他俨然已是十足的大人；在一两位耽于遐想的少女眼中，他甚至堪称阳刚之气开到尽头的花朵；然而他又该能从中年人那里，赢得本该属于一个男孩子的那种宽厚的纵容。或许你会说，二十八岁的男人在七十岁的人看来，也不过是个孩子。不过，本书的书页里是不会出现什么七旬老人的。我们的长者不过是四十五六岁到五十出头；我们得为他们物色这样一个年纪：它既能唤起他们友善的兴趣，又多半不会止步于此，还会引出他们的怜惜与渴慕的赞赏，再往后，引出他们的宽容、他们的耐心、甚至他们的宽恕。

因此我想，当伯特伦·科普（Bertram Cope）开始引动那一小群人的兴味时——他们住在丘奇顿（Churchton）四条通往校园的榆树大道之间——他不过二十四岁上下，肯定满不过二十五岁。如果说二十八岁是理想的年纪，那么最好的年纪正因为稍稍赶在它前头，才越发是好上加好。

当然，科普并不是本科生——对于这样一个物种，许多丘奇顿人懒洋洋地不肯施以青眼。「他们来了，又走了，」这些殷实而安逸的市民说道；「再说，他们多多少少都是一个模样，多多少少都叫人无利可图。」何况那座更大的都会，凭着它全部富饶的资源和五花八门的机会，不过一小时路程之遥。丘奇顿的大半真正日子是过在自己地界之外的，能在这地界之内受到社交款待的学生，实在是个例外。

不，伯特伦·科普不是本科生。他是一名讲师，正从从容容地朝一个学位稳步用功。他指望拿一个文学硕士（M.A.），甚至哲学博士（Ph.D.）。说不定文学博士（Litt.D.）也在往后岁月的颁赠之列。但不管怎样，没有什么比「创作」更美妙的事了——除了讲授「创作」。

===Original===
“Why haven’t we known you before?” Medora T. Phillips asked him at a small reception. Mrs. Phillips spoke out loudly and boldly, and held his hand as long as she liked. No, not as long as she liked, but longer than most women would have felt at liberty to do. And besides speaking loudly and boldly, she looked loudly and boldly; and she employed a determined smile which seemed to say, “I’m old enough to do as I please.” Her brusque informality was expected to carry itself off﻿—and much else besides. “Of course I simply *can’t* be half so intrepid as I seem!” it said. “Everybody about us understands that, and I must ask your recognition too for an ascertained fact.”

“Known me?” returned Cope, promptly enough. “Why, you haven’t known me because I haven’t been here to *be* known.” He spoke in a ringing, resonant voice, returning her unabashed pressure with a hearty good will and blazing down upon her through his clear blue eyes with a high degree of self-possession, even of insouciance. And he explained, with a liberal exhibition of perfect teeth, that for the two years following his graduation he had been teaching literature at a small college in Wisconsin and that he had lately come back to Alma Mater for another bout: “I’m after that degree,” he concluded.

“Haven’t been here?” she returned. “But you *have* been here; you must have been here for years﻿—for four, anyhow. So why haven’t we﻿ ﻿… ?” she began again.

===Chinese===
「我们以前怎么竟不认识你呢？」梅多拉·T·菲利普斯（Medora T. Phillips）在一次小型招待会上问他。菲利普斯太太说话高声大嗓、毫无忌惮，握着他的手，想握多久便握多久。不，不是想握多久便握多久，而是握得比大多数女人自觉可以放肆的限度还要久。她不光说话高声大胆，连目光也是高声大胆的；她还使出一副斩钉截铁的微笑，仿佛在说：「我的年纪，足以任我做主。」她那副粗率的放诞不拘，指望能自行过关——连同此外的许多东西一起。「当然，我实在不可能有看上去的那一半大胆！」那微笑说道。「我们周围的每一个人都懂得这一点，我也必须请你承认这一既定的事实。」

「认识你？」科普应声而答，倒也够利落。「哎呀，你们之所以不认识我，是因为我一直不在这儿供人认识嘛。」他的嗓音清亮宏朗，怀着一片热诚的善意回应她那不知羞缩的紧握，一双清澈的蓝眼睛灼灼下视，带着十足的从容自若，甚至带着几分满不在乎。他还大大方方露出一口完美的牙齿，解释说毕业后的两年里，他一直在威斯康星（Wisconsin）一所小小的学院教文学，新近刚回到母校再战一回合：「我追的就是那个学位，」他归结道。

「一直不在这儿？」她回敬道。「可你明明在这儿待过；你一定在这儿待了好几年——不管怎么说，总有四年。那我们怎么竟会没有……？」她又起了个头。

===Original===
“Here as an undergraduate, yes,” he acknowledged. “Unregarded dust. Dirt beneath your feet. In rainy weather, mud.”

“Mud!” echoed Medora Phillips loudly, with an increased pressure on his long, narrow hand. “Why, Babylon was built of mud﻿—of mud bricks, anyway. And the Hanging Gardens﻿ ﻿… !” She still clung, looking up his slopes terrace by terrace.

Cope kept his self-possession and smiled brilliantly.

“Gracious!” he said, no less resonant than before. “Am I a landscape garden? Am I a stage-setting? Am I a﻿—?”

Medora Phillips finally dropped his hand. “You’re a wicked, unappreciative boy,” she declared. “I don’t know whether to ask you to my house or not. But you may make yourself useful in *this* house, at least. Run along over to that corner and see if you can’t get me a cup of tea.”

Cope bowed and smiled and stepped toward the tea-table. His head once turned, the smile took on a wry twist. He was no squire of dames, no frequenter of afternoon receptions. Why the deuce had he come to this one? Why had he yielded so readily to the urgings of the professor of mathematics?﻿—himself urged in turn, perhaps, by a wife for whose little affair one extra man at the opening of the fall season counted, and counted hugely. Why must he now expose himself to the boundless aplomb and momentum of this woman of forty-odd who was finding amusement in treating him as a “college boy”? “Boy” indeed she had actually called him: well, perhaps his present position made all this possible. He was not yet out in the world on his own. In the background of “down state” was a father with a purse in his pocket and a hand to open the purse. Though the purse was small and the hand reluctant, he must partly depend on both for another year. If he were only in business﻿—if he were only a broker or even a salesman﻿—he should not find himself treated with such blunt informality and condescension as a youth. If, within the University itself, he were but a real member of the faculty, with an assured position and an assured salary, he should not have to lie open to the unceremonious hectorings of the socially confident, the “placed.”

===Chinese===
「在这儿当本科生，不错，」他承认。「无人理会的尘土。你们脚下的灰土。赶上下雨，便是泥。」

「泥！」梅多拉·菲利普斯高声应和，把他那只又长又窄的手握得更紧。「哎呀，巴比伦就是用泥造的——至少是泥砖造的。还有那座空中花园……！」她仍旧攥着不放，仿佛沿着他的层层坡台，一级一级往上仰望。

科普保持着自若，粲然一笑。

「天哪！」他说，嗓音不减先前的洪亮。「我是一座园林吗？我是一幅舞台布景吗？我是一座——？」

梅多拉·菲利普斯终于松开了他的手。「你是个淘气又不识抬举的男孩子，」她断言。「我都不知道该不该请你上我家去。不过至少在这家人家里，你还可以派点用场。快到那个角落去，看你能不能给我弄杯茶来。」

科普鞠了一躬，笑着朝茶桌走去。头一旦转过去，那笑容便扭出一丝苦相。他本不是什么女士们的护花使者，也不是午后招待会的常客。他究竟着了什么魔要来这一场？为什么数学教授一撺掇，他就这么轻易地依从了？——教授自己，说不定又被太太撺掇着；对她那档子小小的活动而言，秋季开场时多出一个男人，是有分量的，而且分量极重。为什么他此刻就必须听任这位四十开外的女人那无边无际的气焰与冲劲作践？她正拿他当「大学生」寻开心呢。她方才当真管他叫过「孩子」：好吧，或许他眼下的身份正使这一切成为可能。他还未曾独自闯荡世界。在「州南」老家的幕后，有一位口袋里揣着钱袋的父亲，和一只肯解开钱袋的手。钱袋虽小，手虽不情愿，再这一年他还得部分仰仗这两样。他若是已经入了商界就好了——哪怕只做个经纪人，甚至只做个推销员——也不至于被人当作后生小子，受这样直愣愣的放肆随意和居高临下的对待。他若是在大学里面真是一名正式的教员，有稳当的职位、稳当的薪水，也就不必敞开了任凭那些社交上有把握的、已经「安了位」的人们不加客气地呼来喝去。

===Original===
He regained his smile on the way across the room, and the young creature behind the samovar, who had had a moment’s fear that she must deal with Severity, found that a beaming Affability﻿—though personally unticketed in her memory﻿—was, after all, her happier allotment. In her reaction she took it all as a personal compliment. She could not know, of course, that it was but a piece of calculated expressiveness, fitted to a particular social function and doubly overdone as the wearer’s own reaction from the sprouting indignation of the moment before. She hoped that her hair, under his sweeping advance, was blowing across her forehead as lightly and carelessly as it ought to, and that his taste in marquise rings might be substantially the same as hers. She faced the Quite Unknown, and asked it sweetly, “One lump or two?”

“The dickens! How do *I* know?” he thought. “An extra one on the saucer, please,” he said aloud, with his natural resonance but slightly hushed. And his blue eyes, clear and rather cold and hard, blazed down, in turn, on her.

“Why, what a nice, friendly fellow!” exclaimed Mrs. Phillips, on receiving her refreshment. “Both kinds of sandwiches,” she continued, peering round her cup. “Were there three?” she asked with sudden shrewdness.

===Chinese===
穿过房间的一路上，他重新挂起了笑容；茶炊后面那位年轻的人儿，方才还有一瞬担心要应付一位「严苛」，却发现一位满面生辉的「和蔼」——虽说她在自己的记忆里还没给他发过号牌——终究是她更幸福的份额。她在情绪的反拨之下，把这一切当作了对她个人的恭维。她当然无从知道，这不过是一副刻意经营的表示，专为某一场合的社交功能而设，又作为佩戴者对片刻之前才冒头的那股愤懑的反拨，加倍地过了火。她巴望自己的头发在他大步进逼之下，正像应有的那样轻灵随意地拂过前额，还巴望他对于马眼形宝石戒指的趣味，同她的基本一致。她迎着这位全然的未知者，甜甜地问：「一块方糖，还是两块？」

「搞什么名堂！我上哪儿知道去？」他心想。「劳驾在茶托上另外多搁一块，」他出声说，天生的洪亮略略压低。他那双蓝眼睛，清澈而颇为冷淡生硬，这回轮到他灼灼下视她了。

「哎呀，多和气可亲的一位小伙子！」菲利普斯太太接过茶点，大声赞叹。「两样三明治都来，」她端着茶杯继续说，目光从杯沿边四下逡巡。「一共有三样吧？」她忽然精明地问。

===Original===
“There were macaroons,” he replied; “and there was some sort of layer-cake. It was too sticky. These are more sensible.”

“Never mind sense. If there is cake, I want it. Tell Amy to put it on a plate.”

“Amy?”

“Yes, Amy. *My* Amy.”

“Your Amy?”

“Off with you﻿—parrot! And bring a fork too.”

===Chinese===
「有杏仁蛋白饼，」他答道；「还有一种什么夹层蛋糕。那蛋糕太黏。这几样更实惠。」

「别管什么实惠不实惠。有蛋糕，我就要。叫艾米（Amy）把它盛到盘子里。」

「艾米？」

「对，艾米。我的艾米。」

「你的艾米？」

「去吧——多嘴的鹦鹉！再带把叉子来。」

===Original===
Cope lapsed back into his frown and recrossed the room. The girl behind the samovar felt that her hair was unbecoming, after all, and that her ring, borrowed for the occasion, was in bad taste. Cope turned back with his plate of cake and his fork. Well, he had been promoted from a “boy” to a “fellow”; but must he continue a kind of methodical dogtrot through a sublimated butler’s pantry?

“That’s right,” declared Mrs. Phillips, on his return, as she looked lingeringly at his shapely thumb above the edge of the plate. “Come, we will sit down together on this sofa, and you shall tell me all about yourself.” She looked admiringly at his blue serge knees as he settled down into place. They were slightly bony, perhaps; “but then,” as she told herself, “he is still quite young. Who would want him anything but slender?﻿—even spare, if need be.”

As they sat there together﻿—she plying him with questions and he, restored to good humor, replying or parrying with an unembarrassed exuberance﻿—a man who stood just within the curtained doorway and flicked a small graying moustache with the point of his forefinger took in the scene with a studious regard. Every small educational community has its scholar manqué﻿—its haunter of academic shades or its intermittent dabbler in their charms; and Basil Randolph held that role in Churchton. No alumnus himself, he viewed, year after year, the passing procession of undergraduates who possessed in their young present so much that he had left behind or had never had at all, and who were walking, potentially, toward a promising future in which he could take no share. Most of these had been commonplace young fellows enough﻿—noisy, philistine, glaringly cursory and inconsiderate toward their elders; but a few of them﻿—one now and then, at long intervals﻿—he would have enjoyed knowing, and knowing intimately. On these infrequent occasions would come a union of frankness, comeliness and élan, and the rudiments of good manners. But no one in all the long-drawn procession had stopped to look at him a second time. And now he was turning gray; he was tragically threatened with what might in time become a paunch. His kind heart, his forthreaching nature, went for naught; and the young men let him walk under the elms and the scrub-oaks neglected. If they had any interest beyond their egos, their fraternities, and (conceivably) their studies, that interest dribbled away on the quadrangle that housed the girl students. “If they only realized how much a friendly hand, extended to them from middle life, might do for their futures﻿ ﻿… !” he would sometimes sigh. But the youthful egoists, ignoring him still, faced their respective futures, however uncertain, with much more confidence than he, backed by whatever assurances and accumulations he enjoyed, could face his own.

===Chinese===
科普重新皱起眉头，再次穿过房间。茶炊后面的姑娘只觉得自己的头发终究不称景，自己那枚为这场合借来的戒指也品味恶劣。科普端着一盘蛋糕和一把叉子转了回来。好啊，他已经从「孩子」擢升为「小伙子」了；可他还得继续在这种升华版配膳室里，一本正经地小跑当差吗？

「这就对了，」科普回来时，菲利普斯太太宣告，一边恋恋地端详着盘沿之上他那一根模样周正的大拇指。「来，我们一起坐到这张沙发上去，你把你自己的事情原原本本讲给我听。」他在座位上坐定下来时，她赏鉴地看了看他的蓝哔叽膝盖。那膝盖或许略显瘦骨嶙峋；「不过呢，」她自忖，「他还年轻得很。除了清瘦，谁还想要他别的样子呢？——必要的话，清瘦到清癯也使得。」

两人这样并肩坐着——她连连发问，他也恢复了兴致，毫无窘态地、兴头十足地或答或挡——这时，一个站在挂帘门洞里侧的男人，正用食指尖轻捻着一把染霜的小髭，细细打量这一幕。每一个小小的教育社区都有它那位失意的学者——它在学界幽影间流连不去的人，或是对那些幽影的魅力时断时续涉猎一番的人；在丘奇顿，担任这个角色的便是巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph）。他自己并非校友，却年复一年观看着本科生们络绎不绝走过的行列；这些人在自己年轻的当下，拥有那么多他早已抛在身后、或从来不曾拥有的东西，而且正潜在地走向一个大有前途的未来，那未来里却没有他的一份。这些人大多不过是些平平常常的小伙子——吵吵嚷嚷，市侩气十足，对长辈露骨地草率轻慢；可是其中也有那么几个——隔上许久偶尔出一个——他本乐于结识，乐于深交。在这种难得的机缘里，会现出坦诚、俊美与锐气连同良好教养之根柢的合体。可是在这一列绵延不尽的行列里，还没有一个人停下来对他看过第二眼。如今他头发正在转灰；他的肚子也在悲剧性地受到威胁，早晚可能发展成一副便便大腹。他那副好心肠，他那乐于向外伸探的天性，全都付诸东流；年轻人任他无人理睬地在榆树和矮栎底下走动。他们除了自我、兄弟会，以及（姑且算上）功课外，倘若还有什么别的兴趣，那兴趣也点点滴滴淌尽在女生们聚居的那方庭院里了。「他们若是明白，一只从人生中年向他们伸来的友谊之手，能为他们的前程做多少事……！」他有时会这样叹息。可是这些年轻的自利之徒依旧对他视若无睹，各自面对自己的前程——尽管那前程未可逆料——信心却比他充沛得多；而他，纵有他所享有的一切保障与积蓄撑腰，面对自己的前程也没有这份信心。

===Original===
“To be young!” he said. “To be young!”

Do you figure Basil Randolph, alongside his portière, as but the observer, the *raisonneur*, in this narrative? If so, you err. What!﻿—you may ask﻿—a rival, a competitor? That more nearly.

It was Medora Phillips herself who, within a moment or two, inducted him into this role.

A gap had come in her chat with Cope. He had told her all he had been asked to tell﻿—or all he meant to tell: at any rate he had been given abundant opportunity to expatiate upon a young man’s darling subject﻿—himself. Either she now had enough fixed points for securing the periphery of his circle or else she preferred to leave some portion of his area (now ascertained approximately) within a poetic penumbra. Or perhaps she wished some other middle-aged connoisseur to share her admiration and confirm her judgment. At all events﻿—

===Chinese===
「年轻多好啊！」他说。「年轻多好啊！」

你是否把这位立在门帘旁的巴兹尔·伦道夫，只当作本篇叙事里的旁观者、那位说理者（*raisonneur*）？若是如此，那你就错了。什么！——你要问——一个情敌，一个竞逐者？这样说倒更接近了。

而把这一角色授予他的，正是梅多拉·菲利普斯本人，前后不过一两分钟。

她同科普的闲谈出现了一个空当。他已经把人家要他讲的都讲了——或者说，把他愿意讲的都讲了：无论如何，人家已经给了他充分的机会，就年轻人最心爱的话题——他自己——大发了一通议论。要么是她如今已掌握了足够的定点，足以箍定他那个圆的圆周；要么是她宁愿把他那片区域（现已大致查明）的一角，留在一片诗意的半影里。又或者，她巴望有另一位中年的行家来分享她的赞赏、印证她的判断。总之——

===Original===
“Oh, Mr. Randolph,” she cried, “come here.”

Randolph left his doorway and stepped across.

“Now you are going to be rewarded,” said the lady, broadly generous. “You are going to meet Mr. Cope. You are going to meet Mr.﻿—” She paused. “Do you know,”﻿—turning to the young man﻿—“I haven’t your first name?”

“Why, is that necessary?”

“You’re not ashamed of it? Theodosius? Philander? Hieronymus?”

“Stop!﻿—please. My name is Bertram.”

===Chinese===
「哦，伦道夫先生，」她喊道，「到这儿来。」

伦道夫离开门洞，走了过来。

「现在你要得到报偿了，」这位夫人说，一副宽宏大量的气派。「你就要认识科普先生了。你就要认识——」她顿了顿。「你可知道，」——她转向那年轻人——「我还没有你的教名呢？」

「哎呀，这也有必要吗？」

「你不会为它害臊吧？西奥多西乌斯？菲兰德？海罗尼穆斯？」

「打住！——求你了。我的名字是伯特伦。」

===Original===
“Never!”

“Bertram. Why not?”

“Because that would be too exactly right. I might have guessed and guessed﻿—!”

“Right or wrong, Bertram’s my name.”

“You hear, Mr. Randolph? You are to meet Mr. Bertram Cope.”

Cope, who had risen and had left any embarrassment consequent upon the short delay to Basil Randolph himself, shot out a hand and summoned a ready smile. Within his cuff was a hint for the construction of his forearm: it was lean and sinewy, clear-skinned, and with strong power for emphasis on the other’s rather short, well-fleshed fingers. And as he gripped, he beamed; beamed just as warmly, or just as coldly﻿—at all events, just as speciously﻿—as he had beamed before: for on a social occasion one must slightly heighten good will﻿—all the more so if one be somewhat unaccustomed and even somewhat reluctant.

===Chinese===
「不会吧！」

「伯特伦。有什么不可以？」

「因为这名字合适得未免太一丝不差了。我原可以一猜再猜——！」

「对也罢，错也罢，伯特伦就是我的名字。」

「听见了吗，伦道夫先生？你要认识的是伯特伦·科普先生。」

科普早已站起身来，把这点短促耽搁可能招致的任何尴尬都留给了巴兹尔·伦道夫本人；他倏地伸出一只手，召来一副现成的笑容。他的袖口之内，透露着那条前臂的构造：精瘦而多筋，皮肤光洁，握向对方那几根偏短而丰润的手指时，颇有一股用以强调的力道。他一边紧握，一边满面堆笑；笑得同先前一样热，或者一样冷——无论如何，一样地煞有介事：因为在社交场合，人总得把善意稍稍加码——倘若这人对这种场合还相当不惯、甚至颇有几分不情愿，就愈发该如此。

===Original===
Mrs. Phillips caught Cope’s glance as it fell in all its glacial geniality.

“He looks down on us!” she declared.

“How down?” Cope asked.

“Well, you’re taller than either of us.”

“I don’t consider myself tall,” he replied. “Five foot nine and a half,” he proceeded ingenuously, “is hardly tall.”

“It is we who are short,” said Randolph.

===Chinese===
菲利普斯太太接住了科普落下来的目光，那目光满带着一派冰川般的和蔼。

「他这是居高临下地瞧咱们！」她断言。

「怎么个居高临下法？」科普问。

「这个嘛，你比我们俩谁都高呀。」

「我可不认为自己高，」他答道。「五英尺九英寸半，」他坦然直言，「算不上高。」

「是我们个子矮，」伦道夫说。

===Original===
“But really, sir,” rejoined Cope kindly, “I shouldn’t call you short. What is an inch or two?”

“But how about me?” demanded Mrs. Phillips.

“Why, a woman may be anything﻿—except too tall,” responded Cope candidly.

“But if she wants to be stately?”

“Well, there was Queen Victoria.”

“You incorrigible! I hope I’m not so short as that! Sit down, again; we must be more on a level. And you, Mr. Randolph, may stand and look down on us both. I’m sure you have been doing so, anyway, for the past ten minutes!”

===Chinese===
「可是说真的，先生，」科普好意接道，「我总不该说您矮。一两英寸算得了什么？」

「那我呢？」菲利普斯太太追问。

「哎呀，女人家多高都可以——只除了太高，」科普坦率地答道。

「可她若想望一副庄严的仪态呢？」

「这个嘛，不是有过维多利亚女王么。」

「你这家伙真是无可救药！但愿我没矮到那个地步！再坐下吧；咱们得彼此齐平些。您呢，伦道夫先生，就请站着，居高临下地瞧我们两个吧。反正过去这十分钟里，您毫无疑问一直是这么瞧着的！」

===Original===
“By no means, I assure you,” returned Randolph soberly.

Soberly. For the young man had slipped in that “sir.” And he had been so kindly about Randolph’s five foot seven and a bit over. And he had shown himself so damnably tender toward a man fairly advanced within the shadow of the fifties﻿—a man who, if not an acknowledged outcast from the joys of life, would soon be lagging superfluous on their rim.

Randolph stood before them, looking, no doubt, a bit vacant and inexpressive. “Please go and get Amy,” Mrs. Phillips said to him. “I see she’s preparing to give way to someone else.”

===Chinese===
「绝无此事，我向您保证，」伦道夫正色答道。

正色答道。因为那年轻人方才不动声色地带进了那声「先生」。而且，关于伦道夫那五英尺七英寸挂零的身量，他的态度又是那么和善。而且，对一个已然深入五十岁阴影之中的人，他表现得又是那么该死的温柔体贴——这个人，即便还不算公认地被人生的欢乐放逐在外，也很快要拖拖沓沓、多而无用地落在那些欢乐的边沿上了。

伦道夫站在两人面前，神情无疑有几分茫然呆滞、不见表情。「请去把艾米带来，」菲利普斯太太对他说。「我看她正打算让位给别人呢。」

===Original===
Amy﻿—who was a blonde girl of twenty or more﻿—came back with him pleasantly and amiably enough; and her aunt﻿—or whatever she should turn out to be﻿—was soon able to lay her tongue again to the syllables of the interesting name of Bertram.

Cope, thus finally introduced, repeated the facial expressions which he had employed already beside the tea-table. But he added no new one; and he found fewer words than the occasion prompted, and even required. He continued talking with Mrs. Phillips, and he threw an occasional remark toward Randolph; but now that all obstacles were removed from free converse with the divinity of the samovar he had less to say to her than before. Presently the elder woman, herself no whit offended, began to figure the younger one as a bit nonplussed.

“Never mind, Amy,” she said. “Don’t pity him, and don’t scorn him. He’s really quite self-possessed and quite chatty. Or”﻿—suddenly to Cope himself﻿—“have you shown us already your whole box of tricks?”

===Chinese===
艾米——一个二十岁上下的金发姑娘——跟着他走了回来，神情相当愉快和气；她的姨妈——或者说，不管她日后证实是门什么亲——很快又能把舌头搭回到「伯特伦」这个有趣名字的音节上了。

科普就这样被最终引见完毕，把他在茶桌旁已经用过的那几副面部表情又重演了一遍。但他没有添出新的花样；这场合所提示、甚至所要求的话，他反而找不出那么多来了。他继续同菲利普斯太太交谈，也偶尔朝伦道夫那边抛去一两句话；可是如今一切障碍都已扫清，尽可以同茶炊后那位女神自由攀谈了，他对她反而比先前更无话可说。不多时，那位年长的妇人——她本人毫不见怪——便开始把年轻的那位看作是有点儿狼狈失措了。

「别在意，艾米，」她说。「别可怜他，也别小看他。他其实相当镇定，也相当健谈。要不，」——突然转向科普本人——「你是不是已经把你那一整匣子把戏全亮给我们看过了？」

===Original===
“That must be it,” he returned.

“Well, no matter. Mr. Randolph can be nice to a nice girl.”

“Oh, come now﻿—”

“Well, shall I ask you to my house, after this?”

===Chinese===
「想必正是如此，」他答道。

「好啦，不要紧。伦道夫先生自会对一位好姑娘献殷勤。」

「哦，别来这一套——」

「那么，经过了这一出，还要不要请你上我家去呢？」

===Original===
“No. Don’t. Forbid it. Banish me.”

“Give one more chance,” suggested Randolph sedately.

“Why, what’s all this about?” said the questioning glance of Amy. If there was any offense at all, on anybody’s part, it lay in making too much of too little.

“Take back my plate, somebody,” said Mrs. Phillips.

===Chinese===
「不要。别请。明令禁止。把我放逐了吧。」

「再给一次机会吧，」伦道夫一本正经地提议。

「哎呀，这到底是怎么回事呀？」艾米那目光在发问。倘若这中间当真有什么开罪之处——不管在谁一方——那开罪也只在于小题大做了。

「谁替我把盘子端回去吧，」菲利普斯太太说。

===Original===
Randolph put out his hand for it.

“This sandwich,” said Amy, reaching for an untouched square of wheat bread and pimento. “I’ve been so busy with other people.﻿ ﻿…”

“I’ll take it myself,” declared Mrs. Phillips, reaching out in turn. “Mr. Randolph, bring her a nibble of something.”

“*I* might﻿—” began Cope.

===Chinese===
伦道夫伸手来接。

「这块三明治，」艾米说着，伸手去取一块无人动过的全麦面包夹红椒。「我方才一直忙着照应别人。……」

「我自己来拿，」菲利普斯太太说着，也伸出手去。「伦道夫先生，去给她弄点什么垫垫。」

「我倒可以——」科普开了个头。

===Original===
“You don’t deserve the privilege.”

“Oh, very well,” he returned, lapsing into an easy passivity.

“Never mind, anyway,” said Amy, still without cognomen and connections; “I can starve with perfect convenience. Or I can find a mouthful somewhere, later.”

“Let us starve sitting,” said Randolph. “Here are chairs.”

===Chinese===
「你不配享这份荣幸。」

「哦，很好，」他答道，一头滑进一种舒舒服服的被动里。

「不过横竖不要紧，」艾米说——她至今姓氏、来历两阙如；「我可以安安稳稳地饿肚子。要不，回头随便在哪儿寻一口吃的也使得。」

「咱们就坐着挨饿吧，」伦道夫说。「这儿有椅子。」

===Original===
The hostess herself came bustling up brightly.

“Has everybody﻿ ﻿… ?”

And she bustled away.

“Yes; everybody﻿—almost,” said Mrs. Phillips to her associates, behind their entertainer’s back. “If you’re hungry, Amy, it’s your own fault. Sit down.”

And there let us leave them﻿—our little group, our cast of characters: “everybody﻿—almost,” save one. Or two. Or three.

===Chinese===
女主人本人神采焕发、风风火火地赶了过来。

「各位都……？」

说罢又风风火火地走开了。

「是啊；各位——差不离了，」菲利普斯太太背着那位做东的女主人，对她的同伴们说。「你要是饿了，艾米，那是你自己的不是。坐下吧。」

就让我们在那里离开他们吧——我们这一小伙人，我们这一班角色：「各位——差不离」，只缺一位。或者两位。或者三位。

## X Cope at His House Party / 第十章 科普在他的乡舍聚会

===Original===
"You look as fit as two fiddles," said Medora Phillips, at the top of her sandhill.

"We are," declared Randolph. "Have the rest of the orchestra arrived?"

"Most of us are here, and the rest will arrive presently. Listen. I think I hear a honk somewhere back in the woods."

The big room of the house, made by knocking two small rooms together, seemed fairly full already, and other guests were on the back porch. The Graces were there, putting the finishing-touches to the table﻿—Helga had not come, after all, but had gone instead, with her young man, to spend a few sunny afternoon hours among the films. And one of the young businessmen present at Mrs. Phillips’ dinner was present here; he seemed to know how to handle the oil-stove and the pump (with the cooperation of the chauffeur), and how to aid the three handmaidens in putting on the knives, forks, plates and napkins that Helga had decided to ignore. The people in the distant motorcar became less distant; soon they stopped in a clearing at the foot of the hill, and before long they appeared at the top with a small hamper of provisions.

"Oh, why didn’t you ask *us* to bring something!" cried Cope. Randolph shrugged his shoulders: he saw himself lugging a basket of eatables through five miles of sand and thicket.

"You’ve brought yourself," declared Mrs. Phillips genially. "That’s enough."

===Chinese===
「你们看上去精神得赛过两把提琴，」梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）在她那座沙丘的顶上说道。

「正是如此，」伦道夫（Randolph）宣称。「乐队的其余人等都到齐了吗？」

「我们多半已经到了，其余的马上就到。听。我好像听见林子深处什么地方传来一声汽车喇叭响。」

屋子里那个由两个小间打通而成的大间，此刻似乎已颇为满座，另有几位客人正在后廊上。那几位「美惠女神」都在，正给餐桌做最后的收拾——赫尔加（Helga）到底还是没来，而是同她的年轻男友出去了，要把午后晴明的几个钟头消磨在影戏中间。菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）晚宴上在座的青年商界人士里，有一位也到了场；他似乎懂得怎样摆弄油炉和水泵（有汽车司机从旁协作），也懂得怎样协助三位女侍，把赫尔加决意撒手不管的刀叉、盘碟和餐巾一一摆上。远处汽车里的人渐渐不再遥远；不多时，他们停在山脚下一片空地上，没过多久，便提着一只小小的食品篮出现在丘顶了。

「哦，你怎么不叫*我们*带点东西来！」科普（Cope）喊道。伦道夫耸了耸肩：他仿佛看见自己提着一篮吃食，在五英里的沙地与丛莽之间跋涉。

「你们把自己带来了，」菲利普斯太太和颜悦色地宣称，「这就够了。」

===Original===
There was room for the whole dozen on the dining-porch. The favored few in one corner of it could glimpse the blue plane of the lake, or at least catch the horizon; the rest could look over the treetops toward the changing colors of the wide marshes inland. And when the feast was over, the chauffeur took his refreshment off to one side, and then amiably lent a hand with the dishes.

"Let me help wipe," cried Cope impulsively.

"There are plenty of hands to help," returned his hostess. She seemed to be putting him on a higher plane and saving him for better things.

One of the better things was a stroll over her tumultuous domain: the five miles he had already covered were not enough.

===Chinese===
用餐的游廊上容得下全部一打人。占了它一角有利位置的少数宠儿，能瞥见湖泊那片蓝色的平面，或者至少望得见天际线；其余的人则可以越过树梢，眺望内陆广阔沼泽上变幻的色彩。宴罢，司机把自己那份茶点挪到一旁去享用，随后又和和气气地搭手收拾杯盘。

「让我来帮着擦干吧，」科普一时冲动地喊道。

「帮工的手多着呢，」女主人答道。她似乎要把他安置在一个更高的层次上，留他去做更好的事情。

更好的事情之一，便是在她那片起伏不宁的领地上散步一遭：他已经走过的那五英里，还不够。

===Original===
"I’ll stay where I am," declared Randolph, who had taken this regulation jaunt before. He followed Cope to the hook from which he was taking down his hat. "Admire everything," he counselled in a whisper.

"Eh?"

"Adjust yourself to our dominant mood without delay or reluctance. Praise promptly and fully everything that is ours."

===Chinese===
「我就待在原地不动了，」伦道夫宣称——这趟规定节目的漫游，他从前已领教过。他跟着科普走到挂钩前，科普正从那里取下帽子。「什么都得赞赏，」他悄声进言。

「嗯？」

「毫不迟疑、毫不勉强地使你自己顺应我们的主调。凡属我们所有的一切，都要即刻而充分地赞美。」

===Original===
The party consisted of four or five of the younger people and two or three of the older. Most of them had taken the walk before; Cope, as a novice, became the especial care of Mrs. Phillips herself. The way led sandily along the crest of a wooded amphitheatre, with less stress on the prospect waterward than might have been expected. Cope was not allowed, indeed, to overlook the vague horizon where, through the pine groves, the blue of sky and of sea blended into one; but, under Medora Phillips’ guidance, his eyes were mostly turned inland.

"People think," she said, "that ‘the Dunes’ means nothing beyond a regular row of sandhills following the edge of the water; yet half the interest and three-quarters of the variety are to be found in behind them. See my wide marsh, off to the southeast, with those islands of tamarack here and there, and imagine how beautiful the shadows are toward sunset. Look at that thick wood at the foot of the slope: do you think it is flat? No, it’s as humpy and hilly as anything ever traversed. Only this spring a fascinating murderer hid there for weeks, and last January we could hear the howls of timber-wolves driven down from Michigan by the cold. And see those tall dead pines rising above it all. I call them the Three Witches. You’ll get them better just a few paces to the left. This way." She even placed her hand on his elbow to make sure that her tragic group should appear to highest advantage. Yes, he was an admirable young man, giving admirable attention; thrusting out his hat toward prospects of exceptional account and casting his frank blue eyes into her face between-times. Charmingly perfect teeth and a wonderful sweep of yellow hair. A highly civilized faun for her highly sylvan setting. Indifferent, perhaps, to her precious Trio; but there were other young fellows to look after *them*.

===Chinese===
一行人里有四五个年轻人、两三位年长的。他们多半从前走过这条路；科普作为新手，便成了菲利普斯太太本人的特别照料对象。道路在沙土间蜿蜒，沿着一座林木环抱的圆形剧场的丘顶行进，对朝水一面景致的着墨，竟比人们预想的为少。的确，也不许科普忽略了那道朦胧的天际线——透过松林，天空与湖海的碧色在那里交融为一；但在梅多拉·菲利普斯的引领下，他的目光大半是转向内陆的。

「人们以为，」她说，「『沙丘』（the Dunes）无非是指沿着水边排列整齐的一溜沙冈；可是一半的意趣、四分之三的变化，都藏在那排沙冈的后面。瞧我那片开阔的沼泽，就在东南边，落叶松的小岛星罗棋布，你想想看，日暮时分那些影子该有多美。再看坡脚下那片茂密的树林：你以为它是平的吗？不，它坑坑洼洼、丘陵起伏，跟人们走过的任何地方都不相上下。就在今年春天，一位迷人的杀人犯还在里面藏了好几个星期呢；去年一月，我们还能听见被严寒从密歇根（Michigan）驱赶下来的林狼的嚎叫。还有，瞧那些高出于这一切之上的枯松。我管它们叫『三女巫』（Three Witches）。往左边再走几步，看得更真切。这边来。」她甚至把手搭在他的臂肘上，以确保她那悲怆的一组景致能呈现出最佳的效果。是啊，他是个令人赞赏的青年，投来令人赞赏的专注；朝着格外紧要的景致伸出手里的帽子，间或又把那双坦率的蓝眼睛投向她的脸。一口迷人而完美的牙齿，一头漂亮茂盛的黄发。在她这极富林野情趣的布景里，他是一头极有教养的农牧之神。对她那宝贝的三人组，他或许无动于衷；好在自有别的年轻人去照应*她们*。

===Original===
Cope praised loudly and readily. The region was unique and every view had its charm﻿—every view save one. Beyond the woods and the hills and the distant marshes which spread behind all these, there rose on the bluish horizon a sole tall chimney, with its long black streak of smoke. Below it and about it spread a vast rectangular structure with watchtowers at its corners. The chimney bespoke light and heat and power furnished in quantities﻿—power for many shops, manned by compulsory workers: a prison, in short.

"Why, what’s that?" asked Cope tactlessly.

Medora Phillips withheld her eyes and sent out a guiding finger in the opposite direction. "Only see the red of those maples!" she said; "and that other red just to the left﻿—the tree with the small, fine leaves all aflame. Do you know what it is?"

===Chinese===
科普高声而爽快地赞美着。这一带独一无二，每一处景色都有它的迷人之处——除了一处而外。在树林、山丘以及铺展在这些后面的远方沼泽之外，淡蓝的地平线上耸起一根孤零零的高烟囱，拖着一长道黑烟。烟囱底下、四围，铺展着一座庞大的长方形建筑，四角立着望楼。那烟囱显示着大批供应的光、热与动力——供许多工场使用的动力，由强制劳役的工人操作：简而言之，一座监狱。

「哎呀，那是什么？」科普不合时宜地问道。

梅多拉·菲利普斯把目光收住不看，朝相反的方向伸出一只引路的手指。「且看那些枫树的红！」她说，「还有偏左的那一抹红——那棵生着细小叶片、通体如焚的树。你知道它是什么吗？」

===Original===
"I’m afraid not."

"It’s a tupelo. And this shrub, right here?" She took between her fingers one large, bland indented leaf on a small tree close to the path.

Cope shook his head.

"Why, it’s a sassafras. And this?"﻿—she thrust her toe into a thick, lustrous bed of tiny leaves that hugged the ground. "No, again? That’s kinnikinnick. Oh, my poor boy, you have everything to learn. Brought up in the country, too!"

===Chinese===
「怕是不知道。」

「那是紫树（tupelo）。还有这丛灌木，就在这儿？」她用手指拈起路旁一棵小树上一片肥大、光洁而带缺刻的叶子。

科普摇了摇头。

「哎呀，这是黄樟（sassafras）。还有这个？」——她把脚尖插进一厚层紧贴地面的、油亮的小叶子里。「又不知道？那是熊果（kinnikinnick）。哦，我可怜的孩子，你有的是要学的。还是在乡下调大的呢！」

===Original===
"But, really," said Cope in defense, "Freeford isn’t so small as *that*. And even in the country one may turn by preference to books. Try me on primroses and date-palms and pomegranates!"

Medora broke off a branch of sassafras and swished it to and fro as she walked. "See," she said; "three kinds of leaves on the same tree: one without lobes, one with a single lobe, and one with two."

"Isn’t Nature wonderful," replied Cope easily.

===Chinese===
「不过，说真的，」科普辩解道，「弗里福德（Freeford）可没有*那么*小。而且就算在乡下，人也可以偏好读书嘛。拿报春花、枣椰和石榴来考考我！」

梅多拉折下一枝黄樟，一面走一面让它左右挥动。「瞧，」她说，「同一棵树上有三种叶子：一种没有裂片，一种只有一个裂片，一种有两个。」

「大自然岂不奇妙，」科普从容地应道。

===Original===
Meanwhile the young ladies sauntered along﻿—before or behind, as the case might be﻿—in the company of the young businessman and that of another youth who had come out independently on the trolley. They appeared to be suitably accompanied and entertained. But shiftings and readjustments ensued, as they are sure to do with a walking-party. Cope presently found himself scuffling through the thin grass and the briery thickets alongside the young businessman. He was a clever, companionable chap, but he declared himself all too soon, even in this remote Arcadia, as utterly true to type. Cope was not long in feeling him as operating on the unconscious assumption﻿—unconscious, and therefore all the more damnable﻿—that the young man in business constituted, ipso facto, a kind of norm by which other young men in other fields of endeavor were to be gauged: the farther they deviated from the standard he automatically set up, the more lamentable their deficiencies. A few condescending inquiries as to the academic life, that strange aberration from the normality of the practical and profitable course which made the ordinary life of the day, and the separation came. "Enough of *him*!" muttered Cope to himself presently, and began to cast about for other company. Amy Leffingwell was strolling along alone: he caught a branch of haw from before her meditative face and proffered a general remark about the beauty of the day and the interest in the changing prospect.

Amy’s pretty pink face brightened. "It *is* a lovely day," she said. "And the more of this lovely weather we have in October﻿—and especially in November﻿—the more trouble it makes."

"Surely you don’t want rain or frost?"

"No; but it becomes harder to shut the house up for good and all. Last fall we opened and closed two or three times. We even tried coming out in December."

===Chinese===
与此同时，几位年轻姑娘正信步而行——或前或后，视情形而定——身边有那位青年商人，还有一位独自乘电车出来的小伙子作陪。她们看上去有人相伴、有其消遣，颇为受用。可是换位与重整随之而来，结伴同行的队伍里这是免不了的。科普不多时便发觉，自己正同那位青年商人一道，在稀疏的草地和多刺的丛莽间窸窣穿行。他是个聪明而好相处的小伙子，可是即便在这处僻远的阿卡迪亚（Arcadia），他也过早地暴露了自己，彻头彻尾地正是那一类人。科普不久便感觉到，他是在一个不自觉的假定之下行事——不自觉，因而愈发可恶——即从商的年轻人本身便构成一种规范，其他行业中别的年轻人都要拿它来度量：离他自动立起的标准偏差得越远，他们的缺憾便越可悲。几句居高临下的关于学院生活的问询——那种偏离了构成当今日常生活之常态的、实际而有利道路的怪异行径——之后，分手便到来了。「*他*可真让人受够了！」科普过不多时暗自嘀咕，开始另觅同伴。艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）正独自一路闲逛：他从她那若有所思的脸庞前拨开一枝山楂，就今日天气之美、眼前景致变幻之趣，发了一通泛泛的议论。

艾米那张娇美的粉脸明朗起来。「今天*确实*是个好天，」她说。「这样的好天气十月里越多——尤其到了十一月——惹出的麻烦也就越多。」

「你总不会是盼着下雨或者下霜吧？」

「不是；可是要把房子一劳永逸地封闭过冬，就愈发难下决心了。去年秋天，我们开了又关、关了又开，折腾了两三回。我们甚至还试过十二月里出来呢。」

===Original===
"In mackintoshes and rubber boots?"

"Almost. But the boots are better for February. At least, they would have been last February."

"It seems hard to imagine such a future for a place like this﻿—or such a past."

"Things can be pretty rough, I assure you. And the roads are not always as good as they are today." And when the pump froze, she went on, they had to depend upon the lake; and when the lake froze they had to fall back on melted snow and ice. And even when the lake didn’t freeze, the blowing waters and the flying sands often heaped up big ridges that quite cut them off from the open sea. Then they had to prospect along those tawny hummocks for some small inlet that would yield a few buckets of frozen spray, keeping on the right side of the deep fissures that held the threat of icebergs to be cast loose at any moment; "and sometimes," she added, in search of a little thrill, "we would get back toward shore to find deep openings with clear water dashing beneath﻿—we had been walking on a mere snow-crust half the time."

===Chinese===
「穿着胶布雨衣和橡胶套鞋？」

「差不多。不过橡胶长靴还是二月里更用得着。至少，去年二月要是有的话就好了。」

「这样一处地方竟会有这般将来——或这般过去——似乎很难想象。」

「日子会相当难熬的，我向你保证。道路也不总像今天这样好走。」她接着说，水泵冻住的时候，他们只好指望湖水；湖水冻住的时候，就只得退而求其次，化雪化冰来用。甚至湖水不冻的时候，飞溅的水沫和扬起的沙土也常常堆起一道道大的冈垄，把他们同外湖完全隔断。于是他们只得沿着那些黄褐色的雪冈一路勘探，寻找某个能舀出几桶冻结浪花的小水湾，同时小心待在那些深裂缝的稳妥一侧——裂缝里悬着冰山随时崩脱的威胁。「有时候，」她补上一句，想讨一点惊险的战栗，「我们回到近岸处，却发现脚下是深深的裂口，清澈的水在底下奔涌——原来我们有大半天，一直只走在一层雪壳上。」

===Original===
"Most interesting," said Cope accommodatingly. He saw no winter shore.

"Yes, February was bad, but Mrs. Phillips wanted to make sure, toward the end of the winter, that the house hadn’t blown away﻿—nor the contents; for we have housebreakers every so often. And Hortense wanted to make some ‘color-notes.’ I believe she’s going to try for some more today."

"Today is a good day﻿—unless the October tints are too obvious."

"She says they are not subtle, but that she can use them."

===Chinese===
「真是有趣极了，」科普随和地说。他眼里可看不见什么冬日的湖岸。

「是啊，二月糟糕透了，不过菲利普斯太太想在冬末弄个确实：房子没被风刮走——屋里的东西也都还在；因为我们这儿隔三差五就有撬门贼光顾。霍滕丝（Hortense）呢，想做几幅『色彩笔记』。我看她今天还想再试几幅呢。」

「今天是个好日子——只怕十月的色彩太直白了。」

「她说它们不够微妙，不过还能用。」

===Original===
Well, here he was, talking along handily enough. But he had no notion of talking for long about Hortense. He preferred returning to the weather.

"And what does such a day do for you?" he asked.

"Oh, I suppose it helps me in a general way. But *my* notes, of course, are on paper already."

Yes, he was walking alongside her and holding his own﻿—thus far. She seemed a pretty enough, graceful enough little thing; not so tall by an inch or so as she appeared when seated behind that samovar. On that day she had been reasonably sprightly﻿—toward others, even if not toward him. Today she seemed meditative, rather; even elegiac﻿—unless there was a possible subacid tang in her reference to Hortense’s color-notes. Aside from that possibility, there was little indication of the "dexterity" which Randolph had asked him to beware.

"On paper already?" he repeated. "But not all of them? I know you compose. You are not saying that you are about to give composition up?" A forced and awkward "slur," perhaps; but it served.

===Chinese===
瞧，他这不正顺顺当当地聊着嘛。可是关于霍滕丝，他可没打算多谈。他宁可回到天气上去。

「这样的天气，对你又有什么用处呢？」他问。

「哦，我想它总归对我有点泛泛的益处。不过*我*的笔记，当然，早已写在纸上了。」

是啊，他此刻走在她身旁，应付自如——到目前为止。她似乎是个够标致、够优雅的小人儿；比她坐在那只茶炊后面时显出来的身量，要矮上一英寸上下。那一天她还算活泼——对别人，纵使不对他。今天她却似乎颇有心事；甚至带着几分哀婉——除非她提到霍滕丝的色彩笔记时，话里那一丝微酸算得上例外。撇开这层可能不谈，看不出多少伦道夫告诫他提防的那种「机敏灵巧」来。

「早已写在纸上了？」他重复道。「可并不是全部吧？我知道你作曲。你总不会是要说，你打算放弃作曲了吧？」或许是一句生硬而笨拙的「讥诮」；可它奏效了。

===Original===
She gave a little sigh. "Pupils don’t want *my* pieces," she said. "Scales; exercises﻿…"

"I know," he returned. "Themes﻿—clearness, mass, unity.﻿… It’s the same."

They looked at each other and smiled. "We ought not to think of such things today," she said.

===Chinese===
她轻轻叹了口气。「学生们不要*我*的曲子，」她说。「音阶；练习曲……」

「我知道，」他答道。「习作——明晰、分量、统一……都是一回事。」

两人相视而笑。「今天我们不该想这些的，」她说。

===Original===
Mrs. Phillips came along, shepherding her little flock for the return. "But before we *do* turn back," she adjured them, "just look at those two lovely spreading pines standing together alone on that far hill." The small group gazed obediently﻿—though to many of them the prospect was a familiar one. Yes, there stood two pines, one just a little taller than the other, and just a little inclined across the other’s top. "A girl out here in August called them Paolo and Francesca. Do you think," she asked Cope, "that those names are suitable?"

"Oh, I don’t know," he replied, looking at the trees thoughtfully. "They seem rather﻿—static; and Dante’s lovers, if I recollect, had considerable drive. They were ‘*al vento*’﻿—on the wind﻿—weren’t they? It might be less violent and more modern to call your trees Pelleas and Melisande, or﻿—"

"That’s it. That’s the very thing!" said Medora Phillips heartily. "Pelleas and Melisande, of course. That girl had a very ordinary mind."

"I’ve felt plenty of wind on the dunes, more than once," interjected Hortense.

===Chinese===
菲利普斯太太赶了过来，把她那一小群羊儿聚拢回程。「不过，在咱们*当真*折返之前，」她叮嘱大家，「且看看远处那座山冈上，两株亭亭如盖的松树孤零零地相依而立。」这一小群人恭顺地凝望——尽管对其中许多人来说，这景致早已熟稔。是的，那里立着两株松树，一株只比另一株略高些，又只微微地朝另一株的顶上倾侧。「八月间有位姑娘在这儿，管它们叫保罗与佛兰切斯卡（Paolo and Francesca）。你看，」她问科普，「这两个名字合适吗？」

「哦，我说不好，」他望着那两株树，若有所思地答道。「它们未免有些——静态；但丁笔下的恋人，如果我没记错，可是有相当冲劲的。他们『随风飘荡』（al vento）——乘在风上——不是吗？把你这两株树叫作佩利亚斯与梅丽桑德（Pelleas and Melisande），或许不那么激烈，也更现代些，要不——」

「正是。正是这个！」梅多拉·菲利普斯由衷地说。「佩利亚斯与梅丽桑德，当然。那姑娘的头脑实在平常得很。」

「沙丘上的风，我可是领教过不止一回了，」霍滕丝插嘴道。

===Original===
"Or Darby and Joan," Cope continued. "Not that I’m defending that poor creature, whoever she was. They seem to be a pretty staid, steady-going couple."

"Don’t," said Medora. "Too many ideas are worse than too few. They confuse one."

And Amy Leffingwell, who had seemed willing to admire him, now looked at him with an air of plaintive protest.

"‘Darby and Joan’!" muttered Hortense into a sumach bush. "You might as well call them Jack and Jill!"

"They’re Pelleas and Melisande," declared Mrs. Phillips, in a tone of finality. "Thank you so much," she said, with a smile that reinstated Cope after a threatened lapse from favor.

===Chinese===
「要不就叫达比与琼（Darby and Joan），」科普接着说。「倒不是我要替那个可怜的人儿辩护，不管她究竟是谁。它们看上去，是一对相当安稳、按部就班的夫妇。」

「别说了，」梅多拉说。「主意太多比太少更糟。它们把人搅糊涂。」

而艾米·莱芬韦尔，方才似乎还愿意倾慕他的，此刻望着他，带着一点哀怨的抗议之色。

「『达比与琼』！」霍滕丝冲着一丛盐肤木咕哝道。「倒不如干脆叫它们杰克与吉尔呢！」

「它们就是佩利亚斯与梅丽桑德，」菲利普斯太太以一锤定音的口气宣称。「多谢你了，」她说，笑容里，方才险些失宠的科普重又得了恩眷。

## XVIII Cope at the Call of Duty / 第十八章 科普应义务之召

===Original===

Cope took his own time in calling upon the Ashburn Avenue circle; but he finally made, in person, the inquiries for which those made by telephone were an inadequate substitute. Yet he waited so long that, only a few hours before the time he had set, he received a sweet but somewhat urgent little note from Amy Leffingwell suggesting his early appearance. He felt obliged to employ the first moments of his call in explaining that he had been upon the point of coming, anyway, and that he had set aside the present hour two or three days before for this particular purpose: an explanation, he acknowledged inwardly, which held no great advantage for him.

“Why am I spinning such stuff?” he asked himself impatiently.

Amy’s note of course minimized her aid to him and magnified his aid to her. All this was in accord with established form, but it was in still stronger accord with her determination to idealize his share in the incident. His arm *had* grasped hers firmly﻿—and she felt it yet. But when she went on to say﻿—not for the first time, nor for the second﻿—how kind and sympathetic he had been in supporting her chin against those slapping waves when the shore had seemed so far away, he wondered whether he had really done so. For a moment or two, possibly; but surely not as part of a conscious, reasoned scheme to save.

“She was doing all right enough,” he muttered in frowning protest.

Neither did he welcome Mrs. Phillips’ tendency to make him a hero. She was as willing as the girl herself to believe that he had kept Amy’s chin above water﻿—not for a moment merely, but through most of the transit to shore. He sat there uneasily, pressing his thumbs between his palms and his closed fingers and drawing up his feet crampingly within their shoes; yet it somewhat eased his tension to find that Medora Phillips was disposed to put Amy into a subordinate place: Amy had been but a means to an end﻿—her prime merit consisted in having given him a chance to function. Any other girl would have done as well. A slight relief, but a welcome.

===Chinese===

科普（Cope）不慌不忙，由着自己的意思，隔了好些时日才去回访阿什伯恩大道（Ashburn avenue）的那一圈子人；但他终于亲自登门，作了那番当面致意——先前在电话里的那些问询，原是不能充分替代的。然而他等得太久，以至于距他约定的时辰只差几个小时时，收到了艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）一张甜甜蜜蜜而又略带催促的短笺，示意他宜早些光临。他只得把拜访开头的片刻工夫用来解释：说自己本来就快要来了，还说眼下这个时辰，是两三天前便特意留出、专为此番目的的——他心里承认，这番辩白对自己并没有多大好处。

「我干吗编这些个话？」他不耐烦地自问道。

艾米的短笺自然把她给他的帮助说小，把他给她的帮助说大。这一切都合乎成例，但更合乎她的一片心意——她打定主意要把他在这桩意外里的作为理想化。他的手臂*确曾*紧紧挽住过她的手臂——那感觉她至今还留着。可是当她接二连三地说起——这已不是第一遍，也不是第二遍——说当日海岸远得似乎望不到头时，他多么仁厚、多么体贴地托住她的下巴、抵御那一阵阵扑打过来的水浪，他不禁怀疑自己是否真做过这些。兴许有过那么一两下；可断断算不上什么有意识、有盘算的救人方略。

「她自己本来应付得好好的。」他皱着眉、像抗议似的咕哝道。

他也不喜欢菲利普斯太太那股要把他捧成英雄的劲头。她跟姑娘本人一样乐于相信：他让艾米的下巴一直露在水面上——不止片刻工夫，而是差不多撑过了游回岸边的大半程。他坐在那里浑身不自在，拇指紧紧夹在掌心与合拢的手指之间，双脚在鞋里痉挛似的蜷起；不过他发现梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）有意把艾米摆在从属的位置上，这倒使他紧绷的心绪略略松了些：艾米不过是达成目的的一件凭借罢了——她首要的功绩，在于给了他一个施展身手的机会。换作任何别的姑娘，效果也是一样。宽慰虽微薄，却也难得。

===Original===

Another mitigation: the house, the room, was full of people. The other young women of the household were present; even the young businessman who had understood the stove and the pump had looked in: no chance for an intense, segregated appreciation. There had been another weekend at the dunes, when this youth had nimbly ranged the forest and the beach to find wood for the great open fireplace; and he had come, now, at the end of the season, to make due acknowledgments for privileges enjoyed. He, for his part, was willing enough to regard Amy as a heroine; but he considered her as a heroine linked with the wrong man and operative in the wrong place. He cared nothing in the world for Cope, and disparaged him as before﻿—when he did not ignore him altogether. If Amy had but been rescued by him, George F. Pearson, instead of by this Bertram Cope, and if she had been snatched from a disorderly set of breakers at the foot of those disheveled sandhills instead of from the prim, prosy, domestic edge of Churchton﻿—well, wouldn’t the affair have been better set and better carried off? In such case it might have been picturesque and heroic, instead of slightly silly.

Yes, the room was full. Even Joseph Foster had contrived to get himself brought down by Peter: further practice for the day when he should make a still more ambitious flight and dine at Randolph’s new table. He sat in a dark corner of the room and tried to get, as best he might, the essential hang of the situation: the soft, insidious insistence of Amy; the momentum and bravado of his sister-in-law; the veiled disparagement of Cope in which George F. Pearson, seated on a sofa between Carolyn and Hortense, indulged for their benefit, or for his own relief; above all, he listened for tones and undertones from Cope himself. He had never seen Cope before (if indeed it could be said that he really saw him now), and he had never heard his speaking voice save at a remove of two floors. Cope had taken his hand vigorously, as that of the only man (among many women) from whom he had much to expect, and had given him a dozen words in a loud tone which seemed to correspond with his pressure. But Cope’s voice, in his hearing, had lapsed from resonance to non-resonance, and from that to tonelessness, and from that to quietude.﻿ ﻿… Was the fellow in process of making a long diminuendo﻿—a possible matter of weeks or of months? As before, when confronted by what had once seemed a paragon of dash and vigor, he scarcely knew whether to be exasperated or appeased.

Through this variety of spoken words and unspoken thoughts Hortense sat silent and watchful. Presently the talk lapsed: with the best will in the world a small knot of people cannot go on elaborately embroidering upon a trivial incident forever. There was a shifting of groups, a change in subjects. Yet Hortense continued to glower and to meditate. What had the incident really amounted to? What did the man himself really amount to? She soon found herself at his side, behind the library-table and its spreading lampshade. He was silently handling a paper-cutter, with his eyes cast down.

===Chinese===

还有一层宽解：宅子里、客厅里满是客人。家里的另外几位年轻女士都在场；连那位弄明白了炉子与水泵的年轻生意人也来望了一眼：这样一来，便没有机会来一场热烈而专注的单独颂扬了。此前「沙丘」那边又过了一个周末，那小伙子为了给大敞炉觅柴，曾在林间与滩头敏捷地穿梭；如今时值季末，他特地前来，为所享的优遇致以应有的谢忱。至于他自己，倒十分乐意把艾米看作一位女英雄；只是他认为，这位女英雄结缘的是个错误的男人，显身手的又是个错误的地方。他对科普全无半点好感，照旧贬损他——要不然就干脆对他视而不见。倘若救起艾米的是他乔治·F·皮尔逊（George F. Pearson），而不是这位伯特伦·科普；倘若她是从那片蓬乱沙丘脚下乱作一团的碎浪里被抢救出来，而不是从丘奇顿（Churchton）那规整、乏味、居家气十足的岸边——那么，这桩事岂不是设局更佳、收束也更漂亮？那样才算得又入画又英勇，而不至于显得有几分傻气。

是的，屋里满是人。连乔·福斯特（Joseph Foster）也设法让彼得（Peter）把连人带椅的自己搬了下来：算是为日后一次雄心更大的飞行再作演练——到那天，他将到伦道夫（Randolph）的新餐桌旁赴宴。他坐在屋里一个幽暗的角落，尽力摸清眼前局面的底细：艾米那温软绵密、暗暗用劲的执着；他嫂子的声势与张扬；乔治·F·皮尔逊坐在卡罗琳（Carolyn）与霍滕丝（Hortense）之间的沙发上，为了她们的耳朵、也为给自己出气，对科普半遮半掩的贬抑；尤其是，他要捕捉科普本人的声口与言外之音。他从没见过科普（即便现在，恐怕也未必能算真的见着了），也从没听过他说话的声音——除了隔着两层楼的那一回。科普曾用力握过他的手，把他当作（济济女士中间）唯一一个他还大有指望的男人，还高声对他说了十来句话，音量似乎正与手劲相称。可是在他听来，科普的声音已经从洪亮滑向不洪亮，从不洪亮滑向没有声色，又从没有声色归于沉寂。……这家伙莫非正在拖一段漫长的渐弱——说不定要以星期计，甚至以月计？一如从前，面对这个一度看似朝气与劲头十足的完人，他简直不知该恼火还是该宽心。

在这一片出声的言语与不出声的心思之间，霍滕丝始终默坐旁观。不多时，话头冷了下来：任凭人们兴致再高，一小圈人也总不能永远围绕一桩小小的意外添枝加叶。人堆重新组合，话题随之变换。可霍滕丝还在虎着脸出神。这件事究竟算得了什么？这个人本身又究竟算得了什么？不多时她已来到他身旁，在那张书房桌和它撑开的大灯罩后面。他正默然把玩着一柄裁纸刀，两眼低垂。

===Original===

“See me!” she said, in a tense, vibratory tone. “Speak to me!”﻿—and she glowered upon him. “I am no kitten, like Amy. I am no tame tabby, like Carolyn, sending out written invitations. Throw a few poor words my way.”

Cope dropped the paper-cutter. Her address was like a dash of brine in the face, and he welcomed it.

“Tell me; did you look absurd﻿—then?” she dashed ahead.

A return to fresh water, after all! “Why,” he rejoined reluctantly, “no man, dressed in all his clothes, looks any the better for being soaked through.”

===Chinese===

「看着我！」她用一种紧绷而发颤的声调说。「跟我说话！」——她朝他虎起了脸。「我不是艾米那样的小猫儿。也不是卡罗琳那样的温驯家猫，只晓得发书面请帖。随便扔几个可怜的字眼过来给我吧。」

科普放下了裁纸刀。她这番话如同一掬海水泼在脸上，他反倒巴不得。

「告诉我：你当时——样子可笑吗？」她劈头又进逼一句。

总算又回到淡水了！「呃，」他不情愿地应道，「哪个男人穿着一身整装，浑身湿透之后，模样也不会更好看。」

===Original===

“And Amy﻿—she must have looked absolutely ridiculous! That wide, flapping hat, and all! I had been telling her for weeks that it was out of style.”

“She threw it away,” said Cope shortly. “And I suppose her hair looked as well as a woman’s ever does, when she’s in the water.”

“Well,” she observed, “it’s one thing to be ridiculous and another to go on being ridiculous. I hope you don’t mean to do that?”

The pronoun “you” has its equivocal aspects. Her expression, while marked enough, threw no clear light. Cope took the entire onus on himself.

===Chinese===

「还有艾米——她那样子一定可笑透了！那顶宽大的、扑扇扑扇的帽子，还有别的种种！我几个星期前就跟她说，那玩意儿早过时了。」

「她把帽子扔掉了。」科普简短地说。「至于头发，我想女人落了水，头发一向好不到哪里去——她那样就算好的了。」

「哦，」她评道，「一时显得可笑是一回事，可笑个没完是另一回事。我想你不至于打算可笑个没完吧？」

「你」这个代词，自有含糊两可的地方。她的神情尽管分明，却不曾照出清楚的亮光。科普把全部干系径自揽到了自己身上。

===Original===

“Of course no man would choose to be ridiculous﻿—still less to stay so. Do, please, let me keep on dry land; I’m beginning to feel waterlogged.” He shifted his ground. “Why do you try to make it seem that I don’t care to talk with you?”

“Because you don’t. Haven’t I noticed it?”

“I haven’t. It seems to me that I﻿—”

“Of course you haven’t. Does that make it any better?”

“I’m sure the last thing in the world I should want to do would be to﻿—”

“I know. Would be to show partiality. To fail in treating all alike. Even that small program isn’t much﻿—nor likely to please any girl; but you have failed to carry it out, small as it is. Here in this house, there on the dunes, what have I been﻿—and where? Put into any obscure corner, lost in the woods, left off somewhere on the edge of things.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

「当然，没有哪个男人会愿意显得可笑——更不愿意一直可笑下去。千万请你让我待在旱地上吧；我都开始觉得自己泡得发胀了。」他转换了阵地。「你为什么总要叫人觉得，我不乐意跟你说话？」

「因为你本来就不乐意。难道我没留心到吗？」

「我没有。在我看来，我——」

「你当然没有。难道这样就算好些了吗？」

「我敢说，这世上我最不愿意做的事，就是——」

「我知道。就是厚此薄彼。就是不能一视同仁。就这么个小小的章程，本也算不得什么——也未必能讨哪位姑娘欢心；可就连这么点小事，你也没能照办。在这座宅子里，在那边沙丘上，我一直算什么——又在哪儿？被塞进哪个背光的角落，被丢在林子深处，被撂在什么边边角角的地方。」

===Original===

Cope stared and tried to stem her protests. She was of the blood﻿—her aunt’s own niece. But whereas Medora Phillips sometimes “scrapped,” as he called it, merely to promote social diversion and to keep the conversational ball a-rolling, this young person, a more vigorous organism, and with decided, even exaggerated ideas as to her dues﻿ ﻿… Well, the room was still full, and he was glad enough of it.

“I don’t know whether I like you or not,” she went on, in a low, rapid tone; “and I don’t suppose you very much like me; but I won’t go on being ignored.﻿ ﻿…

“Ignored? Why,” stammered Cope, “my sense of obligation to this house﻿—”

She shrugged scornfully. His sense of obligation had been made none too apparent. Certainly it had not been brought into line with her deserts and demands.

===Chinese===

科普瞪眼望着，想止住她的连声抗议。她到底是那一族血脉——她姑妈的嫡亲侄女。只是，梅多拉·菲利普斯有时候「拌拌嘴」——他是这么叫的——无非为了给社交添些兴致、让话头滚个不停；而这一位，机体更为强健，对自己应得的一份，主张得毫不含糊、甚至言过其实……好在屋里人还满着，他为此着实庆幸。

「我不知道自己喜欢你不喜欢，」她接着说，声音低而急促；「我料想你也不怎么喜欢我；可我不能老是这样受人无视。」

「无视？哪里，」科普讷讷地说，「我对这座宅子的感念——」

她轻蔑地耸了耸肩。他那份感念，表现得可实在不算明显。当然，也始终没有摆到与她应得之分、她的要求看齐的位置上。

===Original===

Cope took up the paper-cutter again and looked out across the room. Amy Leffingwell, questioningly, was looking across at him. He could change feet﻿—if that made the general discomfort of his position any less. He did so.

Amy was standing near the piano and held a sheet or two of new music in her hands. And Medora Phillips, with a word of general explication and direction, made the girl’s intention clear. Amy had a new song for baritone, with a violin obbligato and the usual piano accompaniment, and Cope was to sing it. ’Twas an extremely simple thing, quite within his compass; and Carolyn, who could read easy music at sight (“It’s awfully easy,” declared Amy), would play the piano part; and Amy herself would perform the obbligato (with no statement as to whether it was simple or not).

Carolyn approached the task and the piano in the passive spirit of accommodation. Cope came forward with reluctance: this was not an evening when he felt like singing; besides, he preferred to choose his own songs. Also, he would have preferred to warm up on something familiar. Amy took her instrument from its case with a suppressed sense of ecstasy; and it is the ecstatic who generally sets the pace.

The thing went none too well. Amy was the only one who had seen the music before, and she was the only one who particularly wanted to make music now. However, the immediate need was not that the song should go well, but that it should go: that it should go on, that it should go on and on, repetitiously, until it should come (or even not come) to go better. She slid her bow across the strings with tasteful passion. She enjoyed still more than her own tones the tones of Cope’s voice﻿—tones which, whether in happy unison with hers or not, were, after all, seldom misplaced, whatever they may have lacked in heartiness and confidence. It was a short piece, and on the third time it went rather well.

===Chinese===

科普重新拿起裁纸刀，朝屋子那头望去。艾米·莱芬韦尔正满眼疑问地望着他这边。他可以换一换脚——倘若这能稍稍纾解他处境的种种不适的话。他换了。

艾米站在钢琴近旁，手里拿着一两页新乐谱。梅多拉·菲利普斯一句总括的说明加吩咐，便把姑娘的意图点明了。艾米弄到一支新的男中音歌曲，带小提琴助奏和寻常的钢琴伴奏，要由科普来唱。那是一支极其简单的曲子，全然在他力所能及的范围之内；卡罗琳能视奏简单的乐谱（「容易得很。」艾米宣称），由她弹钢琴声部；艾米自己则演奏小提琴助奏（至于那简不简单，则未见交代）。

卡罗琳抱着消极将就的心气，走向这项任务，也走向钢琴。科普不情愿地上前来：今晚不是他想唱歌的晚上；再者，他更愿意自己挑歌。还有，他本想先拿一支熟悉的曲子开嗓。艾米从琴盒里取出乐器，压着一腔心醉神迷；而定节拍的，一般总是那个心醉神迷的人。

事情进行得并不怎么顺当。只有艾米先前见过这份谱子，也只有她此刻特别想奏出音乐来。不过，眼下的当务之急倒不在唱得好，而在唱得下去：要唱下去，一遍又一遍、翻来覆去地唱下去，直到（或者哪怕根本没能）渐入佳境。她满蕴着有分寸的激情，把琴弓滑过琴弦。比她自己的琴音更令她陶醉的，是科普嗓音的音色——那音色不论与她的是否侥幸合拍，毕竟少有安错地方的，无论它可能在热忱与自信方面欠缺些什么。这是一支短曲，唱到第三遍，已经颇为像样了。

===Original===

“How perfectly lovely!” exclaimed Mrs. Phillips, at the right moment.

Cope smiled deprecatingly. “It might be made to go very nicely,” he said.

“It *has* gone very nicely,” insisted Amy; “it did, this last time.” She waved her bow with some vivacity. She had heaved the whole of her young self into the work; she had been buoyed up by Cope’s tones, which, with repetition, had gathered assurance if not expressiveness; and she based her estimate of the general effect on the impression which her own inner nature had experienced. And her impression was heightened when Pearson, forging forward, and ignoring both Cope and Carolyn, thanked her richly and emphatically for her part﻿—a part which, to him, seemed the whole.

Hortense, who had kept her place behind the large lampshade, twisted her interlocked fingers and said no word. Foster, who had disposed himself on an inconspicuous couch, kept his own counsel. After all, *omne ignotum*: Cope’s singing had sounded better from upstairs. At close range a ringing assertiveness had somehow failed.

===Chinese===

「真是太动听了！」菲利普斯太太在恰当的时刻赞叹道。

科普谦抑地笑了笑。「多练练，是可以唱得很不错的。」他说。

「本来就*唱*得很不错，」艾米执意道；「刚才这最后一遍就是。」她颇有生气地挥了挥琴弓。她已把自己年轻的整个身心都掷进了这项工作；科普的音色又把她托浮了起来——那音色经过反复，纵然还谈不到表情，却已聚起了底气；她便依据自己内心所受的印象，来估量总体效果。待到皮尔逊奋力挤上前去，把科普和卡罗琳一概置之不理，为她担当的那一份向她致以浓重而恳切的谢意——在他看来，那一份便是一切——她的印象愈发高涨了。

霍滕丝仍守在大灯罩后面，绞着交扣的十指，一言不发。福斯特安顿在一张不惹眼的躺椅上，也守口如瓶。毕竟，*omne ignotum*（凡未知者自壮丽）：科普的歌声从楼上传下来时要好听些。到了近处，那股铿锵斩截的劲儿不知怎么就泄了。

===Original===

Cope had come with no desire to extend his stay beyond the limits of an evening call. He declined to sing on his own account, and soon rose as if to make his general adieux.

“You won’t give us one of your own songs, then?” asked Medora Phillips, in a disappointed tone. “And at my dinner﻿—”

No, she could not quite say that, at her dinner, Cope, whatever he had failed to do, had contributed no measure of entertainment for her guests.

“Give us a recitation, then,” persisted Medora; “or tell us a story. Or make up”﻿—here she indulged herself in an airily imperious flight﻿—“a story of your own on the spot.”

===Chinese===

科普本无意把这次逗留拖出一场晚间拜访的限度。他谢绝了再为自己单独唱什么，不久便站起身来，仿佛要向众人作总的道别。

「那么，你不肯给我们唱一支你自己的歌了？」梅多拉·菲利普斯用失望的口吻问。「在我那席晚宴上——」

不，这话她还不能就这么说出口：在她那席晚宴上，科普纵有种种未到之处，也并非不曾为她的宾客添过一分兴致。

「那就给我们来一段朗诵，」梅多拉不依不饶；「或者讲个故事。或者当场编造」——说到这里，她任着自己作了一番轻飘飘的颐指气使——「一个你自己现编的故事。」

===Original===

A trifling request, truly. But﻿—

“Heavens!” said Cope. “I am not an author﻿—still less an *improvvisatore*.”

“I am sure you could be,” returned Medora fondly. “Just try.”

Cope sat down again and began to run his eye uncomfortably about the room, as if dredging the air for an idea. Behind one corner of a mirror was a large bunch of drying leaves. They had been brought in from the sand dunes as a decorative souvenir of the autumn, and had kept their place through mere inertia: an oak bough, once crimson and russet; a convoluted length of bittersweet, to which a few split berries still clung; and a branch of sassafras, with its intriguing variety of leaves﻿—a branch selected, in fact, because it gave, within narrow compass, the plant’s entire scope and repertoire as to foliage.

Cope caught at the sassafras as a falling balloonist catches at his parachute.

===Chinese===

这确乎是个微不足道的请求。可是——

「天哪！」科普说。「我不是作家——更谈不上是什么即兴诗人（*improvvisatore*）。」

「我敢说你当得起。」梅多拉宠爱地回道。「只管试试。」

科普重新坐下，目光不自在地在屋里扫来扫去，仿佛要从空气里打捞一个主意。一面镜子的后角上，挂着一大束干燥中的枝叶。那是从沙丘带回来作秋日装饰纪念的，纯粹出于惯性才一直挂在原处：一枝橡树枝，曾经绯红与赭褐交映；一段盘绕屈曲的南蛇藤，上面还挂着几颗绽裂的果子；还有一枝黄樟（sassafras），叶形变化引人入胜——这一枝其实挑得极精，因为它在方寸之间，展示了这种植物在叶形上的全部幅度与全套本领。

科普一把抓住黄樟，好似下坠的气球驾驶员一把抓住自己的降落伞。

===Original===

“Well,” he said, still reluctant and fumbling, “perhaps I can devise a legend: the Legend, let us say, of the Sassafras Bush.”

“Good!” cried Medora heartily.

Pearson, whispering to Amy Leffingwell, gave little heed to Cope and his strained endeavor to please Mrs. Phillips. Foster, quite passive, listened with curiosity for what might come.

“Or perhaps you would prefer folklore,” Cope went on. “Why the Sassafras has Three Kinds of Leaves, or something like that.”

“Better yet!” exclaimed Medora. “Listen, everybody. Why the Sassafras has Three Kinds of Leaves.”

===Chinese===

「好吧，」他说，仍不情愿地摸索着词句，「没准我能编出一则传说：就叫它——《黄樟丛传奇》吧。」

「好！」梅多拉由衷地喊道。

皮尔逊正跟艾米·莱芬韦尔窃窃私语，不大理会科普，也不大理会他为讨菲利普斯太太欢心而作的那番吃力的努力。福斯特则全然被动，只怀着好奇，等听下文。

「或者你们更爱听民间传说，」科普接着说。「《黄樟为何有三种叶子》，诸如此类。」

「那就更好了！」梅多拉赞叹道。「都听着。黄樟为何有三种叶子。」

===Original===

Pearson stopped his buzzings, and Cope began. “The Wood-nymphs,” he said slowly, “were a nice enough lot of girls, but they labored under one great disadvantage: they had no thumbs.”

Hortense pricked up her ears. Did he mean to be personal? If so, he should find that one of the nymphs had a whole hand as surely as he himself had a cheek.

Cope paused. “Of course you’ve got to postulate *something*,” he submitted apologetically.

“Of course,” Medora agreed.

===Chinese===

皮尔逊停止了嗡嗡低语，科普开了腔。「那些林中仙子，」他缓缓地说，「原是一群挺不错的姑娘，只是受着一大不便之苦：她们没有大拇指。」

霍滕丝竖起了耳朵。他这话是有所指吗？若是，那他就会发现：仙子里有一位可是长着整只手的，正如他自己分明长着一张厚脸皮。

科普顿了顿。「当然，总得先假定*一点什么*。」他带着歉意申明道。

「当然。」梅多拉应道。

===Original===

“So when they bought their gloves, or mittens, or whatever their handgear might be called, they usually patronized the hickory or the beech or some other tree with leaves that were﻿—”

“Ovate!” cried Medora delightedly.

“Ovate, yes; or whatever just the right word may be. But a good many of them traded at the Sign of the Sassafras, where they found leaves that were similar, but rather more delicate.”

“I believe he’s going to do it,” thought Foster.

===Chinese===

「所以她们买手套，买连指手套，或不管她们那种手上穿戴叫什么吧——通常光顾的是山核桃树、山毛榉，或是旁的什么叶子——」

「卵形的！」梅多拉喜不自胜地喊道。

「卵形的，正是；或者随便那个最贴切的字眼是什么。不过她们当中有不少人，惯在『黄樟招牌』那家铺子做买卖，在那里见到的叶子相似，却更细巧些。」

「我看他要编成了。」福斯特心想。

===Original===

“Yet the nymphs knew that they lacked thumbs and kept on wanting them. So, during the long, dull winter, they put their minds to it, and finally thumbs came.”

“Willpower!” said Medora.

“And early in April they went to the Sassafras and said: ‘We have thumbs! We have thumbs! So we need a different sort of mitten.’

“The Sassafras was only half awake. ‘Thumbs?’ he repeated. ‘How many?’

“ ‘Two!’ cried the nymphs. ‘Two!’

===Chinese===

「可是仙子们知道自己缺着大拇指，便一直心心念念地想要。于是，在那漫长而无聊的冬天里，她们一心一意地琢磨，大拇指终于长了出来。」

「意志力！」梅多拉说。

「四月初，她们到黄樟那里去，说：『我们有大拇指了！我们有大拇指了！所以要换一种手套了。』」

「黄樟才醒了半醒。『大拇指？』他重复道。『几个？』」

「『两个！』仙子们喊道。『两个！』」

===Original===

“A passing breeze roused the Sassafras. He became at least three-quarters awake.”

“I doubt it,” muttered Hortense.

“ ‘That’s interesting,’ he said. ‘I aim to supply all new needs. Come back in a month or so, and meanwhile I’ll see what I can do for you.’

“In May the nymphs returned with their thumbs and asked, ‘How about our new mittens?’ ”

===Chinese===

「一阵过路的风把黄樟吹得清醒了些。他至少醒了四分之三。」

「我才不信。」霍滕丝咕哝道。

「『那倒有意思，』他说。『凡有新的需求，我一概立志供应。过个把月再来，这其间我瞧瞧能替你们效点什么劳。』」

「五月里，仙子们带着大拇指回来了，问道：『我们的新手套怎么样了？』」

===Original===

The story was really under way now, and Cope went on with more confidence and with greater animation.

“ ‘Look and see,’ said the Sassafras.

“They looked and saw. Among its simple ordinary leaves were several with two lobes﻿—one on each side. ‘Will these do?’

“ ‘Do?’ said the nymphs. ‘We said we had two thumbs, but we meant one on each hand, stupid. Do? We should say not!’

“The Sassafras was mortified. ‘Well,’ he said, ‘that’s all I can manage this season. I’m sorry not to have understood you young ladies and your needs. Come back again next spring.’

===Chinese===

故事如今真的上了正轨，科普讲下去，信心更足，兴致也更活泛了。

「『自己看吧。』黄樟说。」

「她们看了，也看见了。在它那些素净的寻常叶子中间，杂着几张生两片裂片的——每边一片。『这些行吗？』」

「『行吗？』仙子们说。『我们是说过有两个大拇指，可我们的意思是每只手上一个，笨家伙。行吗？断然不行！』」

「黄樟羞惭无地。『唉，』他说，『今年这个季节能办到的，就只有这些了。很抱歉没能弄明白诸位小姐和你们的需求。明年春天再来吧。』」

===Original===

“It was a long time to wait, but they waited. Next May﻿—”

Amy, now unworried by George Pearson, began to get the thread of the thing. Foster was sure the thread would run through. Hortense was still alert for ulterior meanings. Poor Cope, however, had no ambition to spin a double thread﻿—a single one was all he was equal to.

“Next May the nymphs, after nursing their thumbs for a year﻿—”

Hortense frowned.

===Chinese===

「这一等可不短，可她们等了。转年五月——

艾米此刻不再受乔治·皮尔逊的搅扰，开始抓住了故事的线头。福斯特确信这线会一路纺到底。霍滕丝仍警觉地等着弦外之音。然而可怜的科普，并无雄心去纺什么双股的线——单股的一根，已是他的才力所能及的全部了。

「转年五月，仙子们把大拇指将养了一年之后——

霍滕丝皱起了眉。

===Original===

“﻿—came back again; and there, among the plain leaves and the double-lobed leaves, were several fresh bright, smooth ones with a single lobe well to one side﻿—the very thing for mittens. And﻿—”

“Yes, he has done it,” Foster acknowledged.

“And that,” ended Cope rather stridently, as he rose to go on the flood of a sudden yet unexpected success, “is Why the Sassafras﻿—”

“Why the Sassafras has Three Kinds of Leaves!” cried Medora in triumph. Mittens for midsummer made no difficulty.

===Chinese===

「——又回来了；于是在素叶与双裂叶之间，添出了几张新鲜、光亮而平滑的叶子，偏向一侧生着一个单独的裂片——恰是做手套的料子。而且——

「成了，他编成了。」福斯特认可道。

「而这就是，」科普收尾道，嗓音颇为尖利，趁着一阵突然而又出乎意料的成功之势起身欲行，「黄樟为何——

「——黄樟为何有三种叶子！」梅多拉得意洋洋地喊道。至于仲夏的手套，已不在话下。

===Original===

Cope gave Carolyn careful thanks for her support at the piano, and did not see that she felt he too could be a poet if he only would. He went out of his way to shake hands with Hortense, and did not realize how nearly a new quarrel had opened. He stepped over to do the like with Amy; but she went out with him into the hall﻿—the only one of the party who did﻿—and even accompanied him to the front door.

“Thank you so much,” she said, looking up into his face smilingly and holding his hand with a long, clinging touch. “It went beautifully; and there are others that will go even better.”

“Others?” He thought, for an instant, that she was thanking him for his Legend and was even threatening to regard him as a flowing fount of invention; but he soon realized that her mind was fixed exclusively on their duet﻿—if such it was to be called.

“The deuce!” he thought. “Enough is enough.”
