Despite his success with the Sassafras, he went home discomforted and even flustered. That hand was too much like the hand of possession. The girl was stealing over him like a light, intangible vapor. He struck ahead with a quicker gait, as if trying to outwalk a creeping fog. One consolation, however: Hortense had come like a puff of wind. Even a second squall from the same quarter would not be altogether amiss.

And had there not been one further fleeting source of reassurance? Had he not, on leaving, caught through the open door of the drawing room an elevation of Medora Phillips’ eyebrows which seemed to say fondly, indulgently, yet a bit ironically, “Oh, you foolish girl!”? Yet if a girl is foolish, and is going to persist in her folly, a lightly lifted pair of eyebrows will not always stay her course. Her gathering momentum is hardly to be checked by such slender means.

===Chinese===

科普周到地感谢了卡罗琳在钢琴上的襄助，却没看出来，她心里觉得他只要肯，也一样做得了诗人。他又特意绕过去同霍滕丝握手，却没意识到一场新的口角险些就此开了头。他又踱过去，想同艾米照样握一握；她却陪他一同走出门厅——满座宾客中唯有她一人如此——甚至一直送他到大门前。

「太谢谢你了。」她说，笑吟吟地仰望着他的脸，久久依恋不舍地握着他的手。「唱得美极了；还有别的曲子，会唱得更好呢。」

「别的？」一时之间，他还以为她谢的是他那则《传奇》，甚至大有要把他奉为一口滔滔涌流的妙思泉源的架势；但他随即明白，她一心惦记的，只是他们那支二重奏——倘若那也配称作二重奏的话。

「见鬼！」他想。「够了够了。」

黄樟的故事虽告成功，他回家时却心绪不宁，甚至有些慌乱。那只手太像一只占有者的手了。姑娘正像一缕轻渺无形的水汽，悄悄漫上他的周身。他加快脚步向前赶，仿佛想把一场悄悄爬行的雾甩在身后。不过也有一样宽慰：霍滕丝来得像一阵疾风。即便同一方位再刮一场骤风，也不见得全然是坏事。

再说，不是还有过一个转瞬即逝的定心之源吗？出门的时候，他不是从客厅敞开的门里，捕捉到梅多拉·菲利普斯那双扬起的眉毛吗——那似乎在慈爱地、宽容地、却略带揶揄地说：「哦，你这傻姑娘！」然而，一个姑娘既已痴傻，又打算痴傻到底，一对轻轻扬起的眉毛，未必总能挡住她的去路。她愈积愈足的势头，是很难靠这样纤细的手段遏止的。

## XIII Cope Dines Again﻿—and Stays After / 第十三章 科普再度赴宴——并留了下来

===Original===

Medora Phillips’ social activities ran through several social strata and her entertainments varied to correspond. Sometimes she contented herself with mere boy-and-girl affairs, which were thrown together from material gathered within her own household and from the humbler walks of undergraduate life. Sometimes she entertained literary celebrities, and invited the head professors and their wives to meet them. And two or three times a season she gave real dinners to “society,” summoning to Ashburn avenue, from homes even more architectural than her own, the banking and wholesale families whose incomes were derived from the city, but who pillared both the university and the many houses of worship in Churchton itself. And sometimes, when she passed over the older generation of these families in favor of the younger, her courses were more “liberal” than Churchton’s earlier standards quite approved.

On such formal occasions her three young ladies were dispensed with. They were encouraged to go to some sorority gathering or to some fudge-party. On the occasion now meditated she had another young person in mind. This was the granddaughter of one of the banking families; the girl might come along with her father and mother. She was not very pretty, not very entertaining; however, Mrs. Phillips needed one girl, and if she were not very attractive, none the worse. The one girl was for the one young man. The one young man was to be Bertram Cope. Our fond lady meant to have him and to show him off, sure that her choicest circle could not but find him as charming as she herself did. Most of us, at one time or another, have thrust forward our preferences in the same confident way.

Cope made less of an impression than his patroness had hoped for. Somehow his lithe youthfulness, his fine hair and teeth and eyes, the rich resonance of his voice counted for little﻿—except, perhaps, with the granddaughter. The middle-aged people about him were used to young college men and indifferent to them. Cope himself felt that he was in a new environment, and a loftier one. Several of these were important people, with names familiar through the town and beyond. He employed a caution that almost became inexpressiveness. He also found Mrs. Phillips a shade more formal and stately than her wont. She herself, in her furtive survey of the board, was disappointed to find that he was not telling. “Perhaps it’s that girl,” she thought; “she may be even duller than I supposed.” But never mind; all would be made right later. Some music had been arranged and there would be an accompanist who would help him do himself full justice.

“They’ll enjoy him,” she thought confidently.

===Chinese===

梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）的社交活动贯穿好几个社会阶层，她的款待也随之分出等次。有时她满足于只办些少男少女的小聚会，人选无非从自己家中以及本科生生活较为朴素的圈子里凑集而来。有时她宴请文坛名流，并邀各位首席教授携夫人前来相会。而一季里有两三回，她要为「上流社会」设真正的晚宴，把那些银行界与批发业的人家请到阿什伯恩大道（Ashburn avenue）来——他们自家的宅邸比她的还要讲究建筑气派，他们的进项取自这座城市，却又是大学以及丘奇顿（Churchton）本地众多礼拜堂的支柱。而有时她略过这些人家的长辈而属意年轻一辈，席间的做派便比丘奇顿早年的标准所能认可的要更「自由」几分。

逢这样的正式场合，她家的三位年轻小姐便一概免于出席，反倒受鼓励去参加什么姐妹会的聚会，或是做奶油软糖的小聚会。眼下筹划的这场，她心里另有一位年轻人。那是某家银行人家的一位孙女；姑娘可以随父母同来。她算不上漂亮，也说不上有趣；不过菲利普斯太太正需要一位姑娘，倘若她魅力不足，倒也无妨。这一位姑娘，是配那一位年轻人的。而那一位年轻人，便是伯特伦·科普（Bertram Cope）。我们这位痴心的太太打定主意要请他来、要他一显身手，确信她那最精选的圈子必定同她本人一样，觉出他的可爱。我们多数人，都曾在某个时候，以同样的自信把自己心仪的人往前推举过。

科普留给人的印象，却不及他的女恩主所期望的那般。不知怎么，他那灵巧的青春气息，他那姣好的头发、牙齿和眼睛，他那嗓音的丰润悠扬，都没起多大作用——或许除了对那位孙女以外。他周围的中年人见惯了年轻的大学生，对他们无动于衷。科普自己也觉得身处一个新的环境，而且是一个更高的环境。座中有几位是显要人物，名姓在全城乃至城外都耳熟能详。他处处谨慎，几乎到了木然寡言的地步。他还发觉菲利普斯太太比平素端凝庄重了几分。而她本人趁人不备扫视全席，失望地发现他并不出彩。「许是那姑娘的缘故，」她想，「她说不定比我料想的还要乏味。」不过不要紧；回头一切都会圆满。已经安排了些音乐，还会有一位伴奏，帮他把自己的长处发挥尽致。

「他们会喜欢他的。」她满怀信心地想。

===Original===

She had provided an immensity of flowers. There was an excess of light, both from electric bulbs and from candles. And there was wine.

“I think I can have just one kind, for once,” she had said to herself. “I know several houses where they have two﻿—Churchton or not﻿—and at least one where they sometimes have three. If this simple town thinks I can put grape-juice and Apollinaris before such people as these.﻿ ﻿…” Besides, the interesting Cope might interestingly refuse!

As the many courses moved on, Cope smelt the flowers, which were too many, and some of them too odoriferous; he blinked at the lights and breathed the heavy thickening air; and he took﻿—interestingly﻿—a few sips of burgundy﻿—for he was now in Rome, and no longer a successful Protestant in some lesser town of the empire. He had had a hard, close day of it, busy indoors with themes and with general reading; and he recalled being glad that the dinner had begun with reasonable promptitude﻿—for he had bothered with no lunch beyond a glass of milk and a roll. Tonight there had been everything﻿—even to an unnecessary entrée. He laid down a spoon on his plate, glad that the frozen pudding﻿—of whatever sort﻿—was disposed of. Too much of everything after too little. The people opposite were far away; their murmuring had become a mumbling, and he wished it was all over. The granddaughter at his elbow was less rewarding than ever, less justificatory of the effortful small-talk which he had put forth with more and more labor, and which he could scarcely put forth now at all. What was it he was meaning to do later? To sing? Absurd! Impossible! His head ached; he felt faint and dizzy.﻿ ﻿…

“We will leave you gentlemen to your cigars,” he heard a distant voice saying; and he was conscious for an instant that his hostess was looking down the table at him with a face of startled concern.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

她备下了数量惊人的鲜花。灯也过盛了，电灯泡与烛火一齐放光。还有酒。

「我想这一回只上一种酒也使得，」她曾这样自语。「我知道有好几户人家上两种——且不论是不是丘奇顿——至少还有一户，有时上到三种。这座简朴的城要是以为，我能把葡萄汁和阿波利纳里斯（Apollinaris）矿泉水摆到这样的贵客面前……」何况，那位耐人寻味的科普，说不定也会耐人寻味地推辞呢！

一道道菜陆续上来，科普闻着花香——花太多了，有几盆又香气过浓；他对着灯光直眨眼，呼吸着愈见沉浊的空气；他还——耐人寻味地——呷了几口勃艮第葡萄酒（burgundy）——因为他如今身在罗马，不再是帝国某座小城里一个得志的新教徒了。他这一天过得又紧又闷，关在屋里对付学生作文和泛读功课；他记得自己还庆幸过晚宴开得及时——因为午饭他只胡乱用了一杯牛奶、一只圆面包，再没费神别的。今晚样样俱全——甚至多出一道不必要的主菜。他把汤匙搁回盘上，庆幸那道冰冻布丁——不论究竟是哪一种——总算完结了。先是过于匮乏，继而样样太过。对面的人远在天边；他们的低语变成了含混的咕哝，他只盼这一切赶紧收场。肘边那位孙女比先前更无趣味，越发衬不起他费尽力气维持的那点寒暄——这寒暄他出得一次比一次吃力，眼下几乎全然出不动了。他方才打算待会儿做什么来着？唱歌么？荒唐！断无可能！他头也疼了，只觉虚弱眩晕……

「我们告退了，让诸位先生享用雪茄吧。」他听见一个遥远的声音说；有一瞬，他察觉女主人正顺着餐桌朝他这头望来，一脸惊惶的关切……

===Original===

“Don’t try to lead him out,” a deep voice said. “Lay him on the floor.”

He felt himself lowered; some small rug was doubled and redoubled and placed under his head; a large, firm hand was laid to his wrist; and something﻿—a napkin dipped in a glass of water and then folded?﻿—was put to his forehead.

“His pulse will come up in a minute,” he heard the same deep voice say. “If he had taken a step he would have fainted altogether.”

“My poor, dear boy! Whatever in the world﻿ ﻿… !” Thus Medora Phillips.

“Better not be moved for a little,” was the next pronouncement.

===Chinese===

「别扶他出去，」一个低沉的嗓音说。「把他放平在地板上。」

他觉出自己被放平了；一方小地毯折了又折，垫在他头下；一只宽大而坚实的手按上他的腕子；还有一样什么——是浸过一杯水又折起来的餐巾？——敷上了他的前额。

「他的脉搏一分钟之内就会回上来，」他听见同一个低沉的嗓音说。「他方才要是迈出一步，就得整个儿昏过去。」

「我可怜的、亲爱的孩子！这到底是怎么……！」梅多拉·菲利普斯如是说。

「暂且还是别挪动他为好。」这是下一句宣示。

===Original===

Cope lay there inert, but reasonably conscious of what was going on. His eyes gave him no aid, but his ears were open. He heard the alarmed voice of Medora Phillips directing the disconcerted maids, and the rustle and flutter of the garments of other daughters of Eve, who had found him interesting at last. They remarked appreciatively on his pallor; and one of them said, next day, before forgetting him altogether, that, with his handsome profile (she mentioned especially his nose and chin) and with his colorlessness, he looked for a moment like an ancient cameo.

He knew, now, that he was not going to faint, and that he was in better case than he seemed. In the circumstances he found nothing more original to say than: “I shall be all right in no time; just a touch of dizziness.﻿ ﻿…” He was glad his dress-coat could stand inspection, and hoped nobody would notice that his shoes had been half-soled.﻿ ﻿…

After a little while he was led away to a couch in the library. The deep-voiced doctor was on one side of him and Medora Phillips on the other. Soon he was left alone to recuperate in the dark﻿—alone, save for one or two brief, fluttery appearances by Mrs. Phillips herself, who allowed the coffee to be passed without any supervision on her own part.

On the second of these visitations he found voice to say:

“I’m so sorry for this﻿—and so ashamed. I can’t think how it could have happened.”

===Chinese===

科普一动不动躺在那里，对眼前的情形心里却相当有数。眼睛帮不上忙，耳朵却敞开着。他听见梅多拉·菲利普斯惊惶的声音在调度那些手足无措的女仆，又听见其余夏娃的女儿们衣裙的窸窣与翻动——她们终于觉出他有趣来了。她们带着赏鉴的口吻品评他的苍白；其中一位第二天说——在把他忘个干净之前说——他那俊美的侧影（她特别提到他的鼻子和下巴）配上那毫无血色的脸色，一时之间竟像一枚古代的浮雕宝石。

他此刻明白了：自己不会昏过去，实情也比外表看来要好。处此境地，他找不出更有新意的话，只得说：「我一会儿就好；不过是有点头晕……」他庆幸自己的礼服经得起细看，只盼没人留意他的鞋换过前掌……

过了一小会儿，他被搀扶出去，安置在书房一张躺椅上。那位嗓音低沉的医生在他一边，梅多拉·菲利普斯在另一边。不多时，他便被独自留在黑暗中将息——独自一人，只有菲利普斯太太本人进来过一两回，短短一现、心神不定；咖啡由下人自去传递，她全然顾不上照管了。

第二回进来探看时，他才找着嗓子说：

「这事真对不住——也真难为情。我想不出怎么竟会有这样的事。」

===Original===

He *was* ashamed, of course. He had broken up an entertainment pretty completely! Servants running about for him when they had enough to do for the company at large! All the smooth conventions of dinner-giving violently brushed the wrong way! He had fallen by the roadside, a young fellow who had rather prided himself on his health and vigor. Pitiful! He was glad to lie in the dark with his eyes shut tight, tight.

If he had been fifteen or twenty years older he might have taken it all rather more lightly. Basil Randolph, now﻿—But Randolph had not been invited, though his sister and her husband were of the company. Yet had it been Randolph, he would have smiled a wan smile and tried for a mild joke, conscious that he had made an original and picturesque contribution to the affair﻿—had broken the bland banality of routined dinner-giving and had provided woman with a mighty fine chance to “minister” and fuss: a thing she rather enjoyed doing, especially if a hapless, helpless man had been delivered into her hands as a subject.

But there was no such consolation for poor abashed Cope. He had disclosed himself, for some reason or other, a weakling; and he had weakened at a conspicuously wrong time and in a conspicuously mistaken place. He had hoped, over the cigars and coffee, to lay the foundation of an acquaintance with the brother-in-law who was a trustee﻿—to set up an identity in this influential person’s mind as a possible help to the future of Arthur Lemoyne. But the man now in the dining-room, or the drawing-room, or wherever, might as well be in the next state.

There came a slight patter of rain on the bay-window near his head. He began to wonder how he was to get home.

Meanwhile, in the drawing-room, among the ladies, Mrs. Phillips was anxiously asking: “Was the room too warm? Could the wine have been too much for him?” And out in the dining-room itself, one man said, “Heaven knows just how they live;” and another, “Or what they eat, or don’t eat;” and a third, “Or just how hard these young beginners are driven.”

===Chinese===

他*确实*是难为情的，这还用说。他把一场款待搅了个七零八落！仆人们本有满座客人要伺候，却还要为他跑来跑去！请客吃饭的一整套圆熟规矩，被他横冲直撞地全数拂乱了！他这个一向颇以康健与精力自矜的年轻人，竟栽倒在了道旁。可怜见的！他庆幸能躺在黑暗里，把双眼闭得紧紧的、紧紧的。

他若是再年长十五二十岁，对这一切或许就看得淡些。譬如巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph）吧——不过伦道夫并未在受邀之列，虽然他姐姐和姐夫都身在客中。倘若倒是伦道夫遭了这事，他会泛起一丝苍白的一笑，试着说句轻轻的俏皮话，心里明白自己给这场合添了一桩别致而生动的贡献——打破了例行宴请那套温吞的平板，给了女人一个大好机会去「悉心照料」、去大惊小怪：这原是她相当乐于做的事，尤其当交到她手里的，是一个倒霉无助的男人作底子。

可是又窘又愧的科普没有这等安慰。不知怎么，他已经显出了自己是个弱者；而且是在一个扎眼的不巧时刻、一个扎眼的错误地点弱下去的。他本指望趁雪茄与咖啡之间，同那位身为校董的姐夫奠定一面之交——在这位有力人士心里立起一个印象，好为阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）的前程出一份力。可这个人如今无论在餐厅里、客厅里还是别的什么地方，都无异于远在邻州了。

他头旁那扇凸窗上传来轻细的雨点声。他开始盘算自己该怎么回家。

与此同时，客厅里，太太们中间，菲利普斯太太正焦急地问：「是屋子里太热了么？会不会是酒力对他太重了？」而在外面餐厅里，一位先生说：「天知道他们平日过的什么日子。」另一位说：「还有他们吃些什么，又忌些什么。」第三位说：「还有这些年轻的新手，究竟被驱使得多紧。」

===Original===

“Ought he to go out tonight, Doctor?” asked Mrs. Phillips in a whisper, appearing in the dining-room door.

“He might better stay if he can,” replied the authority, who happened to be at the nearer end of the table.

“Of course he can,” she returned. Of course there was a room for him.

When the party finally reassembled in the drawing-room Cope had disappeared. Mrs. Phillips could now enlarge on his attractiveness as a singer, and could safely assure them﻿—what she herself believed﻿—that they had lost a really charming experience. “If you could only have heard him that Sunday!” she concluded.

Cope had said, of course, “I can get home perfectly well,” and, “It’s a shame for me to be putting you out this way,” and so on and on﻿—the things you yourself would have said in the circumstances; but he said them with no particular spirit, and was glad, as he walked uncertainly upstairs, that he had not far to go.

===Chinese===

「他今晚该不该出门，大夫？」菲利普斯太太出现在餐厅门口，低声问道。

「要是能留下，还是留下的好。」那位权威答道，他恰巧坐在餐桌离门近的这一头。

「他当然能留下。」她应道。自然，家里有他住的房间。

待宾客们终于重聚到客厅里时，科普已经不见了。菲利普斯太太此刻正好放手渲染他作为歌者的动人，可以放心地向众人担保——她自己也是这么信的——他们错过了一回真正美妙的享受。「你们要是听过他那个星期天的演唱就好了！」她这样收束。

科普当然说过「我自己完全回得去」，也说过「这样叨扰您，实在过意不去」，诸如此类说个不休——换了你自己处在那境地，也会说这些话；只是他说得没什么精神，待他脚下虚浮地走上楼梯，又庆幸路没有多远。

===Original===

Mrs. Phillips indeed “had a room for him.” She had rooms aplenty. There was the chintz chamber on the third floor, where the Irish poet (who seemed not to expect very much for himself) had been put; and there was the larger, handsomer chamber on the second floor, where the Hindu philosopher (who had loomed up big and important through a vague Oriental atmosphere) had been installed in state. It was a Louis Quinze room, and the bed had a kind of silken canopy and a great deal too much in the way of bolsters and lace coverings. It was thought that the Hindu, judging from the report of the maid next morning, had been moved by some ascetic impulse to sleep not in the bed but on the floor beside it. This was the room now destined for Cope; surely one flight of stairs was enough. But there must be no further practice of asceticism﻿—least of all by a man who was really ill; so Mrs. Phillips, snatching a moment from her guests, herself saw the maid remove the lace pillow-shams and coverlet, and turn down the sheets, and set the thermos-bottle on the stand beside the reading lamp.﻿ ﻿…

“Don’t get up a moment earlier than you feel like doing,” she said, at the door. “Breakfast﻿—”

“Tomorrow is one of my busy days,” replied Cope wanly. “Goldsmith, Sheridan.﻿ ﻿…”

“Well, we have other wage-workers in the house, you know. At seven-thirty, then, if you must.”

“Seven-thirty, if you please. Thank you.”

===Chinese===

菲利普斯太太果然「有他一间房」。她的房间多的是。三楼有一间印花布陈设的屋子，爱尔兰诗人（他似乎并不为自己期求多少）住过；二楼还有一间更大更体面的，印度哲学家（他在一派朦胧的东方气氛围里显得高大而紧要）曾在此庄严下榻。那是一间路易十五式（Louis Quinze）的房间，床上悬着一种丝绸的华盖，衬垫和花边铺盖又未免太多。据第二天女仆的汇报推测，那位印度人想必是动了什么苦行的冲动，不睡床上，却睡在床边的地板上。如今这间屋子注定派给科普；一段楼梯，总归够他走的了。只是决不能再行什么苦行——尤其不能由一个当真病着的人来行；于是菲利普斯太太从宾客中间偷得片刻，亲自看着女仆撤去花边的枕罩与床罩，翻开被单，把热水瓶放到阅读灯旁的小几上……

「比想起的时辰早一刻也不必，」她在门口说。「早餐——」

「明天是我忙的日子之一，」科普怏怏地答道。「哥尔德斯密斯，谢里丹……」

「呃，你知道，家里还有别的挣工钱的人呢。那就七点半吧，要是你非这样不可。」

「就七点半，劳您费心。谢谢。」

===Original===

By the time Mrs. Phillips had returned to her guests, the first of the limousines was standing before the house; its wet top shone under an electric globe. Her own car, meanwhile, obdurately reposed in its garage. Presently a second limousine joined the first, and a third the second; and in another quarter of an hour her guests were well on their way to dispersal. She bade them all goodnight in the best of good humor.

“You’ve never before had quite such an evening as this, I’m sure!” she said, with great gaiety.

“Isn’t it wonderful how she took it all!” said one lady to another, on the back seat of her car. “Anything like that would have thrown me off completely.”

The other lady laughed amusedly. She often found our Medora “great fun.”

Meanwhile, Cope, upstairs, was sinking deeper and deeper into his big, wide, overupholstered bed. And as his body sank, his spirit sank with it. He felt poor, unimportant, ill at ease. In especial, he felt greatly subordinated; he wished that he might have capitulated to a man. Then the mystery of handsome houses and of handsome furnishings came to harass him. Such things were everywhere: how were they got, how were they kept? Should he himself ever﻿—? But no; nothing ahead for years, even in the most favorable of circumstances, save an assistant professorship, with its inconceivably modest emoluments.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

待菲利普斯太太回到宾客中间，头一辆高级轿车已经停在宅前；淋湿的车顶在一盏球形电灯下泛着光。她自己的汽车，却兀自执拗地歇在车库里。不多时，第二辆轿车挨上了第一辆，第三辆又挨上第二辆；再过一刻钟，她的客人已经散得七七八八。她以极好的兴致，向他们一一道了晚安。

「你们以前准没经历过这么一个晚上，我敢说！」她说得兴高采烈。

「她把这一切应付得多么从容，真是了不得！」一位太太在自家汽车后座上对另一位说。「摊上这样的事，我准得方寸大乱。」

另一位太太给逗笑了。她时常觉得我们的梅多拉「顶有趣」。

与此同时，楼上的科普正越陷越深地沉进那张又大又宽、软垫层叠的床里去。身子往下沉，心气也跟着往下沉。他觉得自己贫穷、无足轻重、浑身不自在。尤其是，他深感自己矮了一截；他宁愿自己当初是向一个男人缴械的。接着，华宅与美器的奥秘又来纠缠他。这样的东西遍地都是：它们是怎样挣来的，又是怎样守住的？他自己有朝一日可会——？可是不会；往后多年没有任何前程可言，纵使境遇再顺，也无非一个助理教授的职位，连同那份难以置信的微薄薪俸……

===Original===

And Medora Phillips, in the stir of getting her guests out of the house, had her first vision of him as sinking off to sleep. Somehow or other his fine, straight yellow hair retained its backward sweep with no impairment by reason of turnings and tossings; his clear profile continued to keep itself disengaged from any depression in the pillows; his slender hands were laid in quiet symmetry over the wide edge of the down-turned coverlet. A decorous, unperturbed young old-master﻿ ﻿… Van Eyck﻿ ﻿… Carpaccio.﻿ ﻿…

Cope came down to breakfast a little pale, a little shamefaced; but he felt pretty well revived and he made up in excess of speech and action what he essentially lacked in spirit. Mrs. Phillips descended as early as the three girls﻿—earlier, in fact, than Hortense, who entered informally through the butler’s pantry and apparently in full possession of last night’s facts. Carolyn inquired civilly after his condition; Amy Leffingwell, with her blue eyes intent upon him, expressed concern and sympathy; Hortense, with her lips closely shut in a satirical smile, said nothing at all: a possible exhibition of self-control which gave her aunt some measure of solicitude. It was not always well when she talked, and it was not always well when she kept silent. Mrs. Phillips pressed the toast upon him and recommended the grapefruit. He took both with satisfaction, and a second cup of coffee. With that he felt he could easily walk to his classroom; and the walk itself, in the fresh morning air, would brace him further for his hours of routine with his students.

“What a regular nuisance I’ve made of myself!” he said, on leaving the house.

===Chinese===

而梅多拉·菲利普斯，在送客出门的忙乱之中，初次浮起了他渐入睡乡的模样。不知怎么，他那头细密挺括的金发依旧向后梳拢，并未因辗转翻覆而减了整齐；他清朗的侧影始终不曾陷进枕间的凹窝；一双纤手安详对称地摊在翻折下来的床单宽边上。好一幅端凝恬静、古大师笔下的年轻人像……凡·艾克（Van Eyck）……卡尔帕乔（Carpaccio）……

科普下楼来用早餐，脸色微白，面带赧色；但他自觉将养得差不多了，便以过甚的言语与动作，弥补他心底实在欠缺的元气。菲利普斯太太下得同三位姑娘一样早——事实上比霍滕丝（Hortense）还早，霍滕丝是从配膳间那头随意进来的，昨夜的原委显然已经尽知。卡罗琳（Carolyn）客气地问候他的身体；艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）一双蓝眼睛专注地望着他，殷殷表达关切与同情；霍滕丝却双唇紧闭，噙着讥诮的笑，一言不发：这份克制的功夫，倒叫她姑妈担上了几分心。她开口未必是好事，她不开口也未必是好事。菲利普斯太太劝他多用吐司，又荐他尝尝西柚。两样他都受用满意，还续了一杯咖啡。他觉得就此走去课堂毫不费力；这一趟步行本身，在清晨的新鲜空气里，还能再给他添些劲头，去应付和学生们的那些例行课业。

「我这回可真成了个十足的累赘！」他临出门时说。

===Original===

“Oh, haven’t you, just!” exclaimed Mrs. Phillips joyously. “Your name as an entertainer will be all over town! I’m sure you gave some of those poky people a real touch of novelty!”

Amy Leffingwell was in the front hall at the same time, with her music-roll. They were going the same way, to substantially the same place, to meet about the same hour in the day’s schedule. They went along the street together.

The morning air was brisk and cool after last night’s shower. Like the trees under which they passed, it gave the first decided intimation of autumn. They set off at a lively pace toward the college towers and the lake.

Cope was soon sailing along with his head high, his trim square shoulders much in action, and his feet throwing themselves spiritedly here and there. Amy, who was not very tall, kept up as well as she could.

“This isn’t too fast for you﻿ ﻿… ?” she asked presently.

===Chinese===

「哎呀，可不正是嘛！」菲利普斯太太欢快地叫道。「你这位宴席助兴的名声就要传遍全城了！我敢说，你给那几位闷家伙添了一股真正的新鲜劲儿！」

艾米·莱芬韦尔这时也正在前厅里，夹着她的琴谱卷。两人同路，去处实质上是同一个，日程表上碰头的钟点也差不多。他们便沿街同行。

昨夜一场骤雨过后，清晨的空气清冽凉爽。同他们打底下走过的那些树木一样，这空气也给出了秋天将至的第一个确凿讯息。他们迈开轻快的步子，朝学院的塔楼和湖那边走去。

科普不久便昂着头扬帆般前行，端正方正的双肩大幅度摆动，两只脚精神抖擞地东一下西一下发力。艾米个子不高，只能竭力跟上。

「这个速度对你不算太快吧……？」过了一会儿，她问道。

===Original===

“No; but it may be a little too fast for you. Excuse me; I’ve never learned to keep pace with a woman. But as for myself, I never felt better in my life. Every yard toward the good old lake”﻿—the wind was coming down from the north in a great sweep﻿—“makes me feel finer.”

He slowed up appreciably.

“Oh, not for me!” she said in deprecation. “I like a brisk morning walk as well as anybody. Did you sing at all?” she asked.

“Not a note. They put the soft pedal on me. They ‘muted’ me,” he amended, in deference to her own branch of the profession.

“We came in by the side door about half past nine. It was a dull meeting. I listened for you. Somebody was playing.”

Cope gave a sly smile.

===Chinese===

「不算；不过对你也许稍快了些。恕我失礼；我从来没学过怎样同女人并步而行。至于我自己，这辈子还从没有这么痛快过。朝着那片可爱的老湖每走一码」——风正从北面大片地扫下来——「我都觉得更精神一分。」

他把步子明显放慢了下来。

「哦，不用为我！」她逊谢道。「清早快步走走，我和谁一样地喜欢。你到底唱了没有？」她问。

「一个音符也没唱。他们对我踩了弱音踏板。他们把我『加了弱音』，」他顾到她那一行，如此修正道。

「我们九点半前后从边门进来的。聚会闷得很。我留神听你来着。有谁在弹琴。」

科普狡黠地一笑。

===Original===

“It must have been the poor disappointed woman who was to have accompanied me. She had had a list of three or four of my things﻿—to run them over in her own album, I suppose. Think just how disappointed she must have been to find that she had the whole field to herself!”

“Oh, musicians﻿—even we poor, despised professionals﻿—are not all like that. If it had been arranged for me to accompany you with an obbligato, I shouldn’t have been pleased if opportunity had failed me.”

“Your contribution would have been more important than hers. And your substitution for my failure would have given added interest.”

The talk, having reached the zone of arid compliment, tended to languish. They had now reached Learning’s side of the trolley-tracks, and rills in the great morning flood of the scholastic life were beginning to gather about them and to unite in a rolling stream which flowed toward the campus.

Two or three streets on, the pair separated, she to her work, he to his. For him the walk had been a nothing in particular﻿—he would a little have preferred taking it alone. For her it had been﻿—despite the low level of expressiveness reached on either side﻿—a privilege which had been curtailed much too soon.

===Chinese===

「那准是那位可怜的、大失所望的女士——她原本要给我伴奏的。她手里存着我三四支曲子的单子——我想，是要在她自己的曲谱册里过一遍罢。想想吧，等她发现整个场子归了她一个人，该有多失望！」

「哦，搞音乐的——连我们这些可怜的、遭人白眼的专业人士——也不全是那号人。要是原先安排的是我带助奏（obbligato）给你伴奏，临了没了机会，我也不会高兴的。」

「你的份量原会比她的要紧。而且由你来顶我的失场，也会更添一层趣味。」

两人的谈话既已抵达干涸的恭维地带，便渐趋冷落。这时他们也走到了电车道属于学术的一侧，学术生活清晨洪流中的涓涓细脉，正开始在他们四周汇聚，合成一道滚滚溪流，朝校园淌去。

再走过两三条街，两人便分了手，她赴她的差事，他去他的课堂。于他，这趟步行不过稀松平常——他倒宁可独自一人走。于她，这却是一桩殊荣——尽管双方表露的情意都浅得很——而这殊荣又被截短得太早了。

===Original===

Meanwhile, back in the house, Hortense was detailing the events of the previous evening to Joe Foster; the general access of activity on the morning after had made it desirable that she help with his breakfast.

She went at it with a will.

“Why,” she said, as Foster sat at his coffee, boiled egg and toast, “he keeled over like a baby.”

“Hum!” said Foster darkly. It was as if a shaping ideal had dissipated. Or as if a trace of weakness in one seemingly so young and strong was not altogether unacceptable as a source of consolation.

However, Cope, at half past four that afternoon, was on the faculty tennis-courts, with a racquet in his hand. But one set was enough. “I seem to be a day ahead of my schedule,” he said, pulling out and strolling along homeward.

===Chinese===

与此同时，宅子那头，霍滕丝正把昨夜的种种细说给乔·福斯特（Joe Foster）听；晚宴翌晨阖家上下忙作一团，他的早餐便最好由她来照应。

她讲得津津有味。

「嗐，」福斯特坐在那里用他的咖啡、煮蛋和吐司时，她说，「他像个小娃娃似的，一下就栽倒了。」

「哼！」福斯特阴沉沉地说。仿佛一个正在成形的理想消散了。又仿佛，在一个看似如此年轻强壮的人身上寻着一点虚弱的痕迹，作为宽解的由头，倒也并非全然不受用。

然而，当天下午四点半，科普已经站在教工网球场上了，手里握着球拍。不过打完一盘也就够了。「我好像比自己的日程提前了一天。」他说着退出球局，悠悠然往家里踱去。

## VI Cope Dines﻿—and Tells About It / 第六章 科普赴宴——并细说其详

===Original===
Cope pushed away the last of the themes and put the cork back in the red-ink bottle. Here was a witless girl who seemed to think that Herrick and Cowper were contemporaries. The last sense to develop in the Western void was apparently the sense of chronology﻿—unless, indeed, it were a sense for the shades of difference which served to distinguish between one age and another and provided the raw material that made chronology a matter of consequence at all.

“If there were only one more,” muttered Cope, looking at the pile of sheets under the gas-globe, “I should probably learn that Chaucer derived from Beaumont and Fletcher.”

He reached up and jerked the gas-jet to a different angle. The flame lit, through its nicked, pale-pink globe, a bedroom cramped in size and meagre in furnishings: a narrow bed, dressed to look like a lounge; two stiff-backed oak chairs, not lately varnished; a bookshelf overhead, with some dozen of the more indispensable aids to our tongue’s literature. The table at which he sat was one of plain deal, covered with some Oriental-seeming fabric which showed here and there inkspots that antedated his own pen. He threw up this covering as it fell over the front edge of the table, pulled out a drawer, laid a sheet of paper in the bettered light, and uncorked a black-ink bottle.

“Dear Arthur,” he began.

He looked across to the other chair, with its broken spindles and obfuscated varnish. With things as he wanted them, his correspondent would be sitting there and letter-writing would be unnecessary.

“Dear Arthur,” he repeated aloud, and set himself to a general sketch of the new land and the “lay” of it.

===Chinese===
科普（Cope）把最后几份学生作文推开，将软木塞按回红墨水瓶里。瞧这个没脑子的姑娘，竟仿佛以为赫里克（Herrick）与考珀（Cowper）是同时代人。在西部这片空漠之中，最后才发育起来的官能，看来便是年代先后之感——再不然，便是对种种微妙差异的识力：正是这些差异区分开一个时代与另一个时代，也正是它们提供了那份原始材料，叫年代先后这件事总算成了一桩有分量的事。

「要是再有这么一份，」科普咕哝道，眼睛望着煤气灯罩下那一摞纸页，「我说不定就会得知，乔叟（Chaucer）承袭的是博蒙特与弗莱彻（Beaumont and Fletcher）。」

他抬手把煤气灯嘴拧向另一个角度。火光透过那盏有了豁口的淡粉色灯球，照亮一间逼仄而陈设寒素的卧室：一张窄床，铺陈得看似一具沙发榻；两把直背的橡木椅，漆面久未新刷；头顶一个书架，插着十来册研读英文文学最不可或缺的津梁。他伏案的那张桌子是张白松木桌，罩着一块貌似东方织物的东西，上面东一星西一点地现着墨渍，年代还在他自己那支笔之前。他把垂在桌前沿的罩布往上一掀，拉出一只抽屉，把一张纸摊进亮堂了些的光线里，又拔开一瓶黑墨水。

「亲爱的阿瑟（Arthur），」他起头写道。

他望向对面那把椅子——椅背的细木杆断裂了几根，漆色也晦暗不清。倘若事事都如他所愿，他的通信人此刻就该坐在那里，写信这回事也就用不着了。

「亲爱的阿瑟，」他又出声念了一遍，随即着手为这片新土地及其「格局」作一幅概略的勾勒。

===Original===
> “Three-quarters of them are of course girls,” he presently found himself writing, “which is the common proportion almost everywhere, I presume, except in engineering and dentistry. However, there are four or five men. I’ve been pretty careful, and they still treat me with respect. I’m afraid my course is regarded as a ‘snap.’ Everybody, it seems, can grasp English literature (and produce it). And almost anybody, I begin to fear, can teach it. Judging, that is, from the pay. I’m afraid the good folks at Freeford will find themselves pinched for another year still.”

He glanced across toward the pile of corrected themes. He felt that not everybody was “called,” as a matter of course, to write English, and he stubbornly nourished the belief that toiling over others’ imperfections was more of a job than boards of trustees always realized.

> “Of course,” he presently resumed, “things are rather changed from what they were before. I find more in the way of social opportunities and greater interest shown by the middle-aged. It is no disadvantage to cultivate people who have their own homes; the lunchrooms round the fountain-square are numerous enough, but not so good as they might be. And I don’t know but that an instructor may lose caste by eating among a miscellany of undergraduates. Anyhow, it’s no plan to pursue for long.”

He sat for a moment, lost in thought over recent social experiences.

===Chinese===
> 「他们中间四分之三当然是姑娘，」不一会儿他发觉自己正如此写道，「我想这在几乎哪儿都是寻常比例，工程科和牙科除外。不过，也有四五个男的。我一向相当检点，他们也依旧对我执礼甚恭。只怕我这门课被看作一门『省力课』。看来人人都能把握英文文学（还能创作它）。而且我开始担心，几乎随便什么人都能教它。这，是从薪水来判断的。只怕弗里福德（Freeford）的好人们还得再紧巴巴地过上一年。」

他朝那摞批改过的作文瞥了一眼。他觉得，并非人人都理所当然地「蒙召」去写英文；而且他固执地滋养着一个信念：在旁人的瑕疵上辛苦劳作，实在是比校董会们一向所明了的更像一份正经差事。

> 「当然，」过了片刻他又续道，「如今的情况与从前颇有不同。我在社交机会方面所得更多，中年人也表现出更大的兴趣。结交自置宅邸的人们并非坏事；喷泉广场一带的午餐馆倒也够多，只是没有本可以做到的那么好。而且说不定，一个讲师混杂在一帮五花八门的本科生中间用饭，是会有失身份的。无论如何，这不是长久之计。」

他坐了片刻，为近来的社交阅历出了会儿神。

===Original===
> “One very good house has lately been opened to me,” he continued. “I dined there last Thursday evening. It’s really quite a mansion﻿—a great many large rooms: picture-gallery, ballroom, and all that; and the dinner itself was very handsomely done. You know my theory﻿—a theory rather forced upon me, in truth, by circumstances﻿—that the best way to enjoy a good meal is to have had a string of poor ones. Well, since coming back, and with no permanent arrangements made, I have had plenty of chance for getting into position to appreciate the really first-class. There was a color-scheme in pale pink﻿—ribbons of that color, pink icing on the cakes, and so on. The same thing could be done, and done charmingly, in light green﻿—with pistache ice-cream. Of course the candle-shades were pink too.”

His eye wandered toward a small triangular closet, made off from the room by a flimsy and faded calico-print curtain.

> “I had my dress-suit cleaned and pressed, but the lapels of the coat came out rather shiny, and I thought it better to hire one for the occasion. There was no trouble about a fit﻿—I have standardized shoulders, as you know.
> “Of course I miss you all the time, and I assuredly missed you just here. If it is really true, as you write, that you are holding your summer gains and weigh twelve pounds more than you did at the end of June, and if you are thinking of getting a new suit, please bear in mind that my own won’t last much longer. I have the chance, now, to go out a good deal and to meet influential, worthwhile people. In the circumstances I ask you not to bant. One rather spare man in a pair of men is enough.
> “My hostess, a Mrs. Phillips, I met at a tea during my first week. This tea was given by a lady in the mathematical department, and she and her husband were at the dinner. They are people in the early or middle thirties, I judge, and were probably put in as a connecting link between the two sections of the party. Mrs. Phillips herself is a rich widow of forty-odd﻿—forty-five or six, possibly﻿—though I am not the very best judge in such matters: no need to tell you that, on such a point, my eye and my general sense are none too acute. The only other middle-aged (or elderly) person present was a Mr. Randolph, who is perhaps fifty, or a little beyond, yet who appears to have his younger moments. There were some girls, and there were two young men in business in the city﻿—neighbors and not connected with the University at all. ‘For which relief,’ etc.﻿—since it *is* a bit benumbing to move in academic circles exclusively;﻿—I should hate to feel that a really professorial manner was stealing over me. Well, everybody was lively and gay, except at first Ryder (he’s the math. man); but even he limbered up finally. Mrs. Phillips herself has a great deal of action and vivacity﻿—seemed hardly more than thirty. Well, I could be pretty gay too with a lot of money behind me; and I think that, for another year or so, I can contrive to be gay without it. But after that.﻿ ﻿…
> “I wish you had been there instead of Ryder. If you are really going to be twenty-seven in November﻿—as I figure it﻿—you might yourself have served as a connecting link between youth and age. No, no; I take it back; I didn’t mean it. I wouldn’t have you seem older for anything, and you know it.
> “There were three girls. They all live in the house itself, forming a little court: Mrs. P. seems to need young life and young attentions. So not one of them had to be taken home﻿—there’s usually *that* to do, you know. Not that it would have mattered much, as the distances would have been short and the night was clear starlight. But they could all stay where they were, and I walked home in quite different company.”

===Chinese===
> 「有一户极好的人家近来向我敞开了门，」他继续写道。「上礼拜四晚上我在那儿用了晚宴。那真是一座十足的华宅——许许多多的大房间：画廊、舞厅，一应俱全；晚宴本身也办得十分阔气。你知道我的理论——老实说，那理论多半是处境强加于我的——享用一顿美餐的最好法子，就是先连着吃上一串粗茶淡饭。嗯，自从回来以后，一应长远的安排都还没有定下，我有的是机会把自己摆到能够鉴赏真正头等席面的位置上。席间有一套淡粉色的配色——缎带是这个颜色，点心上的糖霜也泛粉，诸如此类。同一套路数改用浅绿也办得到，而且办得一样妩媚——配开心果冰淇淋。自然，烛罩也是粉的。」

他的目光游向那间小小的三角形壁橱——是用一道轻薄的、褪了色的印花布帘从房间里隔出来的。

> 「我那套礼服送洗熨烫过了，只是上衣的翻领压得相当发亮，我想这个场合还是租一套的好。合身与否毫无周折——你知道，我的肩膀是标准化的。
> 「我自然时时刻刻都惦念你，此刻在此地尤其惦念。倘若你信上所写属实——夏天长起来的分量都保持住了，比六月底重了十二磅——倘若你又正打算置办一套新装，请务必记着：我自己这套也撑不了多久了。我如今有的是机会出门交际，结识有权势、值得结交的人物。当此情形，我请求你万万不要节食。一对男子当中，有一个清瘦些的也就够了。
> 「我的女主人是一位菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips），我来到后的头一个礼拜，在一次茶会上与她相识。那场茶会是数学系一位太太办的，她和她的丈夫都出席了这次晚宴。我看他们是三十出头、三十五六岁年纪的人，多半是被安插在席上，充当两拨宾客之间的联络环节。菲利普斯太太本人是位四十开外的富有寡妇——也可能四十五六了——虽说在这类事情上我算不得最高明的裁判：不必告诉你，在这样一个问题上，我的眼力和我的总体感觉都不怎么敏锐。在座的另一位中年（或者已近老境）人士是位伦道夫先生（Mr. Randolph），约莫五十岁，或者稍稍出头，却似乎自有他年轻的时刻。另有几位姑娘，还有两位在城里经商的青年男子——是街坊近邻，与大学毫不相干。『为此谢天谢地』，云云——因为只在学界圈子里周旋，终究有点叫人发木；我可不愿意感到一种道地的教授派头正悄悄爬上我身。嗯，人人都活泼欢快，起初只有赖德（Ryder）除外（他就是那位数学教师）；不过到头来连他也放松开了。菲利普斯太太本人极其富于活力与生气——看上去简直不到三十。唉，背后垫着一大笔钱，我也尽可以快活得很；而且我想，再有一年上下，没有钱我也能设法快活。可再往后。……
> 「我倒希望你当时替了赖德坐在那儿。倘若你果真如我盘算的那样，到十一月便满二十七岁，你自己原可以充当青春与老成之间的联络环节。不，不；我收回这话；我不是那个意思。无论如何我都不愿你显得老气一分，这你是知道的。
> 「姑娘们有三位。她们都住在宅子本体之内，组成一个小小的宫廷：菲利普斯太太似乎需要年轻的生命与年轻的殷勤。于是她们谁也不必由人送回家——往常总有这一层事要办，你是知道的。倒也不是说这有多要紧，反正各处路都不远，那晚又是澄净的星光天。只是她们尽可以各安其位，而我便与全然不同的一路同伴步行回了家。」

===Original===
Cope threw back his Oriental table-cover once more and drew out a few additional sheets of paper.

> “One of them is an artist. She paints portraits, and possibly other things. Oh, I was going to say there is an art-gallery at the top of the house. Her husband﻿—I mean Mrs. Phillips’﻿—was a painter and collector himself; and after dinner we went up there, and a curious man came in, propelling a wheeled chair﻿—a sort of death’s-head at the feast.﻿ ﻿… But don’t let me get too far away from the matter in hand. She is dark and a bit tonguey﻿—the artist-girl; and I believe she would be sarcastic and witty if she weren’t held down pretty well. I think she’s a niece: the relationship leaves her free, as I suppose she feels, to express herself. If you like the type you may have it; but wit in a woman, or even humor, always makes me uncomfortable. The feminine idea of either is a little different from ours.
> “Another girl is a musician. She plays the violin﻿—quite tolerably. Yes, yes, I recall your views about violin-playing: it’s either good or bad﻿—nothing between. I’ll say this, then: she played some simple and unpretentious things and did them very deftly. Simple, unpretentious: oddest thing in the world, for she is a recent graduate of our school of music and began this fall as an instructor. Wouldn’t you have expected to find her demanding a chance to perform a sonata at the least, or pining miserably for a concerto with full orchestra? Well, this young lady I put down as a plain boarder﻿—you can’t maintain a big house on memories and a collection of paintings. She’s a nice child, and I dare say makes as good a boarder as any nice child could.
> “The third girl﻿—if you want to hear any more about them﻿—seems to be a secretary. Think of having the run of a house where a social secretary is required! I’m sure she sends out the invitations and keeps the engagement-book. Besides all that, she writes poetry﻿—she is the minstrel of the court. She does verses about her chatelaine﻿—is quite the mistress of self-respecting adulation. *She* would know the difference between Herrick and Cowper!”﻿ ﻿…

Cope pulled out his watch. Then he resumed.

===Chinese===
科普又把那块东方风味的桌布往上一掀，再抽出几张信纸。

> 「她们中有一位是艺术家。她画肖像，兴许也画别的。哦，我本想说的是，宅子顶层有一间美术馆。她的丈夫——我是说菲利普斯太太的丈夫——本人就是位画家兼收藏家；晚宴之后我们上到了那里，一个古怪的男人推着一辆轮椅进来——简直是宴席上的一颗骷髅头。……不过，别让我离手头的事扯得太远。她肤色深，话头也多——这位艺术家姑娘；我相信，倘若不是被按捺得相当严实，她准会又刻薄又俏皮。我想她是位外甥女：这层亲眷关系让她——我猜她自己这样觉得——尽可以畅所欲言。你要是喜欢这一类型，尽可以让给你；可是女人的机智，或者哪怕只是幽默，总叫我浑身不自在。她们对这两样的观念，跟我们男人的略微不同。
> 「另一位姑娘是音乐家。她拉小提琴——相当过得去。是，是，我记得你对小提琴演奏的见解：要么好，要么糟——没有中间可言。那么我这样说吧：她拉了几支质朴而不事张扬的曲子，处理得十分灵巧。质朴、不事张扬：这可是天底下最稀奇的事，因为她是我们音乐学院新近毕业的学生，今秋刚出任讲师。你原不曾料到她会要求至少来一支奏鸣曲的机会，或是凄凄切切地渴盼一支有整队乐队协奏的协奏曲吧？嗯，这位年轻女士，我把她记作一个普普通通的寄宿者——一座大宅子总不能靠回忆和一批藏画来维持。她是个可人的孩子，我敢说，随便哪个可人的孩子做寄宿者能做得多好，她也就做到了那个地步。
> 「第三位姑娘——要是你还想再听几句的话——似乎是个秘书。想想看，能在一座需要社交秘书的宅子里通行无阻！我敢断定，请柬由她发出，约会簿也归她执掌。除此种种之外，她还写诗——她是这座宫廷的行吟诗人。她为她那位堡中女主人作诗——颇谙那门不失自尊的颂扬之道。她才分得清赫里克与考珀呢！」……

科普掏出怀表看了看，随后又续写下去。

===Original===
> “It’s half past ten, but I think I’ll run on for a few moments longer. If I don’t finish, I can wind up tomorrow.﻿—Mr. Randolph sat opposite me. He looked at me a lot and gave attention to whatever I said﻿—whether said to him, or to my neighbors right and left, or to the whole table. I didn’t feel him especially clever, but easy and pleasant﻿—and friendly. Also a little shy﻿—even after we had gone up to the ballroom. I’m afraid that made me more talkative than ever; you know how shyness in another man makes me all the more confident and rackety. Be sure that voice of mine rang out! But not in song. There was a piano upstairs, of course, and that led to a little dancing. Different people took turns in playing. I danced﻿—once﻿—with each of the three girls, and twice with my hostess; then I let Ryder and the two young businessmen do the rest. Randolph danced once with Mrs. Phillips, and that ended it for him. My own dancing, as you know, is nothing to brag of: I think the young ladies were quite satisfied with the little I did. I’m sure *I* was. You also know my views on round dances. Why dancing should be done exclusively by couples arranged strictly on the basis of contrasted sexes﻿ ﻿… ! I think of the good old days of the Renaissance in Italy, when women, if they wanted to dance, just got up and danced﻿—alone, or, if they didn’t want to dance alone, danced together. I like to see soldiers or sailors dance in pairs, as a straightforward outlet for superfluous physical energy. Also, peasants in a ring﻿—about a maypole or something. Also, I very much like square dances and reels. There were enough that night for a quadrille, with somebody for the piano and even somebody to ‘call off,’﻿—but whoever sees a quadrille in these days? However, I mustn’t burn any more gas on this topic.
> “I sat out several dances between Mrs. Phillips and Mr. Randolph. He thought he had done enough for her, and she thought I had done enough for them all. And one of the young businessmen did enough for that springy, still-young Mrs. Ryder. Once, indeed, Mrs. Phillips asked me if I wouldn’t like to try a third dance with her (she goes at it with a good deal of old-time vivacity and vim); but I told her she must know by this time that I was something of a bungler. ‘I wouldn’t quite say that,’ she returned, smiling; but we continued to sit there side by side on a sort of bench built against the wall, and she seemed as well pleased to have it that way as the other. She did, however, speak about a little singing. I told her that she must have found me something of a bungler there, too, and reminded her that I couldn’t play the accompaniments of my best songs at all. Arthur, my dear boy, I depend on *you* for that, and you must come down here and do it. No singing, then. But Mrs. Phillips was not quite satisfied. Wouldn’t I recite something? Heavens! Well, of course I know lots of poems﻿—*c’est mon métier*. I repeated one. Then other volunteers were called upon﻿—it was entertaining with a vengeance! The young ladies had to chip in also﻿—though they, of course, were prepared to. And one of the young businessmen did some clever juggling; and Mrs. Ryder sang a little French ballade; and Mr. Randolph﻿—poor man!﻿—was suddenly routed out of his placidity, and responded as well as he could with one or two little stories, not very pointed and not very well told. But I judge he makes no great claim to being a raconteur﻿—he was merely paying an unexpected tax as gracefully as he could.
> “Well, as I was saying, the man in the wheeled chair came in. Of course he hadn’t been down to dinner﻿—I think I saw a tray for him carried along the hall. As he was working his way through the door, I suppose I must have been talking and laughing at my loudest; and that big, bare room, done in hard wood, made me seem noisier still. He sort of stopped and twitched, and appeared to shrink back in his chair: I presume my tones went straight through the poor twisted invalid’s head. He must have fancied me (from the racket I was making) as a sort of free-and-easy Hercules (which is not quite the case), if not as the whole football squad rolled into one. Whether he really saw me, then or thereafter, I don’t know; he wore a sort of green shade over his eyes. Of course I met him in due form. I tried not to give his poor hand too much of a wring (another of my bad habits); but he took all I gave and even seemed to hang on for a little more. He sat quietly to one side for a while, and I tried not to act the bull of Bashan again. Anyhow, he didn’t start a second time. Presently he pulled out rather unceremoniously: the two young businessmen had begun a sort of burlesque fandango, and their feet were pretty noisy on the bare floor. He started off after looking toward the piano and then toward me; and Mrs. Phillips glanced about as if to hint that any display of surprise or of indulgence would be misplaced. Poor chap!﻿—well, I’m glad he didn’t see me dancing.
> “We broke up about eleven, and Mr. Randolph suggested that, as we lived in the same general direction, we might walk homeward together. Great heaven! it’s eleven﻿—and five after﻿—now! Enough, in all conscience, for tonight. You shall have the rest tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
> 「十点半了，不过我想再赶一小会儿。要是写不完，明天再收尾。——伦道夫先生坐在我对面。他频频打量我，我无论说什么他都留神听——无论是对他说的，还是对左右邻座说的，还是对满桌人说的。我不觉得他分外聪明，只觉得他自在、可亲——而且友好。还有几分腼腆——连我们上了楼、进了舞厅之后依旧如此。恐怕这就使我的话比先前更多了；你知道，别的男人一腼腆，我就愈发自信、愈发咋呼。我那副嗓门准是响彻全屋了！不过不是响在歌里。楼上自然有一架钢琴，于是引出了一场小小的舞蹈。不同的人轮流弹奏。我同三位姑娘每人跳了一支——只一支——又同女主人跳了两支；余下的就让赖德和那两位青年商人去效劳吧。伦道夫同菲利普斯太太跳了一支，就此完事。我自己的舞艺，你是知道的，没什么可夸口的：我想几位年轻小姐对我尽的那一点力都已相当满意。我敢说我是满意的。你也知道我对圆舞的见解。为什么跳舞这回事，该专由严格按两性相异搭配起来的一对对人包办……！我想起意大利文艺复兴的那些好日子：那时的女人若想跳舞，起身便跳——独自跳，或者，若不愿独舞，便结伴同跳。我爱看士兵或水手成双起舞，那是过剩体力的一条直截了当的出路。还有，庄户人围成一圈——绕着一根五朔节花柱什么的。还有，我极喜欢方舞和里尔舞。那晚的人数尽够跳一场四对舞，有人弹琴，甚至有人可以『喊拍子』——可如今这年头，谁还见得着一回四对舞呢？不过，这个题目上我可不能再多烧煤气了。
> 「我在菲利普斯太太与伦道夫先生两人之间坐着，歇了好几支舞。他觉得自己为她效的力已经够了，而她觉得我为他们全体效的力已经够了。两位青年商人中的一位，又为那位脚步有弹性、风韵犹然的赖德太太效够了力。确有一回，菲利普斯太太问我，愿不愿意同她再试第三支舞（她跳起来带着不少旧日的活泼与劲头）；可我告诉她，到这会儿她总该如何道了，我多少是个笨拙的生手。『我倒不至于这么说，』她微笑着回道；可我们仍旧并排坐在靠墙砌起的一条长凳上，而她似乎觉得这样也好、那样也罢，同样称心。不过她倒是提起了一桩小小的歌唱。我告诉她，在那上头她想必也发现我多少是个生手，还提醒她，我那些最得意的歌，伴奏我是一点也弹不来的。阿瑟，我亲爱的孩子，这一层我全指望你，你一定得到这儿来替我弹。于是没有唱歌。可菲利普斯太太还不甚满足。我不肯朗诵一点什么吗？天哪！唉，我自然背得出许许多多的诗——*c'est mon métier*，这本是我的行当。我背诵了一首。接着又点将了别的自愿者——这场余兴可真是够味儿！几位年轻小姐也不得不凑上一份——虽说她们自然是有备而来。两位青年商人中的一位耍了几手巧妙的抛接戏法；赖德太太唱了一小支法国歌谣；伦道夫先生——可怜的人！——被突然从他那份恬静里轰了出来，尽力应付着讲了一两个小故事，不见得多有味，也不见得讲得多好。不过我料想他也并不怎么自命为说故事的好手——他不过是尽量漂亮地缴一笔意外的税罢了。
> 「嗯，我方才说到，那个坐轮椅的男人进来了。他自然没有下楼来用正餐——我记得看见过一只托盘沿着走廊给他送去。正当他从门口挪进来的时候，想必我正说到最响、笑到最欢处；而那间硬木镶饰、空空荡荡的大房间，又把我衬得愈发喧闹。他仿佛顿了一顿、抽搐了一下，在椅子里似乎朝后一缩：我猜想，我的声浪径直穿透了那可怜的、周身扭曲的病号的脑袋。他准是把我（凭我闹出的这一片声响）当成了某种散漫不羁的赫拉克勒斯（Hercules）（实情不尽如此），再不然，就是当成了整支橄榄球队拧做一人的好汉。他当时或后来究竟看见过我没有，我不知道；他眼睛上罩着一副绿色的遮光罩。我自然循着礼数与他相见。我尽量不去把他那只可怜的手握得太狠（这也是我的坏毛病之一）；可我给的力气他一概领受，甚至还像是攀着不放，想再多讨一点。他安安静静地在一边坐了一阵，我也尽量不再去当一回巴珊的公牛（《圣经》里那些咆哮的壮牛）。总之，他没有再惊跳第二回。不多时，他相当唐突地径自退了出去：两位青年商人已经开场了一种滑稽模仿的凡丹戈舞，两人的脚在光地板上踩得怪响。他朝钢琴那边望了望，又朝我这边望了望，便动身走了；菲利普斯太太的目光四下里扫了一圈，仿佛在示意：无论惊讶还是纵容的表示，此刻都不合时宜。可怜的家伙！——唉，幸亏他没有看见我跳舞。
> 「我们十一点来钟散了席，伦道夫先生提议，既然我们住的大方向相同，不妨结伴一路走回去。天哪！已经十一点——都过了五分——了！凭良心说，今晚尽够了。其余的，明天奉告。」

## XI Cope Enlivens the Country / 第十一章 科普为乡间生色

===Original===
As they drew near the house they heard the tones of a gramophone. This instrument rested flatly on a small table and took the place of a piano, which would have been a fearful thing to transport from town and back. It was jigging away merrily enough, with a quick, regular rhythm which suggested a dance-tune; and when the party reentered the big room it was seen that a large corner of the center rug was still turned back. Impossible that anybody could have been dancing on the Sabbath; surely everybody understood that the evangelical principles of Churchton were projected on these occasions to the dunes. Besides, the only women left behind had been two in their forties; the men in their company were even older. Medora Phillips looked at Randolph, but he was staring inexpressively at the opposite wall. She found herself wondering if there were times when the mere absence of the young served automatically to make the middle-aged more youthful.

“Well, we’ve had a most lovely walk,” she declared. She crossed to the far corner of the room, contriving to turn down the rug as she went, and opened up a new reservoir of records. She laid them on the table rather emphatically, as if to say, “*These* are suited to the day.”

“I hope you’re all rested up,” she continued, and put one of the new records on the machine. The air was from a modern opera, true; but it was slow-going and had even been fitted out with “sacred” words. Everybody knew it, and presently everybody was humming it.

“It ought not to be hummed,” she declared; “it ought to be sung. You can sing it, Mr. Cope?”

“Oh yes, indeed,” replied Cope, readily enough. “I have the breath left, I think—or I can very soon find it.”

“Take a few minutes. I’ll fill in with something else.”

===Chinese===
他们走近宅子时，便听到了留声机的乐声。这架乐器平放在一张小桌上，权且顶替了钢琴——要把一架钢琴从城里运来又运回去，可是件了不得的麻烦事。它正欢快地奏着，轻快而规整的节奏一听便知是支舞曲；待一行人重新走进大房间，只见中央地毯上还有好大一角仍翻卷着。总不至于真有谁在安息日跳了舞吧；想必人人都明白，每逢这种时节，丘奇顿（Churchton）的那些福音派原则是要投射到沙丘这边来的。况且，留在屋里的女客不过是两位四十上下的人，与她们做伴的男士年纪还要更大些。梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）朝伦道夫（Randolph）望去，他却面无表情地盯着对面的墙壁。她不由得暗自纳闷：有时候，年轻人一离开，是否就自然而然地叫中年人显出几分年轻来。

「好啦，我们这趟散步真是美极了，」她宣称道。她穿过房间走到远处的角落，一路顺势把那幅地毯翻了回去，又打开一批新添置的唱片。她把唱片搁到桌上时颇带着重强调，仿佛在说：「*这些*才配得上今天。」

「诸位都歇息过来了吧，」她接着说，一面把一张新唱片放上了机器。诚然，这曲子出自一部现代歌剧；不过它行进舒缓，甚至还给配上了「神圣」的歌词。人人都听得耳熟，不多时人人便都哼了起来。

「这曲子不该只是哼哼，」她宣称道，「该放声唱出来才是。您能唱吧，科普先生？」

「哦，能，当然能，」科普应答得相当爽快。「我想我还有这口气在——不然也很快就能找回来。」

「先缓上几分钟。我先放点别的垫一垫。」

===Original===
They listened to an inconclusive thing by a wobbling soprano, and then Mrs. Phillips put the other record back.

The accompaniment to the air was rather rich and dense, and the general tone-quality was somewhat blatant. But Cope stood up to it all, and had the inspiration to treat the new combination as a sort of half-joke. But he was relieved from the bother of accompanying himself; his resonance overlaid in some measure the cheap quality of the record’s tone; he contrived to master a degree of momentum to let himself go; and the general result was good—much better than his attempt at that tea. Hortense and Carolyn looked at him with a new respect; and Amy, who had been willing to admire, now admired openly. Cope ended, gave a slight grimace, and sauntered away from the table and the instrument. He knew that he had done rather well.

“Bravo!” loudly cried one of the ladies, who felt that she was under suspicion of having taken a step or two in the dance. And, “Oh, my dear,” said Mrs. Phillips to her, sotto voce, “isn’t he utterly charming!”

Cope wiped his brow. The walk had made him warm, and the singing had made him warmer. One or two of the women were using chance pamphlets as fans (despite Mrs. Phillips’ ill-concealed doubts), and everybody showed a willingness to keep in the draught from the open windows.

===Chinese===
他们先听了一支曲子，由一位音颤不稳的女高音唱得不了了之，随后菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）把另一张唱片换了上去。

这支歌的伴奏相当繁密丰厚，整体音色也略嫌浮嚣。可科普把这些都顶了下来，还灵机一动，把这套新奇的组合当作半桩玩笑来对待。好在他免去了自弹自唱的烦难；他的共鸣多少盖住了唱片音色的廉价质地；他又设法蓄起一股势头，好让自己放开来唱；总体效果是好的——远比他在那次茶会上的尝试出色。霍滕丝（Hortense）和卡罗琳（Carolyn）以刮目相看的敬意望着他；至于艾米（Amy），本来就有心倾慕，如今索性把倾慕之情公开表露了出来。科普唱罢，微微做了个怪相，从桌子和那架乐器旁边信步踱开。他知道自己唱得相当不错。

「好哇！」一位女士高声喝彩，她自觉正因方才在舞步里迈过一两步而受人猜疑。接着，菲利普斯太太压低嗓门对她说：「哦，亲爱的，他岂不是迷人极了！」

科普抹了抹额头。散步已使他发热，唱歌更使他热上加热。有一两位女士顺手拿小册子当扇子扇（尽管菲利普斯太太毫不掩饰她对此的疑虑），人人都乐意守在敞开的窗户送进来的过堂风里。

===Original===
“Is it close here?” asked the hostess anxiously. “The day is almost like summer. If the water is anywhere nearly as warm as the air is. … Let me see; it’s a quarter to four. I have a closetful of bathing suits, all sizes and shapes and several colors, if anybody cares to go in.”

“Don’t!” cried Cope explosively.

She looked at him with interest. “Have you been trying it?”

“I have. On the way along the shore. I assure you, however warm the air may be, the bathing season is over.”

===Chinese===
「这儿有些闷热吧？」女主人焦切地问道。「这天简直跟夏天差不多了。要是水温也能赶得上气温的一半就好。……让我看看；现在是三点四十五分。我有一柜子游泳衣，什么尺寸样式都有，颜色也有好几种——谁要是想下水，尽管去。」

「可别！」科普猛地喊道。

她饶有兴味地望着他。「您已经试过了？」

「试过了。就在沿湖滨来的路上。我向您担保，不管空气有多暖，游泳的季节是过去了。」

===Original===
“Well, I rather thought something had been happening to you. Mr. Randolph, is it as bad as he says?”

“I’ll take his word,” replied Randolph. “And I think all of us had better do the same.”

“We might go down to the beach, anyway,” she said. “Hortense wants to make her color-notes, and the color will be good from now on.”

Several of the party threaded their way down over the sliding sandy path which led through the pines and junipers. Cope was willing to go with the others—on the present understanding. He objected to promiscuous bathing even more strongly than he objected to promiscuous dancing.

===Chinese===
「哦，我方才倒真猜着您是遇上什么事了。伦道夫先生，当真有他说的那么糟吗？」

「我信他的话，」伦道夫答道。「我想我们大家最好也都信。」

「不管怎么说，我们不妨到海滩那边去，」她说。「霍滕丝要记她的色彩笔记，从现在起，那色彩正好经看。」

一行人中有几位沿着那条溜滑的沙径鱼贯而下，小径从松树与杜松间穿过。科普愿意随众人同去——以眼前这一谅解为前提。他反对混杂共浴，比反对混杂共舞还要来得强烈。

===Original===
There were some new cumuli in the east, out above the water, and they began to take the late afternoon sun. Hortense cast about for just the right point of view, with Carolyn to help on “atmosphere” and two young men to be superserviceable over campstool, sketch-block and box of colors. She brought back a few dabs which may have served some future use;—at all events they served as items in a social record.

Cope and Amy, with some of the others, strolled off in the opposite direction. The water remained smooth, and some of the men idly skipped stones. One of them dipped in his hand. “Cold?” he exclaimed; “I should say!”

Amy looked admiringly at Cope, as one who had braved, beyond season, the chill of the great deep, and he tried to reward her with a “thought” or two. He had skipped stones himself between dips, and Randolph had made a reflection which he could now revise and employ.

===Chinese===
东方的水面上方聚起了几团新生的积云，开始承接午后偏晚的阳光。霍滕丝四处寻那个恰到好处的取景点，卡罗琳从旁协助经营「氛围」，还有两位青年男子殷勤过甚地张罗折凳、写生簿和颜料盒。她带回寥寥几笔涂痕，将来或许能派上些用场；——无论如何，它们算是给社交纪事添了几笔材料。

科普和艾米，同另外几个人一道，朝相反的方向信步而去。湖面依旧平滑，几位男士闲来打着水漂。其中一个把手伸进水里蘸了蘸。「冷吗？」他嚷道，「那还用说！」

艾米钦慕地望着科普，把他看作一位不顾时令、慨然一试深渊寒流的人物；他便想用一两条「感想」来酬答她。他自己方才也是在一次次下水之间打的水漂，伦道夫当时发过一通议论，如今正好拿来修改一番为我所用。

===Original===
“See!” he said, as a flat, waveworn piece of slate left the hand of the young businessman and careered over the water; “one, two, three—six, eight—ten, thirteen; and then down, down, after all—down to the bottom. And so we end—every one of us. The great thing is to crowd in all the action we can before the final plunge comes—to go skipping and splashing as hard and long and fast and far as we may!”

A valuable thought, possibly, and elaborated beyond Randolph’s sketchy and casual utterance; but Amy looked uncomfortable and chilled and glanced with little favor at a few other flat stones lying at her feet. “Please don’t. Please change the subject,” she seemed to ask.

She changed it herself. “You sang beautifully,” she said, with some return of warmth—even with some approach to fervor.

===Chinese===
「瞧！」他说；这时一块被波浪磨平的扁石片正脱开那位青年商人的手，在水面上连跳着飞窜。「一、二、三——六、八——十、十三；终究还是沉下去，沉下去——沉到了底。我们的结局也是如此——每一个都一样。要紧的是趁最后一扑没来之前，把能有的动作尽量都挤进去——尽我们所能，跳得又卖力、又长久、又迅疾、又远，一路飞溅着欢跳下去！」

这番感想或许颇有价值，也比伦道夫那番粗略随口的议论铺陈得远甚；可艾米显得又局促又心寒，对躺在自己脚边的另外几块扁石也没了好脸色。「请别说了。请换个话题吧。」她仿佛在这样恳求。

她自己把话题换掉了。「您唱得美极了，」她说，语气里添了几分回暖——甚至近乎热切。

===Original===
“Oh, I can sing,” he returned nonchalantly, “if I can only have my hands in my pockets, or waving in the air, or anywhere but on a keyboard.”

“I wish you had let them persuade you to sing another.” She was not only willing to admire, but desirous: conscientious amends, perhaps, for an earlier verdict. “One or two more skips, you know, after getting started.”

“Oh, once was enough. A happy coincidence. The next might have been an unhappy one.”

“You have never learned to accompany yourself?”

“As you’ve seen, I’m a rather poor hand at it; I’ve depended a good deal on others. Or, better, on another.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我是能唱的，」他漫不经心地答道，「只要我的双手能揣在口袋里，或是挥在半空，或是搁在哪儿都行——就是别搁在键盘上。」

「我但愿您当时依了他们的劝说，再唱上一首。」她不光是有心倾慕，更是心向往之：这或许是为早先下的那句断语，作一番出于良知的弥补。「您知道的，起了头之后，总还可以再多跳上那么一两跳。」

「哦，一回就够了。那是凑巧赶上的好运气。再来一回，说不定就是坏运气了。」

「您从来没学过给自己伴奏吗？」

「您也见到了，我干这个是不大在行的；我一直相当依赖别人。或者不如说，依赖另一个人。」

===Original===
She looked at him earnestly. “Have you ever sung to an obbligato?”

“None of my songs, thus far, has called for one. An obbligato? Never so much honored. No, indeed. Why, to me it would seem almost like singing with an orchestra. Imagine a cello. Imagine a flute—still I’m not a soprano going mad. Or imagine a saxophone; that might be droll.”

He gave out a sort of dragging bleat. She did not smile; perhaps she felt such an approach to waggery unworthy of him. Perhaps she was holding him up to the dignity of the natural scene, and to the importance of the occasion as she conceived it.

Cope had no desire to figure as a *comique*, and at once regained sobriety. “Of course,” he admitted, “we are not at a *thé dansant* or a cabaret. Such things ought not to be thought of—here.”

===Chinese===
她恳切地望着他。「您可曾和着助奏（obbligato）唱过歌？」

「我唱的歌，迄今为止还没有哪一首用得上助奏。助奏？从来没受过这等抬举。没有，真没有。哎呀，在我看来，那简直就近乎跟着一整支乐队唱歌了。想想一把大提琴。想想一支长笛——不过我又不是个发狂的女高音。或者想想一支萨克斯管；那也许倒挺滑稽。」

他发出一声拖沓的羊鸣般的怪调。她没有笑；或许她觉得，他这样向插科打诨凑近，有失他的身份。或许她是要他以眼前自然景致的庄严自持，也以她心中这个场合的重要意义自重。

科普无意扮成滑稽角色（comique），当即恢复了庄重。「当然，」他承认道，「我们又不是在茶舞会（thé dansant）上，也不是在卡巴莱。这类事情本不该想起——在这里尤其不该。」

===Original===
She turned her eyes on him again, with a new look of sympathy and understanding. Perhaps understanding between them had failed or lapsed but a moment before.

“How all of this shames the town!” she said.

“And us—if we misbehave,” he added.

===Chinese===
她又把目光转向他，带着一种新的同情与领会。方才片刻之间，两人间的领会或许曾失灵，或曾中断。

「这一切让城里多么相形见绌！」她说。

「也让我们相形见绌——要是我们行为失检的话，」他补上一句。

===Original===
Mrs. Phillips came scurrying along, collecting her scattered guests, as before. “Tea!” she said. “Tea for one or two who must make an early start back to town. Also a sip and a bite for those who stay.”

She moved along toward Hortense and her little group. Hortense’s “color-notes” did not appear to amount to much. Hortense seemed to have been “fussed”—either by an excess of company and of help, or by some private source of discontent and disequilibrium.

“Come,” Mrs. Phillips cried to her, “I need every Martha to lend a hand.” Hortense rose, and one of her young men picked up her campstool.

“So glad you haven’t got to go early,” said Mrs. Phillips to Randolph and Cope. “In fact, you might stay all night. It will be warm, and there are cots and blankets for the porch.”

===Chinese===
菲利普斯太太又急匆匆地赶了过来，像先前那样把散在各处的宾客收拢起来。「用茶了！」她说。「给一两位必须早些动身回城的人备了茶。给留下的人也备下一口润喉、一口点心。」

她朝霍滕丝和她那一小伙人走去。霍滕丝的「色彩笔记」似乎没成什么气候。霍滕丝像是被闹得「心绪不宁」了——不是被过多的陪伴和帮忙搅的，就是被某桩私底下的不快与失措搅的。

「来吧，」菲利普斯太太朝她喊道，「我需要每一个马大（Martha）都来搭把手。」霍滕丝站起身来，服侍她的那班青年男子中有一个拾起了她的折凳。

「真高兴你们用不着早走，」菲利普斯太太对伦道夫和科普说。「说实在的，你们不妨在这儿过夜。夜里会挺暖和，廊子上现成有帆布床和毯子。」

===Original===
“Thanks, indeed,” said Cope. “But I have a class at eight-fifteen tomorrow morning, and they’ll be waiting to hear about the English Novel in the Eighteenth Century, worse luck! Fielding and Richardson and—”

“Are you going to explain Pamela and Clarissa to them?” asked Hortense. She was abrupt and possibly a bit scornful.

Cope seemed to scent a challenge and accepted it. “I am. The women may figure on the covers, but the men play their own strong part through the pages.”

“I seem to recall,” contributed Mrs. Phillips, “that Sir Charles Grandison figured both ways.”

“That prig!” said Hortense.

===Chinese===
「多谢美意，」科普说。「可是明天早上八点一刻我有课，他们正等着听十八世纪英国小说呢，真倒霉！菲尔丁（Fielding）啊，理查逊（Richardson）啊，还有——」

「您打算给他们讲解《帕梅拉》（Pamela）和《克拉丽莎》（Clarissa）吗？」霍滕丝问道。她这话突兀，或许还带着几分讥诮。

科普像是嗅出了一道战书，便接下来。「我正要讲。女人家尽可以占住封面，可通篇书页里唱重头戏的还是男人。」

「我倒记得，」菲利普斯太太插言道，「《查尔斯·格兰迪森爵士》（Sir Charles Grandison）可是两样都占了。」

「那个道学君子！」霍滕丝说。

===Original===
“Well, if you can’t stay overnight,” Mrs. Phillips proceeded, “at least stay a few hours for the moonlight. The moon will be almost full tonight, and the walk across the marshes to the trolley-line ought to be beautiful. Or Peter could run you across in eight or ten minutes.”

She did not urge Randolph to remain in the absence of Cope, though Randolph’s appearance at his office at ten in the morning would have surprised no one, and have embarrassed no one.

Tea was served before the big fireplace in which a small flame to heat the kettle was rising. Randolph set his empty cup on the shelf above.

===Chinese===
「好吧，既然你们不能过夜，」菲利普斯太太接着说，「那至少再待几个钟头，赏一赏月色。今晚月亮差不多圆满，穿过沼泽地走到电车线的那一路想必很美。再不然，彼得（Peter）也能开车送你们过去，八九分钟就到了。」

她没有劝伦道夫在科普走后单独留下，尽管伦道夫哪怕上午十点钟才在自己办公室露面，也不会叫任何人吃惊，不会叫任何人难堪。

茶就设在那个大壁炉前面，炉膛里一小簇给烧水壶加热的火苗正冉冉升起。伦道夫把自己喝空的茶杯搁到了上方的壁架上。

===Original===
“Notice,” said Mrs. Phillips to him, “that poem of Carolyn’s just behind your cup: ‘Summer Day in Duneland.’ ” It was a bit of verse in a narrow black frame, and the mat was embellished with pen-and-ink drawings of the dunes, to the effect of an etching. An etcher, in fact, a man famous in his field, had made them, Mrs. Phillips explained.

“And at the other end of the shelf,” she advised him, “is a poem in free verse, done by a real journalist who was here in June. See: ‘Homage to Dunecrest’—written with a blue pencil on a bit of driftwood.”

“Sorry *we* can’t leave any souvenir behind,” said Cope, who had stolen up and was looking at the “poem” over Randolph’s shoulder. “But one must (first) be clever; and one must (second) know how to put his cleverness on record.”

“I shall remember *your* record,” she returned with emphasis. Cope smiled deprecatingly; but he felt sure that he had sung well.

===Chinese===
「您留意到没有，」菲利普斯太太对他说，「您杯子后面就是卡罗琳的那首诗：《沙丘地带的夏日》（Summer Day in Duneland）。」那是一首镶在窄条黑框里的小诗，衬纸上以钢笔画点缀出沙丘的景致，做出蚀刻版画的效果。菲利普斯太太解释说，画这些的其实是一位蚀刻画家，在他那一行里是位有名人物。

「还有架子另一头，」她提醒他说，「有一首自由体的诗，出自六月间来过这里的一位真正的报人之手。您看：《献给沙丘之巅的礼赞》（Homage to Dunecrest）——用蓝铅笔写在一块浮木上。」

「可惜*我们*没能留下什么纪念，」科普说；他不知什么时候悄悄凑了过来，正隔着伦道夫的肩头端详那首「诗」。「不过人得（第一）有才思；还得（第二）懂得怎样把自己的才思记录在案。」

「*您*的这份记录我可是会记住的，」她加重语气回敬道。科普谦逊地笑了笑；可他心里确信，自己方才唱得很好。

===Original===
The moonlight, when it came, was all that Medora Phillips had promised. There was another stroll on the beach, with Cope between Medora and Carolyn. Then he and Randolph took the causeway across the marsh, stopped the trolley by burning a newspaper on the track, and started on the long trip home.

As the car ran along jerkily from station to station, the earlier void of Duneland became peopled indeed. The extraordinarily mild day had drawn out hundreds—had given the moribund summer-excursion season a new lease of life. Every stoppage brought so many more young men in soiled khaki, with shapeless packs on their backs, and so many more wan maidens, no longer young, who were trying, in little bands, to capture from Nature the joys thus far denied by domestic life; and at one station a belated squad of the “Lovers of Landscape”—some forty or fifty in all—came flooding in with the day’s spoils: masses of asters and goldenrod, with the roots as often as not; festoons of bittersweet, and sheaves of sumach and golden glow; and one ardent spirit staggered in under the weight of an immense brown paper bag stuffed with prickly pear. As the tight-packed company slid along, children drowsed or whimpered, short-tempered young men quarreled with the conductor, elderly folk sat in squeezed, plaintive resignation. … Soon the lights of foundry fires began to show on the sky; then people started dropping off in the streets of towns enlivened by the glitter of many saloons and an occasional loud glare from the front of a moving-picture theater. …

Through these many miles Randolph and Cope sat silent: there seemed to be a tacit agreement that they need no longer exert themselves to entertain each other. Cope reached home shortly before midnight. By next morning many of the doings of the previous day had quite passed from his mind. Yet a few firm impressions remained. He had had a good swim, if but a brief one, with a companion who had been willing, even if not bold; he had imposed an acceptable nomenclature upon a somewhat anonymous landscape; and, in circumstances slightly absurd, or at least unfavorable, he had done his voice and his method high credit in song. All else went for next to nothing.

===Chinese===
月光来时，正尽如梅多拉·菲利普斯所许诺的那般好。海滩上又散了一回步，科普走在梅多拉和卡罗琳两人中间。随后他和伦道夫取道长堤穿过沼泽，在轨道上点燃一张报纸拦停了电车，踏上了漫长的归程。

电车一站一站颠簸前行，沙丘地带先前的空旷此刻果真挤满了人。这暖得出奇的一天引出了好几百口人——给本已垂死的夏季郊游季节又续了一口气。每一次停靠，都涌上来更多穿脏卡其布的青年男子，背上驮着不成形状的行囊；也涌上来更多面色憔悴的姑娘——已不复年轻的姑娘——她们结成一小伙一小伙，想向大自然去索取家居生活至今不肯给予的欢乐；在其中一站，一支姗姗来迟的「风景爱好者」（Lovers of Landscape）队伍——总共约四五十人——带着一日的战利品蜂拥而入：成抱的紫菀和一枝黄花，多半还带着根；一串串南蛇藤，一束束盐肤木和金光菊；还有一位热心分子，扛着一只塞满仙人掌的大棕纸袋，踉踉跄跄地挤上车来。这一车挤得严严实实的人向前滑行，孩子们昏昏欲睡或呜咽啜泣，性急的青年男子同售票员争吵，上了年纪的人挤坐一处，一副认命哀苦的神气。……不久，铸造炉的火光开始映上天际；随后，人们开始在沿街各镇的街面上陆续下车——那些街市因众多酒馆的灯彩、还有电影院门前偶尔迸出的一片喧嚣强光，而显得热闹生辉。……

这一程许多英里，伦道夫和科普默然对坐：两人之间似乎有个默契，不必再费力气去应酬彼此。午夜前不久，科普到了家。到第二天早晨，前一天许多的行状已从他脑中淡忘净尽。不过仍有几样牢固的印象留了下来。他游了一场痛快的泳——虽然短暂——同游的伙伴也还情愿，纵然说不上大胆；他给一片原本无名可称的风景安上了一套还说得过去的命名；还有，在几分荒唐、至少是不利的境况之下，他以歌声为自己的嗓音与方法挣得了极高的体面。至于其余种种，几乎都算不得什么了。

## XV Cope Entertains Several Ladies / 第十五章 科普招待几位女士

===Original===
Cope’s excuse, involving the expected visit of a relative, may not have been altogether sincere, but it received, within a week or so, the substantial backing of actuality: a relative came. She was an aunt—his father’s sister—and she came at the suggestion of a concerned landlady. This person, made anxious by a languid young man who had begged off from his classes and who was likely to need more attention than her scanty margin of leisure could grant, had even suggested a hospital while yet it was easy for him to reach one. Though Cope meant to leave her soon, it did not suit him to leave her quite as soon as this; and so Aunt Harriet came in from Freeford to look the situation over and to lend a hand if need be. She spent two nights in a vacant chamber at transient rates; was grudgingly allowed to prepare his “slops,” as he called them, in the kitchen; and had time to satisfy herself that, after all, nothing very serious was the matter.

Randolph did not meet this relative, but he heard about her; and her coming, as a sort of family representative, helped him still further in his picture of the *res angusta* of a small-town household: a father held closely to office or warehouse—his own or someone else’s; a sister confined to her schoolroom; a mother who found the demands of the domestic routine too exacting even to allow a three-hour trip to town; and a brother—Randolph added this figure quite gratuitously out of an active imagination and a determined desire not to put any of the circle to the test of a personal encounter—and a brother who was perhaps off somewhere “on the road.”

The one who met Aunt Harriet was Medora Phillips, and the meeting was brief. Medora had heard from Amy Leffingwell of Cope’s absence from his classroom. She herself became concerned; she felt more or less responsible and possibly a bit conscience-stricken. “Next time,” she said, “I shall try to have the ventilation right; and I think that, after this, I shall keep to birch beer.”

===Chinese===
科普（Cope）先前那个托词——说是有位亲戚要来探望——未必全然出于真心，不过一星期上下，这托词竟得到了事实的切实佐证：果真来了位亲戚。是位姑妈——他父亲的姐妹——她是应一位忧心的房东太太之请而来的。这位房东太太被一个恹恹无力的年轻人弄得焦灼不安：他告了课假，往后恐怕需要人照料，远非她那点可怜的闲暇余裕所能应付；她甚至提议，趁他眼下还挪得动、去得起，索性先进医院去。科普虽说打算不久便离开她，可要他走得这么快，也未免不合他的心意；于是哈丽雅特姑妈（Aunt Harriet）从弗里福德（Freeford）赶来，看看情形如何，必要时搭上一把手。她以临时房客的价钱在一间空屋里歇了两夜；好不容易才获准在厨房里为他调制他所谓的「汤汤水水」；也有工夫让自己放了心：毕竟没有什么大不了的毛病。

伦道夫（Randolph）没有见到这位亲戚，只是听说了她；她作为家族的一位代表前来，越发帮他补足了心目中那幅小镇人家 *res angusta*（家计窘迫）的图景：一位父亲被事务所或仓库拴得死死的——不管是他自己的还是别人的；一个妹妹圈在教室里；一位母亲觉得家常事务的种种要求太过严苛，连进一趟城三个钟头也匀不出来；还有一个兄弟——这个人物，是伦道夫凭着一副活跃的想象，外加一股打定主意不肯让这一家子任何一位经受当面结识之考验的执念，全然凭空添上的——这兄弟说不定正在外头什么地方「跑码头」呢。

见到哈丽雅特姑妈的是梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips），会面很简短。梅多拉已从艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）那里听说科普缺课的事。她自己也不免挂起心来；她自觉多少有些责任，说不定还有几分良心不安。「下一回，」她说，「我可得把通风弄妥帖；而且我想，经过这一遭，我就只喝桦树啤酒了。」

===Original===
Medora called up Amy at the music-school, one afternoon, at about four. She assumed that the day’s work was over, told Amy she was “going around” to see Bertram Cope, and asked her to go with her. “You may act as my chaperon,” she said; “for who knows where or how I shall find him?”

As they neared the house a colored man came out, carrying a small trunk to a mud-bespattered surrey. “What! is he going?” said Medora, with a start. “Well, anyway, we’re in time to say goodbye.” Then, “What’s the matter, Jasper?” she asked, having now recognized the driver and his conveyance.

“Got a lady who’s gettin’ away on the four forty-three.”

“Oh!” said Medora, with a gasp of reassurance.

Cope’s aunt said goodbye to him upstairs and was now putting on her gloves in the lower hall, in the company of the landlady. Medora appraised the visitor as a semi-rustic person—one of some substance and standing in her own community; marriage, perhaps, had provided her with means and leisure. She had been willing to subordinate herself to a university town apprehended as a social organism, and she now seemed inclined to accept with docility any observations made by a confident urbanite with a fair degree of verve.

===Chinese===
一天下午四点来钟，梅多拉打电话到音乐学校找艾米。她料想当日的功课已经结束，便告诉艾米说自己要「过去看看」伯特伦·科普，邀她同去。「你就权充我的陪护人吧，」她说，「谁知我会把他找着找不着、又在什么情形下找着呢？」

两人快到那所房子时，一个有色人种男子走了出来，提着一只小提箱，朝一辆溅满泥浆的四轮马车走去。「怎么！他要走了？」梅多拉吃了一惊，随即说：「也好，反正我们还赶得上道个别。」接着，她认出了车夫和那辆车，便问：「怎么回事，贾斯珀（Jasper）？」

「有位太太要赶四点四十三分的火车走。」

「哦！」梅多拉松了一口气，轻轻惊呼一声。

科普的姑妈在楼上同他道了别，此刻正在楼下门厅里，由房东太太作陪，戴着手套。梅多拉把这位来客估量为一个半乡土气的人——在自己乡里也算有些资财和身份；婚姻，大概，给了她殷实与清闲。她一向甘心屈居于这座在她眼中俨然一个社会有机体的大学城之下，此刻似乎也乐于温顺地接受一位满怀自信、颇有几分锐气的都市妇人发表的一切高见。

===Original===
“These young men,” said Medora dashingly, “are too careless and proud.”

“Proud?” asked the other. She felt clearly enough that her nephew had been careless; but pride is not often acknowledged among the members of an ordinary domestic circle.

“They’re all mind,” Medora went on, with no lapse of momentum. She knew she must work in brief, broad effects: the surrey was waiting and the train would not delay. “They sometimes forget that their intellectual efforts must rest, after all, on a good sensible physical basis. They mustn’t scorn the body.”

The departing visitor gave a quick little sigh of relief. The views of this fashionable and forthputting woman were in accord with her own, after all.

“Well, I’ve told Bert,” she said, buttoning her second glove, “that he had better take all his meals in one place and at regular hours. I’ve told him his health is of just as much account as his students and their studies.” She seemed gratified that, on an important point, she had reached unanimity with an influential person who was to remain behind; and she got away without too long delaying the muddy surrey and the ungroomed sorrel.

Medora Phillips looked after her with a grimace. “Think of calling him ‘Bert’!”

===Chinese===
「这些年轻人哪，」梅多拉挥洒地说，「太不当心、太傲气了。」

「傲气？」对方问道。她心里很明白，自己的侄子确实是不当心；可傲气嘛，寻常人家的圈子里是不大肯认账的。

「他们满脑子全是心智，」梅多拉接着说，势头丝毫不减。她知道自己必须速战速决、以简驭繁：四轮马车在等着，火车可不等人。「他们有时候忘了，智力上的劳作归根到底得有一个好好儿的、过得硬的身体打底子。万不可小看这副皮囊。」

临行的客人轻轻松了口气。这位时髦而进取的太太的见解，毕竟与她自己的相合。

「喏，我已经告诉伯特（Bert），」她一边扣上第二只手套一边说，「他顶好把三餐都放在一个地方、按着钟点吃。我告诉过他，他的身子骨跟他的学生和他们的功课一样要紧。」看来她颇为欣慰，在一个要紧问题上，同一位行将留在此地的有影响的人物取得了一致；她随即动身，没让那辆泥污的四轮马车和那匹未及梳理的栗色马耽搁太久。

梅多拉·菲利普斯目送她离去，做了个鬼脸。「亏她叫他『伯特』！」

===Original===
Cope, when advised, came down in a sort of bathrobe which he made do duty as a dressing-gown. He took the stairs in a rapid run, produced an emphatic smile for the parlor threshold, and put a good measure of energy into his handshakes. “Mighty good of you to call,” he said to Mrs. Phillips. “Mighty good of you to call,” he said to Amy Leffingwell.

Well, he was on his feet, then. No chance to feel anxiously the brow of a poor boy in bed, or to ask if the window was right or if he wouldn’t like a sip of water. Life’s little disappointments…!

To Amy Leffingwell he seemed pale, and she felt him as glad to sit down at once in the third and last chair the little room offered. She noticed, too, an inkstain on his right forefinger and judged that the daily grind of theme-correction was going on in spite of everything.

“Did you meet my aunt before she got away?” he asked.

“We did,” said Medora, “and we are going to add our advice to hers.”

“That’s very nice of you,” he rejoined, flattered. “But within a couple of months,” he went on, with a lowered voice and an eye on the parlor door, “I shall be living in a different place and in quite a different way. Until then.…” He shrugged. His shrug was meant to include the scanty, unpretending furnishings of the room, and also the rough casual fare provided by many houses of entertainment out of present sight.

===Chinese===
科普得了通报，便裹着一件浴衣下楼来——他把浴衣权当晨衣使。他一阵风似的奔下楼梯，到客厅门口绽出一个热切的微笑，握手也握得格外用力。「多谢两位光来看我，」他对菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）说。「多谢两位光来看我，」他又对艾米·莱芬韦尔说了一遍。

得，他可是已经站起来了。没机会满怀焦虑地去摸一摸病榻上可怜男孩的额头了，也没机会问一问窗子开得对不对、想不想呷一口水了。人生的小小失意……！

在艾米·莱芬韦尔眼里，他脸色发白；她也觉出他巴不得立刻在这小房间仅有的第三把、也就是最后一把椅子上坐下。她还注意到他右手食指上有一块墨水渍，心想不管怎样，批改作文的每日苦工还在照常进行。

「我姑妈临走之前，你们见着了吗？」他问。

「见着了，」梅多拉说，「我们还打算在她的忠告之外，再添上我们的一份。」

「那真是两位太好心了，」他应道，颇感受用。「不过不出两三个月，」他压低了嗓音继续说，一边留意着客厅的门，「我就会住在一个不同的地方，过一种大不相同的日子了。在那之前嘛……」他耸了耸肩。这一耸肩，意在把这间屋子寒素而不事修饰的陈设都包括进去，也包括眼下看不见的、许多供膳场所供应的粗糙随便的吃食。

===Original===
“I almost feel like taking you in myself,” declared Medora boldly.

“That’s still nicer of you,” he said very promptly and with a reinforcement of his smile. “But I’m on the upgrade, and pretty soon everything will come out as smooth as silk. I shall have ten days at home, for the holidays; then, after that, the new dispensation.”

Amy Leffingwell tempered her look of general commiseration with a slight lapse into relief. There was no compelling reason why she should have commiserated; perhaps it all came from a desire to indulge in an abandonment to gentleness and pity.

“Do you know,” said Cope, with a sort of embarrassed laugh, “I feel as if I were letting myself become the focus of interest. Oughtn’t I to do something to make the talk less personal?”

===Chinese===
「我简直想干脆把你自己收留到我家来，」梅多拉大胆宣称。

「那更是太太您的好心了，」他毫不迟疑地说，笑容又添了几分。「不过我正走着上坡路呢，用不了多久，事事都会顺遂得像丝绸一样溜滑。假期我要回家过上十天；往后呢，就是新格局了。」

艾米·莱芬韦尔那一脸泛泛的怜惜之色，稍稍让位于一丝宽慰。其实并没有什么非怜惜不可的缘由；这多半是出于一种心愿——想纵容自己沉溺于温存与怜恤之中。

「你们知道吗，」科普带着几分赧然的笑说，「我觉得自己简直要变成大家关注的中心了。我是不是该做点什么，让话题别这么围着个人转？」

===Original===
He glanced about the meagre little room. It gave no cue.

“I’m sure Amy and I are satisfied with the present subject,” returned Medora.

But Cope rose, and gathered his bathrobe—or dressing-gown—about him. “Wait a moment. I have some photographs I can show you—several of them came only yesterday. I’ll bring them down.”

As soon as he had disappeared into the hall, Mrs. Phillips gave a slight smile and said quickly:

“For heaven’s sake, Amy, don’t look so concerned, and mournful, and sympathetic! Anybody might think that, instead of your being my chaperon, I was yours!”

===Chinese===
他环顾这间寒碜的小屋。屋里找不出一点由头。

「我和艾米对眼下这个话题，可再满意不过了，」梅多拉回敬道。

可是科普站起身来，把那件浴衣——或者叫晨衣——往身上拢了拢。「稍等片刻。我有几张照片可以给两位看——其中几张昨天才寄到。我这就去拿下来。」

他一消失在门厅里，菲利普斯太太便微微一笑，急忙说道：

「看在老天爷的份上，艾米，别那么一副忧心忡忡、悲悲切切、怜悯不已的样子！人家还当不是你陪着我、倒是我陪着你了呢！」

===Original===
“He doesn’t look at all well,” said Amy defensively.

“He might look better; but we can’t pity a young man too openly. Pity is akin to embarrassment, for the pitied.”

Cope came downstairs the second time at a lesser pace. He carried a sheaf of photographs. Some were large and were regularly mounted; others were but the informal products of snap-shottery.

He drew up his chair nearer to theirs and began to spread his pictures over the gray and brown pattern on his lap.

“You know I was teaching, last year, at Winnebago,” he said. “Here are some pictures of the place. Science Hall,” he began, passing them. “Those fellows on the front steps must be a graduating class.

===Chinese===
「他气色实在不好，」艾米辩解似的说。

「他本可以气色好些；可是对一个年轻人，咱们不能怜悯得太露骨。对被怜悯的人来说，怜悯近乎难堪。」

科普第二回下楼时，脚步放缓了些。他捧着一叠照片。有几张大，端端正正裱在卡纸上；其余的不过是随手抓拍的非正式产物。

他把椅子挪近她们的，开始把照片一张张摊在膝头那条灰棕相间的衣料图案上。

「你们知道，我去年在温尼贝戈（Winnebago）教书，」他说。「这儿有几张那地方的照片。科学馆（Science Hall），」他一边递过去一边开始讲解。「正门台阶上那帮小伙子，准是一个毕业班。

===Original===
“The Cathedral,” he continued. “And I think that, somewhere or other, I have a group-picture of the choir.

“Sisterhood house,” he went on. “Two or three of them standing out in front.”

“Sisterhood?” asked Mrs. Phillips, with interest. “What do they do?”

Cope paused. “What do they do, indeed? Well, for one thing, they decorate the altar—Easter, Harvest home, and so on.”

“That isn’t much. That doesn’t take a house.”

===Chinese===
「大教堂，」他接着说。「我记得在哪儿还有一张唱诗班的合影。

「姐妹会会所（Sisterhood house），」他继续道。「有两三位会员站在门前。」

「姐妹会？」菲利普斯太太饶有兴味地问。「她们是做什么的？」

科普顿了一顿。「她们做什么？可不是嘛。喏，首先一条，她们装点祭坛——复活节啦，收获节啦，等等。」

「这可算不上什么。这也用不着一所房子。」

===Original===
“Well, I suppose they visit, and teach. Sort of neighborhood centre. Headquarters. Most of them, I believe, live at home.”

“Dear me! Is Winnebago large enough to require settlement-work?”

“Don’t drive me so! I suppose they want to tone in with the cathedral as a special institution. ‘Atmosphere,’ you know. Some tracts of our great land are rather drab and vacant, remember. Color, stir—and distinction, you understand.”

“Is Winnebago ritualistic?”

“Not very. While I was there a young ‘priest,’ an offshoot from the cathedral, started up a new parish in one of the industrial outskirts. He was quite earnest and eloquent and put up a fine service; but nobody except his own father and mother went to hear him preach.”

Mrs. Phillips returned to the Sisterhood house.

===Chinese===
「呃，我想她们也走访，也授课。算是街坊的一个中心。一处总部。我相信她们大多数人住在自己家里。」

「哎呀！温尼贝戈也大到需要开办慈善服务社（settlement-work）了吗？」

「别这样逼我呀！我想她们是要同大教堂这座特殊机构协调一致。『气氛』，你们是知道的。别忘了，我们这个泱泱大国的某些地带实在灰暗而空乏。要有色彩，有热闹——还要有气派，你们明白。」

「温尼贝戈的教会崇尚仪式吗？」

「不怎么崇尚。我在那儿的时候，有个年轻的『司铎』，从大教堂分出来，在一片工业区的边上开辟了新教区。他相当诚恳，口才也好，把礼拜办得很像样；可是除了他自己的父母，谁也不去听他讲道。」

菲利普斯太太又回到姐妹会会所这个话题上。

===Original===
“Are they nice girls?” she asked acutely.

“Oh, I guess so. I met two or three of them. Nice girls, yes; just trying to be a little different. Here’s the boathouse, and some of the fellows in their rowing-clothes. Some sailboats too.”

“Can you sail?” asked Amy. She had the cathedral-choir in one hand and now took the boat-club in the other. She studied both pictures intently, for both were small and crowded.

“Why, I have all the theory and some of the practice. Those small inland lakes are tricky, though.”

“Probably no worse than ours,” said Mrs. Phillips. “Do help poor Amy,” she went on. “*Are* you in either of these groups?”

“No. Didn’t I tell you I was trying to get away from the personal? I’m not in any of these pictures.” Amy unconsciously let both half-drop, as if they held no particular interest, after all. And the hand into which the next photograph was put gave it but lukewarm welcome.

===Chinese===
「她们是好姑娘吗？」她话里带话，问得尖尖的。

「哦，我看是吧。我见过其中两三位。是好姑娘，没错；只是想稍微显得与众不同一点。这是船屋，几个小伙子穿着赛艇服。还有几张帆船。」

「你会驾帆船吗？」艾米问。她一手拿着大教堂唱诗班那张，此刻又把船艇俱乐部那张接到了另一只手里。两张照片她都端详得仔仔细细，因为都又小又挤。

「嘿，理论我全有，实践也有一点。不过那些小小的内陆湖诡诈得很。」

「恐怕不见得比我们这儿的更诡诈，」菲利普斯太太说。「快帮帮可怜的艾米吧，」她接着说，「你*究竟*在不在这两拨人里头？」

「不在。我不是说过，我正想法子摆脱个人话题吗？这些照片里都没有我。」艾米不知不觉让两张照片半垂了下去，仿佛它们终究也没什么特别的意趣。而接住下一张照片的那只手，对它也只是不冷不热地一接。

===Original===
Mixed in with these general subjects were several of a more personal nature: groups of twos and threes, and a number of single figures. One face and figure, as Mrs. Phillips presently came to notice, occurred again and again, in various attitudes and costumes. It was a young man of Cope’s own age—or perhaps two or three years older. He was of Cope’s own height, but slightly heavier, with a possible tendency to plumpness. The best of the photographs made him dark, with black, wavy hair; and in some cases (where sunlight did not distort his expression) he indulged a determined sort of smile. He figured once, all by himself, in choir vestments; again, all by himself, in rowing toggery; a third time, still by himself, in a costume whose vague inaccuracy suggested a character in amateur theatricals.

“Who is this?” inquired Mrs. Phillips, with the last of these in hand.

Cope was prompt, but vague.

===Chinese===
混杂在这些泛泛题材中间的，还有几张更带私人性质的：两人一堆、三人一伙的合影，以及若干单身人像。有一张面孔、一副身量，菲利普斯太太不久便注意到，一而再、再而三地出现，姿态服装各不相同。那是一个与科普同龄的青年——或许年长两三岁。他与科普一般高，只是略重些，说不定有发福的趋势。拍得最好的那几张里，他肤色黝黑，一头黑色鬈发；有几张（阳光没有扭曲他表情的）里，他纵着自己摆出一副斩钉截铁式的微笑。他有一回单独入镜，穿的是唱诗班法衣；又有一回，还是独自一人，穿一身赛艇行头；第三回，依旧单枪匹马，那身戏装含混而不讲究，令人想到业余戏剧里的某个角色。

「这是谁？」菲利普斯太太手里捏着最后这一张，问道。

科普答得很快，却含糊其辞。

===Original===
“Oh, that’s a chum of mine, up there. He belongs to a dramatic club. They give *The School for Scandal* and *Caste*, and—well, more modern things. They have to wear all sorts of togs.”

“And here he is again? And here? And here?”—shuffling still another picture into view.

“Yes.”

“He’s fond of costume, isn’t he?”

“Very versatile,” returned Cope, lightly and briefly. “Clothes to correspond.”

Mrs. Phillips began to peer again at the picture of the choir-group. “Isn’t he here too?”

===Chinese===
「哦，那是我那边的一个好朋友。他参加了一个戏剧社。他们演《造谣学校》（*The School for Scandal*）和《门第》（*Caste*），还有——唔，更现代的戏码。各式行头都不得不穿。」

「这儿又是他？这儿？还有这儿？」——她又把一张照片捻到了眼前。

「是。」

「他很爱穿行头，是不是？」

「多才多艺着呢，」科普轻巧而简短地回道。「什么行头都相应齐备。」

菲利普斯太太又眯起眼，细看唱诗班合影那张。「这里面不也有他吗？」

===Original===
“Yes. With the first tenors. There you have him—third from the left, just behind that row of little devils in surplices.”

“You and he sing together?”

“Sometimes—when we *are* together.”

“‘Larboard Watch’ and ‘Suona la Tromba’ and—?”

“Oh, heavens!” said Cope. He threw up his head quite spiritedly. There was now more color in his cheeks, more sparkle in his eyes, more vibration in his voice. Amy looked at him with a vanishing pity and a growing admiration.

“Let us fellows be of our own day and generation,” he added.

===Chinese===
「有。在第一男高音里。喏，那就是他——左起第三个，就在那一排穿白色罩袍的小鬼头后面。」

「你跟他一起唱歌？」

「有时候——当我们*确实*在一处的时候。」

「《左舷值班》（‘Larboard Watch’）啦，《号角吹响》（‘Suona la Tromba’）啦，还有——？」

「哦，老天爷！」科普说。他相当提神地把头往后一扬。此刻他颊上多了血色，眼里多了光彩，嗓音里多了颤动。艾米望着他，怜惜渐消，钦佩渐长。

「让我们这些小伙子守着自己所处的时代和辈分吧，」他补了一句。

===Original===
“Willingly,” said Mrs. Phillips. “But my husband was fond of ‘Larboard Watch’; I heard him sing in it before we were married. Shall I ever hear you sing together?” she asked.

“Possibly. He is coming down here early in January. To look after me.”

“After you?” Mrs. Phillips reviewed the photographs once more. “I imagine you may sometimes have to look after him.”

Cope sobered a little. “Sometimes,” he acknowledged. “We shall look after each other,” he amended. “We are going to live together.”
