“Oh, then, he is coming to *stay*? You’ve been a long time in reaching the point. And why do you say ‘possibly’ when I ask about your singing together? Aren’t you coming to my house ‘together’?”

===Chinese===
「乐意之至，」菲利普斯太太说。「不过我丈夫生前喜欢《左舷值班》；成亲以前，我听过他唱这支歌。我还能有机会听听你们俩一起唱吗？」她问。

「也许吧。他一月初要下我这儿来。来照应我。」

「照应你？」菲利普斯太太把那叠照片又翻检了一遍。「依我想，有时候恐怕倒得你去照应他吧。」

科普的神色沉了沉。「有时候，」他承认道。「我们互相照应，」他改口说。「我们要住到一起了。」

「哦，这么说，他是要来*长住*了？你可花了老半天才说到点子上。再说，我问你们一起唱歌的事，你怎么只答一声『也许』？你们不是要『一起』上我家来吗？」

===Original===
“I withdraw the ‘possibly.’ Probably.”

“And now withdraw the ‘probably.’ Make it ‘certainly.’”

“Certainly.”

“‘Certainly,’—of course.”

“That’s better,” murmured her companion.

Then Mrs. Phillips must know the newcomer’s name, and must have an outline of the proposed plan. And Amy Leffingwell began to look with renewed interest on the counterfeit form and features of the young man who enjoyed Bertram Cope’s friendly regard. And so the moments of “entertainment”—Cope’s in turn—went on.

===Chinese===
「我收回『也许』。多半会。」

「现在把『多半』也收回去。改成『一定』。」

「一定。」

「『一定』——那是自然。」

「这就好多了，」她的同伴低声道。

接着，菲利普斯太太便非要问出这位新来者的姓名不可，非要问出那个拟议中安排的大概不可。艾米·莱芬韦尔则重新带着兴致，端详起这位承蒙伯特伦·科普友谊相待的青年在照片上的形貌容颜。于是这番「款待」的时光——这回轮到科普做东——便这般继续下去。

===Original===
“I’m glad he really appears to like *somebody*,” declared Mrs. Phillips, on the way home; “it makes him seem quite human.” Inwardly, she was resolving to have both the young men to dine at the earliest possible date. It was not always practicable to invite a single young man as often as you wished. Having two to ask simplified the problem considerably.

Cope, flushed and now rather tired, walked upstairs with his photographs, took a perfunctory sip from a medicine-glass, looked at the inkstain on his finger, and sat down at his table. Two or three sheets of a letter were lying on it, and he reread a paragraph or so before dipping his pen.

===Chinese===
「我很高兴，他看上去总算真心喜欢*某个人*了，」回家的路上，菲利普斯太太宣称道；「这让他显得颇有几分人气了。」心里头，她已拿定主意，要尽早把这两位青年一同请来吃饭。只邀一位单身青年，想邀几回邀几回，并不总是行得通的。有两位可请，问题便大大简化了。

科普脸上发红，此刻也已相当疲惫，拿着照片上楼去，从药杯里敷衍地呷了一口，看了看指上的墨水渍，在书桌前坐了下来。桌上摊着两三页信纸，他重读了一两段，才蘸笔挥写。

===Original===
> “You were rather exacting about that weekend excursion. Mr. R. was all right, and a few days of new air and new scenes would have done me a lot of good. Still, I acknowledge your first claim. But remember that I gave up Indian Rock for you, even if you didn’t give up Green Bay for me. I hope the fellow who took you hasn’t got anything further to propose. If he has, I ask for a tip in turn.
> “Naturally it wasn’t the easiest thing in the world to explain to him, and I haven’t seen him since. But I can truly say that a relative *did* come, and that she was needed—or thought she was.”

===Chinese===
> 「那次周末出游的事，你也未免太苛求了。R 先生并没有什么不是，几天新鲜空气、新鲜景致，本会对我大有好处。尽管如此，我承认你的权利在先。可是别忘了，为了你，我连印第安岩（Indian Rock）都放弃了；而你呢，却不肯为了我放弃绿湾（Green Bay）。但愿带你去的那个家伙没有再提什么进一步的主意。若是提了，那我反过来也讨一点内幕消息。
> 「自然，那件事要向他解释清楚，本不是世上顶容易的差事，打那以后我也没再见过他。不过我可以老老实实地说：一位亲戚*确实*来了，而且也确有需要——或者说，人家自以为有需要。」

===Original===
He picked up his pen for a fresh paragraph.

> “The new photos—added to those I had—have come in quite nicely. They have just helped me entertain a couple of callers. Women have abounded in these parts today: Mrs. Peck, scurrying about more than usual; an aunt from home, getting away with her baggage—more than she needed to bring; and then the two who have just gone. It all makes me feel like wanting to take part in a track-meet or a ballgame—though, as I am now, I might not last two minutes at either. The lady who called was Mrs. Phillips. I thought she might as well know that you were coming. Of course you are already invited, good and plenty, to her house. Look in old music-books and see if you can’t find ‘Larboard Watch.’ If it turns out you can get away *before* the holidays, come down and go out with me to Freeford for Christmas. I have had some rather glum hours and miss you more than ever. I have been within arm’s length of one of the University trustees (who can probably place me *now*!)—but I don’t know just how much that can be counted upon for, if for anything. Show yourself—that will help.

“B.”

===Chinese===
他提起笔来，续写新的一段。

> 「新寄到的照片——添在我原有的那些上头——来得正凑巧。方才还帮我招待了两位来客呢。今天这一带女人简直多得成灾：佩克太太（Mrs. Peck），比平日更是忙得团团转；一位家里来的姑妈，带着行李动身了——行李带得比需要的还多；再有就是刚走的那两位。这一切害得我直想上场去赛一场田径、或打一场球——不过照我眼下这副样子，哪一样恐怕也撑不过两分钟。来访的那位太太是菲利普斯太太。我想不妨让她知道知道你要来的事。当然，她家早已把你邀了个十足十。翻翻旧歌本，看能不能找着《左舷值班》。要是假期*之前*就能抽开身，就先到我这边来，然后同我一道回弗里福德过圣诞。我这几日有过几段相当阴郁的时辰，比以往任何时候都更想念你。我曾在大学一位董事的一臂之遥之内（他如今*多半*能给我安插个位置了！）——不过这究竟能指望上多少——若说指望得上什么的话——我可说不准。你自己现身吧——那自有帮助。

「B.」

## XXVII Cope Escapes a Snare / 第二十七章 科普脱身于一个圈套

===Original===
Lemoyne’s first week in his new berth held him rather close, and Cope was able to move about with less need of accounting for his every hour. One of his first concerns was to get over his sitting with Hortense Dunton. His “sitting,” he said: it was to be the first, the only and the last.

He came into her place with a show of confidence, a kind of blustery bonhomie. “I give you an hour from my treadmill,” he declared brightly. “So many books, and such dry ones!”

Hortense, who had been moping, brightened too. “I thought you had forgotten me,” she said chidingly. Yet her tone had less acerbity than that which she had employed, but a few moments before, to address him in his absence. For she often had in mind, at intervals longer or shorter, Cope’s improvisation about the Sassafras﻿—too truly that dense-minded shrub had failed to understand the “young ladies” and their “needs.”

===Chinese===
勒莫因（Lemoyne）到新差事上后的第一个星期被拴得相当紧，科普（Cope）便能四处走动，不必再为每个钟头的去向一一声明。他头几桩挂心的事里，有一桩就是把替霍滕丝·邓顿（Hortense Dunton）坐堂当模特的这一回事了结掉。他的那趟「坐堂」，照他自己说：这将是头一回，也是仅有的一回，更是最后一回。

他来到她的画室，摆出一副自信的姿态，一股咋咋呼呼的和气劲儿。「我从我那架苦役踏车里给你匀出一个钟头来，」他兴冲冲地宣称。「书这么多，又都这么干燥无味！」

一直闷闷不乐的霍滕丝也亮堂了起来。「我还当你把我忘了呢，」她带着责备的口气说。不过比起几分钟前她背地里数落他时所用的那副腔调，眼下的酸味已经淡了几分。因为她总时不时地——间隔或长或短——想起科普那番关于黄樟（sassafras）的即兴发挥：那棵脑袋不开窍的灌木，千真万确没能领会那些「年轻小姐」和她们的「需要」。

===Original===
“My thesis,” he said. “From now on, it must take a lot of my thought and every moment of my spare time.” He looked at the waiting canvas. “Clinch it today. Hurry it through.”

He spoke with a factitious vivacity which almost gave a sense of chill. She looked at him with a shade of dissatisfaction and discomfort.

“What! must it all be done in a drive?” she asked.

“By no means. Watch me relax. Is that my chair? See me drop into complete physical and mental passivity﻿—the kef of the Arabs.”

He mounted the model-throne, sank into the wide chair, and placed his hands luxuriously on its arms. His general pose mattered little: she had not gone beyond his head and shoulders.

===Chinese===
「我的论文，」他说。「从今往后，它要占去我不少心思，还要占去我闲暇的每时每刻。」他望了望那块候着的画布。「今天就把它定下来。赶紧画完。」

他说话时那股做作的欢快劲儿，几乎叫人觉出一丝寒意。她望着他，神色里带着几分不满和不自在。

「什么！这一切难道都得赶着劲儿一气呵成么？」她问。

「绝非如此。且看我放松。那是我的椅子吗？且看我整个人沉入身心两方面的彻底无为——阿拉伯人所谓的『凯夫』（kef）。」

他登上模特宝座，一坐陷进那把宽大的椅子，两只手舒舒服服地搭上扶手。他整体的姿势无关紧要：她的画笔还没有越出他的头和肩膀。

===Original===
Hortense stared. Would he push her on the moment into the right mood? Would he have her call into instant readiness her colors and brushes? Why, even a modest amateur must be allowed her minutes of preparation and approach.

“Passivity?” she repeated, beginning to get under way. “Shall I find you very entertaining in that condition?”

“Entertaining? Me, the sitter? Why, I’ve always heard it was an important part of a portrait-painter’s work to keep the subject interested and amused.”

He smiled in his cold, distant way. The north light cut across the forehead, nose and chin which made his priceless profile. The canvas itself, done on theory in a lesser light, looked dull and lifeless.

Hortense felt this herself. She did not see how she was going to key it up in a single hour. As she considered among her brushes and tubes, she began to feel nervous, and her temper stirred.

===Chinese===
霍滕丝直瞪着他。他这是要她当下就进入状态么？要她把颜料和画笔即刻调集停当么？哎呀，就连一个谦逊的业余爱好者，也总得容她有几分钟的预备和酝酿吧。

「无为？」她重复道，手上慢慢启动起来。「在那种状态里，我还能觉得你很有趣么？」

「有趣？我，坐着的人？哎呀，我一向听说，让被画的人兴致盎然、心情愉悦，本是肖像画家分内要紧的一环呢。」

他带着那种冷淡疏远的劲儿微微一笑。北面的天光横掠过他的额头、鼻梁和下巴——正是这些构成了他无价的侧影。那幅画布本身，是按着次一等光线的路数画成的，看上去灰暗而没有生气。

这一点霍滕丝自己也感到了。她看不出自己怎么能在单独一个钟头里把调子提起来。她在画笔和颜料管之间斟酌权衡，渐渐觉得心里发慌，火气也搅动起来。

===Original===
“You have a great capacity for being interested and amused,” she said. “Most men are like you. Especially young ones. They are amused, diverted, entertained﻿—and there it ends.”

Cope felt the prick. “Well, we are bidden,” he said; “and we come. Too many of us have little to offer in return, except appreciation and goodwill. How better appreciate such kindness as Mrs. Phillips’ than by gratefully accepting more of it?” (Stilted copybook talk; and he knew it.)

“You haven’t been accepting much of it lately,” she returned, feeling the point of a new brush. She spoke with the consciousness of empty evenings that might have been full.

“Hardly,” he replied. And he felt that this one word sufficed.

===Chinese===
「你倒真有一副兴致勃勃、自得其乐的好本事，」她说。「大多数男人都同你一个样。尤其是年轻的。他们被逗乐了，被取悦了，被款待得开开心心——也就到此为止了。」

科普觉出了那根刺。「唉，人家相邀，」他说，「我们就来了。我们中间太多的人拿不出多少回报，只有感激和善意。对菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）这样的好意，除了怀着感激多多领受，还有什么更好的领情方式呢？」（拘板得像习字帖上的套话；这一点他自己心里有数。）

「你近来领受得可不多，」她回敬道，一面试着一支新画笔的笔锋。她开口时，心里正想着那些本可以过得充实、如今却空落落的夜晚。

「是不多，」他答道。他觉得这一个字便尽够了。

===Original===
“Well, the coast will be clear after the twentieth of April.”

“That is the date, then, is it?” The more he thought of the impending ceremony, the more grateful he was for his escape. Thankfulness had salved the earlier wound; no pain now came from his touching it.

“Yes; on that day the house will see the last of them.”

“The wedding, then, will﻿—?”

“Yes. Aunt Medora says, ‘Why go to Iowa?﻿—you’re at home here.’ Why, indeed, drag George away out to Fort Lodge? Let her own people, who are not many, come to us. Aunt will do everything, and do it handsomely.”

She slanted her palette and looked toward the skylight. Cope’s own glance swept noncommittally the green burlap walls. Both of them were seeing pictures of the wedding preparations. Hortense saw delivery-boys at the front door, with things that must be held to the light or draped over chairs. She saw George haling Amy to the furniture-shops and to the dealers in wallpaper. She saw them in cosy shaded confab evening after evening, in her aunt’s library. It was a period of joy, of self-absorption, of unsettlement, of longing, of irritation, of exasperation﻿—oh, would it never end! Cope saw a long string of gifts and entertainments, a diamond engagement-ring, a lavishly-furnished apartment﻿ ﻿… How in the world could he himself have compassed all this? And how blessed was he among men that he had not been obliged to try!

===Chinese===
「嗯，四月二十日一过，这儿就清净了。」

「那么，日子就定在那一天了？」他越想那场迫近的婚礼，就越庆幸自己得以脱身。感激之情早已敷平了先前那处创口；如今再去触碰，已引不出什么疼痛了。

「是的；那一天，这宅子就把他们送走了。」

「那么，婚礼将——？」

「是的。梅多拉姑妈（Aunt Medora）说：『何必去艾奥瓦（Iowa）？——你在这儿就是在自己家里。』可不是嘛，何苦把乔治（George）一路拖到福特洛奇（Fort Lodge）那么远的地方去？她自家人本来也没有几个，让他们到我们这儿来好了。姑妈会包办一切，而且样样办得体面漂亮。」

她斜过调色板，朝天窗望去。科普自己的目光则不置可否地扫过绿麻布的墙面。两人眼前都浮现出婚礼筹备的种种图景。霍滕丝看见的是送货的小伙计一拨拨来到前门，捧着那些必须举到亮处验看、或得搭上椅背披挂的东西。她看见乔治拽着艾米（Amy）跑家具店、跑壁纸行。她看见他们一连多少个晚上，在姑妈的书房里凑着舒适的灯影密密商谈。那是一段欢欣的、全然忘我的、心神不定的、渴盼的、烦躁的、恼火的日子——哦，它就没有个尽头么！科普看见的则是一长串的礼品与款待、一枚订婚钻戒、一套陈设豪奢的公寓……他自己究竟凭什么能耐置办齐这一切呢？而他身在世人之中又是何等有福，竟不曾被迫去尝试！

===Original===
Hortense went through some motions with her brush, yet seemed to be looking beyond him rather than at him.

“There will be a bridal-trip of a week or so,” she concluded; “and they will be in their new home on the first of May.”

“Very good,” said Cope. He thought he was thinking to himself, but he spoke aloud. “And that ends it.” This last he really did say to himself.

He sank more comfortably into his chair, kept his face properly immobile, and spoke no further word. Hortense brought back her gaze to focus and worked on for a little time in silence. The light was good, her palette was full, her brushes were well-chosen, her eyes were intent on his face. It was a handsome face, displayed to the best advantage. She might look as long as she liked, and a long look preceded every stroke.

===Chinese===
霍滕丝执笔做了几个挥洒的动作，目光却像是越过他，而不是落在他身上。

「还有一趟一星期上下的新婚旅行，」她归结道，「五月一日，他们就进新居了。」

「很好，」科普说。他自以为是在心里盘算，嘴上却出了声。「这一来就了结啦。」这后半句，倒真是说给自己听的。

他往椅子里陷得更舒服些，端端正正地让面部保持不动，再没有多说一个字。霍滕丝把目光收拢回来对准焦距，默默画了一小会儿。光线正好，调色板上的颜料满满的，画笔挑得也精当，她的双眼专注地盯住他的脸。那是一张俊美的脸，正展示在最显好处的角度。她爱看多久便可以看多久，而每落一笔之前，都先有一番久久的端详。

===Original===
Presently she paused, opening her eyes wider and holding aloft her brush. “There will be a bride’s-maid,” she said.

“The deuce!” he thought. “That didn’t end it!” But he said nothing aloud.

“Guess who!”

“Why, how should *I*﻿—?”

“Guess!” she cried peremptorily, in a tone of bitter derision. “You won’t? Well, it’s Carolyn﻿—our poor, silly Carolyn! And what do you suppose she has started in to do? She is writing an epitha﻿—an epithal﻿—”

“﻿—﻿—﻿amium,” contributed Cope. “An epithalamium.”

===Chinese===
过了一会儿她停了下来，眼睛睁得更大，画笔高高举着。「会有一位伴娘，」她说。

「见鬼！」他想。「这么一说，那事还没了结！」但他嘴里什么也没有说。

「猜猜是谁！」

「哎呀，*我*怎么会猜得——？」

「猜呀！」她发号施令似的喊道，声调里带着刻毒的讥诮。「不猜？好吧，是卡罗琳（Carolyn）——我们那位可怜的、傻里傻气的卡罗琳！你猜她动手做起什么来了？她在写一首贺婚……贺婚……」

「——诗，」科普替她接上。「一首贺婚诗（epithalamium）。」

===Original===
“Yes, an epithalamium!” repeated Hortense, with an outburst of jarring laughter. “Isn’t she absurd! Isn’t she ridiculous!”

“Is she? Why, it seems to me a delicate attention, a very sweet thought.” If Carolyn could make anything out of Amy﻿—and of George﻿—why, let her do it.

“You *like* her poetry!” cried Hortense in a high, strained voice. “You enjoy her epithalamiums, and her﻿—sonnets.﻿ ﻿…”

Cope flushed and began to grow impatient. “She is a sweet girl,” he said; “and if she wishes to write verse she is quite within her rights.”

===Chinese===
「对，一首贺婚诗！」霍滕丝重复道，爆出一阵刺耳扎人的笑声。「她岂不荒唐！岂不滑稽！」

「是吗？在我看来，这倒是一片细致入微的殷勤，一个很温存的念头。」倘若卡罗琳真能从艾米——还有乔治——身上写出点什么来，那就让她写好了。

「你居然*喜欢*她的诗！」霍滕丝用又高又紧的嗓音喊道。「你欣赏她的贺婚诗，还有她的——十四行诗……」

科普涨红了脸，渐渐不耐烦起来。「她是个甜美的姑娘，」他说，「她若想写诗，那完全是她的自由。」

===Original===
“ ‘Sweet’! There you go again! ‘Sweet’﻿—twice. She ought to know!”

“Perhaps she does know. Everybody else knows.”

“And perhaps she doesn’t!” cried Hortense. “Tell her! Tell her!”

Cope stared. “She is a sweet girl,” he repeated; “and she has been filling very discreetly a somewhat difficult position﻿—”

===Chinese===
「『甜美』！你又来了！『甜美』——都说了两回了。她应该知道！」

「说不定她正知道呢。别的人可都知道。」

「也说不定她不知道！」霍滕丝喊道。「告诉她！告诉她呀！」

科普瞪眼望着她。「她是个甜美的姑娘，」他重复道，「而且她一直非常审慎地填补着一个多少有些为难的位子——」

===Original===
He knew something of the suppressed bitterness which, in subordinate places, was often the lot of the pen. He found himself preferring, just here, “pen” to “typewriter”: he would give Carolyn a touch of idealization﻿—though she had afflicted him with a heavy stroke of embarrassment.

“ ‘Difficult position’?” shrilled Hortense. “With Aunt Medora the very soul of kindness? I like that! Well, if you want to rescue her from her difficult position, do it. If you admire her﻿—and love her﻿—tell her so! *She’ll* be grateful﻿—just read those sonnets over again!”

Hortense dropped her palette and brushes and burst into outrageous tears.

Cope sat bolt upright in that spacious chair. “Tell her? I have nothing to tell her. I have nothing to tell anyone!”

===Chinese===
他多少懂得，身处从属地位的人，「笔杆子」那份压在心底的苦涩常常是什么样的命运。此刻他发觉，自己情愿取「笔杆」而不取「打字机」来指她：他要给卡罗琳涂上一层理想化的光彩——尽管她曾用重重一笔给他涂上过浓浓一层难堪。

「『为难的位子』？」霍滕丝尖声叫道。「有梅多拉姑妈这个仁慈的化身在，还为难什么？亏你说得出口！好啊，你要是想把她从她那为难的位子里搭救出来，那就搭救吧。你要是仰慕她——还爱她——那就告诉她！*她*会感激的——只消把那些十四行诗再读一遍！」

霍滕丝撂下调色板和画笔，放声大哭起来，哭得不管不顾。

科普在那把宽敞的椅子里直挺挺地坐正了。「告诉她？我没有什么可告诉她的。我没有什么可告诉任何人的！」

===Original===
His resonant words cut the air. They uttered decision. He did not mean to make the same mistake twice.

Hortense drew across her eyes an apron redolent of turpentine and stepped toward the throne.

“Nothing? Why this sudden refuge in silence?” she asked, almost truculently, even if tremulously. “You usually find enough words﻿—even though they mean little.”

“I’m afraid I do,” he admitted cautiously.

===Chinese===
他那洪亮的字句划破空气。字字吐露着决断。同一个错处，他不打算再犯第二回。

霍滕丝拉过一条松节油味扑鼻的围裙抹了抹眼睛，朝那座模特宝座走了过来。

「没有什么？怎么忽然躲进沉默里去了？」她问，声音发着颤，口气却近乎咄咄逼人。「你往常有的是话——哪怕那些话没有什么意思。」

「只怕是有的，」他谨慎地承认。

===Original===
“You have nothing to tell anyone? Nothing to tell﻿—me?”

Cope rose. “Nothing to tell anyone,” he repeated. “Nothing.”

“Then let me tell you something.” There was an angry thrill in her voice. “For I am not so selfish and cold-hearted as you are. I have seen nobody but you all these months. I have never tried harder to please anybody. You have scarcely noticed me﻿—you have never given me a glance or a thought. You could interest yourself in that silly Amy and in our foolish Carolyn; but for me﻿—me﻿—Nothing!”

Cope came down from the throne. If she had lavished her maiden thoughts on him, by day or evening or at night, he had not known and could hardly be supposed to know. Indeed, she had begun by treating him with a cursory roughness; nor had he noticed any great softening later on.

===Chinese===
「你没有什么要告诉任何人？没有什么要告诉——我？」

科普站起身来。「没有什么要告诉任何人，」他重复道。「没有。」

「那就让我来告诉你一点什么吧。」她的嗓音里有一道愤怒的震颤。「因为我可不像你这样自私、这样冷心肠。这几个月来，我眼里就没有别人，只有你。我从来没有那么卖力地想讨过谁的喜欢。你却几乎不曾留意过我——你从来没有分给我一个眼色、一个念头。你倒能对那个傻乎乎的艾米、对我们那位犯傻的卡罗琳兴味十足；可对我——对我——什么也没有！」

科普从宝座上走了下来。倘若她果真曾把少女的心思大把大把挥霍在他身上——白日里也好，黄昏也好，深夜也好——他并不知道，也很难指望他能知道。说实在的，她起初待他那副草草的粗鲁劲儿，他也不曾留意到后来有什么大的软化。

===Original===
“Listen,” he said. Under the stress of embarrassment and alarm his cold blue eyes grew colder and his delicate nostrils quivered with an effect a little too like disdain. “I like you as well as another; no more, no less. I am in no position to think of love and marriage, and I have no inclination that way. I am willing to be friends with everybody, and nothing more with anybody.” The sentences came with the cruel detachment of bullets; but, “Not again, not twice,” was his uppermost thought. Any bluntness, any ruggedness, rather than another month like that of the past holiday season.

He took a step away and looked to one side, toward the couch where his hat and coat were lying.

“Go, if you will,” she said. “And go as soon as you like. You are a contemptible, cold-hearted ingrate. You have grudged me every minute of your company, everywhere﻿—and every second you have given me here. If I have been foolish it is over now, and there shall be nothing to record my folly.” She stepped to the easel and hurled the canvas to the floor, where it lay with palette and brushes.

Cope stood with his hat in his hand and his coat over his arm. He seemed to see the open volume of some “printed play.” After all, there was a type which, even under emotional stress, gave a measure of instinctive heed to structure and cadence. Well, if there was relief for her in words, he could stand to hear her speak for a moment or two more, not longer.

===Chinese===
「听着，」他说。在窘迫与警觉的双重压力之下，他那双冷淡的蓝眼睛变得更冷了，秀气的鼻翼翕动着，那神气未免太像轻蔑。「我待你同待旁人一样；不多，也不少。我眼下没有资格去想爱情和婚姻，也丝毫没有往那边去的心思。我愿意同所有人做朋友，也仅止于同任何人做朋友。」这些句子像子弹一样，带着冷酷的无情一句句迸出来；可是，他心里压倒一切的念头是：「不能再来了，不能有第二回。」再怎么生硬、再怎么粗鲁都使得，只别再过上一个月像刚过去的那个假期季节一样的日子。

他挪开一步，眼睛望向一旁那张长沙发，他的帽子和外衣正搁在那儿。

「走哇，你要走就走，」她说。「爱多早走就多早走。你是个可鄙的、冷心肠的忘恩负义之徒。不论在哪儿，你陪我的一分一秒都是勉勉强强掏出来的——还有你在这儿给我的每一秒钟。我若是犯过傻，如今也算完了，往后不会留下任何东西，来记录我的傻。」她跨到画架前，一把将画布掼在地上，它便同调色板和画笔作伴，躺在那儿了。

科普站在那里，帽子拿在手里，外衣搭在臂弯上。他仿佛看见一部「印成的剧本」摊开在眼前。毕竟，世上有这么一类人，即便处在激情的重压之下，也还本能地顾着几分篇章结构与抑扬顿挫。好吧，倘若言辞能让她得到宽解，那么再多听她说上一两分钟，他也还站得住，只是不能再长了。

===Original===
“One word yet,” she said in a panting voice. “Your Arthur Lemoyne. That preposterous friendship cannot go on for long. You will tire of him; or more likely he will tire of you. Something different, something better will be needed﻿—and you will live to learn so. I should be glad if I never saw either one of you again!”

She turned her stormy face away, and Cope slipped out with a blended sense of mortification, pain and relief.

===Chinese===
「还有一句话，」她喘着气说。「你那位阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）。那段荒唐透顶的交情长不了。你会厌倦他；或者更可能，是他厌倦你。到头来会有别的、更好的东西来顶替——你迟早总有一天会明白的。要是从今往后你们两个我一个也不再见到，我才高兴呢！」

她把那张暴雨般阴沉的脸扭了过去，科普溜了出去，心里屈辱、痛苦与如释重负，百味杂陈。

## XIX Cope Finds Himself Committed / 第十九章 科普发觉自己已然许身

===Original===
Amy Leffingwell, having written once, found it easier to write again. And having strolled along the edge of the bluff with Cope on that fateful Sunday, she found it natural to intercept him on other parts of the campus (where their paths might easily cross), or to stroll with him, after casual encounters carefully planned, through sheets of fallen leaves under the wide avenues of elms just outside. Her third note almost summoned him to a rendezvous. It annoyed him; but he might have been more than annoyed had he known of her writing, rather simply, to a rather simple mother in Fort Lodge, Iowa, about her hopes and her expectations. Her mother had, of course, heard in detail of the rescue; and afterward had heard in still greater detail, as the roseate limelight of idealization had come to focus more exactly on the scene. She had had also an unaffected appreciation﻿—or several﻿—of Cope’s personal graces and accomplishments. She had heard, lastly, of Cope’s song to her daughter’s obbligato: a duet *in vacuo*, since Carolyn had been suppressed and the surrounding company had been banished to a remote circumference. What wonder that she began to see her daughter and Bertram Cope in an admirable isolation and to intimate that she hoped, very soon, for definite news?

Well, not a few of us have met an Amy Leffingwell: some plump-faced, pink-cheeked child, with a delicate little concave nose not at all “strong,” and a fine little chin none too vigorously moulded, and a pair of timid candid blue eyes shadowed by a wisp or so of fluffy hair﻿—and have not always taken her for what she was. She “wouldn’t hurt a kitten,” we say; and we assume that her “striking out a line for herself” is the last thing she would try to do. Yet such an unimpressive and disarming façade may mask large chambers of stubbornness and tenacity.

Amy knew how long and hard she had thought of Cope, and she asked for some evidence that he had been thinking long and hard of her. She desired a “response.” But, in fact, he had been thinking of her only when he must. He thought of her whenever he saw himself caught in that flapping sail, and he thought of her whenever he recalled that she had taken it on herself to select his songs. But he did not want her to make out-and-out demands on his time and attention. Still less did he want her to talk about “happiness.” This had come to be her favorite topic, and she discoursed on it profusely: he was almost ungracious enough to say that she did so glibly. “Happiness”﻿—that conventional bliss toward which she was turning her mind as they strolled together on these late November afternoons﻿—was for him a long way ahead. How furnish a house, how clothe and feed a wife?﻿—at least until his thesis should be written and a place, with a real salary, found in the academic world. How, even, buy an engagement ring﻿—that costly superfluity? How even contrive to pay for all the small gifts and attentions which an engagement involved? Yet why ask himself such questions? For he was conscious of a fundamental repugnance to any such scheme of life and was acutely aware that﻿—for awhile, at least, and perhaps for always﻿—he wanted to live in quite a different mode.

===Chinese===
艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）写过一回信，再写就容易了。在那个关系重大的星期日与科普（Cope）沿峭壁边缘散过一回步之后，她便觉得，在校园的其他地方拦截他（两人的路径轻易便可交汇）也是自然而然的事，或者——在精心策划的「不期而遇」之后——与他一同漫步，踏过校园外宽阔榆树大道上层层叠叠的落叶。她的第三张字条几乎是召他赴约了。这使他恼火；不过，倘若他知道她正如何直白地给艾奥瓦州福特洛奇（Fort Lodge）一位同样直率的母亲去信，倾诉她的希望与期许，他恐怕就不止恼火而已了。她母亲当然已经详详细细听说了那次搭救；后来，随着理想化的玫瑰色聚光灯愈来愈精确地聚焦到那一幕上，她听到的细节又更加详尽。对于科普本人的风度与才艺，她也有过由衷的赏识——而且不止一回。最后，她还听说了科普为她女儿的助奏伴唱：一曲*in vacuo*（真空里）的二重唱，因为卡罗琳（Carolyn）已被压制下去，周围的一圈人也已被贬逐到遥远的圆周之外。于是，她开始把女儿和伯特伦·科普看作令人艳羡、独立无双的一对，并暗示自己盼着不久便有确定的消息，这又何足为奇呢？

唉，我们中间不止一人遇见过一位艾米·莱芬韦尔：一个圆脸粉颊的孩子，小巧的凹鼻梁全说不上「坚毅」，秀气的下巴也塑造得不够果决，一双羞怯而坦诚的蓝眼睛，被几缕蓬松的发丝掩映着——而我们未必总能认清她的本来面目。我们说她「连一只小猫都不肯伤害」；我们想当然地以为，「自己闯出一条路来」这种事她是断不会去尝试的。然而，这样一副不起眼、不设防的门面背后，却可能藏着满贮固执与顽韧的深深厅堂。

艾米知道自己思念科普有多久、多切，她便要求得到某种凭据，证明他也在长久而深切地思念着她。她要的是「回应」。可实际上，他只在不得已时才想到她。每当忆起自己被困在那面拍打的船帆里，他便想到了她；每当记起她自作主张替他选定歌曲，他也想到了她。但他不要她对他的时间与注意力提出彻头彻尾的要求。他更不要她谈「幸福」。这已成了她最爱的话题，她滔滔不绝地大谈特谈：他几乎要不客气地说，她谈得未免太流利轻巧了。「幸福」——十一月末这些午后两人并肩漫步时，她的心思正转向的那种约定俗成的至乐——对他来说还远在天边。怎样置办一所宅子？怎样供给一位妻子的衣着饮食？——至少要等到论文写就、在学术界谋得一个有真正薪水的位置之后。甚至，怎样买一枚订婚戒指——那件昂贵的赘物？又怎样付得起订婚所牵涉的一切小礼物与小殷勤？可是，又何必自问这些呢？因为他分明意识到，自己对这样一种人生蓝图怀有根本的抵触，并且敏锐地觉察到——至少在一段时期内，也许竟是永远——他想过的是一种截然不同的生活。

===Original===
Amy’s confident assumptions began to fill the house, to alter its atmosphere. Medora Phillips, who had begun by raising her eyebrows in light criticism, now lowered them in frowning protest. She had found Cope “charming”; but this charm of his was to add to the attractiveness of her house and to give her a high degree of personal gratification. It was not to be frittered away; still less was it to be absorbed elsewhere. Hortense, who had been secretly at work on a portrait-sketch of Cope in oil, and rather despising herself for it, now began to make another bold picture in her own mind. She saw herself handing out the sketch to Cope in person, with an air of high bravado; she might say, if bad came to worse, that she had found some professional interest in his color or in his “planes.” On one occasion Medora hardily requisitioned Cope for an evening at the theatre, in the city; miles in and miles back she had him in her car all to herself; and if Amy, next day, appeared to feel that wealth and organization had taken an unfair advantage of simple, honest love, Medora herself was troubled by no stirrings of conscience.

The new atmosphere reached even Foster on the top floor; and when, one evening in mid-December, he finally carried out his long-meditated plan to dine with Randolph, the household situation was uppermost in his mind. That he had not the clearest understanding of the situation did not diminish his interest in it. Though he sat in the dark, and far apart, some sense all his own, cultivated through years of deprivation, came to his aid. Peter brought him down the street and round the corner; and Randolph’s Chinaman, fascinated by his green shade and his tortuous method of locomotion (once out of his wheeled-chair), did the rest. “You had better stay all night,” Randolph had suggested; and he was glad to avoid a second awkward trip on the same evening.

===Chinese===
艾米那些满有把握的想定，开始充塞整座宅子，改变了它的气氛。梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）起初不过扬起眉毛、轻描淡写地示意不满，如今却蹙眉垂目，公然抗议了。她原觉得科普「魅力迷人」；可他这份魅力，本该为她宅子的吸引力添彩，给她本人以高度的满足。它不该被零星虚耗掉，更不该被别处吸收了去。霍滕丝（Hortense）原本一直在偷偷画一幅科普的油画肖像速写，并且颇为瞧不起自己这样做，如今又开始在心里勾勒另一幅大胆的图画。她设想自己把那幅速写当面递给科普，一派昂然炫示的姿态；倘若情势不妙，她大可以说，她对他的肤色或「块面」产生了几分职业上的兴趣。有一回，梅多拉悍然征用科普进城看了一晚戏；来回数英里的路上，她让他单独待在自己的汽车里；第二天，艾米纵然显得觉得财富与手腕占了质朴真挚的爱情一个不公道的便宜，梅多拉自己却没受半点良心的骚动。

这股新气氛甚至传到了顶楼福斯特（Foster）的身边；十二月中旬的一个晚上，他终于实行了筹划已久的赴伦道夫（Randolph）家晚餐的计划，其时心中念念不忘的正是家中的这一局面。他对局势虽谈不上有最清楚的了解，兴趣却未因此稍减。他虽然坐在暗处、远离众人，一种独属于他的知觉——多年幽闭磨砺出来的——前来为他助阵。彼得（Peter）送他沿街而下、转过街角；余下的一段路，便由伦道夫的中国仆役照应了——那人对他的绿色灯罩和他（一离开轮椅后）迂回曲折的行进方式大为着迷。「你最好在这儿过夜。」伦道夫提议道；他也乐于免去当晚再经历一趟同样狼狈的行程。

===Original===
Foster had wondered whether Cope would be present. He had not asked to meet him﻿—for he hardly knew whether he wished to or not. Though this was an “occasion,”﻿—and his﻿—he had left Randolph to act quite as he might choose. There was a third chair at table and Randolph delayed dinner ten minutes while waiting for it to be filled.

“Well, let’s go in and sit down,” he said presently, with a slight twist of the mouth. He spoke lightly, as if it were as easy for Foster to sit down as for himself. But Foster got into his place after a moment and contrived to spread his napkin over his legs.

“I expected Bertram Cope,” Randolph went on; “but he isn’t here, and I have no word from him and do not know whether﻿—”

He paused, obviously at a loss.

===Chinese===
福斯特原先捉摸不定科普会不会来。他没有要求与科普相见——因为他自己也几乎不知道究竟想不想见。虽说这是一桩「盛会」——而且是他本人的——他却全凭伦道夫自行斟酌安排。餐桌上摆了第三把椅子，伦道夫把晚饭推迟了十分钟，等着那把椅子有人来坐。

「唉，我们进去坐下吧。」过了一会儿他说道，嘴角微微一歪。他说得轻巧，仿佛福斯特坐下这件事，同他自己坐下一样容易。可福斯特还是过了一会儿才就了位，并设法把餐巾铺展在膝上。

「我原以为伯特伦·科普会来的，」伦道夫接着说；「可他没来，也没捎一句话来，我不知道是不是——」

他顿住了，显然一时语塞。

===Original===
“Not here?” repeated Foster. “Is there, then, one place where he is not?”

“Why, Joe﻿—!”

“Our house is full of him!” Foster burst out raucously. He had removed the green *abat-jour*, for the candle-shades (as they sometimes will) were performing their office. In the low but clear light his face seemed distorted.

“He rises to my floor like incense. The very halls and stairways reek with his charms and perfections.”

“Well, you escape him here,” said Randolph ruefully.

“The whole miserable place is steaming with expectation﻿—with the deadly aroma of a courtship going stale. I can’t stand it! I can’t stand it!”

===Chinese===
「没来？」福斯特重复道。「这么说，世上竟还有一个他不曾出现的地方？」

「哎呀，乔——！」

「我们那座宅子里满是他！」福斯特粗声嚷了起来。他已经摘下了那副绿色的*abat-jour*（遮光眼罩），因为几个烛罩（它们有时就是这样）正在履行各自的职责。在低而清亮的光线里，他的脸似乎都变了形。

「他像熏香一样袅袅升上我住的楼层。连门厅和楼梯间都充斥着他的魅力与完美，气味熏天。」

「唉，你在这儿总算躲开他了。」伦道夫怏怏地说。

「整座倒霉的宅子蒸腾着期盼——弥漫着一场求爱由鲜转馊的致命气味。我受不了啦！我受不了啦！」

===Original===
“Courtship?”

“You may think it takes two, but it doesn’t. That foolish girl has thrown the whole place into discomfort and confusion; and I don’t know who’s for or who’s against﻿—”

“What foolish girl?” asked Randolph quickly. Sing-Lo was at his elbow, changing plates: it was assumed, justly enough, that he would not be able to follow the intricacies of a situation purely occidental.

“Our Amy,” replied Foster, with a dash of bitterness.

“Amy Leffingwell?” asked Randolph, still more quickly.

===Chinese===
「求爱？」

「你以为总得两个人才成，其实不然。那个傻丫头把全家搅得鸡犬不宁、乱作一团；我都分不清谁赞成、谁反对——」

「哪个傻丫头？」伦道夫急忙问。辛洛（Sing-Lo）正在他手边换盘子：大家想当然地认为——这倒也公道——他理不清这桩纯属西方人情的局面。

「我们的艾米呗。」福斯特答道，带着几分怨苦。

「艾米·莱芬韦尔？」伦道夫问得愈发急了。

===Original===
Foster had blind eyes, but alert ears. He felt that Randolph was surprised and displeased. And indeed his host was both. That boy fallen maladroitly in love? thought Randolph. It was a second check. He had exerted himself to show a friendliness for Cope, had expected to enjoy him while he stayed on for his months in town, and had hoped to help push his fortunes in whatever other field he might enter. He had even taken his present quarters﻿—no light task, all the details considered﻿—to make Cope’s winter agreeable, no less than his own. And now? First the uncounted-upon friend from Wisconsin with whom Cope was arranging to live; next, this sudden, unexpected affair with that girl at Medora’s. Did the fellow not know his own mind? Could he formulate no hard-and-fast plan? Here Randolph, in his disappointment, inconsistently forgot that a hard-and-fast plan was largely his real annoyance and grievance. Then he remembered. He looked at the vacant place, and tried for composure and justice.

“I shall probably hear some good reason, in due time,” he said.

“I hope so,” rejoined Foster; “but it takes these young fellows to be careless﻿—and ungrateful.” He made no pretense of ignoring the fact that Randolph had moved into this apartment more on account of Cope than for any other reason.

“H’m, yes,” responded Randolph thoughtfully. “I suppose it is the tendency of a young fellow who has never quite stood on his own legs financially to accept about everything that comes his way, and to accept it as a matter of course.”

“It is,” said Foster.

===Chinese===
福斯特双目失明，耳朵却警觉。他觉出伦道夫又惊又不快。他的主人也确实两者兼有。那小子竟笨手笨脚地堕入了情网？伦道夫想。这已是第二遭挫折了。他曾尽力对科普表示友好，指望在他客居城里那几个月里好好地享受与他相交，还希望无论他日后投身哪个行当，都能助他一臂之力、推进他的前程。他甚至特意置办了眼下这处寓所——把种种细节考虑在内，绝非轻省的事——为的是让科普这个冬天过得惬意，不亚于他自己。可如今呢？先是那位从威斯康星来的、全然不曾料及的朋友，科普竟安排要与他同住；接着，又是同梅多拉家里那个姑娘突起的一段出人意料的纠葛。这家伙究竟有没有主意？他就拿不出一条斩钉截铁的既定方针么？失望之余，伦道夫在这里自相矛盾地忘了：一条斩钉截铁的既定方针，恰恰大半正是他真正恼怒与埋怨的所在。随后他想起来了。他望着那个空位，努力求取镇定与公允。

「到时候我大概总会听到一个充分的理由的。」他说。

「但愿如此，」福斯特应道；「可这些小伙子就是改不掉漫不经心——外加忘恩负义。」伦道夫搬进这套公寓，与其说是为了别的缘故，不如说更多是为了科普；对这一层，福斯特并不佯装不知。

「唔，是啊，」伦道夫若有所思地答道。「我想，一个在经济上从未真正自立过的年轻人，难免什么好处碰上来都照单全收，而且收得理所当然。」

「正是。」福斯特说。

===Original===
“I know that *I* was that way,” continued Randolph, looking studiously at the nearest candle-shade. “I was beyond the middle twenties before I quite launched out for myself, and any kindness received was taken without much question and without much thanks. I presume that he still has some assistance from home.﻿ ﻿…”

He dropped youthful insouciance over favors received to consider the change that marriage makes in a young man’s status. “I wouldn’t go so far as to assert that a young man married is a man that’s marred﻿—”

“This *is* stiff doctrine,” Foster acknowledged.

“But somehow he does seem done for. He is placed; he is cut off from wide ranges of interesting possibilities; he offers himself less invitingly to the roving imagination.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我知道*我*当年就是那样，」伦道夫接着说，一面定睛细看离他最近的那个烛罩。「我过了二十五六岁才真正自立门户，人家给我的种种好处，我受了也不多问，也不怎么道谢。我料想他如今还在靠家里接济。……」

他把青年人对受惠的漫不经心搁在一旁，转而思量婚姻在一个青年男子身份上引起的变化。「我不至于把话说得那么绝，断言小伙子一结婚就完结——」

「这*确乎*是条生硬的教条。」福斯特承认道。

「可不知怎么，他看上去的确像是完了。他有了定局；他与一大片饶有趣味的种种可能隔绝了；他向四处游走的想象力所呈现的身姿，也不再那么诱人了。……」

===Original===
Meanwhile Cope, with Randolph’s invitation driven altogether from his mind by more urgent matters, was pacing the streets, through the first snow-flurries of the winter, and was wondering, rather distractedly, just where he stood. Precisely what words, at a very brief yet critical juncture, had he said, or not said? Exactly how had he phrased﻿—or failed to phrase﻿—the syllables which constituted, perhaps, a turning-point in his life?

Amy Leffingwell had demanded his attendance for one more walk, that afternoon, and he had not been dextrous enough, face to face with her, to refuse. She had expressed herself still more insistently on “happiness”﻿—(on hers, his, theirs; the two were one, in her view)﻿—and on a future shared together. In just what inadequate way had he tried to fend her off? Had he said, “I shall have to wait?” Or had his blundering tongue said, instead, “We should have to wait?”﻿—or even worse, “We shall have to wait?” In any event, he had used that cowardly, temporizing word “wait”﻿—for she had instantly seized upon it. Why, yes, indeed; she was willing to wait; she had expected to wait.﻿ ﻿…

He turned out from an avenue lighted with electric globes, past which the snowflakes were drifting, and entered a quieter and darker side-street. In the dusk she had put up her face, expecting to be kissed; and he, partly out of pity for the expression that came when he hesitated, and partly out of pure embarrassment and inexpertness, had lightly touched her lips. That had sealed it, possibly. He saw her sitting in rapt fancy in her bedroom﻿—if not more vocal in the rooms below. He saw her writing to an unseen mother in a tone of joyful complacency, and looking at her finger for a ring which he could not place there. He saw the distaste of his own home circle, to which this event had come at least a year too soon. He saw the amazement, and worse, of Arthur Lemoyne, whose plans for coming to town were now all made and to whom this turn would prove a psychological shock which might deter him from coming at all. But, most of all, he saw﻿—and felt to the depths of his being﻿—his own essential repugnance to the life toward which he now seemed headed. What an outlook for Christmas! What an unpleasant surprise for his parents! What opportunity in Amy Leffingwell’s holiday vacation at Fort Lodge to reinforce the written page by the spoken word! Still forgetful of his engagement with Randolph, he continued to walk the streets. He turned in at midnight, hoping he might sleep, and trusting that morning would throw a less sinister light on his misadventure.

Long before this, Joseph Foster had been put to bed, by Sing-Lo, in this spare room. It was Foster’s crutch, rather than a knightly sword, which leaned against the doorjamb; and it was Foster’s crooked members, rather than the straight young limbs of Cope, which first found place among the sheets and blankets of that shining new brass bedstead.

===Chinese===
与此同时，科普——伦道夫的邀约已被更要紧的事从脑海里整个儿驱走——正在街头踱步，穿行于今冬最初的雪霰之间，心慌意乱地捉摸着自己究竟站在什么地步。在那极短暂而又极紧要的关口，他到底说了、或者没说哪些字句？那些兴许构成他一生转折点的音节，他究竟是怎么措辞的——又或者，是怎么没能说出口的？

那天下午，艾米·莱芬韦尔又要求他再陪她散一次步，而当面相对，他不够机敏，拒绝不得。她把「幸福」——（她的、他的、他们的；在她看来，两人本是一体）——以及共有的未来，表白得愈发急切了。他究竟是以怎样不够周全的方式试图搪塞她的？他说的是「我只好等等」吗？还是他那笨拙的舌头说的竟是「我们该等一等」？——或者更糟，是「我们将不得不等」？无论怎样，他用上了那个怯懦的、敷衍搪塞的「等」字——因为她立刻把它牢牢抓住了。可不是么，真的；她愿意等；她本来就料定了要等。……

他从一条点着电球路灯的大街拐了出来——雪花正从那一盏盏灯旁飘过——走进一条更安静、更昏暗的小街。暮色里，她曾仰起脸来，等着被吻；而他，一半出于怜悯——他一迟疑，她脸上便浮起那副神情——一半出于纯粹的窘迫与笨拙，轻轻触碰了一下她的嘴唇。事情也许就这一下定了局。他仿佛看见她坐在自己卧室里痴想联翩——若不是在楼下几间屋子里说得更响的话。他仿佛看见她正以满心得意的口吻给那位未曾谋面的母亲写信，一边望着自己的手指，盼一枚他无力戴上去的戒指。他仿佛看见自己家中一圈人的反感——这件事对他们来得至少早了一年。他仿佛看见阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）的惊愕，以及比惊愕更甚的情绪——勒莫因进城来的种种安排如今已全部就绪，这个变故对他将是一记心理上的打击，说不定会叫他索性不来了。而最要紧的是，他看见——并且痛彻肺腑地感觉到——自己对于眼下似乎正走向的那种生活怀有的根本反感。这可怎么回家过圣诞节！这对他的父母将是何等的不快之惊！艾米·莱芬韦尔假期回福特洛奇，又将是怎样的一个机会，好用口头的言语来加强纸上的篇章啊！他仍旧把与伦道夫的约会忘得一干二净，继续在街头走着。午夜时分他才回寓就寝，巴望能睡得着，并且指望到了早晨，这场飞来横祸会显得不那么凶多吉少。

早在这一切之前的许久，辛洛已经把约瑟夫·福斯特（Joseph Foster）安顿在这间客房里睡下了。倚在门柱上的，是福斯特的拐杖，而非骑士的佩剑；最先躺进那张锃亮的新铜床的床单与毯子之间的，是福斯特弯曲的肢体，而非科普挺直的年轻四肢。

## XXXI Cope Gets New Light on His Chum / 第三十一章 科普对密友有了新的认识

===Original===
Cope had the luck to get back to Churchton with little further in the way of homage. He was careful with Carolyn; she had perhaps addressed him in a sonnet, and she might go on and address him in an ode. He thought he had done nothing to deserve the one, and he would do almost anything to escape the other. She was a nice pleasant quiet girl; but nice pleasant quiet girls were beginning to do such equivocal things in poetical print!

Having returned to town by a method that put the minimum tax on his powers, Cope was in shape, next day, for an hour on the faculty tennis-courts. He played with no special skill or vigor, but he made a pleasing picture in his flannels; and Carolyn, who happened to pass﻿—who passed by at about five in the afternoon, lingered for the spectacle and thought of two or three lines to start a poem with.

Cope, unconscious of this, presently turned his attention to Lemoyne, who was on the eve of his first dress rehearsal and who was a good deal occupied with wigs and lingerie. Here one detail leads to another, and anyone who goes in wholeheartedly may go in dreadfully deep. Their room came to be strown with all the disconcerting items of a theatrical wardrobe. Cope soon reached the point where he was not quite sure that he liked it all, and he began to develop a distaste for Lemoyne’s preoccupation with it. He came home one afternoon to find on the corner of his desk a long pair of silk stockings and a too dainty pair of ladies’ shoes. “Oh, Art!” he protested. And then﻿—not speaking his essential thought﻿—“Aren’t these pretty expensive?”

===Chinese===
科普还算走运，回到丘奇顿（Churchton）之后，再没有领受多少进一步的敬意。他对卡罗琳（Carolyn）处处留心：她也许已经在十四行诗里向他致过意了，说不定还会再接再厉，在一首颂歌里向他致意。他自问不曾做过任何当得起前者的营生，而后一种嘛，他几乎什么都肯干，只为躲开它。她是个温柔可亲、娴静宜人的好姑娘；可是这种温柔可亲、娴静宜人的好姑娘，如今竟也开始在诗的铅字间干出这般暧昧的勾当来了！

由于回城的路子把对体力的耗损减到了最低，第二天科普便有精神到教工网球场上去打一个钟头的球。他打得并不见怎样精湛或卖力，可是身穿法兰绒球装的他自成一幅悦目的图画；而卡罗琳恰巧打那里经过——下午五点钟光景打那里经过——便为这道风景驻足流连，想出了两三行诗句，可以拿来起一首诗的头。

科普对此浑然不觉，转眼又把心思放到了勒莫因（Lemoyne）身上。此公正处在首次彩排的前夜，为假发和女式内衣忙得不亦乐乎。这种事一处细节牵着另一处，谁若是全身心投入进去，便可能陷得深不可拔。他们的房间里渐渐摊满了一副行头中种种叫人不知所措的物件。科普很快便到了这样一个地步：他说不准自己是否还喜欢这一切了，并且开始对勒莫因的这份专注生出反感。一天下午他回到家里，发现书桌一角放着一双长筒丝袜，还有一双过分精巧的女鞋。「哎呀，阿特！」他抗议道。随即——心里真正想的那句并没有说出口——「这些东西岂不是相当破费么？」

===Original===
“The thing has got to be done right,” returned Lemoyne. “Feet are about the first thing they notice.”

At the actual performance Lemoyne’s feet were noticed, certainly; though perhaps not more than his head. His wig, as is usually the case with dark people, was of a sunny blond hue. Its curls, as palpably artificial as they were voluminous, made his eyes look darker and somehow more liquid than ever. The contrast was piquant, almost sensational. Of course he had sacrificed, for the time, his small moustache. Lemoyne was not “Annabella” herself, but only her chief chum; yet shorter skirts and shorter sleeves and a deliberately assumed feminine air helped distinguish him from the hearty young lads who manoeuvred in the chorus.

Just who are those who enjoy the epicene on the stage? Not many women, one prefers to think; and surely it arouses the impatience, if not worse, of many men. Most amateur drama is based, perhaps, on the attempted “escape”: one likes to bolt from his own day, his own usual costume, his own range of ideas, and even from his own sex. Endeavors toward this last are most enjoyable﻿—or least offensive﻿—when they show frank and patent inadequacy. It was Arthur Lemoyne’s fortune﻿—or misfortune﻿—to do his work all too well.

===Chinese===
「这事非得办得妥帖不可，」勒莫因答道，「脚差不多是他们头一件注意的东西。」

正式演出的时候，勒莫因的那双脚固然被人注意到了；不过惹眼的也许还不如说是他的脑袋。他的假发——头发乌黑的人通常如此——是一顶明媚的金黄色。那一鬈鬈假发，假得露骨，也蓬得可观，衬得他的眼睛比往日更显乌黑，也更显水汪汪的。这番对照够味极了，几乎算得上耸动视听。当然，他一时也牺牲了自己那一撇小胡子。勒莫因并不是《安娜贝拉》本人，不过是她的头号密友；然而短了一截的裙子、短了一截的袖子，加上一副刻意端出来的女儿姿态，都有助于把他同合唱队里那些生龙活虎的棒小伙子们区分开来。

到底是些什么人，会欣赏舞台上的雌雄莫辨呢？人们宁可相信，没有几个女人；而它无疑会激起许多男人的不耐烦，倘若不是更糟的话。大多数业余演剧，其根基恐怕就在那场企图的「出逃」上：人人都乐意逃离自己的时代、自己惯常的衣着、自己的思想范围，甚至逃离自己的性别。而朝着这最后一项所作的努力，唯有在显出坦白而一望而知的力不从心时，才最叫人受用——或者说最不冒犯人。阿瑟·勒莫因（Arthur Lemoyne）的造化——或者说劫数——恰是把他的活儿干得太好了。

===Original===
Mrs. Phillips found his performance as little to her taste as she had anticipated. Carolyn Thorpe got as much enjoyment out of the gauche carriage and rough voices of the “chorus girls” as she had expected, but was not observed to warm toward “Annabella’s” closest friend. The Pearsons, back from their wedding trip, had seats near the big crimson velvet curtain. Pearson himself openly luxuriated in the amusing ineptitude of two or three beskirted acquaintances among the upper classmen, but frowned at Lemoyne’s light tenor tones and mincing ways. Of course the right sort of fellow, even if he had to sing his solo in the lightest of light tenors, would still, on lapsing into dialogue, reinstate himself apologetically by using as rough and gruff a voice as he could summon. Not so Lemoyne: he was doing a consistent piece of “characterization,” and he was feminine, even overfeminine, throughout.

“I never liked him, anyway,” said George to Amy.

Amy gave a nod of agreement. Yet why this critical zeal? There was but one man to like, after all.

“That makeup! That low-cut gown!” said George, in further condemnation. “There’s such a thing as going too far.”

===Chinese===
菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）发觉，他的表演果然同她预料的一样不合自己的口味。卡罗琳·索普（Carolyn Thorpe）从「合唱队姑娘们」的笨拙身段和粗砺嗓音里，得到了她所预期的那么多乐趣，可是谁也不见她对《安娜贝拉》的那位至交热络起来。皮尔逊（Pearson）一家度完蜜月归来，座位紧挨着那幅深红色天鹅绒大幕。皮尔逊本人公然纵情受用两三位裙装上场的高年级旧识那逗笑的笨拙，可是对勒莫因轻细的男高音和忸怩作态却直皱眉头。当然啦，正经人家的好青年，哪怕不得不以最轻飘飘的男高音唱罢独唱，一转入对白，也总会使出他所能召唤出的最粗粝沙哑的嗓音，抱歉似的把自己挽回过来。勒莫因不然：他做的是一件首尾贯通的「人物塑造」，自始至终都是女儿态，甚至是过了头的女儿态。

「反正我从来就不喜欢他，」乔治（George）对艾米（Amy）说。

艾米点头表示赞同。可这份挑剔的热忱却是从何说起呢？说到底，可喜欢的男人只有一个。

「那副妆容！那件袒胸露背的裙子！」乔治进一步声讨道，「凡事总有个『太过分』一说。」

===Original===
Basil Randolph met Cope in the back lobby at the close of the performance. The dramatic season in the city itself had begun to languish; besides that, Randolph, in order to maintain his place on the edge of the life academical, always made it a point to remember the Grayfriars each spring.

“A very thorough, consistent piece of work﻿—your friend’s,” said Randolph. He spoke in a firm, net, withholding tone, looking Cope full in the face, meanwhile. What he said was little, perhaps, of what was in his mind; yet Cope caught a note of criticism and of condemnation.

“Yes,” he almost felt constrained to say in reply, “yes, I know what you did for him﻿—for me, rather; and possibly this is not the outcome foreseen. I hope you won’t regret your aid.”

Randolph went past him placidly. He seemed to have little to regret. On the contrary, he almost appeared to be pleased. He may have felt that Lemoyne had shown himself in a tolerably clear light, and that it was for Cope, should he choose, to take heed.

===Chinese===
散场之际，巴兹尔·伦道夫（Basil Randolph）在后厅里遇见了科普。城里本部的戏剧季节已开始萎顿；除此以外，伦道夫为了保住自己在学院生活边缘上的一席之地，每年春天总不忘照拂一下「灰衣修士」。

「一件十分周到、首尾贯通的作品——你那位朋友的。」伦道夫说。他说话的声调坚定、干脆、有所保留，一面正颜直视科普的脸。他说出口的，比起心里的，也许只是一小半；然而科普还是听出了批评与谴责的调子。

「是，」他几乎觉得不能不这样回答，「是，我知道您为他出过什么力——不如说，是为我；或许这并不是当初预见的结果。我希望您不至于懊悔您的援手。」

伦道夫心平气和地从他身旁走了过去。他似乎并没有多少可懊悔的。恰恰相反，他几乎显得颇为快意。他也许觉得，勒莫因已经把自己展示得相当清楚了，至于留心与否，全凭科普自己愿不愿意。

===Original===
Two days later, Randolph gave his impression of the performance to Foster. “It’s just what I should have expected,” declared the cripple acrimoniously. “I’m glad you never had any taste for the fellow; and I should have been quite as well pleased if I hadn’t found you caring for the other.”

Randolph took refuge in a bland inexpressiveness. There was no need to school his face: he had only to discipline his voice.

“Oh, well,” he said smoothly, “it’s only a passing *amitié*﻿—something soon to be over, perhaps.” He used an alien word because he could not select, on the instant, from his stock of English, the word he needed, and because he was not quite sure what idea he wanted to express. “I only wish,” he went on, in the same even tone, “that this chap had been doing better by his work. At one early stage of the rehearsals there was a lot of registration and fee-paying for the new term. Well, if he hasn’t been satisfactory, they needn’t blame me. Let them blame the system that diverts so much time and attention to interests quite outside the regular curriculum.”

===Chinese===
两天以后，伦道夫向福斯特（Foster）说出了他对这场演出的观感。「这正不出我所料，」那位残疾青年刻毒地宣称。「我很高兴你从来没对那家伙有过一丝兴致；至于另一位，我若是不曾发现你对他那般上心，本也会一样高兴的。」

伦道夫遁入了一副温吞吞的毫无表情里。他的脸无须着意管束：他只消管住嗓音便够了。

「哦，这个嘛，」他圆滑地说，「不过是一段一时的 *amitié*（交情）——兴许很快就到头了。」他用了一个外国字眼，一是因为一时之间，他无法从自己的英语库存里拣出所需的那个词，二是因为他自己也不大拿得准想表达的是什么意思。「我只希望，」他以同样平稳的声调接着说，「这个小伙子当初肯把功课对付得像样些。排演初期，正有一大堆新学期的注册缴费。唉，他若是叫人不满意，他们也不必怪我。要怪，就怪那个制度吧——它把那么多的时间和心思，都引到正经课程以外的事情上去了。」

===Original===
“You talk like a book!” said Foster, with blunt disdain.

“Language﻿—” began Randolph.

“﻿—was made to conceal thought,” completed the other. “Stop talking. Stop thinking. Or, if you must think, just get your thoughts back on your business.”

===Chinese===
「你说话跟背书似的！」福斯特带着直露的轻蔑说。

「语言嘛——」伦道夫起了个头。

「——本来就是造出来遮掩思想的，」另一位替他续完了。「别说话了。别想了。或者，你非想不可的话，就把心思收回到你自己的正经事上去。」

===Original===
Foster might have expressed himself still more pungently if he had been aware, as Cope was, of an episode which took place, behind the scenes, at the close of the performance. Lemoyne’s singing and dancing in the last act had had a marked success: after all, people had come to enjoy and to applaud. Following two or three recalls, a large sheaf of roses had been passed over the footlights; for a close imitation of professional procedure was held to give the advantage of strict vraisemblance. This “tribute” Lemoyne took in character, with certain graces, pirouettes and smiles. His success so mounted to his head (for he was the one person in the case who approximated a professional effect) that after he had retired he could not quiet down and leave his part. He continued to act offstage; and in his general state of ebulliency he endeavored to bestow a measure of upwelling femininity upon another performer who was in the dress of his own sex. This downright fellow, in cutaway and silk hat, did not understand﻿—or at least had no patience with a role carried too far. He brusquely cleared himself of Lemoyne’s arm with a good vigorous push. This effort not only propelled Lemoyne against some scenery and left him, despite the voluminous blond wig, with a bruise on his forehead; it immediately pushed him out of his part, and it ended by pushing him out of the organization and even out of the University.

===Chinese===
倘若福斯特知道——像科普知道的那样——散场之际在幕后发生的一段插曲，他的话只怕还会说得更加尖刻。勒莫因在最后一幕里的且歌且舞大获成功：毕竟，人们来看戏，原是为了取乐和鼓掌的。两三次谢幕之后，一大捧玫瑰从台口递了上来；因为惟妙惟肖地仿照职业戏班的程式行事，被认为有着严格 *vraisemblance*（逼真）的好处。这份「敬意」，勒莫因以剧中人的身份受了，还添上几分别致的姿态、脚尖旋转和盈盈笑靥。成功如此冲上他的头顶（在这桩事里，他是唯一接近职业水准的人），以致退场之后他竟平静不下来，走不出自己的角色。他继续在台下演着；在那一派沸腾的心境里，他竭力想把满腔涌起的女儿态分赠一些给另一位穿本性别服装的演员。这位直性子的汉子，燕尾服、丝礼帽，全然不解其意——至少，对一个演过了头的角色毫无耐心。他结结实实猛力一推，把勒莫因的胳膊甩脱了。这一推不但把勒莫因搡到布景上，撇开那顶蓬厚的金色假发不谈，还在他额头上留下一块青痕；这一推立时把他推出了角色，末了更把他推出了那个社团，甚至推出了这所大学。

===Original===
“Keep off, will you!” said the young élégant crudely.

Lemoyne’s “atmosphere” dissipated suddenly. His art-structure collapsed. As he looked about he saw plainly that the other man’s act was approved. He had carried things too far. Well, such are the risks run by the sincere, self-revealing artist.

When all this reached Cope, he felt a personal chagrin. Truly, the art of human intercourse was an art that called for some care. Lemoyne’s slight wound left no trace after forty-eight hours﻿—perhaps his “notices” in “The Index” and “The Campus” had acted as a salve; but certain sections of opinion remained unfriendly, and there was arising a new atmosphere of distaste and disapproval.

===Chinese===
「躲远点，行不行！」那位年轻的时髦客粗鲁地说。

勒莫因的「氛围」霎时消散了。他的艺术大厦坍塌了。他环顾四周，分明看出众人赞许的是那另一位的行为。他把事情做得太过头了。唉，真诚的、自我袒露的艺术家，冒的正是这样的风险。

这一切传到科普耳中时，他感到一阵切身的懊恼。真的，与人交接这门艺术，确是一门需要用心讲究的艺术。勒莫因那点轻伤，四十八个钟头过后便没了痕迹——也许是《索引报》和《校园报》（The Campus）上关于他的那些「剧评」起了膏药的作用；可是舆论的某些角落依旧存着敌意，而且一种新的反感与不以为然的气氛正在升起。

===Original===
The college authorities had not been satisfied, for some time, with his clerical labors, and some of them thought that his stage performance﻿—an “exhibition” one of them termed it﻿—called for reproof, or more. They laid their heads together and Lemoyne and Cope were not long in learning their decision. Lemoyne was pronounced a useless element in one field, a discrepant element in another, a detriment in both. His essentially slight connection with the real life of the University came to be more fully recognized. Alma Mater, in fine, could do without him, and meant to. Censure was the lot of the indignant boys who officered the society, and who asked Lemoyne to withdraw; and complete scission from the nourishing vine of Knowledge was his final fate.

No occupation; no source of income. Winnebago was cold; nor was it to be warmed into ardor by press-notices. It had seen too many already and was tired of them.

The two young men conferred. Again Basil Randolph was their hope.

===Chinese===
院方当局对于他的文书劳务，一段时间以来早已心怀不满，其中几位还认为，他这场舞台演出——有一位名之为「一场展览」——理应申斥，或更重的处置。他们聚首商议，勒莫因和科普不久便得知了他们的决定。勒莫因被断为：在这一领域是无用的分子，在另一领域是扞格难入的分子，在两处皆是有害的分子。他同大学真实生活之间那份本质上的浅薄关联，得到了更充分的体认。总而言之，母校没有他也过得去，并且打定了主意就这么办。那些义愤填膺、执掌社团的青年学生对他施以申诫，并请勒莫因退社；而同知识那株滋养人的葡萄藤彻底断绝，便是他最后的下场。

没有职业；没有进项。温尼贝戈（Winnebago）是冷淡的；几则报端捧场也烘不热它的热忱。这类东西它见得太多，早已腻烦了。

两个年轻人商量了一番。他们的希望又一次落在巴兹尔·伦道夫身上。

===Original===
“He ought to be able to do something for me in the city,” said Lemoyne. “He’s acquainted in business circles, isn’t he?”

Cope bent over him﻿—paler, thinner, more solicitous. “I’ll try it,” he said.

Cope once more approached Randolph, but Randolph shook his head. He had no faith in Lemoyne, and he had done enough already against his own interests and desires.

Lemoyne fluttered about to little effect for a few weeks, while Cope was finishing up his thesis. Beyond an accustomed and desired companionship, Lemoyne contributed nothing﻿—was a drag, in truth. He returned to Winnebago a fortnight before the convocation and the conferring of degrees; and it was the understanding that, somehow, he and Cope should share together a summer divided between Winnebago and Freeford. Randolph was left to claim Cope’s interest, if he could.

===Chinese===
「他在城里总该能替我谋个什么事吧，」勒莫因说，「他在商界有些熟人，不是么？」

科普朝他俯下身去——更苍白，更清瘦，也更殷切。「我去试试，」他说。

科普又一次去求伦道夫，可伦道夫摇了摇头。他对勒莫因本无信心，况且违背自身利益与心愿的事，他做的已经够多了。

其后有几个星期，勒莫因四处扑腾，收效甚微，而科普正在收尾他的论文。除了一份习以为常、彼此情愿的陪伴之外，勒莫因别无所补——说实话，是个累赘。毕业典礼暨学位颁授之前两周，他回了温尼贝戈；两人的默契是：这个夏天由温尼贝戈与弗里福德（Freeford）对半分账，他同科普一道过。至于伦道夫，若是他有本事，便任他去争取科普的情分吧。

## XVI Cope Goes A-Sailing / 第十六章 科普扬帆出游

===Original===

Cope was himself in a few days. He set aside his aunt’s counsel in regard to a better regimen, as well as her more specific hints, made in view of the near approach of rough weather, that he provide himself with rubbers and an umbrella, even if he would not hear of a raincoat. “Am I made of money?” he asked. He gave a like treatment to some intimations contributed by Medora Phillips during her call: he met them with the smiling, polite, half-weary patience which a man sometimes employs to inform a woman that she doesn’t quite know what she is talking about. He was presently in as active circulation, on the campus and elsewhere, as ever. The few who looked after him at all came to the view that he possessed more mettle than stamina. He had no special fondness for athletics; he was doing little to keep﻿—still less to increase﻿—a young man’s natural endowment of strength and vigor. Occasional tennis on the faculty courts, and not much else.

So the vast gymnasium went for little with him, and the wide football field for less, and the great lake, close by, for nothing. This last, however, counted for little more with anyone else. Those who knew the lake best were best content to leave it alone. As a source of pleasure it had too many perils: “treacherous” was the common word. Its treachery was reserved, of course, for the smiling period of summer; especially did the great monster lie in wait on summer’s Sunday afternoons. Then the sun would shine on its vast placid bosom and the breeze play gently, tempting the swimmer toward its borders and the light pleasure craft toward its depths. And then, in mid-afternoon, a sudden disastrous change; a quick gale from the north, with a wide whipping-up of white caps; and the morrow’s newspapers told of bathers drowned in the undertow, of frail canoes dashed to pieces against piers and breakwaters, and of gay, beflagged steam-launches swamped by the newly-risen sea miles from shore: the toll of fickle, superheated August. But in the late autumn the immense, savage creature was more frankly itself: rude, blustery, tyrannical﻿—no more a smiling, cruel hypocrite. It warned you, often and openly, if warning you would take.

It was on the last Sunday afternoon in October that Cope and Amy Leffingwell were strolling along its edge. They had met casually, in front of the chapel, after a lecture﻿—or a service﻿—by an eminent ethical teacher from abroad﻿—a bird of passage who must pipe on this Sunday afternoon if he were to pipe at all. Cope, who had lain abed late, made this address a substitute for the forenoon service he had missed. And Amy Leffingwell had gone out somewhat for the sake, perhaps, of walking by the house where Cope lived.

===Chinese===

几天之后，科普便恢复了原样。姑妈劝他对起居养生多加讲究；眼看恶劣天气将临，她还更进一步地暗示：就算雨衣他连听都不要听，好歹也给自己置办一双胶鞋、一把雨伞——他把这些统统撇在了一边。「我是钱做的吗？」他问。对于梅多拉·菲利普斯来访时贡献的几点含蓄提点，他也作了同样的处置：他以含笑的、客气的、半带倦怠的耐心来应对——男人有时便用这种态度告诉一个女人：她所谈的事，她自己也不大明白。不久之后，他在校园里、在别处，便照旧活跃地往来周旋，一似往常。少数当真留意他的人得出这样的看法：他锐气有余而耐力不足。他对体育并无特别的爱好；他也很少做什么来保持——更谈不上增益——一个年轻男子天赋的气力与元气。偶尔在教员球场上打打网球，此外便没有什么了。

于是，那座巨大的体育馆于他没有多大用处，宽阔的橄榄球场用处更小，而近在咫尺的大湖则毫无用处。不过这最后一样，在旁人身上也强不了多少。最了解这湖的人，最乐于由它去。作为取乐之源，它凶险太多：「阴险」是人们的通用字眼。它的阴险，当然，是专门留给夏天那满面堆笑的时节的；尤其是在夏天的星期日下午，这头巨兽伏着伺机而动。那时节，太阳照在它辽阔平静的胸襟上，微风轻轻吹拂，引诱泳客游向它的边缘，引诱轻快的游船驶向它的深处。随后，到了下午三点来钟，突来一场灾变；北面疾风乍起，白帽浪大片大片地掀起来；第二天的报纸便报道：泳者溺于回流之中，单薄的独木舟在突堤和防波堤上撞得粉碎，张灯结彩、喜气洋洋的小汽艇在离岸数英里新起的浪涛里没顶沉没：这便是反复无常、酷热过度的八月所收的贡赋。可是到了深秋，这庞大凶蛮的家伙倒更坦白地显出本相：粗鲁、狂暴、专横——不再是一个满面堆笑、心肠冷酷的伪君子。它常常公开向你发出警告，只要你肯领受。

正是在十月最后一个星期日的下午，科普和艾米·莱芬韦尔沿着湖滨信步而行。他们是在礼拜堂前偶然相遇的，刚听完一位来自国外的知名伦理教师的一场演讲——或者叫布道——此君是只候鸟，若是还要开口，就非得在这个星期日下午开口不可。科普睡了个晚觉，便拿这场讲演来顶替他错过的上午礼拜。至于艾米·莱芬韦尔嘛，她之所以出门，多少或许是存心要打科普住的那所房子旁边走一遭。

===Original===

They passed the Science building, with its tower crowned by an ornamental openwork iron pyramid for wireless, and the segregated group of theological dormitories through whose windows earnest ringing young voices were sometimes heard at the practice of sermon-delivery, and the men’s club where the billiard tables were doubtless decorously covered with their customary Sunday sheets of black oilcloth, and took intuitively the path which led along the edge of the bluff. Beyond them, further bluffs and a few low headlands; here a lighthouse, there a water-tower; elsewhere (and not so far) the balconied roof of the lifesaving station, where the boats, light and heavy, were manned by muscular students: their vigilance and activity, interspersed with long periods of leisure or of absence, helped them to “pay their way.” Out toward the horizon a passenger steamer en route to some port farther north, or a long ore-freighter, singularly uneventful between bow and far-distant afterhouse, on its way down from the iron-ranges of Superior.

The path was narrow, but Cope, unexpectedly to himself, had no complaint to make. Really, the girl did better here, somehow, than lots of other girls would have done on a wide sidewalk. Most of them walked too close to you, or too far from you, altering the interval suddenly and arbitrarily, and tending to bump against you when you didn’t expect it and didn’t want it. They were uncertain at crossings; if it was necessary for them to take your arm, as it sometimes became, in the evening, on a crowded street, why, they were too gingerly or else pressed too close; and if it happened to rain, you sometimes had to take a cab, trafficking with a driver whose tariff and whose disposition you did not know: in fact, a string of minor embarrassments and expenses.﻿ ﻿…

But the way, this afternoon, was clear and easy; and there were no annoyances save from other walkers along the same path. The sun shone brightly at intervals. A fresh breeze swept the wide expanse streaked with purple and green and turned an occasional broken wave-crest toward the western light. Some large cumuli were abroad﻿—white, or less white, or even darkling﻿—the first windy sky of autumn.

===Chinese===

他们走过科学馆——馆塔顶上冠着一座镂花的铁架塔，作无线电之用——又走过自成一区的几栋神学宿舍，时而可以听见窗内年轻热切、清朗响亮的嗓音在练习讲道；再走过男士俱乐部，那里的弹子台想必照例蒙上了星期日惯用的黑色油布，收拾得规规矩矩；然后凭着直觉，踏上了那条沿断崖边缘而去的小径。小径之外，是更远处的断崖和几处低矮的岬角；这边一座灯塔，那边一座水塔；在别处（也不算远）是救生站那带晒台的屋顶——站里大大小小的船只都由筋骨结实的学生们操持：他们那份警觉与勤勉，间杂着长长的闲暇或缺勤，帮着他们「挣出了自己的用度」。朝地平线那边望去，或是一艘驶往更北面某港的客轮，或是一条长长的矿砂船——从苏必利尔湖的铁矿区顺流南下，船头到遥远的船尾楼之间，一派异样的波澜不惊。

小径很窄，可是连科普自己也没料到，他竟毫无怨言。说真的，这姑娘在这里走得，不知怎么，竟比许多别的姑娘在宽阔的人行道上走得还强。她们大多跟你贴得太近，或者离你太远，间隔忽而任意地骤然一变，又往往在你意想不到、也不乐意的时候朝你撞过来。过街口时她们拿不定主意；倘若必要她们挽你的手臂——晚间在拥挤的街上，有时难免如此——唉，她们不是过分小心翼翼，就是贴得过紧；倘若赶巧落雨，你有时还不得不雇一辆汽车，同一位你既不知他的价目、也不知他的脾气的司机打交道：总之，是一连串细碎的尴尬与花销。……

不过今天下午，路上明朗好走；除了同一条小径上别的行人之外，再无烦扰。太阳时时明亮地照着。一阵清风扫过那片曳着紫色与绿色条痕的辽阔水面，不时把一道破碎的浪峰掀向西边的天光。天上散着几大团积云——白的，不太白的，甚至已经发暗的——这是秋天第一幅多风的天空。

===Original===

Cope and Amy passed the lifesaving station, where a few people sat about idly and where one or two visitors pressed noses against glass panes to view the boats within; and they reached presently a sort of little public park which lay along the water. Here a small pier ran out past the shallows, and in front of a shack close by it a man sat resignedly near a group of beached and upturned rowboats. One or two others were still in the water, as was a small sloop. The fellow sat there without expectations: the season was about over; the day was none too promising for such as knew. His attitude expressed, in fact, the accumulated disappointment and resignation of many months. Perhaps he was a newcomer from the interior﻿—some region of ponds and rivers﻿—and had kept through an uneventful summer the notion that so big a spread of water would surely be put to use. The sail of the sloop, half-lowered, flapped in the breeze, and little else stirred.

Our young people overlooked both man and boat.

“It’s the same lake,” said Amy Leffingwell, rather dreamily, after a common silence of several minutes.

===Chinese===

科普和艾米走过救生站，几个人懒散地坐在附近，一两位游客把鼻子抵在玻璃窗上，观看里头的船只；不久他们便到了一处临水的小小公园。这里有一座小栈桥伸出浅滩之外，紧挨着栈桥的一间棚屋前，一个男人听天由命地坐在一小群拖上岸来、底朝天的划艇旁边。另外一两条船还泡在水里，一条小单桅帆船也是。那家伙坐在那里不抱指望：季节快要完了；在懂行的人看来，这天气也不见得有多吉利。他的姿态，其实道尽了积攒了好几个月的失望与认命。说不定他是从内地——某个池塘与河流之乡——新来的，在波澜不兴的一整个夏天里，一直抱着一个念头：这么大一片摊开的水面，总会有人来派用场。单桅帆船的帆降下一半，在风里噼啪抖动，此外几乎没有别的什么东西动一动。

我们这一对年轻人，对那个人、那条船都没有理会。

「还是这同一个湖啊，」两人一同沉默了几分钟之后，艾米·莱芬韦尔颇为出神地说。

===Original===

“The same,” returned Cope promptly. “It’s just what it was a year ago, a century ago; and a millennium ago, I suppose﻿—if there was anyone here to notice.”

She turned on him a rueful, half-protesting smile. “I wasn’t thinking of a century ago. I was thinking of a month ago.”

“A month ago?”

“Yes; when we were walking along the dunes.”

“Oh, I see. Why, yes, it is the same old lake, though it seems hard to realize it. Foreground makes so much difference; and so does﻿—well, population. I mean the human element, or the absence of it.”

===Chinese===

「是同一个，」科普应声而答。「一年前它是这个样子，一个世纪前也是；我想一千年前也是如此——倘若那时这里有谁看得见的话。」

她回身朝他绽出一丝怅然、半带抗议的微笑。「我想的不是一个世纪前。我想的是一个月前。」

「一个月前？」

「是啊；那时候咱们正沿着沙丘散步来着。」

「哦，我明白了。哎呀，可不是，还是那个老湖，尽管叫人不容易意识到这一点。前景造成的差别太大了；还有——嗯——人口。我是指人的因素，或者说，人的不在场。」

===Original===

Amy pondered.

“The one drawback, there, was that we couldn’t go out on the water.”

“Go out? I should say not. No pier for miles, and the water so shallow that hardly more than a canoe could land. Still, those fishermen out there manage it. But plain summerites, especially if not dressed for it, would have an unpleasant time imitating them.”

Amy cast her eye about. Here was a shore, a pier, a boat, a man to let it.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

艾米沉吟起来。

「在那边，唯一的缺憾，就是咱们没法到水上去。」

「到水上去？我看不行。方圆几英里没有一座栈桥，水又浅得除了独木舟几乎什么也靠不了岸。不过，外头那些渔民自有办法。可寻常的避暑客，尤其是没穿对衣裳的，要学他们的样，可就要吃一番苦头了。」

艾米四下里打量。这里有岸，有栈桥，有船，还有一个出租船的人。……

===Original===

“Would you like to go out?” asked the man himself perfunctorily, as from the depths of a settled despair. He pointed a thumb over his shoulder toward the sloop.

The two young people looked at each other. Neither looked at the sky. “Well, I don’t know,” replied Cope slowly. The sloop was on a pretty small scale; still, it was more to manage than a catboat.

“You have the theory, you know,” said Amy demurely, “and some practice.”

===Chinese===

「想出去一趟吗？」那人自己开口问，例行公事似的，仿佛出自一腔定型的绝望。他朝身后竖起拇指，指着那条单桅帆船。

两个年轻人对望了一眼。谁也没有望一望天。「唔，我说不上来，」科普慢吞吞地答。这条单桅帆船规模相当小；不过比起一条猫式帆船，它要操心的地方更多。

「你有理论，这你是知道的，」艾米端庄地说，「还有些实践。」

===Original===

Cope looked at her in doubt. “Can you swim?” he asked.

“Yes,” she returned. “I have some practice, if not much theory.”

“Could you handle a jib?”

“Under direction.”

“Well, then, if you really wish﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

科普将信将疑地望着她。「你会游泳吗？」他问。

「会，」她答道。「我有些实践，尽管理论不多。」

「你能对付一面前帆吗？」

「有人指点的话，能。」

「那么，好吧，要是你真愿意的话……」

===Original===

The misanthrope, with a twisted smile, helped them get away. The mainsail took a steady set; but the jib, from the first, possessed an active life of its own.

“Not that rope,” cried Cope; “the other.”

“Very well,” returned Amy, scrambling across the cockpit. And so it went.

===Chinese===

那个愤世者咧嘴一笑，笑得有点歪，帮他们解缆出发。主帆稳稳地吃住了风；可那面前帆，打从一开始就自有一副活蹦乱跳的性子。

「不是那根绳，」科普喊道；「是另一根。」

「好的，」艾米答道，手脚并用地爬过座舱。事情就这样进行下去。

===Original===

In six or eight minutes their small catastrophe overtook them. There came a sudden flaw from out one of the racing gray cumuli, and a faint cry or two from the distant shore. Theory had not put itself into practice as quickly as the emergency required﻿—all the less so in that it had to work through a crew encumbered with a longish skirt and a close jacket. The sloop keeled over; Cope was instantly entangled with the mainsail and some miscellaneous cordage; and Amy, with the water soaking her closely-fitting garments, found herself clutching the cockpit’s edge.

She saw Cope’s predicament and let go her hold to set him free. He helped shake himself loose with a loud forced laugh and a toss of the head to get his long hair out of his eyes. “We’ll leave the wreck,” he spluttered, “and make for the shore.” The shore, fortunately, was scarcely more than a hundred yards away﻿—yet never had the great twin towers of the library seemed so distant or the wireless cage on Science hall so futile.

They swam, easily, side by side, he supporting her in her cramped clothes at the start, and she, a bit concerned, somewhat supporting him toward the end. Meanwhile, there was some stir at the lifesaving station, a quarter of a mile down the shore.

===Chinese===

六到八分钟之后，他们那场小小的灾祸便追上了他们。一阵疾风陡地从天边奔驰的一团灰色积云里窜出，远处岸上传来一两声微弱的喊叫。理论还没有像危急情形所要求的那样快地化作实践——更何况这理论还必须透过一名被长裙和紧身短上衣拖累的船员来运转。单桅帆船倾侧过去；科普霎时缠进了主帆和一堆杂七杂八的索具里；艾米呢，紧身衣裳浸饱了水，发觉自己正死死抓住座舱的边沿。

她看见科普的窘境，便松开手好让他脱身。他放声挤出一阵大笑，又把头一甩，把长头发从眼睛里甩出去，帮着自己挣脱开来。「咱们弃船，」他噗噗地吐着水沫说，「往岸上去。」那岸，幸好，不过一百码开外——然而图书馆那两座巍峨的双塔从来不曾显得这么遥远，科学馆顶上那座无线电铁笼也从来不曾显得这么无用。

他们并肩从容地游着，起初是他搀着衣裳累赘的她，到后来，倒是有点挂虑的她，多少搀住了他。与此同时，下游四分之一英里的救生站那边起了一阵骚动。

===Original===

The last hundred feet meant mere wading, though there was some variability among the sand ridges of the bottom; but the water, at its deepest, never reached their shoulders. Their small accident now began to take on the character of a ceremonial﻿—an immersion incident to some religious rite or observance; and the little Sunday crowd collecting on the water’s edge might have been members of some congregation sympathetically welcoming a pair of converts to the faith.

“Let’s hold our heads high and walk straight,” said Cope, his arm in hers; “heaven knows whom we are likely to meet. And throw your hat away﻿—you’ll look better without it. Lord knows where mine is,” he added, as he ran a smoothing hand over his long locks.

“Very well,” she said, casting away her ruined, ridiculous headgear with her free arm. The other, in his, was giving more support to him, she felt, than he was giving to her.

===Chinese===

最后一百英尺不过是蹚水，虽说湖底的沙脊高高低低；但水最深的地方也从不曾没到他们的肩头。他们这场小小的意外，此刻开始带上了典礼的意味——仿佛某种宗教仪式或仪文中应有的一场浸洗；水边渐渐聚拢的一小群星期日看客，俨然是哪个教会的会众，正满怀着同情，迎接一对皈依的新人。

「咱们昂起头、笔直地走，」科普说道，手臂挽着她的手臂；「天知道咱们会撞见什么人。把你的帽子扔了吧——不戴它你反倒好看。我的帽子飞哪儿去了，只有天知道，」他补上一句，一面用手抹过自己长长的发绺，把它捋平。

「好吧，」她说，用空着的那条胳膊扔掉了那顶泡毁了、可笑的帽子。她觉得，挽在他臂弯里的另一条胳膊，给他的扶持，倒比他给她的更多。

===Original===

Just as they were about to reach dry land, amidst the congratulations and the amused smiles of the little group at the foot of the bluff, the belated crew of lifesavers swept up in their smallest boat and insisted on capturing them.

“Oh, Mr. Cope,” said a familiar voice, “please let us save you. We haven’t saved a soul for months.”

Cope recognized one of his own students and surrendered, though a kindly house-owner on the bluff had been quick to cry across the intervening yards of water his offer of hospitality. “All right,” he said; “take us back to your place, where we can dry and telephone.” He hoped, too, that they might have to encounter fewer people at the other spot than at this.

===Chinese===

正当他们将要踏上干地的时候，在断崖脚下那一小群人的道贺和含笑的注视当中，姗姗来迟的救生队员们驾着他们最小的船疾扫而至，非要把他们两个「俘获」到手不可。

「哎呀，科普先生，」一个熟悉的嗓音说，「请让我们救救你吧。我们好几个月没救过一条性命了。」

科普认出这是他自己的一名学生，便投降了，尽管断崖上一位好心的宅第主人早已隔着中间那几码宽的水面，高声喊出了留他们款待的美意。「好吧，」他说；「把我们带回你们那儿去，好烤干衣裳、打个电话。」他还指望，在那一处要照面的人能比这一处少一些。

===Original===

Meanwhile, another boat belonging to the station had set out to aid the owner of the sloop in its recovery. It was soon righted and was brought in. There was no damage done, and there was no charge that Cope could not meet, as he learned next day to his great relief.

The station gave him a dry outfit of clothes, assembled from here and there, and telephoned to Mrs. Phillips to bring fresh garments for Amy. Neither had time to get a chill. A pair of kindly servant-maids, who were loitering on the shore with their young men, insisted on carrying the heroine of the afternoon into retirement, where they expeditiously undressed her, rubbed her, and wrapped her in a quilt snatched from a lifesaving bed. Amy was cold indeed, and inclined to shiver. She understood, now, why Cope had not encouraged that bathing party at the dunes.

In a few minutes Medora Phillips tore up in her car, with Helga and a mountain of clothing and wraps. She was inclined to make the most of the occasion, and she did so. With Helga she quickly superseded the pair of sympathetic and ready maids, whom she allowed to fade into the background with too scant recognition of their services; and when she had got Amy thoroughly warmed and rehabilitated she turned her thought toward Cope. Here, certainly, was a young scholastic recluse who had an admirable faculty for getting into the public eye. If one section of Churchton society had talked about his performance at her dinner, all sections of it would now be discussing his new performance on the high seas. Suddenly she was struck with the notion that possibly his first lapse had not left him in condition to stand this second one.

===Chinese===

与此同时，站里的另一条船已经出发，去协助船主把那条单桅帆船扶正取回。船很快就正了过来，给拖了回去。船没有损伤，也没有科普付不起的账——第二天他得知这一点，大大地松了一口气。

救生站给了他一套干衣服，是从各处拼凑而来的，又打电话给菲利普斯太太，请她给艾米送几身干爽衣裳来。两个人谁都没有工夫着凉。两名好心的女仆——她们正各自同自己的年轻男友在岸边闲逛——执意把这位下午的女主角搀进僻静处，在那里手脚利落地替她脱了衣裳，给她擦身，又用从救生站的床铺上抓来的一床被子把她裹住。艾米确实冷了，直想打颤。她如今明白了，当初在沙丘那边，科普为什么不热心张罗那场游泳会。

不消几分钟，梅多拉·菲利普斯便乘车疾驰而至，带着赫尔加，还有一大堆小山似的衣裳披巾。她素来乐于把这种场合的用处发挥到十足，这次也果真发挥了个够。她带着赫尔加，很快便接管、取代了那两名热心而麻利的女仆——对她们的殷勤效劳，她只草草认可了一星半点，便任由她们退到幕后去了；等她把艾米彻底焐暖、收拾停当，她便把心思转到科普身上。瞧，这位年轻的学院隐士，可真有一种令人叹服的本事：总能把自己送进大众的眼皮底下去。倘若丘奇顿社交界中曾有一路人马谈论过他在她晚宴上的那番表演，那么如今全城各路人马都要来议论他这回在「公海」之上的新表演了。忽然间她被一个念头击中：他上回那场病倒，说不定还没有让他的身子骨恢复到经得起这第二场折腾的地步。

===Original===

“How are you feeling?” she asked anxiously. “No chill? No shock?”

“I’m all right,” he declared. “One of the boys has just given me a drink of﻿—of﻿—” But it was a beverage the use of which was not generally approved in Churchton.

Mrs. Phillips turned round suddenly. “Amy, did you have a drink, too, of﻿—of﻿—of﻿—if ‘Of’ is what you call it?”

“I did,” said Amy firmly; “and I feel the better for it.”

“Well, get in, then, and I’ll take you home.”

===Chinese===

「你觉得怎么样？」她焦急地问。「没着凉吧？没受惊吧？」

「我没事，」他声称。「有个小伙子刚刚给我喝了一口——一口——」可那是一种在丘奇顿不获公众认可的饮料。

菲利普斯太太霍地转过身来。「艾米，你是不是也喝了一口——一口——一口——要是那东西的名字就叫『一口』的话？」

「喝了，」艾米斩钉截铁地说；「而且我觉得喝了反倒好些。」

「那好，上车吧，我送你回家。」

===Original===

Peter grinned from the front seat of the car; Mrs. Phillips placed herself between the two victims on the back one; the lifesavers, who had kept the discarded garments to dry, gave them all a few smiles and hand wavings; the two young women and their two young men looked on with some deference; the general crowd gave a little mock-cheer before turning its Sunday leisure to other forms of interest; and the small party whirled away.

Amy leaned a tired, moist head, but a happy one, on Mrs. Phillips’ shoulder. “He was so quick,” she breathed, “and so brave, and so strong.” She professed to believe that he had saved her life. Cope, silent as he looked straight ahead between Peter and Helga, was almost afraid that she had saved his.

===Chinese===

彼得在汽车前座上咧着嘴笑；菲利普斯太太在后排两位落难者中间坐定；救生队员们留住了那两身换下来的衣裳以便晾干，朝他们这一行人微笑挥手；那两名年轻女子和她们各自的年轻男友怀着几分敬意望着这一幕；围观的人群发出了一小阵打趣的欢呼，随即把星期日的闲暇转向别的有趣物事；这一小队人马便风也似的去了。

艾米把一颗疲倦的、湿漉漉的、然而快活的头，靠在菲利普斯太太的肩上。「他多么敏捷，」她低声说，「多么勇敢，多么有力。」她一口咬定，是他救了她的性命。科普沉默着，坐在彼得和赫尔加之间直望着前方，心里却几乎担心：救了他的命的，倒是她。

## XX Cope Has a Distressful Christmas / 第二十章 科普过了一个愁苦的圣诞节

===Original===

Cope awakened at seven. After an early interval of happy lightness, there came suddenly and heavily the crushing sense of his predicament. How monstrous it was that one instant of time, one ill-considered action, one poorly-chosen word could clamp a repellent burden on a man for the rest of his life!

Well, he must expect telephone messages and letters. They came. That afternoon Mrs. Peck had “a lady’s voice” to report: “It sounded like a *young* lady’s voice,” she added. And she looked at Cope with some curiosity: a “young lady” asking for him over the wire was the rarest thing in the world.

Next day came the first note. The handwriting was utterly new to him; but his intuition, applied instantly to the envelope, told him of the source. The nail, driven, was now to be clinched. She had the right to ask him to come; and she did ask him to come﻿—“soon.”

===Chinese===

科普（Cope）七点钟便醒了。最初先有一段愉快轻盈的短暂时光，随后，他那窘境的碾压之感便突然而沉重地袭来。时间的一瞬、一次欠思量的举动、一句择词失当的话，竟能把一副可憎的重负牢牢钳在一个人的余生上，这是何等悖理的事啊！

唉，他得料到会有电话和信件来。它们果真来了。当天下午，佩克太太（Mrs. Peck）有一副「女士的嗓音」可禀报：「听上去像一位*年轻*女士的声音呢。」她补了一句。她带着几分好奇打量科普：一位「年轻女士」打电话来找他，这可是天下最稀罕的事了。

第二天，第一张字条来了。那笔迹他全然陌生；可他的直觉一经触到信封，便告诉了他信的来历。钉子既已敲进，如今该把钉尖敲弯扣死了。她有权要求他前去；她也确实要求他前去——「尽快」。

===Original===

Cope’s troubled eyes sought the calendar above his table. How many days to Christmas? How much time might he spend in Freeford? How long before Christmas might he arrange to leave Churchton? The holidays at home loomed as a harbor of refuge. By shortening as far as possible the interval here and by lengthening as far as possible the stay with his family, he might cut down, in some measure, the imminent threatenings of awkwardness and constraint; then, beyond the range of anything but letters, he might study the unpleasant situation at his leisure and determine a future course.

He set himself to answer Amy’s note. He hoped, he said, to see her in a few days, but he was immensely busy in closing the term-work before the holidays; he also suggested that their affair﻿—“their” affair!﻿—be kept quiet for the present. Yet he had all too facile a vision of beatific meditations that were like enough to give the situation away to all the household; and he was nervously aware of Amy Leffingwell as continually on the verge of bubbling confidences.

He also wrote to Lemoyne. His letter was less an announcement than a confession.

===Chinese===

科普烦恼的双眼寻觅着桌子上方的日历。离圣诞节还有多少天？他可以在弗里福德（Freeford）盘桓多少时日？他可以安排在圣诞前多早离开丘奇顿（Churchton）？家中的假期隐然耸现为一座避难的港湾。尽量缩短这边的时日、尽量延长与家人同住的日子，那迫在眉睫的尴尬与拘束的威胁，他多少可以削减几分；然后，到了除了信件之外什么都够不着的地方，他可以从容研究这个不愉快的局面，并决定未来的方针。

他定下心来回复艾米（Amy）的字条。他说，他希望几天内见到她，但节前收束学期功课，他忙得不可开交；他还提议，他们的事——「他们的」事！——暂且保密。然而，他太容易想见那副有福陶醉的沉思神态，那神态十有八九会把这局面泄露给全宅的人；他也不安地意识到，艾米·莱芬韦尔（Amy Leffingwell）时刻处在心事脱口而出的边缘。

他也给勒莫因（Lemoyne）写了信。那封信与其说是通报，毋宁说是忏悔。

===Original===

“I like this!” began Lemoyne’s reply, with abrupt, impetuous sarcasm. “You have claimed, more than once,” he went on, “to have steadied me and kept me out of harm’s way; but I’ve never yet made any such demands on you as you are making on me. This thing can’t go on, and you know it as well as I do. Nip it. Nip it now. Don’t think that our intimacy is to end in any such fashion as this, for it isn’t﻿—especially at this particular time.”﻿ ﻿…

Lemoyne proceeded to practical matters.

> “If that room is still free, engage it from the first of January. I will have a few things sent down. Father is weakening a little. Anyhow, I’ve got enough money for a couple of months. I will join you in Freeford between Christmas and New Year’s (nearer the latter, probably), and we will go back together.”﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

「这我倒喜欢！」勒莫因的回信这样开头，带着突兀而激烈的讥诮。「你不止一回声称，」他接着写道，「说你扶稳了我、使我免于祸事；可我从来不曾对你提出过这样的要求，像你如今对我提出的这样。这件事不能继续下去了，这一点你知道得同我一样清楚。掐掉它。现在就掐掉它。别以为咱们的交情会以这样的一种方式告终，因为它不会——尤其是在这个特别的时刻。」……

勒莫因接着谈起了实际事务。

> 「要是那个房间还空着，就从一月一日起把它租下。我会让人把几件东西寄过去。父亲那边有点松动了。无论如何，我手头的钱够维持两个月。我将在圣诞与新年之间到弗里福德与你相会（多半靠近新年那边），然后我们一起回去。」……

===Original===

Cope rather took heart from these rough, outspoken lines. Lemoyne was commonly neither rough nor outspoken; but here was an emergency, involving his own interests, which must be dealt with decisively. Cope seemed to feel salvation on the way. Perhaps that was why he still did so little to save himself. He took the new room; he had one meeting with Amy; and he left for home at least two days before he was strictly entitled to do so.

The meeting took place in Mrs. Phillips’ drawing-room; he would trust himself to no more strolls on the campus, to no more confabs in college halls. There was protection in numbers, and numbers seldom failed beneath Medora Phillips’ roof. They failed this time, however. Mrs. Phillips and Hortense were away at a reading; only Amy and Carolyn were at home. Cope seized on Carolyn as at a straw. He thanked her warmly again for her halting offices in the matter of that last song, and he begged that he might hear some of her recent verse. His appeal was vehement, almost boisterous: Carolyn, surprised, felt that he was ready at last to grant her a definite personality.

Amy tried in vain to remove Carolyn from the board. But Carolyn, like Hortense, had finally joined the ranks of the “recognized”; she was determined (being still ignorant, Cope was glad to see, regarding Amy’s claims) to make this recognition so marked as to last beyond the moment. She played a little﻿—not well. She read. She even accompanied Amy to the door at the close of Cope’s short stay. He shook hands with them both. He had decided that he would do no more than this with Amy, in any event, and Carolyn’s presence made his predetermined course easy, even obligatory. Yet he went out into the night feeling, somehow, that he had acted solely on his resolution and that he might consider himself a man of some decisiveness, after all. Amy had looked disappointed, but had contrived to whisper that she would write from Iowa. That, of course, was to be looked for, and would represent the combined efforts of herself and her home circle; yet he had a fortnight for consideration and counsel.

===Chinese===

科普倒是从这些粗率直率的字句里得了些底气。勒莫因平日既不粗率，也不直率；可眼下是一桩急事，牵涉到他自己的利益，必须果断处置。科普仿佛觉得救星已在路上。也许正因如此，他才仍旧这样懒于自救。他租下了那个新房间；他与艾米见了一面；然后动身回了家——至少比他严格有权离开的日子早了两天。

这次会面安排在菲利普斯太太（Mrs. Phillips）的客厅里；他再也不敢信托自己去校园里散步，不敢再去学院楼里絮语。人多便有掩护，而在梅多拉·菲利普斯（Medora Phillips）的屋顶下，人数是很少短缺的。然而这一回却短缺了。菲利普斯太太和霍滕丝（Hortense）外出听朗诵去了；家里只有艾米和卡罗琳（Carolyn）。科普抓住卡罗琳，如同抓住一根救命稻草。他再度热情感谢她在上一支歌那桩事上那番迟疑不定的斡旋，并恳求听几行她近来的诗作。他的恳求热烈得近乎喧嚷：卡罗琳又是惊讶，又觉得他总算肯承认她是一个有独立身份的人了。

艾米设法把卡罗琳从棋盘上除掉，却是徒劳。但卡罗琳同霍滕丝一样，终于跻身「受认可者」的行列；她打定主意（科普乐见她对艾米的权利主张仍一无所知）要使这份认可彰明较著，足以延续到此刻之外。她弹了一会儿琴——弹得不怎么样。她朗诵了诗。科普短短逗留的尾声，她甚至还陪艾米一同送到门口。他与两人一一握了手。他已拿定主意，无论如何对艾米不再越此一步，而卡罗琳的在场使他的既定方针变得轻松，甚至成了义不容辞。然而走出屋来、步入夜色时，他不知怎么竟觉得，自己是全然依着决断行事，未尝不可自认为是个颇有决断力的人了。艾米面有失望之色，却还是设法悄声说了一句，她要从艾奥瓦（Iowa）写信来。这当然在意料之中，而且将代表着她与她家乡一圈人的共同努力；不过他还有两个星期可供考虑和商议。

===Original===

Cope, during his first few days at home, was moody and abstracted: his parents found him adding little to the Christmas cheer. His mother, always busy over domestic cares and now busier than ever, thought that he must have been working too hard. She would stand in the kitchen door with a half-trimmed pie on one hand and ponder him as he sat in the dining-room, staring absorbedly at the Franklin stove. His father, who saw him chiefly in the evening, by the gaslight of the old-fashioned house, found his face slightly pinched: was his pocket pinched too, and would he be likely, before leaving, to ask help toward making up a deficit? His sister Rosalys, who lived a life of dry routine, figured him as deep in love. He let several days pass without hinting what the real situation was.

There was interest all round when, the day before Christmas, the postman came along the bleak and flimsy street and left a letter for him. Cope was away from the house, and Rosalys, studying the envelope’s penmanship and even its postmark, found vague confirmation of her theory: some college girl﻿—one of his own students, probably﻿—was home on vacation just as he was. If so, a “small town” person of caste and character like themselves; not brilliant, but safe. She set up the letter edgewise on the back parlor mantelpiece.

When Cope came in at noon and saw the letter, his face fell. He put it in his pocket, sat silent at table, and disappeared as soon as the meal was over. Rosalys, whose pupils were off her mind for a few days and who had thought to spare, began to shade her theory.

===Chinese===

科普回到家里的头几天，郁郁寡欢、心不在焉：父母发现他给圣诞的欢乐添不了多少光彩。他母亲素来为家务操劳，如今更是忙上加忙，心想他必是用功过度了。她会一手托着只修了一半的馅饼站在厨房门口，端详坐在餐室里的儿子，而他正出了神地凝望那座富兰克林火炉。他父亲主要在晚间见到他，在老式宅子的煤气灯光下，发觉他的脸微微消瘦了：是不是他的钱袋也瘪了，临走之前，他会不会开口求援、以补亏空？他姐姐罗莎莉丝（Rosalys）过着一板一眼的枯燥日子，把他想象成热恋正深。他一连让好几天过去，丝毫没有暗示真实处境如何。

圣诞前一天，邮差沿着那条萧索而屋舍单薄的街道走来，给他留下了一封信，全家上下顿时都来了兴致。科普不在家，罗莎莉丝端详信封上的笔迹、连邮戳也不放过，觉得隐隐证实了她的猜想：某位女大学生——多半是他自己的学生——正同他一样放假在家。倘真如此，那便是一位同他们一样有门第、有品性的「小镇」人物；不算出色，但稳妥。她把那封信竖着立在后客厅的壁炉架上。

中午科普回来看见那封信，脸便沉了下来。他把它揣进口袋，饭桌上闷坐无言，一用完饭便不见人影。罗莎莉丝的学生们这几天不用她惦记，她本有大把闲暇可供悬想，如今便开始给自己的猜想罩上层层阴影。

===Original===

Cope read the letter in the low-ceiled back bedroom (the ceiling sloped away on one side) which had been his for so many years. Those years of happy boyhood﻿—how far away they seemed now, and how completely past! Surely he had never thought to come back to these familiar walls to such effect as this.﻿ ﻿… Well, what did it say?

It said, in its four pages (yes, Amy had really limited herself thus), how joyous she was that the dear Christmas season had brought her such a beautiful love-gift; it said that mother was so pleased and happy﻿—and even mentioned a sudden aunt; it said how willingly she would wait on until.﻿ ﻿…

That evening Cope made his announcement. They were all seated round the reading-lamp in the back parlor, where the old Brussels carpet looked dim and where only venerated age kept the ornate French clock from seeming tawdry. Cope looked down at the carpet and up at the clock, and spoke.

===Chinese===

科普在后卧室里读那封信——那间低顶的屋子（顶棚往一侧斜下去）多年来一直是他的。那些幸福的少年岁月——如今显得多么遥远，又是多么彻底地过去了！他万万不曾想到，回到这几堵熟悉的墙壁之间，竟会是为着这样一桩事由。……那么，信上说了些什么呢？

信上说了——足有四页（是的，艾米当真只限制自己到了这个地步）——亲爱的圣诞时节带给她这样一件美丽的爱情礼物，她是多么欢喜；信上说母亲又是多么称心快活——甚至还提到一位突然冒出来的姨妈；信上说她会多么心甘情愿地一直等下去，直到……。

当晚，科普宣布了他的消息。一家人正围坐在后客厅的阅读灯下，那里，旧日的布鲁塞尔地毯显得晦暗，那座华丽的法国时钟，也全凭受人敬重的年岁才免于显得俗艳。科普低头望了望地毯，又抬头望了望时钟，开口说话了。
