Well, he laughed a little, here and there﻿—obligingly, I might say. But there was no ‘chew’ in the thing for him﻿—nothing to fill his intellectual maw. He’s a serious youngster, after all﻿—exuberant as he seems. I felt him appraising me as a gay old irresponsible.﻿ ﻿…”

“ ‘Old’﻿—you are not to use that word. Come, don’t say that he﻿—that he venerated you!”

“Oh, not at all. During the six hours we were together﻿—train, club, theatre, and train again﻿—he never once called me ‘sir’; he never once employed our clumsy, repellent Anglo-Saxon mode of address, ‘mister’; in fact, he never employed any mode of address at all. He got round it quite cleverly﻿—on system, as I soon began to perceive; and not for a moment did he forget that the system was in operation. He used, straight through, a sort of generalized manner﻿—I might have been anywhere between twenty and sixty-five.”

They were now in front of the stationer’s show-window, and there were few people in the quiet thoroughfare to jostle them.

===Chinese===

收款柜员把凭单交给了她。她把凭单放进手提包，又设法绕过一辆婴儿车，两人便上街去了。

「你那出『戏』又怎么样？」她接着说。「如今我们能称之为戏剧的，大约也就这么些了。」

「那个嘛，或许就没有那么顺当了。我带他去看了一出『喜剧』——如今人们便这样称呼他们那闹剧加笑料的大杂烩。『喜剧』！——但愿梅瑞狄斯能亲眼看看！嗯，他倒也笑了笑，这里一声，那里一声——可以说是碍于情面。可那东西里没有一点可供他咀嚼的——填不满他智识的胃纳。他到底是个严肃的年轻人——尽管看上去兴致蓬勃。我觉出他把我掂量成了一个轻浮草率、不负责任的老家伙。……」

「『老』——这个词你可不许用。得啦，别说他——别说他把你敬仰了！」

「哦，全然没有。我们相处的六个钟头里——火车、俱乐部、戏院，再是火车——他一次也没有叫过我『先生』；也一次没有用过我们那套笨拙讨嫌的盎格鲁-撒克逊称呼法，称人『某先生』；实际上，他压根什么称呼都没有用。他把这一关绕得相当巧妙——是照着一整套章法来的，我很快就察觉了；而这套章法一经施行，他一刻也没有忘记它正在施行。他自始至终用着一种泛泛的、不分老少的作派——我尽可以是他二十岁到六十五岁之间的任何年纪。」

这时两人已走到文具店的橱窗前，清静的大街上行人寥寥，无人挤撞。

===Original===

Medora smiled.

“How clever; how charming!” she said. “Leaving you altogether free to pick your own age. I hope you didn’t go beyond thirty-five. You must have been quite charming in your early thirties.”

“That’s kind of you, I’m sure; but I don’t believe that I was ever ‘charming’ at *any* age. I think you’ve used that word once too often. I was a quiet, studious lad, with nice notions, but possibly something of a prig. I was less ‘charming’ than correct. The young ladies had the greatest confidence in me﻿—not one of them was ever ‘afraid.’ ”

“Why, how horrid! How utterly unsatisfactory! Nor their mothers?”

“No. And I’m still single, as you’re advised. And I’m not sure that the young gentlemen cared much more for me. If I had had a little more ‘gimp’ and verve, I might have equalled the particular young gentleman of whom we have been discoursing. But.﻿ ﻿…”

His obviously artificial style of speech concealed, as she guessed, some real feeling.

===Chinese===

梅多拉莞尔一笑。

「多聪明；多可爱！」她说。「让你全然自由，自己挑自己的年纪。我希望你没有挑过三十五岁。你三十出头的时候，一定相当可爱。」

「多谢你的好意；不过我不信自己在任何年纪上『可爱』过。我看你这个字眼已经用得过头了。我从前是个安静好学的少年，见识不俗，但也许有几分道学气。我与其说『可爱』，不如说端正无失。年轻小姐们对我怀着最大的信任——没有一位曾经『芳心忐忑』。」

「哎呀，多糟糕！多叫人不满意！她们的妈妈也不曾吗？」

「不曾。而且，如你所知，我至今独身。我也不敢说年轻先生们对我的好感就多多少。当年我若多有一点『劲儿』和锐气，或许就能比得上我们方才谈论的这一位年轻人了。可是。……」

照她猜来，他这副分明做作的谈吐底下，藏着几分真情。

===Original===

“Oh, if you insist on disparaging yourself﻿ …!”

“I was quite as coolly correct as I apprehend him to be; and if I could only have contrived to compass the charming, as well, who knows what﻿—?”

“You don’t like my word. Is there a better, a more suitable?”

“No. You have the mot juste.”

He threw a finger through the wide pane of glass. “Is that the sort of thing you are after? Those boxes of pale gray are rather good.”

“I never buy from the show-window. Come in, and help me choose.”

===Chinese===

「哦，你偏要这样作践自己……！」

「我当年的端正冷静，与我看他今日不相上下；倘若那时我还能设法把『可爱』也一并修成，谁知道会——？」

「你不喜欢我的字眼。有更好的、更合适的吗？」

「没有。你用的正是那个恰切的字眼（*mot juste*）。」

他隔着一大幅玻璃伸指一点。「你找的是这一类东西吗？那几只浅灰色的盒子相当不错。」

「我从不买橱窗里陈列的东西。进来吧，帮我挑挑。」

===Original===

“I love to shop,” he said, in a mock ecstasy. “With others,” he added. “I like to follow money in﻿—and to contribute taste and experience.”

Over the stationer’s counter she said:

“Save Sunday. We are going out to the sandhills.”

“Thank you. Very well. Most glad to.”

“And you are to bring him.”

===Chinese===

「我爱极了买东西，」他说，装出一副心醉神迷的样子。「陪别人买，」他补了一句。「我喜欢看着钱花出去——再搭上我的品味和阅历。」

隔着文具店的柜台，她说：

「把星期日留出来。我们要到沙丘那边去。」

「谢谢。好的。非常乐意。」

「你还要把他带来。」

===Original===

“Him?”

“Bertram Cope.”

“Why, I’ve given him six hours within two or three days. And now you’re asking me to give him sixteen.”

“Sixteen﻿—or more. But you’re not giving them to him. You’re giving them to all of us. You’re giving them to me. The day is likely to be fine and settled, and I’d recommend your catching the 8:30 train. I shall have my full load in the car. And more, if I have to take along Helga. Try to reach us by one, or a quarter past.”

===Chinese===

「他？」

「伯特伦·科普。」

「哎呀，两三天里我已经给了他六个钟头。现在你又要我给他十六个。」

「十六个——或者还不止。不过你不是把它们给他，而是给我们大家，是给我。那天多半晴朗安定，我劝你赶八点半的火车。我的汽车里会坐得满满当当。要是还非带上赫尔加不可，那就更满了。尽量在一点钟、或一点一刻的时候到我们这儿。」

===Original===

Mrs. Phillips had lately taken on a house among the sand dunes beyond the state line. This singular region had recently acquired so wide a reputation for utter neglect and desolation that﻿—despite its distance from town, whether in miles or in hours﻿—no one could quite afford to ignore it. Picnics, pageants, encampments and excursions all united in proclaiming its remoteness, its silence, its vacuity. Along the rim of ragged slopes which put a term to the hundreds of miles of water that spread from the north, people tramped, bathed, canoed, motored and weekended. Within a few seasons Duneland had acquired as great a reputation for *prahlerische Dunkelheit*﻿—for ostentatious obscurity﻿—as ever was enjoyed even by Schiller’s *Wallenstein*. “Lovers of Nature” and “Friends of the Landscape” moved through its distant and inaccessible purlieus in squads and cohorts. Everybody had to spend there at least one Sunday in the summer season. There were enthusiasts whose interest ran from March to November. There were fanatics who insisted on trips thitherward in January. And there were one or two super-fanatics﻿—ranking ahead even of the fishermen and the sand-diggers﻿—who clung to that weird and changing region the whole year through.

Medora Phillips’ house was several miles beyond the worst of the hurly-burly. There were no tents in sight, even in August. Nor was the honk of the motor-horn heard even during the most tumultuous Sundays. The spot was harder to reach than most others along the twenty miles of nicked and ragged brim which helped enclose the wide blue area of the Big Water, but was better worth while when you got there. Her little tract lay beyond the more prosaic reaches that were furnished chiefly in the light green of deciduous trees; it was part of a long stretch thickly set for miles with the dark and sombre green of pines. Our nature-lover had taken, the year before, a neglected and dilapidated old farmhouse and had made it into what her friends and habitués liked to call a bungalow. The house had been put up﻿—in the rustic spirit which ignores all considerations of landscape and outlook﻿—behind a well-treed dune which allowed but the merest glimpse of the lake; however, a walk of six or eight minutes led down to the beach, and in the late afternoon the sun came with grand effect across the gilded water and through the tall pine-trunks which bordered the zigzag path. Medora had added a sleeping porch, a dining-porch and a lean-to for the car; and she entertained there through the summer lavishly, even if intermittently and casually.

“No place in the world like it!” she would declare enthusiastically to the yet inexperienced and therefore the still unconverted. “The spring arrives weeks ahead of our spring in town, and the fall lingers on for weeks after. Come to our shore, where the fauna and flora of the whole country meet in one. All the wild birds pass in their migrations; and the flowers!” Then she would expatiate on the trailing arbutus in April, and the vast sheets of pale blue lupines in early June, and the yellow, sunlike blossoms of the prickly-pear in July, and the red glories of painter’s-brush and bittersweet and sumach in September. “No wonder,” she would say, “that they have to distribute handbills on the excursion-trains asking people to leave the flowers alone!”

===Chinese===

菲利普斯太太新近在州界那边的沙丘丛中置了一所房子。这片奇特的地区，近来以彻底的荒置与苍凉赢得了如此广大的名声，以致——尽管它离城无论按英里算还是按钟点算都不算近——谁也不敢全然把它无视。野餐会、露天演出、扎营与游览，众口一词地宣扬着它的僻远、它的沉寂、它的空无。在那道给北方铺展而来的数百英里水域画上尽头的残破坡脊边缘，人们徒步、洗浴、划独木舟、驾车兜风、度周末。不出几个季度，沙丘地带便赢得了 *prahlerische Dunkelheit*——夸炫的幽晦——的大名，连席勒的《华伦斯坦》素来享有的名声也不过如此。「自然爱好者」和「风景之友」们成小队、成大队地穿行于它那些遥远难至的腹地。人人都得在夏季到那儿消磨至少一个星期日。有热心人，兴趣从三月绵延到十一月。有狂热之士，非要在一月里前往游历。还有一两位超级狂热者——名次甚至排在渔夫和挖沙人之前——一年到头厮守着那片离奇多变的地带。

梅多拉·菲利普斯的房子，在最喧嚣嘈杂的地段再往前好几英里。即便在八月，也望不见一顶帐篷；即便在最闹腾的星期日，也听不见一声汽车喇叭。沿着那二十英里残缺参差、帮着围拢「大湖」辽阔蓝色水面的湖缘，这地方比大多数别处都更难抵达，可是到了那里，也就更不虚此行。她那一小块地皮，坐落在以落叶树的浅绿为主要装点的平淡地段再往里；是一长条绵延数英里、密密覆着松林暗沉浓绿的地带的一部分。我们这位大自然的爱好者，去年接手了一座无人照管、破败倾颓的老农舍，把它改造成她的朋友们和熟客们乐意称作「平房别墅」的宅子。房子当初盖起来时——本着一种全不理会风景与眺望的村野精神——躲在一座林木葱郁的沙丘背后，仅容人对湖面窥得一眼；不过，走上六八分钟便可下到湖滩，而到了傍晚时分，太阳越过镀金的水面，穿过界临蜿蜒小径的高大松干照过来，气象十分壮丽。梅多拉又添了一间露宿廊、一间用餐廊和一间停放汽车的单坡披屋；整个夏天她在那里宴饮待客，出手阔绰，尽管时断时续、不拘章法。

「天下再没有第二个这样的地方！」她会热情洋溢地对那些尚未亲历、因而也尚未皈依的人宣告。「春天比我们城里的春天早到好几个星期，秋天又迟迟流连，多逗留好几个星期。到我们的湖滨来吧，全国的鸟兽草木都在这里汇于一堂。一切野鸟迁徙时都打这里经过；还有那些花儿！」接着她便细细铺陈：四月里蔓生的五月花（trailing arbutus），六月初大片大片淡蓝的羽扇豆，七月里仙人掌那形似骄阳的黄花，九月里画笔花、南蛇藤与盐肤木的红艳。「难怪，」她会说，「他们不得不在游览列车上散发传单，请人们不要糟蹋花儿！」

===Original===

“How shocking!” Cope had cried, with his resonant laugh, when this phase of the situation was brought to his attention. “Are the automobile people any better?”

Randolph had told him of some of the other drawbacks involved in the excursion. “It’s a long way to go, even when you pass up the trolley and make a single big bolt by train. And it leads through an industrial region that is mighty unprepossessing﻿—little beauty until almost the end. And even when you get there, it may all seem a slight and simple affair for the time and trouble taken﻿—unless you really like Nature. And lastly,” he said, with a sidelong glance at Cope, “you may find yourself, as the day wears on, getting a little too much of my company.”

“Oh, I hope that doesn’t mean,” returned Cope, with another ingenuous unchaining of his native resonance, “that you are afraid of getting a little too much of mine! I’m fond of novelty, and nobody can frighten me.”

===Chinese===

「太不像话了！」当事情的这一层被提请他注意时，科普曾这样喊道，带着他洪亮的笑声。「坐汽车的人难道就强些吗？」

伦道夫也对他讲过这次出游牵涉的其他几桩美中不足。「路可不近，就算你放过电车、一口气坐火车直闯过去也一样。一路上要经过一片工业区，那模样实在说不上悦目——快到终点之前几乎谈不上什么美景。就算到了地方，比起所花费的时间与辛劳，一切或许还显得单薄简陋——除非你真心热爱自然。最后，」他侧眼瞟了一下科普，说道，「随着白日推移，你也许会发觉，我这同伴叫你有点消受太多了。」

「哦，我希望这可不意味着，」科普答道，又一次天真无防地放开了他天赋的洪亮嗓音，「你是害怕我的陪伴叫你消受太多！我喜欢新鲜事儿，谁也吓不倒我。」

===Original===

“If that’s the case, let’s get away as early in the day as we can. Breakfasts, of course, are late in every household on Sunday. So let’s meet at the Maroon-and-Purple Tavern at seven-thirty, and make a flying start at eight.”

Sunday morning came clear and calm and warm to the town﻿—a belated September day, or possibly an early intimation of Indian summer﻿—and it promised to be even more delightful in the favored region toward which our friends were journeying. After they had cleared many miles of foundries and railroad crossings, and had paralleled for a last half-hour a distant succession of sandhills, wooded or glistening white, they were set down at a small group of farmhouses, with a varied walk of five miles before them. Half a mile through a shaded country lane; another half-mile along a path that led across low, damp ground through thickets of hazel and brier; a third half-mile over a light soil, increasingly sandy, beneath oaks and lindens and pines which cloaked the outlines of the slopes ahead; and finally a great mound of pure sand that slanted up into a blue sky and made its own horizon.

“We’ve taken things easy,” said Randolph, who had been that way before, “and I hope we have enough breath left for our job. There it lies, right in front of us.”

===Chinese===

「既然这样，咱们就尽量赶一天里最早的时辰动身。星期日家家户户的早饭当然都晚。那咱们七点半在褐紫酒馆（Maroon-and-Purple Tavern）碰头，八点便疾速启程。」

星期日早晨来到城里，晴朗、无风而温暖——一个迟来的九月天，或者竟是「小阳春」提早递来的讯息——而在我们这几位朋友正赶赴的那片受上天眷顾的地带，天色有望愈加可爱。他们驶过了绵延许多英里的铸造厂和铁路道口，又在最后半个钟头里与远处一连串沙丘并行——那些沙丘或覆着林木，或闪着白光——随后被安顿在一小片农舍前，面前是五英里景致多变的步行路。半英里，穿过浓荫覆顶的乡间小径；又半英里，沿一条小道跨过低下潮湿的洼地，穿过榛树与荆棘的密丛；再半英里，走在轻质的土壤上，愈行愈沙，头顶是橡树、椴树与松树，掩住了前方坡冈的轮廓；最后，是一座纯沙的大丘，斜斜地插上蓝天，自成一线天际。

「咱们一路走得从容，」曾走过这条路的伦道夫说，「但愿剩下的气力还够咱们干这一场。它就卧在那儿，正在我们跟前。」

===Original===

“No favor asked here,” declared Cope. He gave a sly, sidewise glance, as if to ask how the other might stand as to leg-muscles and wind.

“Up we go,” said Randolph.

===Chinese===

「到了这儿，可谁也不用让谁，」科普宣称道。他狡黠地斜睨一眼，仿佛在探问对方腿上的筋骨和胸中的气息如今是何成色。

「上吧，」伦道夫说。
